$1$-Lipschitz functions approximate every $1$-Lipschitz one uniformly, and
the Kantorovich--Rubinstein duality gives the bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[explicit ranges]\label{cor:bpranges}
For every even $L\ge2n+4$, $\mathrm{BP}(n,1-\alpha_n)$ holds for every real
$f\in L^2(\mu)$ in the following cases.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=*]
\item $G=\SU(2)$, $\lvert\beta_W\rvert<\frac29$, with
\[
\alpha_n=\frac{\lvert\beta_W\rvert(18n-3)}{(4-18\lvert\beta_W\rvert)n^2},
\]
whenever $\lvert\beta_W\rvert<4n^2/(18n^2+18n-3)$; for $n=2$ this is
$\lvert\beta_W\rvert<\frac{16}{105}$.\footnote{With $c=\lvert\beta_W
\rvert/4$ the condition $18c<1$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:bpstrong} reads
$\lvert\beta_W\rvert<\frac29$, and $\alpha_n<1$ reads $\lvert\beta_W\rvert
(18n-3)<(4-18\lvert\beta_W\rvert)n^2$, that is, $\lvert\beta_W\rvert(18n^2+
18n-3)<4n^2$; at $n=2$ this is $105\lvert\beta_W\rvert<16$. As $n\to
\infty$ the threshold $4n^2/(18n^2+18n-3)$ increases to $\frac29$.} At $\lvert\beta_W\rvert\le\frac18$,
$\alpha_n\le\alpha_2\le0.59$ for every $n\ge2$; at $\lvert\beta_W\rvert\le
\frac15$, $\alpha_n\le0.982$ for every $n\ge9$.
\item $G=\SU(3)$ and $\beta_W\ge0$ with
\[
\zeta(\beta_W)=\frac{3r\sqrt{\epsilon(r)}}{\gamma(r)}<1,\qquad r=2\beta_W,
\]
where $\epsilon$ and $\gamma$ are as in Theorem~\ref{thm:su3}, with
\[
\alpha_n=\frac{\zeta(\beta_W)\,(18n-3)}{18\,(1-\zeta(\beta_W))\,n^2}.
\]
The function $\zeta$ is increasing, $\zeta(0.35)\le0.8584$, and for
$\beta_W\le0.35$ and $n\ge6$, $\alpha_n\le0.3368\,(18n-3)/n^2\le0.9824$.
\item $G\subset\U(N)$ closed and connected with semisimple Lie algebra,
$24N\lvert\beta\rvert<\kappa_G$, with
\[
\alpha_n=\frac{N\lvert\beta\rvert(18n-3)}{(\kappa_G-24N\lvert\beta\rvert)
n^2},
\]
whenever $N\lvert\beta\rvert<\kappa_Gn^2/(24n^2+18n-3)$.
\end{enumerate}
In each case, for fixed $\beta$ in the range, $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ holds
with one $\varpi>0$ for all $n$ from some $n_0(\beta)$ on, uniformly in the
torus, and the best constant tends to $1$ as $n\to\infty$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
(i) As in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:su2}, without the gauge: the law of
$U_e$ is the von Mises--Fisher law $\nu_b$ with $b=4\beta\sum_{p\ni e}\bar
s_p$, a partner moved by $\delta$ in the chordal metric moves $b$ by
$4\lvert\beta\rvert\delta$, and Theorem~\ref{q-thm:quarter} gives $C^0_{ee'}
\le\lvert\beta\rvert=\lvert\beta_W\rvert/4$. Theorem~\ref{thm:bpstrong}(i)
applies with $c/(1-18c)=\lvert\beta_W\rvert/(4-18\lvert\beta_W\rvert)$.
At $\lvert\beta_W\rvert=\frac18$, $\alpha_2=\frac{33}{56}$; at $\lvert
\beta_W\rvert=\frac15$, $\alpha_n=(18n-3)/(2n^2)$ and $\alpha_9=\frac{159}
{162}$; $\alpha_n$ increases with $\lvert\beta_W\rvert$.

(ii) The six staples of a link are products of matrices of $\SU(3)$, so
$b\in rK$, and as in~\eqref{eq:alpha} and Lemma~\ref{lem:su3modulus},
$C^0_{ee'}\le N\beta\Lambda(r)\le c$ with $18c=3r\sqrt{\epsilon(r)}/\gamma
(r)$ in the Hilbert--Schmidt metric; $\zeta$ increases because $\epsilon$
is nondecreasing and $\gamma$ decreasing. By Lemma~\ref{lem:su3haar}(iii)
and the tail bound in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:su3}, at $r=0.7$ the
terms $m_nr^n/n!$, rounded up, are $1$, $0$, $0.1225$, $0.0142917$,
$0.0075032$, $0.0013131$, $0.000332$, $0.000059$ and $0.0000109$, with sum
at most $1.1460099$, and the tail is less than $4\cdot10^{-6}$; so
$Z_*(0.7)\le1.146014$, $e^{-0.14}\ge0.869342$, $\frac{5e^{-0.14}}{9Z_*(0.7)}
\ge0.421432$ and $\epsilon(0.7)\le0.578568<0.76064^2$. With $\gamma(0.7)=
\frac{1191}{640}$, $\zeta(0.35)\le2.1\cdot0.76064\cdot\frac{640}{1191}\le
0.8584$, and $\zeta/(18(1-\zeta))\le0.3368$; at $n=6$, $(18n-3)/n^2=
\frac{35}{12}$.

(iii) Lemma~\ref{lem:bponelink} gives $c=N\lvert\beta\rvert/(\kappa_G-6N
\lvert\beta\rvert)$, so $18c<1$ is $24N\lvert\beta\rvert<\kappa_G$ and
$c/(1-18c)=N\lvert\beta\rvert/(\kappa_G-24N\lvert\beta\rvert)$.

In each case $\alpha_n\to0$; Proposition~\ref{prop:bpscales}(ii) below bounds
the best constant by $1+2/n$.
\end{proof}

For $\SU(3)$, the condition $\zeta<1$ of (ii) holds up to $\beta_W\approx
0.391$ by a numerical evaluation that no statement uses, against
$\frac3{16}$ from (iii); for $\SU(2)$, (i) reaches
$\frac29$ against $\frac1{12}$. These ranges lie inside those of
Secs.~\ref{sec:su2}--\ref{sec:su3blocks}, where the forest gauge gives a
gap directly; what Corollary~\ref{cor:bpranges} adds is the averaged
condition itself, on every torus, for every $n$ past $n_0(\beta)$ and on
all of $L^2(\mu)$. The gap it gives through Corollary~\ref{cor:avggap} is of
order $1/n$ and is not used. The dependence on $n$ is constrained in general
as follows. Write $\varpi^*_n$ for the best constant in $\mathrm{BP}(n,
\cdot)$ on $\Lambda_L$, and $P_V$ for the conditional expectation given the
links outside $V$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:bpscales}
Let $L\ge2m+4$ and $2\le n\le m$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=*]
\item If $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ holds, then so does $\mathrm{BP}(m,(n/m)^4
\varpi)$; that is, $n^4\varpi^*_n\le m^4\varpi^*_m$.
\item $\varpi^*_n\le1+2/n$.
\item Let $W_x$ be the cubes of side $m$. Suppose that $\mathrm{BP}(m,
\varpi)$ holds and that for every $x$, every real $f\in\mathcal A$ and
$\mu$-almost every $U$,
\[
\varpi_{\rm in}P_{W_x}\bigl((f-P_{W_x}f)^2\bigr)\le\frac1{n^4}\sum_{y:\,V_y
\subset W_x}P_{W_x}\bigl((f-P_{V_y}f)^2\bigr).
\]
Then $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi\varpi_{\rm in}(m/(m-n+1))^4)$ holds.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) If $V\subset W$, the $\sigma$-algebra of the links outside $W$ is
contained in that of the links outside $V$, so $P_W=P_WP_V$ and
$\lVert f-P_Wf\rVert^2=\lVert f-P_Vf\rVert^2+\lVert P_Vf-P_Wf\rVert^2$. The
cube $V_y$ of side $n$ lies in $W_x$ exactly when $y-x\in[0,m-n]^4$, so
for each $x$, $\mu((f-P_{W_x}f)^2)$ is at least the mean of $\mu((f-P_{V_y}
f)^2)$ over these $(m-n+1)^4$ points $y$, and each $y$ occurs for
$(m-n+1)^4$ points $x$. Summing, $\sum_x\mu((f-P_{W_x}f)^2)\ge\sum_y\mu((f-
P_{V_y}f)^2)$.

(ii) Let $f=\ReTr U_p$ for a plaquette $p$ at $y$ in the plane $\{\mu,\nu
\}$; $\Var_\mu(f)>0$. As in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:avgdecay},
$\mu((f-\mathcal P_xf)^2)\le\Var_\mu(f)$, with equality to zero unless $V_x$
contains a link of $p$, that is, unless $x\in b-[0,n)^4$ for a base point
$b\in\{y,y+e_\mu,y+e_\nu\}$. By inclusion and exclusion these $x$ number
$3n^4-2n^3(n-1)-n^2(n-1)^2+n^2(n-1)^2=n^4+2n^3$.

(iii) Integrating the hypothesis, $\varpi_{\rm in}\mu((f-P_{W_x}f)^2)\le n^{-4}
\sum_{y:\,V_y\subset W_x}\mu((f-P_{V_y}f)^2)$. Sum over $x$, use
$\mathrm{BP}(m,\varpi)$ and count each $y$ $(m-n+1)^4$ times:
$\varpi\varpi_{\rm in}\Var_\mu(f)\le(m-n+1)^4m^{-4}n^{-4}\sum_y\mu((f-
P_{V_y}f)^2)$.
\end{proof}

Part (iii) is the two-scale argument for block dynamics~\cite{Mar99}; it
transfers the condition from larger cubes to smaller ones, given a gap of
the small-cube dynamics inside each large cube uniform in its exterior.
Part (i) transfers it upwards only with the loss $(n/m)^4$, which, through
Theorem~\ref{thm:avgdecay}, gives the rate $\varpi n^4/(e^2q_mm^5)$ and
nothing in physical units. So $\mathrm{BP}$ at one cube size implies no
useful bound at larger sizes by itself.

The remaining results concern $G=\SU(N)$, $N\ge2$, with center element
$z=e^{2\pi i/N}I$, and use the holonomies of the torus. For a site $y$ let
$P_y$ be the ordered product of the $L$ links $(y+te_0,0)$, $0\le t<L$, the
Polyakov loop of direction $0$ through $y$, and $A=P_0$, $B$ the analogous
product of the links $(te_1,1)$; $A$ and $B$ are based at the site $0$, so
the traces $F_{ab}=\Tr(A^aB^b)$ are gauge invariant. For $c,d\in\Z$ the
map $Z_{c,d}$ that multiplies the links $(y,0)$ with $y_0=0$ by $z^c$ and the
links $(y,1)$ with $y_1=0$ by $z^d$ preserves $\mu$: a plaquette that contains
one of these links contains a second one of the same direction with the
same base coordinate, entering with the opposite orientation, and $z$ is
central. It maps $P_y$ to $z^cP_y$, $A$ to $z^cA$, $B$ to $z^dB$ and
$F_{ab}$ to $z^{ac+bd}F_{ab}$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:bpstokes}
Let $R=[0,a]\times[0,b]$ be a rectangle of $\Z^2$ and $\phi$ a map of its
sites into $\Lambda_L$ that sends unit steps in the two directions to links
in two directions $\mu\ne\nu$ and is injective on plaquettes. The holonomy of
the boundary of $R$, traversed counterclockwise from $(0,0)$, is an ordered
product $\prod_kg_kU_{p_k}^{\pm1}g_k^{-1}$ over the $ab$ plaquettes of
$\phi(R)$, with $g_k\in G$. For every unitarily invariant norm it is within
$\sum_{p\in\phi(R)}\lVert U_p-I\rVert$ of $I$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Holonomy is multiplicative under concatenation of paths and does not change
when a step followed by its reverse is removed. Let $\ell_j$ be the
boundary of $[0,a]\times[0,j]$ from $(0,0)$. After cancellation,
$\ell_{j-1}^{-1}\ell_j$ is the left edge from $(0,0)$ to $(0,j-1)$, the
boundary of the row $[0,a]\times[j-1,j]$ from $(0,j-1)$, and the left edge
back; so $\mathrm{Hol}(\ell_b)$ is an ordered product of conjugates of the
row holonomies. The same argument along a row writes the holonomy of its
boundary as an ordered product of conjugates of the holonomies of the
boundaries of its plaquettes, each a conjugate of $U_p$ or $U_p^*$. For
unitary $X,Y$, $\lVert XY-I\rVert\le\lVert X-I\rVert+\lVert Y-I\rVert$ and
$\lVert gXg^{-1}-I\rVert=\lVert X-I\rVert$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:bpcommute}
For $A,B\in\SU(N)$ put
\[
M(A,B)=\sum_{(a,b)\in I_N}\lvert\Tr(A^aB^b)\rvert^2,
\]
where $I_N=\{0,\dots,N-1\}^2\setminus\{(0,0)\}$. There are constants $c_N>0$
and $\eta_N>0$ such that $M(A,B)\ge c_N$ whenever $\lVert ABA^{-1}B^{-1}-I
\rVert_{\rm HS}\le\eta_N$. For $N=2$ one may take $\eta_2=1$ and
$c_2=0.64$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $A,B$ commute and suppose $M(A,B)=0$. Diagonalise them together, with
eigenvalues $\alpha_k,\beta_k$. The power sums of the $\alpha_k$ of orders
$1,\dots,N-1$ vanish, so by Newton's identities the characteristic
polynomial of $A$ is $x^N+(-1)^N\det A$, and $\alpha_k=\lambda\omega^k$ after
reordering, with $\omega=e^{2\pi i/N}$; likewise $\beta_k=\lambda'\omega^{
\tau(k)}$ for a permutation $\tau$ of $\Z/N\Z$. Then $\Tr(A^aB^b)=\lambda^a
\lambda'^b\sum_k\omega^{ak+b\tau(k)}$ is a multiple of the Fourier
coefficient at $(a,b)$ of the indicator of the graph of $\tau$ in $(\Z/N\Z)^2$.
All coefficients except the one at $0$ vanish, so the indicator is
constant, which is impossible for a set of $N$ points among $N^2$. Hence
$M>0$ on the compact set of commuting pairs. If no $\eta_N,c_N$ existed,
there would be pairs with $\lVert ABA^{-1}B^{-1}-I\rVert\to0$ and $M\to0$,
and a limit point would be a commuting pair with $M=0$.

For $N=2$ put $x=\Tr A$, $y=\Tr B$, $w=\Tr AB$, real numbers in $[-2,2]$. By
the Cayley--Hamilton theorem, $A^{-1}=x-A$, $B^{-1}=y-B$, $\Tr(AB^2)=yw-x$,
$\Tr(A^2B)=xw-y$ and $\Tr(ABAB)=w^2-2$, so
\[
\Tr(ABA^{-1}B^{-1})=x^2+y^2+w^2-xyw-2 .
\]
Since $\lvert\Tr(C-I)\rvert\le\sqrt2\lVert C-I\rVert_{\rm HS}$, the
commutator bound gives $x^2+y^2+w^2-xyw\ge4-\sqrt2$, and with $m=\max(\lvert
x\rvert,\lvert y\rvert,\lvert w\rvert)$, $3m^2+m^3\ge4-\sqrt2>2.432=3(0.8)^2+
0.8^3$. So $m>0.8$ and $M=x^2+y^2+w^2>0.64$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:bpflat}
Let $G=\SU(N)$ and $\beta\ge1$. There is $A_N$ such that $\mu(N-\ReTr U_p)\le
A_N(1+\log\beta)/\beta$ for every plaquette and every $L$; for $N=2$,
$\mu(2-\Tr U_p)\le(\log\beta+6.11)/\beta$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Put
\[
H=\sum_p(N-\ReTr U_p)\ge0,\qquad\phi(t)=\log\int e^{-NtH}dU .
\]
Then $\mu$ has density proportional to $e^{-N\beta H}$, $\phi'(\beta)=-N\mu
(H)$, $\phi$ is convex and $\phi\le0$. Convexity gives $\phi(\beta/2)\ge\phi(\beta)-\frac
\beta2\phi'(\beta)$, hence $\mu(H)\le\frac2{N\beta}\log(1/e^{\phi(\beta)})$.
Let $\delta$ be the geodesic distance of $-\Tr(XY)$. If every link lies
within $r$ of $I$, then $\delta(U_p,I)\le4r$ by bi-invariance, and $N-
\ReTr U\le\frac12\delta(U,I)^2$, so $H\le8r^2\lvert P\rvert$. With $v(r)$ the
Haar measure of the ball of radius $r$ about $I$, $\lvert E\rvert=4L^4$ and
$\lvert P\rvert=6L^4$,
\[
\mu(N-\ReTr U_p)=\frac{\mu(H)}{6L^4}\le\frac2{N\beta}\Bigl(\tfrac23\log
\tfrac1{v(r)}+8N\beta r^2\Bigr),
\]
the plaquettes being equivalent under the symmetries of $\Lambda_L$. Haar
measure has a smooth positive density in exponential coordinates, so $v(r)
\ge v_Nr^{N^2-1}$ for $r\le1$; $r^2=1/(N\beta)$ gives the first claim. For
$\SU(2)$, $U=\cos\theta+u\sin\theta$ in quaternions has $\delta(U,I)=\sqrt2
\theta$ and the law of $\theta$ has density $\frac2\pi\sin^2\theta$, so
$v(\sqrt2s)\ge\frac2\pi\cdot\frac4{\pi^2}\cdot\frac{s^3}3=\frac{8s^3}{3\pi^3}$
for $s\le\frac\pi2$. With $r=\sqrt2s$ and $s^2=1/(32\beta)$ the bracket
times $\frac1\beta$ is at most $\frac1{6\beta}(6\log(32\beta)+6+4\log
\frac{3\pi^3}8)\le(\log\beta+6.11)/\beta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[the averaged condition at fixed cube size]\label{thm:bpfixed}
Let $G=\SU(N)$, $N\ge2$, $\beta\ge0$ and $n\ge2$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=*]
\item If $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ holds on $\Lambda_L$, then
\[
\frac1{L^3}\sum_{y:\,y_0=0}\mu\bigl(\Tr P_0\,\overline{\Tr P_y}\bigr)\le
\frac{2N^2n^2}{\varpi L^2}.
\]
\item Let $N=2$ and $\beta\ge1$. If $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ holds on
$\Lambda_{2n+4}$, then
\[
\varpi\le\frac{38400\,n^4(\log\beta+6.11)}\beta .
\]
\item There is $C_N$ such that for $\beta\ge1$, $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ on
$\Lambda_{2n+4}$ implies $\varpi\le C_Nn^4(1+\log\beta)/\beta$.
\end{enumerate}
In particular, along any sequence $(\beta_k,n_k)$ with $n_k^4(1+\log
\beta_k)/\beta_k\to0$, no $\varpi>0$ satisfies $\mathrm{BP}(n_k,\varpi)$
uniformly in the torus.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) Let $g_y=\ReTr P_y$ and $f=L^{-3}\sum_{y:\,y_0=0}g_y$. By the symmetry
$Z_{1,0}$, $\mu(f)=0$. The loop $P_y$ meets $V_x$ only if
$(y_1,y_2,y_3)$ lies in the projection of $x+[0,n)^4$, which holds for
$n^3$ loops, and $\lVert g_y-\mathcal P_xg_y\rVert_2\le\lVert g_y\rVert_2\le
N$. So $\lVert f-\mathcal P_xf\rVert_2\le Nn^3/L^3$ for each of the $L^4$
points $x$, the right side of $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ is at most $N^2n^2/L^2$,
and $\mu(f^2)\le N^2n^2/(\varpi L^2)$. The same holds for the imaginary
parts, and $\mu(\lvert L^{-3}\sum_y\Tr P_y\rvert^2)$ is the left side, by
translation invariance.

(ii), (iii) Let $L=2n+4\le4n$, $\varepsilon=\mu(\lVert U_p-I\rVert_{\rm HS}^2)=
2\mu(N-\ReTr U_p)$, the same for every plaquette, and let $f$ be the real or
the imaginary part of some $F_{ab}$, $(a,b)\in I_N$. Pick $(c,d)$ with $ac+bd
\not\equiv0$ modulo $N$; the symmetry $Z_{c,d}$ gives $\mu(F_{ab})=0$.

\emph{The right side of $\mathrm{BP}$.} If $V_x$ meets neither $A$ nor $B$,
$\mathcal P_xf=f$. Otherwise the coordinate $x_2$ satisfies $0\in x_2+[0,n)$
modulo $L$, and one of $s=x_2-1$, $s=x_2+n$, read in $(-L/2,L/2]$, has $\lvert
s\rvert\le n$ and $s\notin x_2+[0,n)$. The loops $A'$, $B'$ of directions
$0$, $1$ through $y'=se_2$ avoid $V_x$, so $F'_{ab}=\Tr(A'^aB'^b)$ depends
only on the links outside $V_x$, and $\lVert f-\mathcal P_xf\rVert_2\le
\lVert F_{ab}-F'_{ab}\rVert_2$. Let $\Gamma$ be the holonomy of the straight
path from $0$ to $y'$. Lemma~\ref{lem:bpstokes}, for the rectangle $[0,L]
\times[0,s]$ mapped onto the plane $\{0,2\}$ through $0$, whose boundary is
$A$, the path to $y'$, $A'$ reversed and the path back, gives $A=H_0\Gamma
A'\Gamma^{-1}$ with $\lVert H_0-I\rVert_{\rm op}\le S_0$, the sum of
$\lVert U_p-I\rVert_{\rm op}$ over the $L\lvert s\rvert$ plaquettes of the
strip; likewise $B=H_1\Gamma B'\Gamma^{-1}$ with the strip in the plane $\{1,
2\}$. Telescoping, $\lvert F_{ab}-F'_{ab}\rvert\le N(a\lVert H_0-I\rVert_{
\rm op}+b\lVert H_1-I\rVert_{\rm op})\le N(N-1)(S_0+S_1)$, and by the
Cauchy--Schwarz inequality over the at most $2Ln$ plaquettes of the two
strips, $\mu((S_0+S_1)^2)\le(2Ln)^2\varepsilon$. At most $2Ln^3$ points $x$
have $V_x$ meeting $A\cup B$, so
\[
\frac1{n^4}\sum_x\mu\bigl((f-\mathcal P_xf)^2\bigr)\le8N^2(N-1)^2L^3n\,
\varepsilon .
\]

\emph{The variance.} Lemma~\ref{lem:bpstokes} for the square $[0,L]^2$
mapped onto the plane $\{0,1\}$ through $0$, whose boundary reads $A$, $B$,
$A^{-1}$, $B^{-1}$, gives $\lVert ABA^{-1}B^{-1}-I\rVert_{\rm HS}\le\sum_p
\lVert U_p-I\rVert_{\rm HS}$ over $L^2$ plaquettes, so this commutator
exceeds $\eta_N$ with probability at most $L^2\sqrt\varepsilon/\eta_N$. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:bpcommute}, $\sum_{(a,b)\in I_N}\mu(\lvert F_{ab}\rvert^2)\ge
c_N(1-L^2\sqrt\varepsilon/\eta_N)$. As $\mu(F_{ab})=0$, some $f$ of the
form above has $\Var_\mu(f)\ge c_N(1-L^2\sqrt\varepsilon/\eta_N)/(2(N^2-1))$.
For $N=2$ the traces are real, and some $F_{ab}$ has variance at least
$0.64(1-L^2\sqrt\varepsilon)/3$.

\emph{Conclusion.} If $L^2\sqrt\varepsilon\le\eta_N/2$, $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$
gives $\varpi\le2048(N^2-1)N^2(N-1)^2n^4\varepsilon/c_N$, and for $N=2$,
$\varpi\le300\,L^3n\varepsilon\le19200\,n^4\varepsilon$. In the other case
$\eta_N^2<4L^4\varepsilon\le1024n^4\varepsilon$, and since $\varpi\le2$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:bpscales}(ii), $\varpi\le(2048/
\eta_N^2)n^4\varepsilon$, which for $N=2$ is again at most $19200\,n^4
\varepsilon$. Lemma~\ref{lem:bpflat} bounds $\varepsilon$ by $2(\log\beta+
6.11)/\beta$ for $N=2$ and by $2A_N(1+\log\beta)/\beta$ in general.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the response of a cube at weak coupling]\label{cor:bpresponse}
Let $G=\SU(2)$, $\beta\ge1$ and $n\ge2$, and put $X_n(\beta)=38400\,n^4(\log
\beta+6.11)/\beta$. Then
\[
\Xi_n\ge\frac{n^4}{7n^3+3n^2}\bigl(1-X_n(\beta)\bigr),
\]
and $D^0_L(n,\alpha)$ for one $L\ge2n+4$, hence for all, implies $1-\alpha
\le X_n(\beta)$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:bpstrong}(ii), $D^0_L(n,\alpha)$ does not depend on $L$
and holds with $\alpha=(7n^3+3n^2)\Xi_n/n^4$. If $\alpha<1$,
Proposition~\ref{prop:avgsup}(i) gives $\mathrm{BP}(n,1-\alpha)$ on
$\Lambda_{2n+4}$, and Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed}(ii) gives $1-\alpha\le
X_n(\beta)$; if $\alpha\ge1$ the bound on $\Xi_n$ holds trivially.
\end{proof}

So for $2\le n\le(\beta/(76800(\log\beta+6.11)))^{1/4}$ one link outside
the cube moves its law by at least $n/17$ in total, in the supremum over
exteriors: the response grows linearly with the cube, the behaviour that
Remark~\ref{rem:cubeheuristic} assigns to cubes smaller than the
correlation length.

Fig.~\ref{fig:bpranges} places the two results of this subsection in the
plane of the coupling and the cube side.

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/fig_bpranges.png}
\caption{The averaged condition at strong coupling and at fixed cube size.
(a)~The bound $\log_{10}\bigl(38400\,n^4(\log\beta+6.11)/\beta\bigr)$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed}(ii) for $\SU(2)$ over the cube side $n$ and
$\log_{10}\beta$; where it lies below the plane $\varpi=1$ (red),
$\mathrm{BP}(n,1)$ fails on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$, and the black curve is the
crossing. (b)~For $\SU(2)$ in the variables $\beta_W=4\beta$ and $n$: the
range $\lvert\beta_W\rvert<\frac29$ of Corollary~\ref{cor:bpranges}, where
$\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ holds on every torus for all $n\ge n_0(\beta_W)$
(green); the region where Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed}(ii) excludes
$\mathrm{BP}(n,\frac12)$ on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$ (red); and the continuum
tuning $n\propto e^{3\pi^2\beta_W/11}$ at fixed physical cube size
(dashed, schematic, the power prefactor omitted), which runs through the
region where neither result applies.}
\label{fig:bpranges}
\end{figure*}

\begin{remark}[what is proved about the averaged condition]\label{rem:bpstatus}
Proved: $\mathrm{BP}(n,1-\alpha_n)$ on every torus and on all of $L^2(\mu)$
for $\SU(2)$ with $\lvert\beta_W\rvert<\frac29$, for $\SU(3)$ with $\zeta(
\beta_W)<1$ (in particular $\beta_W\le0.35$, $n\ge6$, $\varpi\ge0.0176$),
and for every compact connected group with semisimple Lie algebra with
$24N\lvert\beta\rvert<\kappa_G$, for all $n$ with $\alpha_n<1$, where
$\alpha_n=O(1/n)$ is explicit (Corollary~\ref{cor:bpranges}); the
supremum form $D^0$ is independent of the torus and follows from a bound
$\Xi_n<n/(7+3/n)$ on the total response of a cube to one link
(Theorem~\ref{thm:bpstrong}); the best constant satisfies $n^4\varpi^*_n
\le m^4\varpi^*_m$ and $\varpi^*_n\le1+2/n$, and it passes from large
cubes to small ones given a uniform inner gap
(Proposition~\ref{prop:bpscales}). For $\SU(N)$ the condition forces the
spatially averaged Polyakov correlator to be $O(n^2/L^2)$, and on the
torus $\Lambda_{2n+4}$ it fails once $\beta\gg n^4\log\beta$; for $\SU(2)$,
$\varpi\le38400\,n^4(\log\beta+6.11)/\beta$, and the cube response
$\Xi_n$ is at least of order $n$ in that regime
(Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed}, Corollary~\ref{cor:bpresponse}). Hence
$\mathrm{BP}(n_k,\varpi)$ uniform in the torus requires $n_k^4\ge\varpi
\beta_k/(C_N(1+\log\beta_k))$ for $\beta_k\ge1$; the continuum tuning, with
$n_k$ of order $\ell/a_k$ and $\beta_k$ of order $\log(1/a_k)$, meets this
requirement, and the obstruction
comes from tori of side $2n+4$, which Theorem~\ref{thm:avgtransfer} does
not need, since it uses an infinite set of $L$. Not proved:
$\mathrm{BP}(n_k,\varpi)$ along the continuum tuning for any non-abelian
group, or at any coupling outside the ranges above; a transfer from small
cubes to large ones without the loss $(n/m)^4$; an obstruction on large
tori at weak coupling. Sec.~\ref{ssec:bpweak} shows that the obstruction on
$\Lambda_{2n+4}$ lies in the centre-charged part of $\mathcal A$, that the
continuum step needs only the local form $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$, and that
the comparison of cube sizes loses exactly $(n/m)^4$
(Remark~\ref{rem:bpweakstatus}); Sec.~\ref{ssec:bpneutral} reduces the
failure of the neutral part on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$ to a lower bound on the law
of the Polyakov loop (Theorem~\ref{thm:bpneutral}).
\end{remark}

\subsection{The averaged condition at weak coupling: centre sectors and
local slow modes}\label{ssec:bpweak}

The condition $\mathrm{BP}(n_k,\varpi)$ along the continuum tuning is the
spectral gap that Theorem~\ref{thm:avgtransfer} needs, and it is not proved
here. This subsection separates the part of the condition that the continuum
step uses from the part that Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed} obstructs, and it
records what each available route to the condition gives. Three facts
organise it. For $\SU(N)$ the cube moves commute with the centre twists of
the torus, so the condition splits into centre sectors; the test functions of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed} are charged, and every local gauge-invariant
function is neutral. The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:avgdecay} uses the
condition only through the spectral measures of local functions, so a local
form of it suffices for the continuum step. Functions of a bounded set of
links satisfy a Poincar\'e inequality for the cube chain uniformly in the
torus, so the slow functions that the condition excludes are spread over
growing sets of links.

The setting is that of Sec.~\ref{ssec:averaged} on the torus $\Lambda_L$,
$L\ge2n+4$ even, with $\mathcal K=L^{-4}\sum_x\mathcal P_x$. Put
\[
\mathcal E_n(f)=\frac1{n^4}\sum_{x\in X}\mu\bigl(\lvert f-\mathcal P_xf
\rvert^2\bigr),\qquad f\in L^2(\mu),
\]
so that $\ip f{(I-\mathcal K)f}=n^4L^{-4}\mathcal E_n(f)$, and
$\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ reads $\varpi\Var_\mu(f)\le\mathcal E_n(f)$ for real
$f\in\mathcal A$; here $\Var_\mu(f)=\mu(\lvert f-\mu(f)\rvert^2)$. Let
$\mathcal A_\C$ be the space of complex gauge-invariant functions in
$L^2(\mu)$. For $G=\SU(N)$, $N\ge2$, put $\omega=e^{2\pi i/N}$ and $z=\omega
I$. For a direction $\nu$ and $t\in\Z/L\Z$ let $Z^\nu_t$ be the map that
multiplies every link $(y,\nu)$ with $y_\nu=t$ by $z$ and fixes the other
links; the maps $Z_{c,d}$ of Sec.~\ref{ssec:bpstrong} are $(Z^0_0)^c
(Z^1_0)^d$.

\begin{proposition}[centre sectors]\label{prop:bpcentre}
Let $G=\SU(N)$, $N\ge2$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=*]
\item Each $Z^\nu_t$ preserves $\mu$, maps $\mathcal A_\C$ onto itself, and
satisfies $(\mathcal P_xf)\circ Z^\nu_t=\mathcal P_x(f\circ Z^\nu_t)$ for
every $x$ and every $f\in L^2(\mu)$.
\item For $f\in\mathcal A_\C$ the function $f\circ Z^\nu_t$ does not depend
on $t$; write $f\circ Z^\nu$ for it.
\item For $r\in(\Z/N\Z)^4$ let $\mathcal A_r=\{f\in\mathcal A_\C:f\circ Z^\nu
=\omega^{r_\nu}f,\ \nu=0,\dots,3\}$. Then $\mathcal A_\C$ is the orthogonal
sum of the $\mathcal A_r$, each $\mathcal A_r$ is invariant under every
$\mathcal P_x$, the constants lie in $\mathcal A_0$, and complex conjugation
maps $\mathcal A_r$ onto $\mathcal A_{-r}$.
\item If $f\in\mathcal A_\C$ depends only on the links with base points in a
box $b+[0,s)^4$ with $s\le L-1$, then $f\in\mathcal A_0$.
\item $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ holds if and only if $\varpi\Var_\mu(f)\le
\mathcal E_n(f)$ for every $r$ and every $f\in\mathcal A_r$; for $r\ne0$ and
$f\in\mathcal A_r$, $\mu(f)=0$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) A plaquette that contains a link $(y,\nu)$ with $y_\nu=t$ lies in a plane
$\{\nu,\nu'\}$ and contains exactly one other link of direction $\nu$, with
the same $\nu$-coordinate and the opposite orientation; a plaquette in a
plane without $\nu$ contains no link of direction $\nu$. Since $z$ is
central, $Z^\nu_t$ leaves every $U_p$ unchanged, and it preserves Haar
measure, so it preserves $\mu$. Since $z$ is central, it also commutes with
every gauge transformation, so it maps $\mathcal A_\C$ into itself, and onto
it, as $(Z^\nu_t)^N$ is the identity. Write $Z^\nu_t$ as $Z_V\times Z'$ on
$G^{V_x}\times G^{E\setminus V_x}$. The density of $\gamma^0_{V_x}(\cdot\mid
U)$ at $\sigma$ is proportional to the product of $\varphi$ over the
plaquettes meeting $V_x$ of the configuration equal to $\sigma$ on $V_x$
and to $U$ elsewhere, and $Z^\nu_t$ leaves these plaquettes unchanged; with
the invariance of Haar measure under $Z_V$, the law $\gamma^0_{V_x}(\cdot
\mid Z^\nu_tU)$ is the image of $\gamma^0_{V_x}(\cdot\mid U)$ under $Z_V$.
Hence
\[
\begin{split}
\mathcal P_x(f\circ Z^\nu_t)(U)&=\int f(Z_V\sigma,Z'U_{E\setminus V_x})\,
\gamma^0_{V_x}(d\sigma\mid U)\\
&=(\mathcal P_xf)(Z^\nu_tU).
\end{split}
\]

(ii) Let $g$ be the gauge transformation equal to $z^{-1}$ at the sites $y$
with $y_\nu=t+1$ and to $I$ elsewhere. It multiplies the links $(y,\nu)$
with $y_\nu=t$ by $z$ and those with $y_\nu=t+1$ by $z^{-1}$, and it fixes
every other link, because $z$ is central and the two ends of a link of
direction $\nu'\ne\nu$ have the same $\nu$-coordinate. Hence $Z^\nu_t$ is
$Z^\nu_{t+1}$ followed by $g$, and $f\circ Z^\nu_t=f\circ Z^\nu_{t+1}$ for
gauge-invariant $f$.

(iii) By (i) and (ii) the maps $f\mapsto f\circ Z^\nu$ are unitary on
$\mathcal A_\C$, commute with one another and have order $N$. So the
operators $\Pi_rf=N^{-4}\sum_j\omega^{-r\cdot j}f\circ(Z^0)^{j_0}\cdots
(Z^3)^{j_3}$, $j\in(\Z/N\Z)^4$, are orthogonal projections onto the
$\mathcal A_r$ with $\sum_r\Pi_r=I$ and $\Pi_r\Pi_{r'}=0$ for $r\ne r'$, and
by (i) they commute with every $\mathcal P_x$. Constants are fixed by every
$Z^\nu$, and $\bar f\circ Z^\nu=\overline{f\circ Z^\nu}$.

(iv) The residue $t=b_\nu-1$ is not among $b_\nu,\dots,b_\nu+s-1$ modulo
$L$, since $s\le L-1$. So $Z^\nu_t$ fixes every link on which $f$ depends,
and $f\circ Z^\nu=f\circ Z^\nu_t=f$ for each $\nu$.

(v) Let $f\in\mathcal A_\C$ and $f_r=\Pi_rf$. Since $\Pi_r$ commutes with
$\mathcal P_x$ and with the projection onto the constants, and the
$\mathcal A_r$ are orthogonal, $\Var_\mu(f)$ and $\mathcal E_n(f)$ are the
sums over $r$ of $\Var_\mu(f_r)$ and $\mathcal E_n(f_r)$. Since $\mathcal
P_x$ maps real functions to real functions, both are also the sums of their
values at $\operatorname{Re}f$ and $\operatorname{Im}f$. So the inequality
for real $f\in\mathcal A$, the inequality for all $f\in\mathcal A_\C$ and the
inequality on every $\mathcal A_r$ are equivalent. For $r\ne0$, $\mu(f)=\ip
1f=0$, since $1\in\mathcal A_0$.
\end{proof}

A local gauge-invariant function, such as a Wilson loop of a contractible
loop or a function of the plaquettes of a box, is therefore neutral, while
the trace of a Polyakov loop of direction $\nu$ lies in $\mathcal A_r$ with
$r=e_\nu$, since the loop crosses each slice $\{y_\nu=t\}$ once.\footnote{The
twists $Z^\nu_t$ are the lattice form of the centre symmetry of the pure
gauge theory on a torus. They change no plaquette, so they are invisible
to the action, and the trace of a Polyakov loop, which they multiply by
$\omega$, is the order parameter of the deconfinement transition at
finite temperature; by Proposition~\ref{prop:bpcentre}(v) its mean is zero
on every torus.} The
continuum step needs the condition only on neutral functions, and only
through local ones. Call $f\in L^2(\mu)$ \emph{local} if it depends only on
the links with base points in some box of side at most $L/2$.

\begin{definition}\label{def:bploc}
Let $G$, $\varphi$ and $\Lambda_L$ be as in Sec.~\ref{ssec:averaged}, $n\ge2$
and $\varpi>0$, and put $\epsilon=\varpi n^4/L^4$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)},leftmargin=*]
\item $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n,\varpi)$: for every local real $f\in\mathcal
A$ and every integer $t\ge0$,
\[
\bigl\lVert\mathcal K^t\bigl(f-\mu(f)\bigr)\bigr\rVert_2\le(1-\epsilon)^t
\bigl\lVert f-\mu(f)\bigr\rVert_2 .
\]
\item For $G=\SU(N)$, $\mathrm{BP}_0(n,\varpi)$: $\varpi\Var_\mu(f)\le
\mathcal E_n(f)$ for every real $f\in\mathcal A_0$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

For a self-adjoint $0\le\mathcal K\le I$ and $g\in L^2(\mu)$ with spectral
measure $\nu_g$, $\lVert\mathcal K^tg\rVert_2^2=\int\lambda^{2t}d\nu_g$; so
the inequality of (a) for all $t$ says that $\nu_g$ is carried by $[0,1-
\epsilon]$ for $g=f-\mu(f)$, the spectral gap of the cube chain on the cyclic
subspace of each local function. Condition (a) involves no function of
links spread over the torus.\footnote{The distinction is the one between
a spectral gap of the whole generator and a gap on the cyclic subspaces of
the observables that are measured. Only the latter enters a bound on
their correlations, and a lower bound on the Rayleigh quotient of a
function does not give it; Appendix~\ref{app:cyclic} proves both
statements in the generality of a self-adjoint contraction.}

\begin{theorem}[the local form of the averaged condition]\label{thm:bplocal}
Let $G$, $\varphi$ and $\Lambda_L$ be as in Sec.~\ref{ssec:averaged}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=*]
\item $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ implies $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n,\varpi)$; for
$G=\SU(N)$, $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ implies $\mathrm{BP}_0(n,\varpi)$, which
implies $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n,\varpi)$.
\item If $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n,\varpi)$ holds on $\Lambda_L$, then
$\varpi\le4$, and both bounds of Theorem~\ref{thm:avgdecay} hold for bounded
$f,g\in\mathcal A$ with $f$ local.
\item Corollary~\ref{cor:avggap} and Theorem~\ref{thm:avgtransfer} hold with
$\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n,\varpi)$, respectively $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n_k,
\varpi)$, in place of $\mathrm{BP}$; so does the form of
Proposition~\ref{prop:spatialflow}\textup{(iii)} stated after
Theorem~\ref{thm:avgtransfer}.
\item If $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n,\varpi)$ holds, then every local real
$f\in\mathcal A$ and every real $h\in\mathcal A$ satisfy
\[
\Cov_\mu(f,h)^2\le\Var_\mu(f)\,\frac{\mathcal E_n(h)}{\varpi}.
\]
If it fails, there are a local real $f\in\mathcal A$ and a real $h\in
\mathcal A$ with $\mathcal E_n(h)<\varpi\Var_\mu(h)$ and $\Cov_\mu(f,h)=
\Var_\mu(h)>0$; for $G=\SU(N)$, $h\in\mathcal A_0$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) Let $\mathcal B$ be $\mathcal A$, or $\mathcal A_0$ for $G=\SU(N)$, and
$\mathcal B^0$ its functions of mean zero. Every $\mathcal P_x$ maps
$\mathcal A$ into itself (Sec.~\ref{ssec:averaged}) and $\mathcal A_0$ into
itself (Proposition~\ref{prop:bpcentre}(iii)), and $\mu(\mathcal P_xg)=
\mu(g)$; so $\mathcal K$ maps $\mathcal B^0$ into itself, and there it is self-adjoint with
$0\le\mathcal K\le I$. If the inequality of $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ holds on
the real functions of $\mathcal B$, then $\ip g{(I-\mathcal K)g}\ge
\epsilon\lVert g\rVert_2^2$ on $\mathcal B^0$, the spectrum of $\mathcal K$
on $\mathcal B^0$ lies in $[0,1-\epsilon]$, and $\lVert\mathcal K^tg
\rVert_2\le(1-\epsilon)^t\lVert g\rVert_2$ for $g\in\mathcal B^0$. A local
real $f\in\mathcal A$ lies in $\mathcal A_0$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:bpcentre}(iv), since $L/2\le L-1$, so $g=f-\mu(f)\in
\mathcal B^0$ in both cases.

(ii) Let $f=\ReTr U_p$ for a plaquette $p$, a local function with $\Var_\mu
(f)>0$, and $g=f-\mu(f)$. By $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n,\varpi)$ with $t=1$,
$\ip g{\mathcal Kg}\le\lVert g\rVert_2\lVert\mathcal Kg\rVert_2\le(1-
\epsilon)\lVert g\rVert_2^2$, so $\ip g{(I-\mathcal K)g}\ge\epsilon\lVert g
\rVert_2^2$. As in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:avgdecay}, $\ip g{(I-
\mathcal K)g}=L^{-4}\sum_x\mu((g-\mathcal P_xg)^2)\le4n^4L^{-4}\lVert g
\rVert_2^2$. Hence $\varpi\le4$. The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:avgdecay}
uses $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ in two places only: for the bound $\varpi\le4$,
just obtained, and in its spectral part, for $\lVert\mathcal K^T(f-\mu(f))
\rVert_2\le e^{-\epsilon T}\lVert f-\mu(f)\rVert_2$, which
$\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n,\varpi)$ gives for local real $f$. The real and
imaginary parts of a local function are local.

(iii) In the proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:avggap},
Theorem~\ref{thm:avgdecay} is applied on $\Lambda_L$, $L\in\mathcal L$
large, to $\vartheta(\bar F)$ and $\tau_tF$, which depend on links with base
points in a box whose side depends on $F$ and $t$ only, hence are local once
$L$ is large; (ii) applies. Theorem~\ref{thm:avgtransfer} and the form of
Proposition~\ref{prop:spatialflow}(iii) use the condition only through
Corollary~\ref{cor:avggap}.

(iv) Let $E$ be the spectral projection of $\mathcal K$ on $L^2(\mu)$ for
the interval $(1-\epsilon,1]$, $f$ local, real and in $\mathcal A$, and $g=
f-\mu(f)$. Under $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n,\varpi)$, $\int\lambda^{2t}d\nu_g\le
(1-\epsilon)^{2t}\nu_g([0,1])$ for all $t$, so $\nu_g((1-\epsilon+\eta,1])=0$
for every $\eta>0$, and $Eg=0$. On the range of $I-E$, $I-\mathcal K\ge
\epsilon$, so for real $h\in\mathcal A$,
\[
\begin{split}
\Cov_\mu(f,h)^2&=\ip g{(I-E)h}^2\\
&\le\lVert g\rVert_2^2\,\frac{\ip{(I-E)h}{(I-\mathcal K)(I-E)h}}\epsilon\\
&\le\lVert g\rVert_2^2\,\frac{\ip h{(I-\mathcal K)h}}\epsilon\\
&=\Var_\mu(f)\,\frac{\mathcal E_n(h)}{\varpi}.
\end{split}
\]
If $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n,\varpi)$ fails, some local real $f\in\mathcal A$
has $\nu_g((1-\epsilon,1])>0$ for $g=f-\mu(f)$; put $h=Eg$. Spectral
projections of $\mathcal K$ are strong limits of polynomials in $\mathcal K$,
so $h$ is real and lies in $\mathcal A$, and in $\mathcal A_0$ for $G=
\SU(N)$, since $g$ does. As $\mathcal K1=1$, $E1=1$ and $\mu(h)=\ip{E1}g=0$;
then $\Var_\mu(h)=\lVert Eg\rVert_2^2=\ip g{Eg}=\Cov_\mu(f,h)>0$, and since
$\nu_h$ is carried by $(1-\epsilon,1]$, $\ip h{(I-\mathcal K)h}=\int(1-
\lambda)\,d\nu_h<\epsilon\lVert h\rVert_2^2$, that is, $\mathcal E_n(h)<
\varpi\Var_\mu(h)$.
\end{proof}

Fig.~\ref{fig:bpsectors} shows the sectors and the spectral picture
behind Theorem~\ref{thm:bplocal}.

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/fig_bpsectors.png}
\caption{Centre sectors and the local form of the averaged condition.
(a)~A three-dimensional section of the torus with the slices $\{y_0=t\}$.
The twist $Z^0_t$ multiplies the links that cross one slice by the central
element $z$ (gold) and leaves every plaquette unchanged; a Polyakov loop
of direction $0$ crosses every slice once and is charged, while a
gauge-invariant function of a box that misses one slice is neutral
(Proposition~\ref{prop:bpcentre}). (b)~Spectral measures of the cube chain
$\mathcal K$, schematic: under $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$ the measure of a
local function puts no mass on $(1-\epsilon,1]$ (green), while a charged
function may carry mass there (red), as the test functions of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed} do at weak coupling (Theorem~\ref{thm:bplocal},
Corollary~\ref{cor:bpcharged}).}
\label{fig:bpsectors}
\end{figure*}

By (iv), under $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$ the functions on which the cube
chain is slow are nearly orthogonal to every local gauge-invariant function,
with the defect of orthogonality bounded by their Dirichlet form, and a
failure of $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$ produces a slow function correlated with a
local one. Slow functions orthogonal to all local ones, and for
$\SU(N)$ slow functions in the charged sectors, do not affect the continuum
step. The Villain obstruction persists for the local form: the proof of
Corollary~\ref{cor:avgcoulomb} uses the condition only through
Theorem~\ref{thm:avgdecay} for the functions $\Psi_p$, and through the bound
$\varpi\le4$, which holds with $\Psi_p$ in place of $\ReTr U_p$, a
nonconstant function of the four links of $p$. So for the Villain theory at
$\beta>\beta_0$ there are no $n$ and $\varpi>0$ such that
$\lVert\mathcal K^t(\Psi_p-\mu(\Psi_p))\rVert_2\le(1-\varpi n^4/\lvert X
\rvert)^t\lVert\Psi_p-\mu(\Psi_p)\rVert_2$ for all $t$, all plaquettes and
the measures with free boundary of all boxes of side at least $2n$. The
local form, like the full one, has to use the non-abelian structure of $G$.

For $\SU(N)$ the obstruction of Sec.~\ref{ssec:bpstrong} lies in the charged
sectors. Let $\mathcal C$ be the set of real $f\in\mathcal A$ with
$\Pi_0f=0$, in the notation of the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:bpcentre}(iii); it is orthogonal to $\mathcal A_0$ and
preserved by every $\mathcal P_x$.

\begin{corollary}[the fixed-size obstruction is charged]\label{cor:bpcharged}
Let $G=\SU(N)$, $N\ge2$. Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed} holds, with the same
constants in \textup{(i)} and \textup{(ii)} and with another constant $C_N$
in \textup{(iii)}, when the inequality of $\mathrm{BP}(n,\varpi)$ is assumed
only for $f\in\mathcal C$. Its hypotheses and conclusions concern only the
restriction of the condition to $\mathcal C$; neither $\mathrm{BP}_0(n,
\varpi)$ nor $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n,\varpi)$ enters it.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
In the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed}(i) the condition is applied to
$L^{-3}\sum_y\ReTr P_y$ and to the corresponding imaginary part. The trace of
$P_y$ lies in $\mathcal A_{e_0}$ and its conjugate in $\mathcal A_{-e_0}$,
and $\pm e_0\ne0$ in $(\Z/N\Z)^4$, so both functions lie in $\mathcal C$. In
the proof of (ii) and (iii), $F_{ab}=\Tr(A^aB^b)$ lies in $\mathcal A_r$ with
$r=(a,b,0,0)\ne0$ for $(a,b)\in I_N$, so the real and imaginary parts of
$F_{ab}$ lie in $\mathcal C$. The only other use of the condition is the
bound $\varpi\le2$ from Proposition~\ref{prop:bpscales}(ii), whose test
function is neutral. In its place take $f_*=\ReTr P_0\in\mathcal C$, a
nonconstant continuous function, so $\Var_\mu(f_*)>0$. $\mathcal P_xf_*=f_*$
unless $V_x$ meets the loop $P_0$, which happens for at most $Ln^3$ points
$x$, and $\mu((f_*-\mathcal P_xf_*)^2)\le\Var_\mu(f_*)$; so $\varpi\le L/n
\le4$ on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$. In the case $\eta_N^2<4L^4\varepsilon\le1024n^4
\varepsilon$ of that proof this gives $\varpi\le(4096/\eta_N^2)n^4
\varepsilon$, which for $N=2$ is at most $19200\,n^4\varepsilon$, so the
constant $38400$ of (ii) is unchanged. $\mathcal C$ is orthogonal to
$\mathcal A_0$, which contains every local function of $\mathcal A$.
\end{proof}

Corollary~\ref{cor:bpcharged} constrains every argument that proves the
condition through a contraction of the cube chain on gauge orbits. On $G^E$ let $d(U,U')=\min_g
d_E(U,gU')$, the minimum over gauge transformations $g$, with $d_E$ as in the
proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:avgsup}; since gauge transformations act by
isometries of $d_E$, $d$ is a pseudometric, constant on pairs of gauge
orbits. Write $W_1^d$ for the Kantorovich distance of $d$. By gauge covariance,
$\mathcal K(gU',\cdot)$ is the image of $\mathcal K(U',\cdot)$ under $g$, so
a contraction of $W_1^{d_E}$ by a factor $1-\kappa$, as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:avgsup}(i), gives one of $W_1^d$ by the same factor.

\begin{corollary}[couplings on gauge orbits]\label{cor:bpcoupling}
Let $G=\SU(2)$, $\beta\ge1$, $n\ge2$ and $L=2n+4$. If there is $\kappa>0$
with
\[
W_1^d\bigl(\mathcal K(U,\cdot),\mathcal K(U',\cdot)\bigr)\le(1-\kappa)\,
d(U,U')
\]
for all configurations $U,U'$ of $\Lambda_L$, then
\[
\kappa\le\frac{38400\,n^8(\log\beta+6.11)}{L^4\beta}.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Let $h$ be gauge invariant and Lipschitz for $d$ with constant $\mathrm{Lip}
(h)$. For a coupling of $\mathcal K(U,\cdot)$ and $\mathcal K(U',\cdot)$
with expected distance $W_1^d$, $\lvert\mathcal Kh(U)-\mathcal Kh(U')\rvert
\le\mathrm{Lip}(h)W_1^d$, so $\mathcal Kh$ is Lipschitz with constant $(1-
\kappa)\mathrm{Lip}(h)$, and it is gauge invariant. As in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:avgsup}(i), with $d\le D\lvert E\rvert$, the spectral
measure of $\mathcal K$ at $h-\mu(h)$ is carried by $[0,1-\kappa]$. Such $h$
are dense in $\mathcal A$: averaging over the compact gauge group is the
orthogonal projection of $L^2(\mu)$ onto $\mathcal A$ and maps continuous
functions to continuous ones, so the continuous functions of $\mathcal A$
are dense in it; a continuous gauge-invariant $f$ is uniformly continuous
for $d$, since $d(U,U')$ small gives $g$ with $d_E(U,gU')$ small and $f(gU')
=f(U')$; and $f_k(U)=\inf_V(f(V)+k\,d(U,V))$ is gauge invariant, Lipschitz
for $d$ with constant $k$, and converges to $f$ uniformly. So the spectral
projection of $\mathcal K$ for $(1-\kappa,1]$ vanishes on the functions of
$\mathcal A$ of mean zero, $\ip f{(I-\mathcal K)f}\ge\kappa\Var_\mu(f)$ on
$\mathcal A$, and $\mathrm{BP}(n,\kappa L^4/n^4)$ holds on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$.
Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed}(ii) gives the bound.
\end{proof}

The proof uses only that $d$-Lipschitz functions are dense in the charged
part $\mathcal C$. If $d$ is replaced by its minimum over the centre twists
as well, $d'(U,U')=\min d_E(U,gZU')$ over gauge transformations $g$ and
products $Z$ of the maps $Z^\nu_t$, the $d'$-Lipschitz functions are neutral,
and the same argument, with the average over the gauge group and the
group generated by the $Z^\nu_t$, gives $\mathrm{BP}_0(n,\kappa L^4/n^4)$,
which Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed} does not constrain. A coupling argument for
the cube chain at weak coupling and small $n$ therefore has to contract a
pseudometric that does not see the centre twists. Since $n^8/(2n+4)^4\ge
n^4/256$, the bound of Corollary~\ref{cor:bpcoupling} is at least
$150\,n^4(\log\beta+6.11)/\beta$, which exceeds one once $n^4\ge\beta/150$;
along the tuning, where $n_k^4$ grows faster than every power of $\beta_k$,
the corollary does not exclude such couplings.

The remaining results hold for every $G$ and $\varphi$ of
Sec.~\ref{ssec:averaged}, on tori and on boxes. Put $\omega_\varphi=\log(
\max\varphi/\min\varphi)$, so that $\omega_\varphi\le2N^2\lvert\beta\rvert$
for the Wilson weight, and for a finite set $S$ of links let $P_S$ be the set
of plaquettes of $P$ that contain a link of $S$; $\lvert P_S\rvert\le6
\lvert S\rvert$.

\begin{proposition}[no local slow modes]\label{prop:bpnoloc}
Let $S\subset E$ be finite and let $f\in L^2(\mu)$ be real and depend only on
the links of $S$. Then
\[
\Var_\mu(f)\le e^{5\omega_\varphi\lvert P_S\rvert}\,\mathcal E_n(f).
\]
In particular, if $\mathcal E_n(f)\le\varpi\Var_\mu(f)$ with $0<\varpi<1$ and
$\Var_\mu(f)>0$, then $\lvert P_S\rvert\ge\log(1/\varpi)/(5\omega_\varphi)$;
for the Wilson weight, $\lvert S\rvert\ge\log(1/\varpi)/(60N^2\lvert\beta
\rvert)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a finite $W\subset E$ write $H_W$ for the normalised Haar measure on
$G^W$ and $\rho_W(\cdot\mid U)$ for the density of $\gamma^0_W(\cdot\mid U)$
with respect to $H_W$. It is proportional to a product of $\lvert P_W\rvert$
values of $\varphi$, so the ratio of two of its values is at most $e^{
\omega_\varphi\lvert P_W\rvert}$, and as its integral is one,
$e^{-\omega_\varphi\lvert P_W\rvert}\le\rho_W\le e^{\omega_\varphi\lvert
P_W\rvert}$. Put $a=\omega_\varphi\lvert P_S\rvert$, and write $P_W$ also for
the conditional expectation given the links outside $W$; then $P_{V_x}=
\mathcal P_x$.

\emph{Step 1.} Let $\nu_S$ be the law of $U_S$ under $\mu$. Its density with
respect to $H_S$ is the mean of $\rho_S(\cdot\mid U)$ over $U$, so it lies in
$[e^{-a},e^a]$, and $r_U=\rho_S(\cdot\mid U)/(d\nu_S/dH_S)\ge e^{-2a}$. Let
$g=f-\mu(f)$, a function on $G^S$ with $\nu_S(g)=0$. Then $P_Sg(U)=\int g\,
r_U\,d\nu_S=\int g\,(r_U-e^{-2a})\,d\nu_S$, and the Cauchy--Schwarz
inequality for the positive measure $(r_U-e^{-2a})\,d\nu_S$ of mass
$1-e^{-2a}$ gives
\[
(P_Sg)^2(U)\le(1-e^{-2a})\int g^2r_U\,d\nu_S=(1-e^{-2a})P_S(g^2)(U).
\]
Integrating, $\mu((P_Sg)^2)\le(1-e^{-2a})\mu(g^2)$, and since $P_S$ is an
orthogonal projection, $\mu((f-P_Sf)^2)=\mu(g^2)-\mu((P_Sg)^2)\ge e^{-2a}
\Var_\mu(f)$.

\emph{Step 2.} $\mu((f-P_Sf)^2)$ is the mean over $U$ of the variance of $f$
under $\gamma^0_S(\cdot\mid U)$, which is at most $\int(f-H_S(f))^2\rho_S\,
dH_S\le e^a\Var_{H_S}(f)$. So $\Var_\mu(f)\le e^{3a}\Var_{H_S}(f)$.

\emph{Step 3.} For $W\subset S$ let $v_W$ be the variance of $f$ in the
links of $W$ under $H_W$, the links of $S\setminus W$ fixed, a function of
these links. With the decomposition $f=\sum_{T\subset S}f_T$ of the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:avgsup}(ii) for $H_S$, $H_S(v_W)=\sum_{T\cap W\ne
\emptyset}\lVert f_T\rVert^2$, and since every link lies in exactly $n^4$ of
the sets $V_x$,
\[
\sum_{x\in X}H_S(v_{S\cap V_x})=\sum_T\lVert f_T\rVert^2\,\#\{x:T\cap V_x
\ne\emptyset\}\ge n^4\Var_{H_S}(f).
\]

\emph{Step 4.} Let $W=S\cap V_x\ne\emptyset$. Since $d\nu_S/dH_S\ge e^{-a}$
and $v_W\ge0$, $H_S(v_W)\le e^a\nu_S(v_W)=e^a\mu(v_W)$. For every configuration $U$,
$v_W\le\int(f-c)^2dH_W$ with $c$ the mean of $f$ under $\gamma^0_W(\cdot
\mid U)$, and $dH_W/d\gamma^0_W\le e^{\omega_\varphi\lvert P_W\rvert}\le
e^a$, so $v_W$ is at most $e^a$ times the variance of $f$ under
$\gamma^0_W(\cdot\mid U)$. Hence $\mu(v_W)\le e^a\mu((f-P_Wf)^2)\le e^a\mu
((f-\mathcal P_xf)^2)$, the last step because the links outside $V_x$ are
among those outside $W$.

Steps 3 and 4 give $\Var_{H_S}(f)\le n^{-4}e^{2a}\sum_x\mu((f-\mathcal P_xf)^2
)=e^{2a}\mathcal E_n(f)$, and with Step 2, $\Var_\mu(f)\le e^{5a}\mathcal
E_n(f)$. For the Wilson weight, $5\omega_\varphi\lvert P_S\rvert\le60N^2
\lvert\beta\rvert\lvert S\rvert$.
\end{proof}

So a sequence of functions with $\mathcal E_n(f_j)/\Var_\mu(f_j)\to0$, on
tori of growing side at fixed $n$ and $\beta$, depends on growing sets of
links,\footnote{The exponent $5\omega_\varphi\lvert P_S\rvert$ is the cost
of comparing the conditional laws of the links of $S$ with Haar measure,
in the manner of the perturbation lemma of Holley and
Stroock~\cite{HS87}; it depends on $S$ through the plaquettes that touch
it, and not on the torus. At fixed $\beta$ it forces
$\lvert S\rvert\to\infty$ as $\varpi\to0$, linearly in $\log(1/\varpi)$.} and no subsequence converges to a function of finitely many links.
This does not give $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$: Proposition~\ref{prop:bpnoloc}
bounds the Rayleigh quotient of a local function, which is the mean of
$1-\lambda$ under its spectral measure, while $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$ asks
that this measure put no mass near $\lambda=1$; by
Theorem~\ref{thm:bplocal}(iv) a failure of it produces a slow function
correlated with a local one.

The transfer of the condition between cube sizes is limited in the
same way. Write $W_x$ for the cubes of side $m$ and $\mathcal E_m$ for the
corresponding form; the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:bpscales}(i) shows
$\mathcal E_m\ge(n/m)^4\mathcal E_n$ on $L^2(\mu)$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:bpsharp}
Let $\varphi\equiv1$, $2\le n\le m$, $R\ge2$, and let $L$ be even with
$L\ge\max(R+m,2m+4)$. There is a real $f\in\mathcal A$ with $\Var_\mu(f)=1$
and
\[
\mathcal E_m(f)\le\Bigl(\frac nm\Bigr)^4\Bigl(\frac{R+m}{R-1}\Bigr)^4
\mathcal E_n(f).
\]
So no inequality $\mathcal E_m\ge c\,\mathcal E_n$ on $\mathcal A$ with $c>
(n/m)^4$ holds on all tori, although $\mathrm{BP}(n,1)$ and $\mathrm{BP}(m,1)$
both hold for this measure.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since $G$ is compact and nontrivial, it has a nontrivial irreducible unitary
representation, whose character has Haar mean zero; its real or imaginary
part, normalised, is a real class function $h$ with $\int h\,dU=0$ and
$\int h^2dU=1$. Let $Q$ be the set of plaquettes of the plane $\{0,1\}$ with
base point $y\in[0,R)^4$ and $y_0$, $y_1$ even. The plaquette at $y$ has the
links $(y,0)$, $(y+e_0,1)$, $(y+e_1,0)$, $(y,1)$. A link $(w,0)$ of such a
plaquette determines $y$, which is $w$ or $w-e_1$ according to the parity
of $w_1$, and a link $(w,1)$ determines it through the parity of $w_0$; so
two plaquettes of $Q$ share no link. Put $f=\prod_{p\in Q}h(U_p)$, a gauge-invariant
function. For one plaquette, integrating $h(U_p)$ over any one of its four
links gives $\int h\,dU=0$, by the invariance of Haar measure under
translation and inversion; so in the decomposition of the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:avgsup}(ii), $h(U_p)$ has the single term $T=p$, and
since the plaquettes of $Q$ involve disjoint links, $f=f_T$ with $T$ the
union of their links, and $\lVert f\rVert_2=1=\Var_\mu(f)$. That proof gives
$k^4\mathcal E_k(f)=N_k$, the number of $x$ with $V_x\cap T\ne\emptyset$ for
cubes of side $k$, $k=n,m$.

The base points of the links of $T$ lie in $[0,R]^4$, so $N_k\le(R+k)^4$,
as $R+k\le L$. If $x\in[0,R-2]^4$, the cube of side $k\ge2$ at $x$ contains
the four points $x+ae_0+be_1$, $a,b\in\{0,1\}$, of $[0,R)^4$, one of which
has even coordinates $0$ and $1$ and is the base point of a plaquette of
$Q$; so $N_k\ge(R-1)^4$. Hence $\mathcal E_m(f)/\mathcal E_n(f)=(n/m)^4
N_m/N_n\le(n/m)^4((R+m)/(R-1))^4$. The last claim is
Proposition~\ref{prop:avgsup}(ii) and $R\to\infty$.
\end{proof}

At $\beta=0$ the gaps at the two sizes are both at least one, while the
Dirichlet forms differ by the factor $(n/m)^4$ on functions spread over
large boxes. An upward transfer without that loss has to use more than the
comparison of the forms, for instance an orthogonal decomposition such as
that of Proposition~\ref{prop:avgsup}(ii), which is specific to product
measures.

\begin{remark}[the routes to the averaged condition at weak
coupling]\label{rem:bproutes}
The results above settle how far the following routes go.

\emph{Constructions.} A test function bounds the best constant from above.
A lower bound needs a Poincar\'e inequality, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:bpnoloc} gives one for every function of a fixed
finite set $S$ of links, uniformly in the torus, with constant
$e^{-5\omega_\varphi\lvert P_S\rvert}$. A Lyapunov argument derives a
Poincar\'e inequality from a local one on a set of configurations and a
drift towards that set; for the cube chain on $\Lambda_L$ the set consists
of configurations of all $4L^4$ links, a small-field set for instance, and
the local inequality it needs is one for the restriction of $\mu$ to that
set, for functions of all links, which Proposition~\ref{prop:bpnoloc} does
not give. A coupling that contracts $d_E$ or the pseudometric $d$ on gauge
orbits gives the condition on $\mathcal A$ on every torus to which it
applies, and Corollaries~\ref{cor:bpresponse} and~\ref{cor:bpcoupling}
bound its rate on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$ for $\SU(2)$; a coupling that contracts a
pseudometric blind to the centre twists gives $\mathrm{BP}_0$, which
suffices by Theorem~\ref{thm:bplocal} and is open. The comparison with the
cube chain of larger cubes reduces to the inner gap of
Proposition~\ref{prop:bpscales}(iii); that proof works sector by sector, so
it gives $\mathrm{BP}_0(n,\cdot)$ from $\mathrm{BP}_0(m,\cdot)$ and the
inner inequality for $f\in\mathcal A_0$. The comparison with the cube chain
of the same size for another measure $\mu'$ through the density
$d\mu/d\mu'$ costs the exponential of the oscillation of its logarithm, a
sum of $6L^4$ plaquette terms; a bound uniform in the torus has to localise
the comparison, which leads back to the inner gap. The comparison of
Dirichlet forms between cube sizes loses exactly $(n/m)^4$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:bpsharp}).

\emph{Forced blow-up.} If $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n_k,\varpi)$ fails for every
$\varpi>0$ along the tuning, Theorem~\ref{thm:bplocal}(iv) gives local
functions $f_k$ and slow functions $h_k$, in $\mathcal A_0$ for $\SU(N)$,
with $\Cov_\mu(f_k,h_k)=\Var_\mu(h_k)$ and $\mathcal E_{n_k}(h_k)/\Var_\mu(
h_k)\to0$, and Proposition~\ref{prop:bpnoloc} gives $60N^2\beta_k\lvert
S\rvert\ge\log(\Var_\mu(h_k)/\mathcal E_{n_k}(h_k))$ for every set $S$ of
links on which $h_k$ depends. A contradiction has to come from a property of these slow
functions. The properties proved here for the tuned measures, tightness of
the marginals, reflection positivity, the transfer matrix, gauge invariance
and centre symmetry, are properties of the measures and of their torus
limits, not of the cube chain, and the paper proves no implication from any
of them to $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$. Such an implication is a converse of
Theorem~\ref{thm:avgdecay}, from decay of correlations to relaxation of the
cube chain. For local dynamics of spin systems the known converses assume a
mixing condition uniform in the boundary condition~\cite{Mar99}, of the type
of the supremum form $D^0$, which Corollary~\ref{cor:bpresponse} excludes for
$n$ below a multiple of $(\beta/\log\beta)^{1/4}$. A limit object along the
tuning would be a slow mode of a dynamics of continuum gauge fields, and no
such dynamics is constructed here. Continuity in $\beta$ does not connect
the regimes: Corollary~\ref{cor:bpranges} holds at fixed $\beta$ for $n\ge
n_0(\beta)$, and on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$ the best constant of the charged part
passes from at least one at $\beta=0$ to $O(n^4\log\beta/\beta)$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed}, Corollary~\ref{cor:bpcharged}); no monotonicity in
$\beta$ is known.

\emph{Residual sets.} Theorem~\ref{thm:bplocal} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:bpcharged} identify the residual: the condition may fail
on charged functions and on slow functions orthogonal to all local ones
without affecting the continuum step, and what has to be excluded is a slow
function correlated with a local neutral one. The Villain theory beyond
$\beta_0$ has such slow functions, by the local form of
Corollary~\ref{cor:avgcoulomb}. The failure of $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$ does
not by itself give a trivial or Gaussian limit, since $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$
is sufficient for a gap and is not shown to be necessary; a trivialisation
argument needs the converse of Theorem~\ref{thm:avgdecay} again.

\emph{Perturbative and static estimates.} By Theorem~\ref{thm:bplocal}(ii),
$\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n,\varpi)$ on infinitely many tori gives exponential
decay of every local gauge-invariant covariance in the torus limit. The
quadratic approximation of the Wilson measure at weak coupling consists of
$\dim G$ copies of the free lattice Maxwell field, whose field strengths have
correlations that decay as a power of the distance. An argument whose
estimates apply equally to that Gaussian approximation, or order by order in
an expansion around it, therefore cannot give $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$; the
Villain theory, to which the setting applies, is a nonperturbative instance
of the same failure. The renormalisation group bounds
of~\cite{Bal84a,Bal85a,Bal89b} concern effective actions in a box of fixed
physical size and do not address the cube chain. The Bakry--\'Emery bound of
Lemma~\ref{lem:bponelink} requires $6N\lvert\beta\rvert<\kappa_G$.
Reflection positivity gives the transfer matrix and the chessboard estimate
bounds probabilities of local events uniformly in the torus; both are
statements about the measure, not about the cube chain.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[what is proved about the averaged condition at weak
coupling]\label{rem:bpweakstatus}
Proved: for $\SU(N)$ the cube moves preserve the centre sectors of the
torus, local gauge-invariant functions are neutral, and the condition splits
into sectors (Proposition~\ref{prop:bpcentre}); the conclusions of
Theorems~\ref{thm:avgdecay} and~\ref{thm:avgtransfer} and of
Corollary~\ref{cor:avggap} follow from the local form $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$,
which is implied by the neutral form $\mathrm{BP}_0$; under
$\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$ slow functions are nearly orthogonal to local ones,
and a failure of it produces a slow function correlated with a local one
(Theorem~\ref{thm:bplocal}); the obstruction of Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed}
holds for the charged part alone, and neither $\mathrm{BP}_0$ nor
$\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$ enters it (Corollary~\ref{cor:bpcharged}); for
$\SU(2)$ a contraction of the cube chain on the gauge orbits of
$\Lambda_{2n+4}$ has rate at most $38400\,n^8(\log\beta+6.11)/(L^4\beta)$
(Corollary~\ref{cor:bpcoupling}); every function of a finite set $S$ of links
satisfies $\Var_\mu(f)\le e^{5\omega_\varphi\lvert P_S\rvert}\mathcal E_n(f)$
on every torus and box (Proposition~\ref{prop:bpnoloc}); and the upward
comparison of the Dirichlet forms loses exactly $(n/m)^4$, already at
$\beta=0$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:bpsharp}). Not proved: $\mathrm{BP}$,
$\mathrm{BP}_0$ or $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$ along the continuum tuning for
any non-abelian group; whether $\mathrm{BP}_0$ or $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$
fails on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$ at weak coupling, of which
Theorem~\ref{thm:bpneutral} reduces the first, for $\SU(2)$, to a lower
bound on the law of the Polyakov loop; a converse of
Theorem~\ref{thm:avgdecay}, from decay of correlations to the relaxation of
the cube chain; a coupling that contracts a pseudometric blind to the centre
twists; and the mass gap.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The neutral sector on small tori}\label{ssec:bpneutral}

Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed} proves that the averaged condition fails on the
torus $\Lambda_{2n+4}$ at weak coupling, and Corollary~\ref{cor:bpcharged}
places that failure in the charged part of $\mathcal A$. This subsection
treats the neutral part for $\SU(2)$. The neutral functions of a Polyakov
loop change under the cube moves only through the curvature, as the charged
ones do, so the right side of $\mathrm{BP}_0$ is small for them; what is
missing is a lower bound on their variance. A flow that turns every
Polyakov loop of one direction inside its own one-parameter subgroup changes
the Wilson action only through the curvature, and integration by parts along
it bounds the concentration of the law of the Polyakov loop. The outcome is
that $\mathrm{BP}_0$ at weak coupling on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$ forces the
Polyakov loop towards the centre and towards the traceless elements of
$\SU(2)$, and that the failure of $\mathrm{BP}_0$ there reduces to one
lower bound on the law of the Polyakov loop.

Throughout, $G=\SU(2)$ with the Wilson weight $\varphi=e^{2\beta\Tr(\cdot)}$,
$\Lambda=\Lambda_L$ with $L$ even, and $H=\sum_p(2-\Tr U_p)$, so that $\mu$
has density proportional to $e^{-2\beta H}$. Every $W\in\SU(2)$ can be
written $W=\cos\theta\,I+\sin\theta\,u$ with $\theta\in[0,\pi]$ and $u$
traceless anti-Hermitian with $u^2=-I$, and $\Tr W=2\cos\theta$; in these
polar coordinates of the three-sphere the Haar measure is $\frac2\pi\sin^2
\theta\,d\theta$ times the uniform measure of $u$. For $W\in\SU(2)$ put
\[
J(W)=\Tr(W)\,\tfrac12(W-W^*)=2\cos\theta\sin\theta\,u ,
\]
a traceless anti-Hermitian matrix that commutes with $W$ and satisfies
$J(gWg^{-1})=gJ(W)g^{-1}$ for $g\in\SU(2)$ and $J(-W)=J(W)$. As in
Sec.~\ref{ssec:bpstrong}, $P_b$ is the Polyakov loop of direction $0$
through the site $b$, the ordered product of the $L$ links $(b+te_0,0)$,
$0\le t<L$, and we put $T_b=\Tr P_b$ and $T=T_0$. By the symmetries of the
torus every $T_b$, and the trace of every Polyakov loop of another direction,
has the law of $T$ under $\mu$.

On the configuration space $G^E$ let $\mathcal V$ be the vector field whose
component at a link $(b,0)$ is $L^{-1}J(P_b)U_{(b,0)}$ and whose components
at the links of the directions $1,2,3$ vanish; it is the velocity at $s=0$
of $U_{(b,0)}\mapsto e^{sJ(P_b)/L}U_{(b,0)}$. Its entries are polynomials in
the entries of the links, so $\mathcal V$ is smooth, and for a function $h$
of class $C^1$ we write $\mathcal Vh$ for the derivative of $h$ along
$\mathcal V$. Put
\[
\Theta=\mathcal VS-\operatorname{div}\mathcal V,\qquad S=-2\beta\sum_p\Tr
U_p,
\]
where $\operatorname{div}$ is the divergence for the product of the Haar
measures.

\begin{lemma}[the holonomy flow]\label{lem:bpflow}
Let $L\ge8$ and $\beta\ge2$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=*]
\item For every site $y$, $\mathcal VP_y=J(P_y)P_y$; in particular
$\mathcal VT=-\frac12T(4-T^2)$.
\item $\operatorname{div}\mathcal V=\sum_{y:\,y_0=0}(2T_y^2-2)$, so
$\lvert\operatorname{div}\mathcal V\rvert\le6L^3$.
\item $\lvert\mathcal VS\rvert\le16\beta\sum_q(2-\Tr U_q)$, the sum over the
plaquettes $q$ of the planes $\{0,\nu\}$, and $\lVert\Theta\rVert_2\le192
L^4(\log\beta+5.44)$.
\item For every real $h$ of class $C^1$, $\mu(\mathcal Vh)=\mu(h\Theta)$ and
$\mu(\mathcal Vh)^2\le\Var_\mu(h)\lVert\Theta\rVert_2^2$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i) For $0\le t<L$ write $U_t$ for the link $(y+te_0,0)$ and put $G_t=U_0
\cdots U_{t-1}$, so that the loop based at $y+te_0$ is $P^{(t)}=U_t\cdots
U_{L-1}U_0\cdots U_{t-1}=G_t^{-1}P_yG_t$. Then
$J(P^{(t)})=G_t^{-1}J(P_y)G_t$, and differentiating the product,
\[
\mathcal VP_y=\sum_{t=0}^{L-1}G_t\,\frac{J(P^{(t)})}L\,U_t\cdots U_{L-1}
=\sum_{t=0}^{L-1}\frac{J(P_y)}L\,P_y=J(P_y)P_y .
\]
With $P_y=\cos\theta+\sin\theta\,u$,
\begin{align*}
\Tr(J(P_y)P_y)&=2\cos\theta\sin\theta\Tr(u\cos\theta+u^2\sin\theta)\\
&=-4\cos\theta\sin^2\theta=-\tfrac12T_y(4-T_y^2).
\end{align*}

(ii) The divergence of a vector field on $G^E$ is the sum over the links $e$
of the divergence in $U_e$ of its component at $e$, the other links fixed.
At $e=(y+te_0,0)$ the component is $L^{-1}J(U_eR)U_e$ with $R=U_{t+1}\cdots
U_{L-1}U_0\cdots U_{t-1}$ independent of $U_e$. The right translation
$U_e\mapsto W=U_eR$ preserves Haar measure and carries this field to
$W\mapsto L^{-1}J(W)W$. Since $J(W)$ commutes with $W$, $e^{sJ(W)}W=\cos(
\theta+s\sin2\theta)+\sin(\theta+s\sin2\theta)\,u$, so in polar coordinates
the field $J(W)W$ is $\sin2\theta\,\partial_\theta$, with divergence
\[
\frac1{\sin^2\theta}\,\partial_\theta\bigl(\sin^2\theta\sin2\theta\bigr)
=8\cos^2\theta-2=2(\Tr W)^2-2
\]
for $0<\theta<\pi$, and by continuity on all of $\SU(2)$. As $\Tr W=T_y$,
the $L$ links of the loop through $y$ contribute $2T_y^2-2$, and there are
$L^3$ such loops.

(iii) Only the plaquettes of the planes $\{0,\nu\}$ contain links of
direction $0$. The plaquette $p$ at $b$ in the plane $\{0,\nu\}$ is $U_p=
U_{(b,0)}U_{(b+e_0,\nu)}U_{(b+e_\nu,0)}^{-1}U_{(b,\nu)}^{-1}$, and with
$J_b=J(P_b)$ and $V=U_{(b,\nu)}$,
\[
\mathcal V\Tr U_p=\frac1L\Tr\bigl(U_p\Delta_p\bigr),
\]
where
\[
\Delta_p=J_b-VJ_{b+e_\nu}V^{-1}=J(P_b)-J(VP_{b+e_\nu}V^{-1}).
\]
The strip $\sigma$ of the $L$ plaquettes at $b+te_0$ in the plane
$\{0,\nu\}$ has boundary holonomy $P_bVP_{b+e_\nu}^{-1}V^{-1}$, so by
Lemma~\ref{lem:bpstokes}, $\lVert P_b-VP_{b+e_\nu}V^{-1}\rVert_{\rm HS}\le
Q_\sigma=\sum_{q\in\sigma}\lVert U_q-I\rVert_{\rm HS}$. For $P,P'\in
\SU(2)$, $\lvert\Tr P-\Tr P'\rvert\le\sqrt2\lVert P-P'\rVert_{\rm HS}$,
$\lVert\frac12(P-P^*)\rVert_{\rm HS}\le\sqrt2$ and $\lVert\frac12(P-P^*)-
\frac12(P'-P'^*)\rVert_{\rm HS}\le\lVert P-P'\rVert_{\rm HS}$, so $J$ is
$4$-Lipschitz for the Hilbert--Schmidt norm and $\lVert\Delta_p\rVert_{\rm
HS}\le4Q_\sigma$. As $\Delta_p$ is anti-Hermitian, $\Tr\Delta_p$ is
imaginary, and $\Tr(U_p\Delta_p)$ is real because $\mathcal V\Tr U_p$ is;
hence $\lvert\Tr(U_p\Delta_p)\rvert=\lvert\operatorname{Re}\Tr((U_p-I)
\Delta_p)\rvert\le4\lVert U_p-I\rVert_{\rm HS}Q_\sigma$. Every plaquette $p$
of the planes $\{0,\nu\}$ lies in exactly one strip $\sigma$, so
\[
\lvert\mathcal VS\rvert\le\frac{8\beta}L\sum_\sigma Q_\sigma^2\le8\beta
\sum_\sigma\sum_{q\in\sigma}\lVert U_q-I\rVert_{\rm HS}^2,
\]
by the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality over the $L$ plaquettes of $\sigma$; with
$\lVert U-I\rVert_{\rm HS}^2=2(2-\Tr U)$ this is the first claim, and
$\lvert\mathcal VS\rvert\le16\beta H$.

Put $\phi(t)=\log\int e^{-2tH}dU$, convex and decreasing, and $M=\log\mu(
e^{\beta H})=\phi(\beta/2)-\phi(\beta)\ge0$. Convexity gives $M\le-\frac
\beta2\phi'(\beta/2)=\beta\mu_{\beta/2}(H)$, where $\mu_{\beta/2}$ is the
Wilson measure at $\beta/2\ge1$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:bpflat} at $\beta/2$
bounds each of the $6L^4$ terms of $\mu_{\beta/2}(H)$ by $2(\log\beta+6.11-
\log2)/\beta$; so $M\le12L^4(\log\beta+5.42)$. For $y\ge0$ and $a\ge0$,
$2ay+y^2\le(2a+2)(e^y-1)$, since $y\le e^y-1$ and $y^2\le2(e^y-1-y)$; so for
every $X\ge0$, $X^2\le a^2+(2a+2)e^{X-a}$, and with $X=\beta H$ and $a=M$,
$\beta^2\mu(H^2)\le M^2+2M+2\le(M+2)^2$. Hence $\lVert\mathcal VS\rVert_2\le
16(M+2)\le192L^4(\log\beta+5.43)$ for $L\ge8$, and with (ii), $\lVert\Theta
\rVert_2\le192L^4(\log\beta+5.43)+6L^3\le192L^4(\log\beta+5.44)$.

(iv) On the compact manifold $G^E$,
\[
\int\mathcal Vg\,dU=-\int g\operatorname{div}\mathcal V\,dU
\]
for every $g$ of class $C^1$. Applied to
$g=he^{-S}$ this gives $\mu(\mathcal Vh)=\mu(h(\mathcal VS-
\operatorname{div}\mathcal V))=\mu(h\Theta)$. With $h=1$, $\mu(\Theta)=0$,
so $\mu(\mathcal Vh)=\mu((h-\mu(h))\Theta)$, and the Cauchy--Schwarz
inequality gives the bound.
\end{proof}

Part (iv) with $h=\psi(T)$ and part (i) give, for every real $\psi$ of class
$C^1$ on $[-2,2]$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bpanticonc}
\mu\bigl(T(4-T^2)\psi'(T)\bigr)^2\le4\Var_\mu\bigl(\psi(T)\bigr)\lVert
\Theta\rVert_2^2 .
\end{equation}
The twist $Z^0$ preserves $\mu$ and reverses the sign of $T$, so the left
side vanishes for odd $\psi$. With $\psi(t)=t^2/4$ it says that
$\Var_\mu(T^2/4)\ge\mu(T^2(4-T^2))^2/(16\lVert\Theta\rVert_2^2)$: a law of
the Polyakov loop with $\mu(T^2(4-T^2))$ bounded away from zero gives $T^2$ a
variance at least of order $1/(L^8\log^2\beta)$.

\begin{theorem}[the neutral sector on small tori]\label{thm:bpneutral}
Let $G=\SU(2)$, $\beta\ge2$, $n\ge2$, $L\ge2n+4$ even, and $T=\Tr P_0$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=*]
\item If $\mathrm{BP}_0(n,\varpi)$ holds on $\Lambda_L$, then
\[
\mu\bigl(T^2(4-T^2)\bigr)^2\le\frac{9\cdot2^{19}\,L^{11}n\,(\log\beta+
6.11)^3}{\varpi\beta}.
\]
\item On $\Lambda_{2n+4}$, $\mathrm{BP}_0(n,\varpi)$ implies
\[
\mu\bigl(T^2(4-T^2)\bigr)\le\frac{4.45\cdot10^6\,n^6(\log\beta+6.11)^{3/2}}
{\sqrt{\varpi\beta}} .
\]
\item If $\mu(T^2(4-T^2))\ge c>0$ on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$, then
$\mathrm{BP}_0(n,\varpi)$ on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$ implies
\[
\varpi\le\frac{9\cdot2^{41}\,n^{12}(\log\beta+6.11)^3}{c^2\beta}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) Let $h=T^2/4$. It is real and gauge invariant; $Z^0$ maps $P_0$ to
$-P_0$ and $Z^\nu$, $\nu\ne0$, fixes it, so $h\in\mathcal A_0$. If $V_x$
meets no link of $P_0$, $\mathcal P_xh=h$, and $V_x$ meets a link of $P_0$
for $Ln^3$ points $x$. For each of these choose $s$ as in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed}(ii), so that $\lvert s\rvert\le n$ and the loop
$A'=P_{se_2}$ avoids $V_x$; that proof gives $P_0=H_0\Gamma A'\Gamma^{-1}$
with $\lVert H_0-I\rVert_{\rm op}\le S_0$, the sum of $\lVert U_p-I
\rVert_{\rm op}$ over at most $Ln$ plaquettes. Then $\lvert T-\Tr A'\rvert=
\lvert\Tr((H_0-I)\Gamma A'\Gamma^{-1})\rvert\le2S_0$, and as $\lvert
\frac d{dt}\frac{t^2}4\rvert\le1$ on $[-2,2]$, $h'=(\Tr A')^2/4$ satisfies
$\lvert h-h'\rvert\le2S_0$. Since $h'$ depends only on the links outside
$V_x$ and $\mathcal P_x$ is the orthogonal projection onto such functions,
\[
\mu\bigl((h-\mathcal P_xh)^2\bigr)\le\mu\bigl((h-h')^2\bigr)\le4\mu(S_0^2)
\le4L^2n^2\varepsilon,
\]
with $\varepsilon=\mu(\lVert U_p-I\rVert_{\rm HS}^2)\le2(\log\beta+6.11)/
\beta$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:bpflat}. So $\mathcal E_n(h)\le n^{-4}Ln^3\cdot4L^2
n^2\varepsilon=4L^3n\varepsilon$, and $\mathrm{BP}_0(n,\varpi)$ gives
$\Var_\mu(h)\le8L^3n(\log\beta+6.11)/(\varpi\beta)$. By~\eqref{eq:bpanticonc}
with $\psi(t)=t^2/4$, $\mu(T^2(4-T^2))^2\le16\Var_\mu(h)\lVert\Theta
\rVert_2^2$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:bpflow}(iii) gives $\lVert\Theta\rVert_2^2\le
36864\,L^8(\log\beta+6.11)^2$. Since $16\cdot8\cdot36864=9\cdot2^{19}$, (i)
follows.

(ii), (iii) For $L=2n+4\le4n$, $L^{11}n\le2^{22}n^{12}$, and $3\cdot2^{41/2}
\le4.45\cdot10^6$.
\end{proof}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/fig_bpneutral.png}
\caption{The neutral sector on small tori. (a)~A great circle of $\SU(2)$
through $I$, $u$, $-I$, $-u$, with the velocity $\sin2\theta\,\partial_\theta$
by which the flow of Lemma~\ref{lem:bpflow} moves a Polyakov loop
$P=\cos\theta\,I+\sin\theta\,u$. The flow fixes the centre $\pm I$ (red) and
the traceless elements $\pm u$ (gold). (b)~The Haar law of $T=\Tr P_0$, with
density $\sqrt{4-T^2}/(2\pi)$, and the weight $T^2(4-T^2)$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bpneutral}; the weight has Haar mean $2$ and vanishes
exactly at $T\in\{-2,0,2\}$, the set on which no flow of this kind gives a
bound.}\label{fig:bpneutral}
\end{figure*}

Under the Haar measure,
\[
\mu\bigl(T^2(4-T^2)\bigr)=16\cdot\frac2\pi\int_0^\pi\sin^4\theta\cos^2
\theta\,d\theta=2 .
\]
The quantity vanishes exactly when $P_0$ lies
almost surely in $\{\pm I\}\cup\{W:\Tr W=0\}$, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:bpneutral}(ii) says that $\mathrm{BP}_0$ with a fixed
$\varpi$ on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$ at $\beta\gg n^{12}\log^3\beta$ forces the law
of the Polyakov loop towards that set. A coupling of the cube chain that
contracts a pseudometric blind to the centre twists at rate $\kappa$ gives
$\mathrm{BP}_0(n,\kappa L^4/n^4)$, so on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$ it forces
$\mu(T^2(4-T^2))\le4.45\cdot10^6n^8(\log\beta+6.11)^{3/2}/(L^2\sqrt{
\kappa\beta})$.

The exceptional set is that of the method. Consider fields whose component
at each link of direction $0$ is $L^{-1}K(P)U$, with $U$ the link and $P$
the Polyakov loop based at its initial site. The cancellation in
Lemma~\ref{lem:bpflow}(iii) uses that $K$ is equivariant under conjugation,
and the part of the field that is odd under the centre contributes nothing,
since for such a field $\mathcal Vh$ is odd under $Z^0$ and has mean zero
for every neutral $h$. An equivariant field $W\mapsto K(W)W$ on $\SU(2)$ has $K(W)$
fixed by conjugation with the stabiliser of $W$, so $K(\pm I)=0$ and, for
$W=\cos\theta+\sin\theta\,u$ with $0<\theta<\pi$, $K(W)=k(W)u$ with $k$
real. If $\Tr W=0$, then $-W=vWv^{-1}$ for every $v\in\SU(2)$ that
anticommutes with $u$, so evenness and equivariance give $k(W)u=K(-W)=v
K(W)v^{-1}=-k(W)u$ and $K(W)=0$. So every even equivariant $K$ vanishes on
$\{\pm I\}\cup\{\Tr W=0\}$, and $\mu(\mathcal Vh)$ tends to zero for every
neutral $h$ of class $C^1$ when the law of the Polyakov loop concentrates
there; integration by parts along such fields then gives no lower bound on
the variance of a neutral function. The law of the Polyakov loop on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$ at weak coupling is
not determined here.

\begin{remark}[what is proved about the neutral sector and what
remains]\label{rem:bpneutralstatus}
Proved: for $\SU(2)$ on every torus with $L\ge8$ and $\beta\ge2$, the law of
the Polyakov loop satisfies the anti-concentration
bound~\eqref{eq:bpanticonc} with $\lVert\Theta\rVert_2\le192L^4(\log\beta+
5.44)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:bpflow}); $\mathrm{BP}_0(n,\varpi)$ on
$\Lambda_{2n+4}$ forces $\mu(T^2(4-T^2))\le4.45\cdot10^6n^6(\log\beta+6.11
)^{3/2}(\varpi\beta)^{-1/2}$, so $\mathrm{BP}_0$ fails there once $\beta\gg
n^{12}\log^3\beta$ whenever $\mu(T^2(4-T^2))$ is bounded below
(Theorem~\ref{thm:bpneutral}); and no vector field of the kind used can
treat a Polyakov loop concentrated on the centre and the traceless
elements. Not proved: a lower bound on $\mu(T^2(4-T^2))$ on $\Lambda_{2n+4}$
at weak coupling, and so whether $\mathrm{BP}_0$ fails there; whether
$\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$ fails there; $\mathrm{BP}_0$ or $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm
loc}$ along the continuum tuning for any non-abelian group; and the mass gap.

The routes to $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n_k,\varpi)$ along the tuning stand as
follows. A coupling blind to the centre twists is constrained on
$\Lambda_{2n+4}$ as above and not on the large tori that
Theorem~\ref{thm:avgtransfer} uses. The comparison with larger cubes gives
$\mathrm{BP}_0(n,\cdot)$ from $\mathrm{BP}_0(m,\cdot)$ and the inner
inequality of Proposition~\ref{prop:bpscales}(iii) for $f\in\mathcal A_0$,
which must hold for almost every configuration outside the cube of side $m$,
that is, a gap of the chain of cubes of side $n$ for the measure of that
cube under every boundary configuration; nothing is proved about it at weak
coupling, and $\mathrm{BP}_0(m,\cdot)$ is the same problem at a larger cube
size. Theorem~\ref{thm:bpneutral} is an implication from a property of the
measure alone, the spread of the Polyakov loop, to the failure of a gap of
the cube chain; a converse of Theorem~\ref{thm:avgdecay} needs an implication
in the opposite direction, from properties of the measure such as
reflection positivity and the transfer matrix to a gap of the cube chain,
and none is proved. A comparison with a chain on block variables in the
range of Corollary~\ref{cor:bpranges} needs two estimates on tori of every
size: single-link bounds as in Theorem~\ref{thm:bpstrong} for the effective
measure of the block variables, and a Poincar\'e inequality for the fine
links given the block variables, uniform in them, which is an inner
inequality of the kind of Proposition~\ref{prop:bpscales}(iii); neither is
proved here, and the bounds of~\cite{Bal84a,Bal85a,Bal89b} concern effective
actions in a box of fixed physical size. What remains for the continuum
step is $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}(n_k,\varpi)$ itself on infinitely many tori
for each $k$, with one $\varpi>0$; by Theorem~\ref{thm:bplocal}(iv) it
bounds the correlation of every slow function with every local one, and its
failure produces a slow neutral function correlated with a local one.
\end{remark}

\section{The open inputs}\label{sec:inputs}



This section states the two unproved inputs of Sec.~\ref{sec:bypass}, the cube
estimate and the limit, and measures the first: what can be controlled
uniformly in the coupling, and what any proof of the cube estimate has to
produce.

\subsection{The two open statements}

Theorem~\ref{thm:transfer} reduces a continuum mass gap to two statements,
neither of which is proved here or, to our knowledge, anywhere else.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\Alph*)},leftmargin=*]
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\item \emph{The cube estimate.} There are $\alpha<1$ and $\ell>0$ such that
$D(n,\alpha)$ holds with $na\le\ell$ along the continuum tuning
$\beta=\beta(a)\to\infty$, uniformly in $a$.
\item \emph{The limit.} The renormalised Schwinger functions converge along
$a_k=2^{-k}a_0$ to Schwinger functions that satisfy the
Osterwalder--Schrader axioms and are not those of the vacuum alone.
\end{enumerate}
(D) and (E) together give the mass gap of the continuum theory; the
remaining Clay requirements, Euclidean invariance and the local structure of
the limit~\cite{JW06}, belong to (E). (D) is a statement about a single
cube of fixed physical size and a single coupling at each spacing; (E) is the
construction problem, on which Ba{\l}aban's renormalisation group
analysis~\cite{Bal84a,Bal85a,Bal89b} gives the ultraviolet part in finite
volume. Theorem~\ref{thm:avgtransfer} draws the conclusion of (D) from an
averaged condition, a spectral gap of the dynamics that resamples cubes of
fixed physical size, on gauge-invariant functions of the Wilson measure on
tori without gauge fixing; that condition is proved at strong coupling
(Corollary~\ref{cor:bpranges}) and is not proved along the tuning, nor is its
local form $\mathrm{BP}_{\rm loc}$, which suffices
(Sec.~\ref{ssec:bpweak}).

For unsmoothed local fields at separated points, Sec.~\ref{ssec:limitE}
reduces (E), given the averaged condition along the tuning, to a moment
bound on separated products whose constants grow at most like a power of the
inverse separation and like $K^n(n!)^\beta$, one rotation of infinite order
and uniqueness of the limit (Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEOS}), from which
reflection positivity in every coordinate direction, clustering in every
direction, the linear growth condition of~\cite{OS75} and, for constants
fixed by one reflected correlation, nontriviality follow; without the rate
in the separation the same inputs give these properties at separated points
only (Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEexact}), and they do not imply temperedness
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnotempered}). For the two-point functions, with
constants fixed by the largest reflected norm, that moment bound is
equivalent to a comparison of reflected norms at short distance
(Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}), and its rate to a power rate in that
comparison (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEtwotempered}); the comparison bounds
the physical gap from above and fails in the strong-coupling range
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}). For $n\ge3$ the moment bound does not
follow from the two-point statements, reflection positivity and the gap, and
its rate does not follow from bounds at each order
(Propositions~\ref{prop:limitEthree} and~\ref{prop:limitEfactorial}).

\begin{remark}\label{rem:cubeheuristic}
The following count relates (D) to a correlation length. Suppose that the
gauge-fixed field responds to a change of one link on the boundary of a cube
through a Poisson kernel with decay length $\xi$ in lattice units. Then the
response summed over the cube is of order $\xi$, so $K_c$ is of order
$n^3\xi$ and $D(n,\alpha)$ holds once $n\gtrsim\xi/\alpha$, that is, once
$\ell\gtrsim\xi a/\alpha$, a fixed multiple of the physical correlation
length. The hypothesis is that the gauge-fixed links decorrelate on the
scale $\xi$, and it is a statement about the gauge, not about the
gauge-invariant observables: the gauge here is built on straight lines, and
in gauges of axial type the gauge-fixed variables can fluctuate on all
scales while gauge-invariant observables cluster. Propositions~\ref{prop:gauss}
and~\ref{prop:gaussforest} carry the count out exactly for Gaussian fields,
and Proposition~\ref{prop:gaussforest} shows that the quadratic
approximation of the forest gauge fluctuates in this way. Where it does,
(D) fails in this gauge and has to be posed for block-averaged variables,
where it becomes part of the renormalisation group problem.
\cite[Sec.~XII\,A]{BBLR26} carries this out in the quadratic approximation.
\end{remark}
