Let $\psi_k(x)=X_{x_0}$, and $A_k$, $f_0$ as in \textup{(iv)}. This field has the properties listed in \textup{(iv)}, and
\textup{(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$)} holds; but $\omega_k(A_k^4)/V_k^2\to\infty$, so
\textup{(M$_{\mathrm{loc}}$)} and \textup{(M)} fail for $n=4$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Since $\Phi$ is real, $A_k(\bar f)=\overline{A_k(f)}$, so by the
Cauchy--Schwarz inequality
\[
\bigl|\omega_k\bigl(A_k(f_1)A_k(f_2)\bigr)\bigr|\le\prod_{i=1}^2
\omega_k\bigl(|A_k(f_i)|^2\bigr)^{1/2},
\]
and it suffices to bound $\omega_k(|A_k(f)|^2)$ for $f=g\otimes h(\cdot-y)$
by $K_LV_k\|g\|_\infty^2$. By spatial translation invariance $y$ may be
taken to be $0$. As in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:mzslice}(i),
$\omega_k(|A_k(f)|^2)$ is $B$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:timekernel} with
$v_n=a_kg(a_kn)u_k$, nonzero for $n$ in a set of $N\le L/a_k+1$ consecutive
integers, so
\[
\begin{split}
\omega_k(|A_k(f)|^2)&\le2\|g\|_\infty^2(a_kN)I_k(a_kN)\\
&\le2\|g\|_\infty^2(L+\delta)\Bigl\lceil\frac{L+\delta}\delta\Bigr\rceil
I_k(\delta),
\end{split}
\]
and Proposition~\ref{prop:mzslice}(i) gives the claim.

(ii) Independence of $\delta$: for dyadic $\delta'>\delta$,
$I^{h'}_k(\delta')\le\lceil\delta'/\delta\rceil I^{h'}_k(\delta)\le
\lceil\delta'/\delta\rceil C\,I_k(\delta')$, and for $\delta'<\delta$,
$I^{h'}_k(\delta')\le I^{h'}_k(\delta)\le C\,I_k(\delta)\le C\lceil\delta/
\delta'\rceil I_k(\delta')$.
Assume (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$), and let $f\in C_c(\R^4)$ be supported in a cube
$Q=[t_Q,t_Q+L]\times Q_3$. As in (i) it suffices to bound
$\omega_k(|A_k(f)|^2)$. By time translation invariance $A_k(f)=\sum_n\tau_n
(a_kU^{h_n}_k)$ with $h_n=f(a_kn,\cdot)$, supported in $Q_3$ with
$\|h_n\|_\infty\le\|f\|_\infty$, and nonzero for $n$ in a set of $N\le
L/a_k+1$ consecutive integers. The identity of the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:msplit}(iv) and translation invariance give
$\omega_k(\bar U^{h_n}_k\tau_jU^{h_{n'}}_k)=\ip{\Psi_{U^{h_n}_k}}{\mathbb
T_k^j\Psi_{U^{h_{n'}}_k}}$ for $j\ge0$, so $\omega_k(|A_k(f)|^2)$ is $B$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:timekernel} with $v_n=a_k\Psi_{U^{h_n}_k}$, and
\[
\begin{split}
\omega_k(|A_k(f)|^2)&\le2(a_kN)\max_nI^{h_n}_k(a_kN)\\
&\le\frac{2(L+\delta)C}{c_-(\delta)}\Bigl\lceil\frac{L+\delta}\delta
\Bigr\rceil\|f\|_\infty^2V_k ,
\end{split}
\]
by (iii) of the lemma, (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) and
Proposition~\ref{prop:mzslice}(i). Conversely, let $g_*\in C_c(\R)$ satisfy
$0\le g_*\le1$, $g_*=0$ outside $[0,1]$ and $g_*=1$ on $[\frac14,\frac34]$,
and let $h'$ be as in (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$). For $a_k\le\frac14$ the $n$ with
$a_kn\in[\frac14,\frac34]$ are $M'\ge1/(4a_k)$ consecutive integers, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:timekernel}(ii) with $v_n=a_kg_*(a_kn)\Psi_{U^{h'}_k}$ and
$\eta=a_k$ gives, with $b=a_k\lceil M'/2\rceil\ge\frac18$,
\[
\omega_k\bigl(|A_k(g_*\otimes h')|^2\bigr)\ge a_kM'\,I^{h'}_k(b)\ge
\frac{I^{h'}_k(\delta)}{4\lceil8\delta\rceil} .
\]
Since $A_k(g_*\otimes\bar h')=\overline{A_k(g_*\otimes h')}$,
(M$_{\mathrm{loc}}$) for $n=2$ on a cube $Q\supseteq[0,1]\times Q_3$ bounds
the left side by $M^{\mathrm{loc}}_2(Q)V_k\le M^{\mathrm{loc}}_2(Q)c_+(\delta)
I_k(\delta)$, which is (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$).

(iii) The bounds are (M$_{\mathrm{loc}}$) with $f_j$ equal to $\bar f$ and
$f$ alternately, since $A_k(\bar f)=\overline{A_k(f)}$. Conversely, for
$f_1,\dots,f_n$ supported in $Q$ and an even $n'\ge n$, H\"older's
inequality gives $|\omega_k(\prod_jA_k(f_j))|\le\prod_j\omega_k(|A_k(f_j)|
^{n'})^{1/n'}$. The case $n=2$ is (M$_{\mathrm{loc}}$) for $n=2$ by the
Cauchy--Schwarz inequality, hence (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) by (ii), and the last
claim is the monotonicity of $L^n$ norms for the probability measure
$\omega_k$.

(iv) The spatial kernel is the sum of two positive definite functions on
$\Z^3$, the first because the Fourier transform of $\kappa$ is positive, the
second because it is $\varepsilon_{\mathbf x}\varepsilon_{\mathbf y}$ with
$\varepsilon_{\mathbf x}=(-1)^{x_1+x_2+x_3}$; both are invariant under the
signed permutations of the spatial coordinates. The field is the stationary
Markov chain of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a), with
$\gamma$ the Gaussian law of this spatial covariance, which gives the
invariance, reflection positivity and the gap, and as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:msplit}(vi) the time factor gives
$\rho_k(u,\sigma)=e^{-m\sigma}$. Put $S_k=\sum_{\mathbf x}a_k^3h(a_k
\mathbf x)\varepsilon_{\mathbf x}$. Grouping $\mathbf x$ with $x_1$ even with
$\mathbf x+e_1$, $|S_k|\le a_k\|\partial_1h\|_\infty(L_h+2a_k)^3$, where
$L_h$ is the side of a cube containing the support of $h$. The part of $V_k$
from the second term of the kernel is
\[
a_k^{-1}\sum_{n,n'}a_k^2g(a_kn)g(a_kn')q_k^{|n-n'|}S_k^2\le a_k^{-1}
\Bigl(a_k\sum_ng(a_kn)\Bigr)^2S_k^2 ,
\]
which tends to $0$, and the same holds for $C_k(0)$. The parts from $\kappa$
are Riemann sums converging to
\[
\begin{split}
&\iint f_0(x)f_0(y)e^{-m|x_0-y_0|}\kappa(\mathbf x-\mathbf y)\,dx\,dy>0,\\
&\iint g(t)g(s)e^{-m(t+s)}dt\,ds\iint h(\mathbf x)h(\mathbf y)
\kappa(\mathbf x-\mathbf y)\,d\mathbf x\,d\mathbf y>0 .
\end{split}
\]
So $\Upsilon_k$ converges, which is (M$_Z$). Let
\[
f_k(t,\mathbf x)=g(t)h(\mathbf x)\cos\bigl(\pi(x_1+x_2+x_3)/a_k\bigr),
\]
a continuous function supported in the support of $f_0$, with
$\|f_k\|_\infty\le\|g\|_\infty\|h\|_\infty$, equal to $g(a_kx_0)h(a_k
\mathbf x)\varepsilon_{\mathbf x}$ at the point $a_kx$. Both terms of the kernel are positive definite, so
\[
\omega_k\bigl(A_k(f_k)^2\bigr)\ge a_k^{-1}\sum_{n,n'}a_k^2g(a_kn)g(a_kn')
q_k^{|n-n'|}\Bigl(\sum_{\mathbf x}a_k^3h(a_k\mathbf x)\Bigr)^2 ,
\]
and the factor of $a_k^{-1}$ on the right converges to
\[
\iint g(t)g(s)e^{-m|t-s|}dt\,ds\,\Bigl(\int h\Bigr)^2>0 ,
\]
while $V_k$ converges. So (M$_{\mathrm{loc}}$) fails for $n=2$, and
with it (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$), by (ii), and (M), by
Proposition~\ref{prop:msplit}(ii).

(v) $P=\Pi+q_k(I-\Pi)$ is symmetric on $L^2(\pi)$ with spectrum
$\{1,q_k\}$ and $1$ simple, and $P\ge0$; the argument of the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a) gives reflection positivity, and the
gap is $ma_k$. The field does not depend on the spatial coordinates, which
gives the invariance. Since $X\perp1$ and $\pi(X^2)=1$, $P^jX=q_k^jX$ and
the covariance is $q_k^{|x_0-y_0|}$, that of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(b); so $\rho_k(u,\sigma)=e^{-m\sigma}$,
and $V_k$ and $C_k(0)$ converge to positive limits, which gives (M$_Z$).
Further $U^{h'}_k=(\sum_{\mathbf x}a_k^3h'(a_k\mathbf x))X_0$, so
$I^{h'}_k(\delta)/I_k(\delta)=|\sum a_k^3h'(a_k\mathbf x)|^2/(\sum a_k^3
h(a_k\mathbf x))^2$, which is bounded for $h'$ as in (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$),
since the denominator converges to $(\int h)^2>0$. For times $s_1\le s_2\le
s_3\le s_4$ with $j_i=s_{i+1}-s_i$, the Markov property and $P^j=\Pi+
q_k^j(I-\Pi)$ give, as in Remark~\ref{rem:markov} and with $X^3=X/\epsilon$,
\[
\E\Bigl[\prod_{i=1}^4X_{s_i}\Bigr]=q_k^{j_1+j_3}\bigl(q_k^{j_2}/\epsilon_k
+1-q_k^{j_2}\bigr)\ge\epsilon_k^{-1}q_k^{s_4-s_1} .
\]
With $F_n=a_k^3\sum_{\mathbf x}f_0(a_kn,a_k\mathbf x)\ge0$, $A_k=\sum_n
a_kF_nX_n$, and $F_n=0$ unless $a_kn\in[t_0,t_1]$, so
\[
\omega_k(A_k^4)\ge\epsilon_k^{-1}e^{-m(t_1-t_0)}\Bigl(a_k\sum_nF_n\Bigr)^4 ,
\]
and $a_k\sum_nF_n\to\int f_0>0$. Since $\epsilon_k=a_k\to0$ and $V_k$
converges, the ratio diverges.
\end{proof}

Part (i) is the part of (M$_{\mathrm{loc}}$) that the positivity of the
transfer matrix gives: test functions in time, on both sides of the
reflection plane and of either sign, cost no more than the packet $f_0$, for
every state with a positive transfer matrix. What remains for $n=2$ is
(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$), a comparison between spatial profiles at one spacing,
which involves neither the reflection nor the tuning. Example (iv) has a
spatial mode at the lattice scale whose variance diverges, and the time
structure of an exact Markov field; example (v) has no spatial structure
and four-point functions of order $1/\epsilon_k$, as in
Remark~\ref{rem:markov}. For a Gaussian field the linear heat flow in space at the flow time
$\tau/a_k^2$ multiplies the variance of the lattice-scale mode of (iv) by
$e^{-c\tau/a_k^2}$, with $c>0$ fixed by the normalisation of the flow, and
removes the divergence there. For the gauge field the flow does not do this
configuration by configuration.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:centralpattern}
Let $G=\SU(N)$, $N\ge2$, $\zeta=e^{2\pi i/N}$ and $c_N=N(1-\cos(2\pi/N))$.
Let $m:\Z\to\Z$ satisfy $m(j+1)-m(j)=1$ for $j\equiv0,1$ and
$m(j+1)-m(j)=0$ for $j\equiv2,3$ modulo $4$, and let $U^\sharp$ be the
configuration with all temporal links equal to $1$ and spatial links
\[
U^\sharp_{(x,x+e_1)}=U^\sharp_{(x,x+e_3)}=1,\qquad
U^\sharp_{(x,x+e_2)}=\zeta^{m(x_1)} .
\]

\textup{(i)} The spatial plaquettes of $U^\sharp$ in the plane of $e_1,e_2$
with base point $x$ are $\zeta^{\pm1}$ for $x_1\equiv0,1$ and $1$ for
$x_1\equiv2,3$ modulo $4$, all other spatial plaquettes are $1$, and
$\Phi_s(gU^\sharp)=gU^\sharp$ for every gauge transformation $g$ and every
$s\in\R$.

\textup{(ii)} Let $\Phi$ be the sum of $N-\ReTr U_p$ over the twelve spatial
plaquettes with a corner at $0$. Then for all $x$ and $s$
\[
\Phi_x\circ\Phi_s(U^\sharp)=2c_N\Bigl(1+\cos\frac{\pi(x_1-1)}2\Bigr).
\]

\textup{(iii)} Let $g\in C_c(\R)$ and $h\in C_c(\R^3)$ be nonnegative and
$h^\sharp_k(\mathbf x)=h(\mathbf x)\cos(\pi(x_1/a_k-1)/2)$, and let
$\omega$ be as in Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:stationary}(iii)}. At every
flow time the $\omega$-essential supremum and infimum of $A_k(g\otimes
h^\sharp_k)$ differ by at least
\[
4c_N\,a_k^4\sum_ng(a_kn)\!\!\sum_{\mathbf x:\,x_1\equiv1\ (4)}\!\!h(a_k
\mathbf x) ,
\]
which converges to $c_N\int g\int h$. For $A_k(g\otimes h)$ the
difference is at least
\[
12c_Na_k^4\sum_ng(a_kn)\sum_{\mathbf x}h(a_k\mathbf x) ,
\]
which converges to $12c_N\int g\int h$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) With the plaquette formula of the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:stationary}(i), the plaquette in the plane of $e_1,e_2$
at $x$ is $\zeta^{m(x_1+1)-m(x_1)}$; in the plane of $e_1,e_3$ all four links
are $1$, and in the plane of $e_2,e_3$ it is $\zeta^{m(x_1)}\zeta^{-m(x_1)}
=1$, because $x$ and $x+e_3$ have the same first coordinate. All links are
central, and the computation of the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:stationary}(i) shows that the gradient in the flow
equation vanishes; uniqueness and gauge covariance give the rest.

(ii) Only the four plaquettes of the plane of $e_1,e_2$ with a corner at
$x$ can be nontrivial; their base points have first coordinates $x_1$ and
$x_1-1$, twice each. So $\Phi_x(U^\sharp)=2c_N(d(x_1)+d(x_1-1))$ with
$d(j)=m(j+1)-m(j)$, which is $2c_N$ times $1,2,1,0$ for $x_1\equiv0,1,2,3$,
and the flow does not move $U^\sharp$.

(iii) With $c_x=\cos(\pi(x_1-1)/2)\in\{1,0,-1,0\}$ for $x_1\equiv1,2,3,0$,
\[
\begin{split}
&\sum_xa_k^4g(a_kx_0)h^\sharp_k(a_k\mathbf x)\,\Phi_x\circ\Phi_s(U^\sharp)\\
&\qquad=2c_N\sum_xa_k^4g(a_kx_0)h(a_k\mathbf x)(c_x+c_x^2) ,
\end{split}
\]
and $c_x+c_x^2$ is $2$ for $x_1\equiv1$ and $0$ otherwise; at the
configuration $1$ the sum is $0$. The proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:stationary}(iii) applies with $U^\sharp$ in place of
$U^*$; the centring constant cancels in the difference. The points with
$x_1\equiv1$ modulo $4$ form a sublattice of index four, which gives the
limit. The second statement is Proposition~\ref{prop:stationary}(iii).
\end{proof}

At every flow time the packet with a spatial profile oscillating at the
lattice scale has, at the stationary configurations, a range of values of
the same order as the smooth packet, the ratio of the two lower bounds
tending to $1/12$. A proof of (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) by way of the flow has
therefore to bound the probability of the configurations near those with
nontrivial central plaquettes, uniformly along the tuning; this is an
estimate on large fields of the Wilson measure at the couplings $\beta_k$,
of the kind of \cite[Sec.~XII\,E]{BBLR26}, compared with the size of
$I_k(\delta)$, which is not known.

\begin{remark}\label{rem:opensplit}
For the flowed fields along a tuning, with a product packet $f_0=g\otimes
h$, the five statements stand as follows.

(M$_{\mathrm{loc}}$): the case $n=1$ holds since the packets are centred,
and the case $n=2$ holds on the products of time profiles with the lattice
translates of $h$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:mlocslice}(i)), for every state with
a positive transfer matrix. Not proved: (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$), equivalent to
the case $n=2$, and the bounds $\omega_k(|A_k(f)|^n)\le C_n(Q)V_k^{n/2}
\|f\|_\infty^n$ for even $n\ge4$, which with it are equivalent to
(M$_{\mathrm{loc}}$). Both concern one spacing at a time, without
reflection; they are not consequences of the gap, $(\mathrm N_u)$, (RC),
(M$_Z$), reflection positivity and the symmetry $\Gamma$, by
Proposition~\ref{prop:mlocslice}(iv), (v), so no argument that uses (D)
only through the transfer-matrix gap, together with $(\mathrm N_u)$, gives
them.

(M$_Z$): equivalent to (Z$_{\mathrm{sl}}$), $I_k(\delta)\le C\,D_k(\delta)$
for the slice field, and independent of $g$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:mzslice}). Not proved. It implies $(\mathrm N_u)$ for
every $u$; it is not implied by $(\mathrm N_u)$, the gap and the lattice
symmetries (Proposition~\ref{prop:msplit}(vi)), nor by the hypothesis of
Theorem~\ref{thm:transfer} (Proposition~\ref{prop:cubecompatible}(ii)). The
missing estimate is a bound on the high-energy spectral weight of the slice
field, with the factor $1/E$, and on $a_k\Var U_k$, by its correlation at a
physical separation.

(W): equivalent to $\Delta^{E_{12}}_k(f_1,\dots,f_n)\to0$ for all $n$ and
$f_j\in C^\infty_c(\R^4)$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:wardform}(ii)); a bound
$C(f_1,\dots,f_n)a_k^p$ with $p>0$ suffices. Not proved. Example (a) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction} has the gap in every direction, (RC),
(M), hence (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) and (Z$_{\mathrm{sl}}$), and (U), and not
(W); so no argument that uses (D) only through the gap, together with
$(\mathrm N_u)$ and (M), gives (W). The lattice
symmetry gives the cancellation of $\Delta^{E_{12}}_k$ only on average over
the angle.

(U1) and (U2), given (RC): (U1) is $\delta_k\to0$, for which
$\sum_k|\delta_k|<\infty$ on a set with dense span suffices
(Proposition~\ref{prop:tuned}(iii)), and (U2) is the total disconnectedness
of $\mathfrak L$. Not proved. Example (iv) of Proposition~\ref{prop:tuned}
and its alternating variant satisfy (RC), (M) and (W), each with one of
(U1), (U2) and not the other, so neither follows from the gap,
$(\mathrm N_u)$, (RC), (M) and (W); (U1) compares two spacings, which no
statement at one spacing contains.

The examples are Gaussian and Markov fields, not lattice gauge theories. They
show that a proof of any of the four open statements has to use properties
of the Wilson measure along the tuning beyond translation and $\Gamma$
invariance, reflection positivity, the transfer-matrix gap, the
normalisation (RC) and the parts of (M) proved or assumed here.
\end{remark}

\emph{The spatial spectral measure of the slice field.} The comparison
(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) is a statement about the distribution in spatial
momentum of the time-integrated correlations of the slice field. In this
paragraph the states are invariant under translations and $\vartheta$ and
reflection positive, with the transfer matrices of
Appendix~\ref{app:transfer}, $\Phi$ is real, $\delta>0$ is dyadic and $k$ is
so large that $n_\delta=\delta/a_k$ is an even integer. Write
$\mathring\varphi_k(x)=\varphi_k(x)-\omega_k(\varphi_k(x))$,
\[
\begin{split}
K_k(z)&=a_k\sum_{j=0}^{n_\delta-1}\omega_k\bigl(\mathring\varphi_k(0,0)\,
\mathring\varphi_k(j,z)\bigr),\\
K^V_k(z)&=a_k^2\sum_{n,n'=0}^{n_\delta-1}\omega_k\bigl(\mathring\varphi_k(n,0)\,
\mathring\varphi_k(n',z)\bigr)
\end{split}
\]
for $z\in\Z^3$. For a finitely supported $c:\Z^3\to\C$ put $\hat c(p)=
\sum_xc_xe^{ip\cdot x}$ on $\mathbf T^3=(\R/2\pi\Z)^3$,
$U^c_k=\sum_xc_x\mathring\varphi_k(0,x)$, and
\[
I^c_k=a_k\sum_{j=0}^{n_\delta-1}\omega_k\bigl(\bar U^c_k\,\tau_jU^c_k\bigr),
\qquad V^c_k=\omega_k\Bigl(\Bigl|a_k\sum_{n=0}^{n_\delta-1}\tau_nU^c_k
\Bigr|^2\Bigr).
\]
For $c_x=a_k^3h'(a_kx)$, $U^c_k=U^{h'}_k$ and $I^c_k=I^{h'}_k(\delta)$. By
translation invariance $I^c_k=\sum_{x,y}\bar c_xc_yK_k(y-x)$ and $V^c_k=
\sum_{x,y}\bar c_xc_yK^V_k(y-x)$. For a closed box $\mathcal B_r(p_0)=
\{p:|p_i-p_{0,i}|\le r,\ i=1,2,3\}$ in $\mathbf T^3$, $r>0$, and a finite
positive measure $\varpi$ on $\mathbf T^3$, put $\bar\varpi(r)=\sup_{p_0}
\varpi(\mathcal B_r(p_0))$. A box of half-side $r'$ is covered by
$\lceil r'/r\rceil^3$ boxes of half-side $r$, so $\bar\varpi(r')\le\lceil
r'/r\rceil^3\bar\varpi(r)$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:spatialmeasure}
\textup{(i)} There are unique finite positive measures $\varpi_k$ and
$\varpi^V_k$ on $\mathbf T^3$ with $K_k(z)=\int e^{ip\cdot z}d\varpi_k(p)$
and $K^V_k(z)=\int e^{ip\cdot z}d\varpi^V_k(p)$ for $z\in\Z^3$. For every
finitely supported $c$
\[
I^c_k=\int|\hat c|^2d\varpi_k,\qquad V^c_k=\int|\hat c|^2d\varpi^V_k,
\qquad\tfrac\delta2I^c_k\le V^c_k\le2\delta I^c_k ,
\]
and $\varpi_k(\mathbf T^3)=K_k(0)\le\delta\|\Phi\|_\infty^2$.

\textup{(ii)} Let $\varpi$ be a finite positive measure on $\mathbf T^3$,
$L\ge4a_k$, and $c_x=a_k^3h'(a_kx)$ with $h'$ supported in a cube of side
$L$ and $|h'|\le1$. Then
\[
\int|\hat c|^2d\varpi\le7\cdot10^6L^6\,\bar\varpi(\pi a_k/L) .
\]

\textup{(iii)} Let $\chi\in C_c(\R^3)$ take values in $[0,1]$, be
supported in a cube $Q'$ of side $\ell\ge4a_k$ and equal $1$ on the cube of
side $\ell/2$ with the same centre, and for $p_0\in\mathbf T^3$ let
$h'(\mathbf y)=\chi(\mathbf y)e^{-ip_0\cdot\mathbf y/a_k}$. Then for every
finite positive measure $\varpi$
\[
\varpi\bigl(\mathcal B_{a_k/(3\ell)}(p_0)\bigr)\le16384\,\ell^{-6}\!\int
|\hat c|^2d\varpi,\qquad c_x=a_k^3h'(a_kx).
\]

\textup{(iv)} Let $h\in C_c(\R^3)$ be real with $\int h\ne0$, supported in
a cube of side $L_h$, and $\rho_h=L_h^{-1}(|\int h|/(72\int|h|))^{1/2}$.
For all large $k$, with $c_x=a_k^3h(a_kx)$, $|\hat c|\ge\frac34|\int h|$ on
$\mathcal B_{a_k\rho_h}(0)$, so $\int|\hat c|^2d\varpi\ge\frac12(\int h)^2
\varpi(\mathcal B_{a_k\rho_h}(0))$ for every finite positive measure
$\varpi$.

\textup{(v)} Let $\varkappa(p)=\sum_{i=1}^34\sin^2(p_i/2)$, $m\ge1$, $h\in
C^{2m}_c(\R^3)$ real, supported in a cube of side $L_h$, $\|h\|_{2m}$ the
largest supremum of a derivative of order $2m$ of $h$, and $\Theta_m=3^m
\|h\|_{2m}(L_h+2m+1)^3$. For $a_k\le1$ and $c_x=a_k^3h(a_kx)$,
$\varkappa^m|\hat c|\le\Theta_ma_k^{2m}$ on $\mathbf T^3$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i) With $u_c=\Psi_{U^c_k}$, the identity of the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:msplit}(iv) gives $\omega_k(\bar U^c_k\tau_jU^c_k)=
\ip{u_c}{\mathbb T_k^ju_c}\ge0$ for $j\ge0$, so $I^c_k\ge0$ and $K_k$ is
positive definite; $V^c_k\ge0$ shows the same for $K^V_k$. For a positive
definite bounded $K$ on $\Z^3$ and an integer $M\ge1$, the function
\[
f_M(p)=M^{-3}\!\!\sum_{x,y\in\{0,\dots,M-1\}^3}\!\!K(y-x)e^{-ip\cdot(y-x)}
\]
is nonnegative, and $\int e^{ip\cdot z}f_M(p)\frac{dp}{(2\pi)^3}=K(z)
\prod_i(1-|z_i|/M)_+$. The measures $f_M\,dp/(2\pi)^3$ have mass $K(0)$, so
by Lemma~\ref{lem:helly} a subsequence converges weakly to a measure with
Fourier coefficients $K(z)$; trigonometric polynomials are dense in
$C(\mathbf T^3)$, which gives uniqueness. Then $\sum_{x,y}\bar c_xc_y
\int e^{ip\cdot(y-x)}d\varpi=\int|\hat c|^2d\varpi$. The numbers $\ip{u_c}{\mathbb
T_k^ju_c}$ are real, so translation invariance and complex conjugation
give
$\omega_k(\overline{\tau_nU^c_k}\,\tau_{n'}U^c_k)=\ip{u_c}{\mathbb
T_k^{|n-n'|}u_c}$ for all $n,n'$, and $V^c_k$ is $B$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:timekernel} with $v_n=a_ku_c$ for $0\le n<n_\delta$. Part (i)
of that lemma gives $V^c_k\le2n_\delta a_kI^c_k=2\delta I^c_k$; part (ii)
with $\eta=a_k$, $M=n_\delta$ and part (iii) give $V^c_k\ge\delta\,a_k
\sum_{j<n_\delta/2}\ip{u_c}{\mathbb T_k^ju_c}\ge\frac\delta2I^c_k$. Finally
$|\omega_k(\mathring\varphi_k(0,0)\mathring\varphi_k(j,0))|\le\|\Phi\|_\infty^2$, since
$\varphi_k$ takes values in $[-\|\Phi\|_\infty,\|\Phi\|_\infty]$.

(ii) Translating $c$ by a lattice vector multiplies $\hat c$ by a phase.
With $n=\lceil L/(2a_k)\rceil$ the support of $c$ may therefore be taken in
$\{|x_i|\le n\}$; it has at most $(L/a_k+1)^3$ points. Let $F_n(t)=
\sum_{|j|\le n}(1-\frac{|j|}{n+1})e^{ijt}=\frac1{n+1}\bigl(\sin\frac{(n+1)t}
2/\sin\frac t2\bigr)^2$ be the Fej\'er kernel and $W_n=2F_{2n+1}-F_n$. Its
coefficients equal $1$ for $|j|\le n$, $\int|W_n|\frac{dt}{2\pi}\le3$, and
from $F_n\le n+1$ and $\sin\frac t2\ge\frac t\pi$ on $[0,\pi]$,
$F_n(t)\le2(n+1)/(1+((n+1)t/\pi)^2)$ for $|t|\le\pi$, so
\[
|W_n(t)|\le\frac{10(n+1)}{1+((n+1)t/\pi)^2}\qquad(|t|\le\pi) .
\]
With $W(q)=\prod_iW_n(q_i)$, $\hat c(p)=\int\hat c(q)W(p-q)dq'$, where
$dq'=dq/(2\pi)^3$, and the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality gives $|\hat c(p)|^2
\le27\int|\hat c(q)|^2|W(p-q)|dq'$. Divide $\mathbf T^3$ into the
$(2n+2)^3$ half-open boxes of side $\pi/(n+1)$ centred at the points
$q+\pi j/(n+1)$, $j\in\{-n,\dots,n+1\}^3$; each lies in a closed box of
half-side $\pi/(2(n+1))\le\pi a_k/L$, and on the box with index $j$ the
$i$-th factor of $|W(p-q)|$ is at most $10(n+1)$ if $j_i=0$ and at most
$10(n+1)/(1+(|j_i|-\frac12)^2)$ otherwise. Since $(1+(x-\frac12)^2)^{-1}$
decreases on $[\frac12,\infty)$, $\sum_{j\ge2}(1+(j-\frac12)^2)^{-1}\le
\frac\pi2-\arctan\frac12\le1.1125$, using $\arctan\frac12\ge\frac12-
\frac1{24}$. The bounds of the $i$-th factor therefore add up to at most
$10(n+1)(1+2(0.8+1.1125))\le50(n+1)$, the term $j=1$ being $0.8$, and
\[
\int|W(p-q)|d\varpi(p)\le\bigl(50(n+1)\bigr)^3\bar\varpi(\pi a_k/L).
\]
Integrating against $|\hat c(q)|^2dq'$ and using $\int|\hat c|^2dq'=
\sum_x|c_x|^2\le a_k^3(L+a_k)^3$ and $n+1\le(L+4a_k)/(2a_k)$,
\[
\int|\hat c|^2d\varpi\le\frac{27\cdot50^3}8(L+a_k)^3(L+4a_k)^3\,
\bar\varpi(\pi a_k/L),
\]
and $\frac{27\cdot50^3}8(L+a_k)^3(L+4a_k)^3\le421875(\frac54)^3\,8\,L^6
<7\cdot10^6L^6$ for $a_k\le L/4$.

(iii) Here $\hat c(p)=\sum_xa_k^3\chi(a_kx)e^{i(p-p_0)\cdot x}$. Let
$\mathbf y_0$ be the centre of $Q'$ and $x_0\in\Z^3$ a nearest lattice
point to $\mathbf y_0/a_k$. For $a_kx\in Q'$, $|x_i-x_{0,i}|\le\ell/(2a_k)+
\frac12\le\frac58\ell/a_k$, so for $p\in\mathcal B_{a_k/(3\ell)}(p_0)$ the
phase $(p-p_0)\cdot(x-x_0)$ is at most $\frac58$ in absolute value and
$|\hat c(p)|\ge\cos(1)\sum_xa_k^3\chi(a_kx)\ge\frac12a_k^3\#\{x:a_kx\in
Q''\}$, $Q''$ the middle cube. It has at least $\lfloor\ell/(2a_k)\rfloor\ge
\ell/(4a_k)$ lattice points in each direction, so $|\hat c(p)|\ge
\ell^3/128$ on the box, and $\varpi(\mathcal B_{a_k/(3\ell)}(p_0))
(\ell^3/128)^2\le\int|\hat c|^2d\varpi$.

(iv) Let $\mathbf x_*$ be the centre of the cube divided by $a_k$. For
$p\in\mathcal B_{a_k\rho_h}(0)$ and $a_kx$ in the cube, $\theta_x=p\cdot
(x-\mathbf x_*)$ satisfies $|\theta_x|\le3a_k\rho_hL_h/(2a_k)$, so
$\theta_x^2/2\le\frac98\rho_h^2L_h^2=|\int h|/(64\int|h|)$, and
\[
\begin{split}
|\hat c(p)|&\ge\Bigl|\sum_xa_k^3h(a_kx)\cos\theta_x\Bigr|\\
&\ge\Bigl|\sum_xa_k^3h(a_kx)\Bigr|-\frac{|\int h|}{64\int|h|}\sum_x
a_k^3|h(a_kx)| .
\end{split}
\]
For large $k$ the Riemann sums are within $\frac18|\int h|$ of $\int h$
and below $2\int|h|$, which gives $|\hat c|\ge(\frac78-\frac1{32})|\int h|$.

(v) With $(\Delta c)_x=\sum_i(c_{x+e_i}+c_{x-e_i}-2c_x)$,
$\widehat{\Delta c}=-\varkappa\hat c$, so $\varkappa^m|\hat c|\le\sum_x|(\Delta^mc)
_x|$. The operator $\Delta^m$ is a sum of $3^m$ products of $m$ second
differences $D_i\phi(x)=\phi(x+e_i)+\phi(x-e_i)-2\phi(x)=\int_{-1}^1(1-|t|)
\partial_t^2\phi(x+te_i)\,dt$, so $|(\Delta^mc)_x|\le3^ma_k^{3+2m}
\|h\|_{2m}$, and $(\Delta^mc)_x=0$ unless $x$ is within distance $m$ of
$a_k^{-1}\operatorname{supp}h$ in each coordinate, which leaves at most
$(L_h/a_k+2m+1)^3$ points.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:boxform}
Let $f_0=g\otimes h$ be a product packet as in
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:sliceratio}}, so that $I_k=\int|\hat c_h|^2
d\varpi_k$ with $(c_h)_x=a_k^3h(a_kx)$.

\textup{(i)} \textup{(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$)} holds at $\delta$ if and only if
there are $\rho>0$ and $C<\infty$ with
\[
\bar\varpi_k(a_k\rho)\le C\,I_k(\delta)\quad\text{for all large }k ;
\]
if it holds for $\rho'$ with $C$, it holds for every $\rho>0$ with
$\lceil\rho/\rho'\rceil^3C$. If
\textup{(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$)} holds with the constant $C$ for the unit cube,
the bound holds with $\rho=\frac13$ and $16384\,C$; conversely the bound
gives \textup{(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$)} with the constant $7\cdot10^6L^6\lceil
\pi/(L\rho)\rceil^3C$ for cubes of side $L$.

\textup{(ii)} If the bound of \textup{(i)} holds with $\rho$ and $C$, then
$K_k(0)\le C\lceil\pi/(a_k\rho)\rceil^3I_k(\delta)$ for all large $k$.

\textup{(iii)} If $\int h\ne0$, \textup{(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$)} holds as soon
as $\bar\varpi_k(a_k\rho_h)\le C\,\varpi_k(\mathcal B_{a_k\rho_h}(0))$ for
all large $k$, with $\rho_h$ as in Lemma~\textup{\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(iv)}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Since $\bar\varpi(r')\le\lceil r'/r\rceil^3\bar\varpi(r)$ and
$\bar\varpi$ is nondecreasing, the bound for $\rho'$ gives it for every
$\rho>0$. If (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) holds for the unit cube, apply
Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(iii) with $\ell=1$ to a fixed $\chi$ and
every $p_0$: $\varpi_k(\mathcal B_{a_k/3}(p_0))\le16384\,I^{h'}_k(\delta)
\le16384\,C\,I_k(\delta)$; if only real $h'$ are admitted, the real and
imaginary parts of the probe give the same with $4\cdot16384\,C$, since
$|\hat c|^2\le2(|\widehat{\operatorname{Re}c}|^2+|\widehat{\operatorname{Im}
c}|^2)$. Conversely, for $h'$ supported in a cube of side
$L$ with $|h'|\le1$, Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(ii) gives
$I^{h'}_k(\delta)\le7\cdot10^6L^6\bar\varpi_k(\pi a_k/L)\le7\cdot10^6L^6
\lceil\pi/(L\rho)\rceil^3\bar\varpi_k(a_k\rho)$ once $a_k\le L/4$.

(ii) $K_k(0)=\varpi_k(\mathbf T^3)$, and $\mathbf T^3$ is covered by
$\lceil\pi/(a_k\rho)\rceil^3$ boxes of half-side $a_k\rho$.

(iii) Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(iv) gives $\varpi_k(\mathcal
B_{a_k\rho_h}(0))\le2I_k(\delta)/(\int h)^2$, and (i) applies with
$\rho=\rho_h$.
\end{proof}

So (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) says that no box of lattice momenta of side of order
$a_k$ carries more of the time-integrated spectral weight than a constant
times $I_k(\delta)$; by (ii) it contains the bound $K_k(0)\le C'a_k^{-3}
I_k(\delta)$ on the time-integrated autocorrelation at one site. Part (iii)
reduces it, for $\int h\ne0$, to a comparison of the weight of every box
with that of the box at zero momentum. Reflection positivity in the spatial
directions gives that comparison when it holds exactly. For $r\ge0$,
$i\in\{1,2,3\}$, let $\iota^{\mathrm s}_i$ and $\iota^{\mathrm b}_i$
be the reflections $x_i\mapsto-x_i$ and $x_i\mapsto-1-x_i$ of $\Z^4$, and
consider
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(SR$_r$)},leftmargin=*]
\item Let $i\in\{1,2,3\}$ and $\iota\in\{\iota^{\mathrm s}_i,
\iota^{\mathrm b}_i\}$. For every $c:\Z^4\to\C$ of finite support in
the half-space $\{x_i\ge r\}$,
\[
\sum_{x,y}\bar c_xc_y\,\omega_k\bigl(\mathring\varphi_k(\iota x)\,
\mathring\varphi_k(y)\bigr)\ge0 .
\]
\end{enumerate}
For a field $\varphi_k(x)=\Phi_x$ without flow, with $\Phi$ a function of
the links within distance $r$ of $0$ in the slice, and a state reflection
positive for the spatial reflections, \textup{(SR$_r$)} holds, since $\Phi$
is $W_3$-invariant; for the flowed fields no $r$ is known.

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:spatialrp}
Assume \textup{(SR$_0$)} for the slice fields at the spacing $a_k$.

\textup{(i)} $\varpi^V_k(\mathcal B_r(p_0))\le\varpi^V_k(\mathcal B_r(0))$
for every $p_0\in\mathbf T^3$ and $r>0$.

\textup{(ii)} Let $h\in C_c(\R^3)$ be real with $\int h\ne0$, and $\rho_h$
as in Lemma~\textup{\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(iv)}. If \textup{(SR$_0$)}
holds for all large $k$, then for every cube of side $L$, all large $k$ and
all $h'$ supported in it with $|h'|\le1$,
\[
I^{h'}_k(\delta)\le\frac{6\cdot10^7L^6}{(\int h)^2}\Bigl\lceil\frac\pi
{L\rho_h}\Bigr\rceil^3I^h_k(\delta) ,
\]
so \textup{(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$)} holds for the spatial factor $h$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) Fix $i$, write $x=(x_i,x_\perp)$, and let $b$ be a finitely supported
function of $x_\perp$. Put $k_b(n)=\sum_{x_\perp,y_\perp}\bar b_{x_\perp}
b_{y_\perp}K^V_k(ne_i+y_\perp-x_\perp)$ for $n\in\Z$. For $\alpha$ finitely
supported in $\Z_{\ge0}$ let $c_{(t,x)}=a_k\mathbf 1_{0\le t<n_\delta}
\alpha_{x_i}b_{x_\perp}$, supported in $\{x_i\ge0\}$. By translation
invariance the sum of (SR$_0$) for $\iota^{\mathrm s}_i$ is
$\sum_{j,l\ge0}\bar\alpha_j\alpha_lk_b(j+l)$, and for $\iota^{\mathrm b}_i$
it is $\sum_{j,l\ge0}\bar\alpha_j\alpha_lk_b(j+l+1)$; both are
nonnegative. On finitely supported sequences put $\ip\alpha\beta_b=\sum_{j,l}
\bar\alpha_j\beta_lk_b(j+l)$ and $(S\alpha)_j=\alpha_{j-1}$, with
$\alpha_{-1}=0$. Then $\ip\cdot\cdot_b$ is positive semidefinite, $S$ is
symmetric with $\ip\alpha{S\alpha}_b\ge0$, and $\|S^m\alpha\|_b^2\le
(\sum_j|\alpha_j|)^2\sup_n|k_b(n)|$ is bounded in $m$. Since $S$ is symmetric, the Cauchy--Schwarz
inequality gives $\|S^j\alpha\|_b^2=\ip\alpha{S^{2j}\alpha}_b\le\|\alpha\|_b
\|S^{2j}\alpha\|_b$ for $j\ge1$, and with $j=1,2,4,\dots$, by induction
\[
\|S\alpha\|_b\le\|\alpha\|_b^{1-2^{-q}}\|S^{2^q}\alpha\|_b^{2^{-q}}
\qquad(q\ge1),
\]
so $\|S\alpha\|_b\le\|\alpha\|_b$. Hence $S$ passes to the completion of
the quotient by the null vectors as a self-adjoint operator with $0\le S\le1$,
and $k_b(n)=\ip{e_0}{S^ne_0}_b$ for $n\ge0$. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:spectralmeasure} there is a finite positive measure $\mu_b$
on $[0,1]$ with $k_b(n)=\int\lambda^nd\mu_b$, real, and $k_b(-n)=
\overline{k_b(n)}$ because $K^V_k$ is positive definite. So $k_b(n)=\int
\lambda^{|n|}d\mu_b(\lambda)$ for all $n\in\Z$.

The measure $\varpi_b$ on the circle with Fourier coefficients $k_b(n)$ is
the image of $|\hat b(p_\perp)|^2d\varpi^V_k(p)$ under $p\mapsto p_i$.
Since $\int e^{int}P_\lambda(t)\frac{dt}{2\pi}=\lambda^{|n|}$ for the Poisson
kernel $P_\lambda(t)=(1-\lambda^2)/(1-2\lambda\cos t+\lambda^2)$, $0\le
\lambda<1$, uniqueness of Fourier coefficients gives $\varpi_b=\int
P_\lambda\frac{dt}{2\pi}\,d\mu_b(\lambda)$, with $P_1\frac{dt}{2\pi}$ read
as the point mass at $0$. Each $P_\lambda$ is even and nonincreasing in
$|t|$ on $[-\pi,\pi]$, so its superlevel sets are arcs centred at $0$, and
for an arc $J$ of length $|J|<2\pi$ and the arc $J_0$ of the same length
centred at $0$,
\[
\begin{split}
\int_JP_\lambda\,dt&=\int_0^\infty|J\cap\{P_\lambda>s\}|\,ds\\
&\le\int_0^\infty|J_0\cap\{P_\lambda>s\}|\,ds=\int_{J_0}P_\lambda\,dt ;
\end{split}
\]
the point mass also gives $J_0$ at least the weight of $J$. So
$\varpi_b(J)\le\varpi_b(J_0)$, that is $\int(\mathbf 1_{J_0}-\mathbf 1_J)
(p_i)\,|\hat b(p_\perp)|^2d\varpi^V_k\ge0$. Every continuous $g\ge0$ on
the transverse torus is a uniform limit of functions $|\hat b|^2$, since
$\sqrt g$ is a uniform limit of trigonometric polynomials, so the signed
measure $A\mapsto\varpi^V_k(\{p_i\in J_0,\ p_\perp\in A\})-\varpi^V_k(\{p_i
\in J,\ p_\perp\in A\})$ is nonnegative. Applying this with $A$ a product of
two closed arcs, successively for $i=1,2,3$, moves each coordinate of the
centre of $\mathcal B_r(p_0)$ to $0$ without decreasing the weight; for
$r\ge\pi$ there is nothing to prove.

(ii) Let $k$ be so large that $a_k\le L/4$ and
Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(iv) applies. Then
Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(i), (ii), (iv) and part (i) give
\[
\begin{split}
I^{h'}_k(\delta)&\le\tfrac2\delta V^{h'}_k\le\tfrac2\delta\,7\cdot10^6L^6
\Bigl\lceil\frac\pi{L\rho_h}\Bigr\rceil^3\varpi^V_k\bigl(\mathcal
B_{a_k\rho_h}(0)\bigr)\\
&\le\tfrac2\delta\,7\cdot10^6L^6\Bigl\lceil\frac\pi{L\rho_h}\Bigr\rceil^3
\frac{2V^h_k}{(\int h)^2}\\
&\le\frac{56\cdot10^6L^6}{(\int h)^2}\Bigl\lceil
\frac\pi{L\rho_h}\Bigr\rceil^3I^h_k(\delta),
\end{split}
\]
using $V^h_k\le2\delta I^h_k(\delta)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:rpexamples}
Let $m>0$ and $q_k=e^{-ma_k}$, let $\varkappa$ and $\Theta_2$ be as in
Lemma~\textup{\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(v)}, and let $\Delta$ be the
spatial lattice Laplacian, $(\Delta\phi)(x)=\sum_{i=1}^3(\phi(x+e_i)+
\phi(x-e_i)-2\phi(x))$.

\textup{(a)} Let $\psi_k$ be the field of
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a)} and $\varphi_k=\Delta^2
\psi_k$. Its law is invariant under translations and $\Gamma$ and reflection
positive for the time reflections through sites and bisecting links, its
transfer matrix has gap $ma_k$, $\rho_k(u,\sigma)=e^{-m\sigma}$,
\textup{(Z$_{\mathrm{sl}}$)} and \textup{(M$_Z$)} hold for every product
packet, and \textup{(SR$_2$)} holds. For every real $h\in C^4_c(\R^3)$ and
every cube $Q_3$, \textup{(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$)} fails.

\textup{(b)} The field $\phi_k=\psi_k+a_k^{-5/2}\xi_k$ of
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:msplit}(vi)} satisfies \textup{(SR$_0$)} and,
for every nonzero real $h\in C_c(\R^3)$, \textup{(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$)}; and
\textup{(Z$_{\mathrm{sl}}$)} fails.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a) The covariance of $\varphi_k$ is $q_k^{|x_0-y_0|}$ times the spatial
kernel with Fourier transform $\varkappa(p)^4\prod_iP_{q_k}(p_i)\ge0$,
$P_q(t)=(1-q^2)/(1-2q\cos t+q^2)$, as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a). That proof, with $\gamma$ the
centred Gaussian law with this spatial covariance, gives reflection
positivity for the time reflections and a transfer matrix with spectrum
$\{q_k^N:N\ge0\}$ and $1$ simple; invariance under translations and
$\Gamma$ holds because $\Delta^2$ commutes with translations and $W_3$. The
slice field is linear, so $D^{h'}_k(r)=q_k^{r/a_k}D^{h'}_k(0)$, which gives
the ratio and
$I_k(\delta)=T_kD_k(0)\le\delta e^{m\delta}D_k(\delta)$ with $T_k=a_k
\sum_{j<n_\delta}q_k^j$; (M$_Z$) follows from
Proposition~\ref{prop:mzslice}(iii). The field $\varphi_k(y)$ is a linear
combination of $\psi_k$ at points $y+z$ with $|z|_1\le2$ in the slice of
$y$, with coefficients invariant under $z_i\mapsto-z_i$, so for $c$
supported in $\{x_i\ge2\}$ the sum of (SR$_2$) is
$\omega_k(\overline{F\circ\iota}\,F)$ for a linear function $F$ of
$\psi_k$ in $\{x_i\ge0\}$, nonnegative by the reflection positivity of
$\psi_k$ in the spatial directions.

In the notation of Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure},
$d\varpi_k=T_k\varkappa^4\prod_iP_{q_k}(p_i)\frac{dp}{(2\pi)^3}$. For
$c_x=a_k^3h(a_kx)$, Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(v) gives $\varkappa^2|\hat
c|\le\Theta_2a_k^4$ for $a_k\le1$. Since
$\int P_q\frac{dt}{2\pi}=1$,
\[
I_k(\delta)\le T_k\Theta_2^2a_k^8 .
\]
Let $Q_3$ have side $\ell$, $\chi$ as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(iii) for $Q'=Q_3$, and $h'_k(\mathbf y)=
\chi(\mathbf y)\cos(\pi(y_1+y_2+y_3)/a_k)$, real, supported in $Q_3$, with
$|h'_k|\le1$; this is the $h'$ of the lemma with $p_0=(\pi,\pi,\pi)$. On
$\mathcal B_{a_k/(3\ell)}(p_0)$, of half-side at most $\frac1{12}$, each
$\sin^2(p_i/2)\ge\cos^2\frac1{24}$, so $\varkappa\ge11$, and $P_{q_k}(p_i)\ge
(1-q_k)/(1+q_k)\ge ma_k/4$ for $ma_k\le1$. The lower bound of the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(iii), $|\hat c'|\ge\ell^3/128$ on that box,
gives
\[
\begin{split}
I^{h'_k}_k(\delta)&\ge T_k\,11^4\Bigl(\frac{\ell^3}{128}\Bigr)^2\Bigl(
\frac{ma_k}4\Bigr)^3\Bigl(\frac{2a_k}{3\ell}\Bigr)^3\frac1{(2\pi)^3}\\
&\ge10^{-5}\,T_k\,\ell^3m^3a_k^6 .
\end{split}
\]
So $I^{h'_k}_k(\delta)/I_k(\delta)\ge10^{-5}\ell^3m^3\Theta_2^{-2}a_k^{-2}
\to\infty$.

(b) The covariance is $\prod_\mu q_k^{|x_\mu-y_\mu|}+a_k^{-5}\mathbf
1_{x=y}$. For $c$ supported in $\{x_i\ge0\}$ the second term contributes
$a_k^{-5}\sum|c_x|^2$ over $x_i=0$ for $\iota^{\mathrm s}_i$ and $0$
for $\iota^{\mathrm b}_i$, and the first is reflection positive by
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a); this is (SR$_0$). The noise is
independent in time, so $I^{h'}_k(\delta)=I^{h'}_{\psi,k}(\delta)+a_k^2
\sum_x|h'(a_kx)|^2$, where the first term, for the field $\psi_k$, is at
most $T_k(\sum_xa_k^3|h'(a_kx)|)^2\le\delta(L+1)^6$ for $h'$ supported in a
cube of side $L$ with $|h'|\le1$ and $a_k\le1$, and the second is at most
$a_k^{-1}(L+1)^3$. For $h\ne0$, $I_k(\delta)\ge a_k^2\sum_xh(a_kx)^2\ge
(2a_k)^{-1}\int h^2$ for large $k$, so $I^{h'}_k(\delta)\le2(\delta+1)
(L+1)^6I_k(\delta)/\int h^2$, which is (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$). By
Proposition~\ref{prop:msplit}(vi), $\Upsilon_k\to\infty$, so (M$_Z$) and,
by Proposition~\ref{prop:mzslice}(iii), (Z$_{\mathrm{sl}}$) fail.
\end{proof}

So exact spatial reflection positivity gives (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) for every
spatial factor with $\int h\ne0$, while positivity beyond a fixed lattice
distance, as for local functions of a reflection positive field, does not;
and neither gives (Z$_{\mathrm{sl}}$). The flow enters through the size of
the lattice-scale part of $\varpi_k$. For a function $\Phi$ as in Sec.~\ref{ssec:osaxioms} put
$\Delta\Phi=\sum_{i=1}^3(\Phi_{e_i}+\Phi_{-e_i})-6\Phi$. It is again a
bounded real gauge-invariant $W_3$-invariant function of finitely many
spatial links of the slice, since $W_3$ permutes the six translates, and
since the flow commutes with translations, the field of $\Delta\Phi$ is
$\Delta\varphi_k$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:laplaceladder}
Let $\Phi$ be as above, and $m$, $h$, $\Theta_m$ as in
Lemma~\textup{\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(v)}. Let
$\varpi^{(m)}_k$ be the measure of Lemma~\textup{\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}}
for $\Delta^m\Phi$.

\textup{(i)} $\varpi^{(m)}_k=\varkappa^{2m}\varpi_k$, and for $a_k\le1$ the
integral $I_k(\delta)$ formed with $\Delta^m\Phi$ is at most $\Theta_m^2
a_k^{4m}K_k(0)\le\Theta_m^2\,\delta\,\|\Phi\|_\infty^2a_k^{4m}$.

\textup{(ii)} If \textup{(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$)} holds for $\Delta^m\Phi$ and
the spatial factor $h$, with the constant $C$ for a cube of side $\ell$,
then for all large $k$
\[
\varpi_k\bigl(\{p:|p|_\infty\ge2\}\bigr)\le1.4\cdot10^8\,\frac{C\Theta_m^2
\delta\|\Phi\|_\infty^2}{\ell^3}\,a_k^{4m-3} .
\]
This holds at every flow time $\tau$, with constants that do not depend on
$\tau$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Summation by parts gives $U^c_k$ for $\Delta^m\Phi$ equal to
$U^{\Delta^mc}_k$ for $\Phi$, and $\widehat{\Delta c}=-\varkappa\hat c$; so
$\int|\hat c|^2d\varpi^{(m)}_k=\int\varkappa^{2m}|\hat c|^2d\varpi_k$ for all
finitely supported $c$, which determines the measure. For $c_x=a_k^3
h(a_kx)$, Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(v) gives
$\int\varkappa^{2m}|\hat c|^2d\varpi_k\le
\Theta_m^2a_k^{4m}\varpi_k(\mathbf T^3)$, with $\varpi_k(\mathbf T^3)=
K_k(0)\le\delta\|\Phi\|_\infty^2$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(i).

(ii) Let $a_k\le\min(1,\ell/4)$ and $r=a_k/(3\ell)\le\frac1{12}$. Divide
$\mathbf T^3$ into $\lceil3\pi\ell/a_k\rceil^3\le(4\pi\ell/a_k)^3$
half-open boxes of side at most $2r$. A box meeting $\{|p|_\infty\ge2\}$
lies in $\mathcal B_r(p_0)$ for its centre $p_0$, which has a coordinate
$|p_{0,i}|\ge2-r\ge\frac\pi2$; on $\mathcal B_r(p_0)$ that coordinate lies
at distance at least $\frac\pi2-\frac1{12}$ from $0$ on the circle, and
$4\sin^2(\frac\pi4-\epsilon)=2(\cos\epsilon-\sin\epsilon)^2\ge2(1-\epsilon-
\frac{\epsilon^2}2)^2\ge1$ for $\epsilon=\frac1{24}$, so $\varkappa\ge1$
there. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(iii) with $Q'$ the cube of side $\ell$,
(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) for $\Delta^m\Phi$ and (i),
\[
\varpi_k(\mathcal B_r(p_0))\le\varpi^{(m)}_k(\mathcal B_r(p_0))\le
16384\,\ell^{-6}C\,\Theta_m^2\,\delta\,\|\Phi\|_\infty^2a_k^{4m} ,
\]
and summing over the boxes gives the bound, since $4\cdot16384\cdot
64\pi^3\le1.4\cdot10^8$; the factor $4$ covers the case in which only real
$h'$ are admitted, as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:boxform}(i). The constants involve $\Phi$ only through $\|\Phi\|_\infty$,
which the flow does not change.
\end{proof}

So a proof of (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) for all the fields $\Delta^m\Phi$, which
are admissible observables, must show that the time-integrated spectral
weight of $\varphi_k$ at lattice momenta tends to zero faster than every
power of $a_k$. Such suppression is what the linear heat flow does to a Gaussian field at
flow time $\tau/a_k^2$; for the gauge field the flow leaves the central
configurations unchanged, and with them their lattice-scale patterns
(Proposition~\ref{prop:centralpattern} and
Proposition~\ref{prop:centralsecond}(iv) below).

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:centralsecond}
Let $G=\SU(N)$, $\zeta=e^{2\pi i/N}$ and $c_N=N(1-\cos(2\pi/N))$. Let
$U^\circ$ be a configuration of the spatial links of a slice with values in
the centre $\{\zeta^j\}$, $X$ a finitely supported assignment of elements
of $\mathfrak{su}(N)$ to the spatial links, and $U^\epsilon_e=U^\circ_e
e^{\epsilon X_e}$. For a plaquette $p$ with links $e_1,e_2$ traversed
forwards and $e_3,e_4$ backwards put $Y_p=X_{e_1}+X_{e_2}-X_{e_3}-X_{e_4}$,
$|Y|^2=-\Tr Y^2$, and let $z_p\in\{\zeta^j\}$ be the plaquette of
$U^\circ$.

\textup{(i)} The finite sum $S(\epsilon)=\sum_p\bigl(\ReTr U^\circ_p-\ReTr
U^\epsilon_p\bigr)$ over the spatial plaquettes of the slice satisfies
$S'(0)=0$ and $S''(0)=\sum_p\operatorname{Re}(z_p)\,|Y_p|^2$.

\textup{(ii)} At $U^\circ=1$, $S''(0)=0$ if and only if there is a finitely
supported $\Lambda:\Z^3\to\mathfrak{su}(N)$ with $X_{(x,y)}=\Lambda(x)-
\Lambda(y)$ for every link, that is, $X$ is an infinitesimal gauge
transformation.

\textup{(iii)} Let $U^\zeta$ have $U^\zeta_{e_*}=\zeta$ on one spatial link
$e_*$ and all other links $1$, and let $S_0''$ be the form of \textup{(ii)}.
For $N\ge5$, $S''(0)\ge\cos(2\pi/N)S_0''(0)\ge0$, with equality to $0$ only
at infinitesimal gauge transformations; for $N=4$, $S''(0)\ge0$; for
$N=2,3$ and $X$ supported on $e_*$, $S''(0)=4\cos(2\pi/N)|X_{e_*}|^2<0$.

\textup{(iv)} For $\Phi$ the sum of $N-\ReTr U_p$ over the twelve spatial
plaquettes with a corner at $0$ and every $s\in\R$, $\Phi_x\circ\Phi_s
(U^\zeta)=c_Nn_x$, where $n_x=4$ at the two endpoints of $e_*$, $n_x=1$ at
the eight other corners of the four plaquettes containing $e_*$, and
$n_x=0$ elsewhere.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Central elements commute with everything, so $U^\epsilon_p=z_p
e^{\epsilon X_{e_1}}e^{\epsilon X_{e_2}}e^{-\epsilon X_{e_3}}e^{-\epsilon
X_{e_4}}=z_pe^{Z_p(\epsilon)}$ with $Z_p(\epsilon)\in\mathfrak{su}(N)$ and,
by the Baker--Campbell--Hausdorff formula, $Z_p(\epsilon)=\epsilon Y_p+
O(\epsilon^2)$. Since $\Tr Z_p=0$, $\Tr e^{Z_p}=N+\frac12\Tr Z_p^2+
O(\epsilon^3)=N-\frac{\epsilon^2}2|Y_p|^2+O(\epsilon^3)$, and only the
finitely many plaquettes meeting the support of $X$ contribute.

(ii) The vectors $\Lambda(x)-\Lambda(y)$ give $Y_p=0$ by telescoping.
Conversely, $S''(0)=\sum_p|Y_p|^2=0$ means that $X$ is a closed
$\mathfrak{su}(N)$-valued cochain on the cubical complex of $\Z^3$. Choose
a box $B$ containing the endpoints of the support of $X$ and a point
$x_\infty\notin B$, and let $\Lambda(x)$ be minus the sum of $\pm X_e$ along
a lattice path from $x_\infty$ to $x$, the sign recording the orientation.
Two paths with the same ends differ by a sum of boundaries of plaquettes, so
$\Lambda$ is well defined, and $X_{(x,y)}=\Lambda(x)-\Lambda(y)$. The
complement of $B$ in $\Z^3$ is connected and $X$ vanishes on its links, so
$\Lambda=0$ there.

(iii) The plaquettes of $U^\zeta$ are $1$ except the four containing
$e_*$, which are $\zeta^{\pm1}$, so $S''(0)=S_0''(0)-(1-\cos\frac{2\pi}N)
\sum_{p\ni e_*}|Y_p|^2$, and $\sum_{p\ni e_*}|Y_p|^2\le S_0''(0)$. For
$N\ge5$, $\cos\frac{2\pi}N>0$, which with (ii) gives the first claim, and
$\cos\frac\pi2=0$ gives the case $N=4$. For $X$ supported on $e_*$,
$Y_p=\pm X_{e_*}$ for the four plaquettes containing $e_*$ and $Y_p=0$
otherwise, and $\cos\pi=-1$, $\cos\frac{2\pi}3=-\frac12$.

(iv) By (i) the first variation of the action of the slice vanishes in
every direction, so the right side of the flow equation vanishes at
$U^\zeta$ and $\Phi_s(U^\zeta)=U^\zeta$ by uniqueness. Each of the four
plaquettes containing $e_*$ contributes $N-\ReTr\zeta^{\pm1}=c_N$ to
$\Phi_x$ for each of its four corners $x$; the two endpoints of $e_*$ are
corners of all four, and each of the other eight corners of exactly one.
\end{proof}

\emph{Spatial reflection positivity of a field and of its Laplacian.}
Theorem~\ref{thm:spatialrp} turns (SR$_0$) for one field into
(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) for that field. The admissible fields are closed under
$\Phi\mapsto\Delta\Phi$, and by Proposition~\ref{prop:laplaceladder} a proof
of (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) for all of them has to suppress the lattice-scale
spectral weight. This paragraph shows that (SR$_0$) cannot hold for $\Phi$
and $\Delta\Phi$ together in a state that clusters in space, that the linear
heat flow destroys (SR$_0$) at every positive flow time, and that (SR$_0$) for
one field forces spectral weight at the lattice scale.

Write $\mathbf T=\R/2\pi\Z$, identified with $(-\pi,\pi]$. A finite positive
measure $\nu$ on $\mathbf T$ is \emph{of Poisson type} if there is a finite
positive measure $\mu$ on $[0,1]$ with
\[
\hat\nu(n)=\int e^{int}d\nu(t)=\int\lambda^{|n|}d\mu(\lambda)\qquad
(n\in\Z),
\]
with $0^0=1$. The moments determine $\mu$, as in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:spectralmeasure}. Since $\int e^{int}P_\lambda(t)\frac{dt}
{2\pi}=\lambda^{|n|}$ for $P_\lambda(t)=(1-\lambda^2)/(1+\lambda^2-2\lambda
\cos t)$, $0\le\lambda<1$, uniqueness of Fourier coefficients gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:poissonform}
\nu=\mu(\{1\})\,\delta_0+f\,\frac{dt}{2\pi},\qquad f=\int_{[0,1)}
P_\lambda\,d\mu(\lambda) .
\end{equation}
For $i\in\{1,2,3\}$ write $p=(p_i,p_\perp)$ for $p\in\mathbf T^3$, with
$p_\perp$ in the torus $\mathbf T^2$ of the other two coordinates, and put
$\varkappa_\perp(p_\perp)=\sum_{j\ne i}4\sin^2(p_j/2)$, so that
$\varkappa(p)=4\sin^2(p_i/2)+\varkappa_\perp(p_\perp)$. For a finite positive
measure $\varpi$ on $\mathbf T^3$ and a continuous $g\ge0$ on $\mathbf T^2$
let $\varpi^i_g$ be the image of $g(p_\perp)\,d\varpi(p)$ under $p\mapsto
p_i$. Call $\varpi$ \emph{of Poisson type in direction $i$} if every
$\varpi^i_g$ is of Poisson type.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:poissontype}
\textup{(i)} Let $\nu_r$ be measures of Poisson type on $\mathbf T$ with
$\sup_r\nu_r(\mathbf T)<\infty$, and let $\nu$ be a finite positive measure
with $\hat\nu_r(n)\to\hat\nu(n)$ for every $n$. Then $\nu$ is of Poisson
type.

\textup{(ii)} If \textup{(SR$_0$)} holds for the slice field at the spacing
$a_k$, then $\varpi^V_k$ is of Poisson type in every direction
$i\in\{1,2,3\}$. The same holds for every real field on $\Z^4$ with
translation invariant, bounded covariance that satisfies the inequalities of
\textup{(SR$_0$)}, with $K^V_k$ formed from its covariance.

\textup{(iii)} If $\nu$ is of Poisson type, then for every integer $n\ge1$
\[
\begin{split}
\hat\nu(0)-\hat\nu(n)&\le n\bigl(\hat\nu(0)-\hat\nu(1)\bigr),\\
\nu\bigl(\{t:2\le|t|\le\pi\}\bigr)&\ge\frac{\pi-2}{2\pi}\bigl(\hat\nu(0)
-\hat\nu(1)\bigr) .
\end{split}
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i) Let $\mu_r$ be the measures of $\nu_r$. Their masses $\mu_r([0,1])=
\hat\nu_r(0)$ converge to $\hat\nu(0)$. If $\hat\nu(0)=0$, then $\nu=0$ and
$\mu=0$ will do. Otherwise Lemma~\ref{lem:helly}, applied to the
probability measures $\mu_r/\hat\nu_r(0)$ for large $r$, gives a subsequence
along which $\mu_r$ converges weakly to a finite positive measure $\mu$ of
mass $\hat\nu(0)$. Since $\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{|n|}$ is continuous on
$[0,1]$, $\hat\nu(n)=\lim\int\lambda^{|n|}d\mu_r=\int\lambda^{|n|}d\mu$.

(ii) For a finitely supported $b$ on the transverse lattice, the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:spatialrp}(i) shows that $k_b(n)=\int e^{inp_i}|\hat
b(p_\perp)|^2d\varpi^V_k(p)$ equals $\int\lambda^{|n|}d\mu_b$ for all
$n\in\Z$, so $\varpi^{V,i}_{k,|\hat b|^2}$ is of Poisson type. That proof uses
only translation invariance, the boundedness and positive definiteness of
$K^V_k$, and (SR$_0$). For a continuous $g\ge0$ on $\mathbf T^2$, $\sqrt g$
is a uniform limit of trigonometric polynomials $\hat b_r$, so $|\hat
b_r|^2\to g$ uniformly, the Fourier coefficients converge, the masses are
bounded, and (i) applies.

(iii) By the representation, $\hat\nu(0)-\hat\nu(n)=\int(1-\lambda^n)d\mu$
and $1-\lambda^n\le n(1-\lambda)$ on $[0,1]$. Further $P_\lambda(t)\ge
(1-\lambda^2)/(1+\lambda)^2=(1-\lambda)/(1+\lambda)\ge(1-\lambda)/2$ for
$0\le\lambda<1$, and the set $\{2\le|t|\le\pi\}$ has measure $2(\pi-2)$, so
by~\eqref{eq:poissonform} its $\nu$-measure is at least
$\frac{\pi-2}{2\pi}\int(1-\lambda)\,d\mu=\frac{\pi-2}{2\pi}(\hat\nu(0)-
\hat\nu(1))$; the atom at $\lambda=1$ contributes $0$ to both sides.
\end{proof}

The measures of $\Delta\Phi$ are those of $\Phi$ times $\varkappa^2$: the
summation by parts of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:laplaceladder}(i)
gives $V^c_k$ for $\Delta\Phi$ equal to $V^{\Delta c}_k$ for $\Phi$, so the
measure $\varpi^V_k$ of $\Delta\Phi$ is $\varkappa^2\varpi^V_k$, and that of
$K_k$ is $\varkappa^2\varpi_k$.

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:srladder}
Let $\Phi$ be as in Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:laplaceladder}}, and
assume \textup{(SR$_0$)} at the spacing $a_k$ for the slice fields of both
$\Phi$ and $\Delta\Phi$.

\textup{(i)} $\varpi^V_k=K^V_k(0)\,\delta_0$ and $\varpi_k=K_k(0)\,\delta_0$.
So $K^V_k$ and $K_k$ are constant on $\Z^3$, and the time average
$Y_k(\mathbf x)=a_k\sum_{n=0}^{n_\delta-1}\mathring\varphi_k(n,\mathbf x)$
satisfies $Y_k(\mathbf x)=Y_k(0)$ almost surely for every $\mathbf x$.

\textup{(ii)} The slice field of $\Delta\Phi$ equals its mean almost
surely at every point: $\Delta\mathring\varphi_k(x)=0$ for every
$x\in\Z^4$.

\textup{(iii)} If $\omega_k(\mathring\varphi_k(0,0)\,\mathring\varphi_k(j,
\mathbf z))\to0$ as $|\mathbf z|\to\infty$ for every integer $j\ge0$, then
$\mathring\varphi_k(x)=0$ almost surely for every $x$, and $C_k(0)=0$ for
every packet $f_0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) Fix $i$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:poissontype}(ii), applied to $\Phi$ with
$g=1,\varkappa_\perp,\varkappa_\perp^2$ and to $\Delta\Phi$ with $g=1$, the
images $\nu_0,\nu_1,\nu_2$ of $\varpi^V_k$, $\varkappa_\perp\varpi^V_k$,
$\varkappa_\perp^2\varpi^V_k$ and the image $\nu'$ of $\varkappa^2
\varpi^V_k$ under $p\mapsto p_i$ are of Poisson type, with measures
$\mu_0,\mu_1,\mu_2,\mu'$ on $[0,1]$. Put $k_l=\hat\nu_l$ and $F=\hat\nu'$.
With $4\sin^2(t/2)=2-e^{it}-e^{-it}$,
\[
\varkappa^2=(2-e^{it}-e^{-it})^2+2(2-e^{it}-e^{-it})\varkappa_\perp+
\varkappa_\perp^2 ,\qquad t=p_i,
\]
so $\nu'$ is $\nu_0$, $\nu_1$, $\nu_2$ multiplied by these trigonometric
polynomials in $t$ and added, and
\[
\begin{split}
F(n)&=6k_0(n)-4k_0(n-1)-4k_0(n+1)\\
&\quad+k_0(n-2)+k_0(n+2)+4k_1(n)\\
&\quad-2k_1(n-1)-2k_1(n+1)+k_2(n) .
\end{split}
\]
Insert $k_l(m)=\int\lambda^{|m|}d\mu_l$. For $n\ge2$ all the exponents
$|n-j|$, $|j|\le2$, equal $n-j$, and the integrands combine to
\[
\lambda^{n-2}(1-\lambda)^4,\qquad-2\lambda^{n-1}(1-\lambda)^2,\qquad
\lambda^n
\]
against $\mu_0$, $\mu_1$, $\mu_2$; these are polynomial identities, valid
at $\lambda=0$ with $0^0=1$. For $n=1$ the integrand against $\mu_0$ is
$6\lambda-4-4\lambda^2+\lambda+\lambda^3$, which for $\lambda>0$ equals
$\lambda^{-1}(1-\lambda)^4-\lambda^{-1}+\lambda$, and the integrand against
$\mu_1$ is $4\lambda-2-2\lambda^2=-2(1-\lambda)^2$.

For $n\ge3$ the three integrands vanish at $\lambda=0$, and $F(n)=\int
\lambda^nd\mu'$. So the finite signed measure
\[
\lambda^3\mu'-\lambda(1-\lambda)^4\mu_0+2\lambda^2(1-\lambda)^2\mu_1-
\lambda^3\mu_2
\]
on $[0,1]$ annihilates every polynomial, hence every continuous function,
and it is zero. Dividing by $\lambda$ and by $\lambda^2$ on $(0,1]$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:srladdermeas}
\begin{split}
\lambda^2\mu'&=(1-\lambda)^4\mu_0-2\lambda(1-\lambda)^2\mu_1+\lambda^2
\mu_2,\\
\lambda\mu'&=\lambda^{-1}(1-\lambda)^4\mu_0-2(1-\lambda)^2\mu_1+\lambda
\mu_2
\end{split}
\end{equation}
as measures on $(0,1]$. For $n=2$, $F(2)=\int\lambda^2d\mu'=\int_{(0,1]}
\lambda^2d\mu'$ and $F(2)=\int_{[0,1]}((1-\lambda)^4d\mu_0-2\lambda
(1-\lambda)^2d\mu_1+\lambda^2d\mu_2)$; integrating the first line
of~\eqref{eq:srladdermeas} over $(0,1]$ and subtracting leaves
$\mu_0(\{0\})=0$. Integrating the second line over $[\epsilon,1]$,
\[
\int_{[\epsilon,1]}\frac{(1-\lambda)^4}\lambda\,d\mu_0\le\nu'(\mathbf T)+
2\nu_1(\mathbf T)
\]
for every $\epsilon>0$, so $\int_{(0,1]}\lambda^{-1}(1-\lambda)^4d\mu_0
<\infty$, and since $(1-\lambda)^4\ge\frac1{16}$ on $(0,\frac12]$,
$\int_{(0,1]}\lambda^{-1}d\mu_0<\infty$. Now, with $\mu_0(\{0\})=0$,
\[
\begin{split}
F(1)&=\int_{(0,1]}\Bigl(\frac{(1-\lambda)^4}\lambda-\frac1\lambda+\lambda
\Bigr)d\mu_0\\
&\quad-2\int_{[0,1]}(1-\lambda)^2d\mu_1+\int\lambda\,d\mu_2,\\
F(1)&=\int\lambda\,d\mu'=\int_{(0,1]}\frac{(1-\lambda)^4}\lambda\,d\mu_0\\
&\quad-2\int_{(0,1]}(1-\lambda)^2d\mu_1+\int\lambda\,d\mu_2 ,
\end{split}
\]
the second by the second line of~\eqref{eq:srladdermeas}. Subtracting,
\[
\int_{(0,1]}\Bigl(\frac1\lambda-\lambda\Bigr)d\mu_0+2\mu_1(\{0\})=0 .
\]
Both terms are nonnegative and $1/\lambda-\lambda>0$ on $(0,1)$, so $\mu_0$
is carried by $\{1\}$, $k_0$ is constant, and $\nu_0$ is a point mass at
$0$. Thus $\varpi^V_k(\{p:p_i\ne0\})=0$ for $i=1,2,3$, and $\varpi^V_k=
\varpi^V_k(\mathbf T^3)\delta_0=K^V_k(0)\delta_0$. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(i), $\frac\delta2\int|\hat c|^2d\varpi_k\le
\int|\hat c|^2d\varpi^V_k\le2\delta\int|\hat c|^2d\varpi_k$ for every
finitely supported $c$. As in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:spatialrp}(i)
the functions $|\hat c|^2$ approximate every continuous $g\ge0$ on
$\mathbf T^3$ uniformly, so $\frac2\delta\varpi^V_k-\varpi_k$ and
$2\delta\varpi_k-\varpi^V_k$ are positive functionals on $C(\mathbf T^3)$,
hence positive measures:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:varpicompare}
\tfrac\delta2\varpi_k\le\varpi^V_k\le2\delta\varpi_k .
\end{equation}
So $\varpi_k(\{p\ne0\})=0$. Finally $Y_k$
is real with $\omega_k(Y_k(0)Y_k(\mathbf z))=K^V_k(\mathbf z)$, so
$\omega_k((Y_k(\mathbf x)-Y_k(0))^2)=2K^V_k(0)-2K^V_k(\mathbf x)=0$.

(ii) The measure of $K_k$ for $\Delta\Phi$ is $\varkappa^2\varpi_k=
K_k(0)\varkappa(0)^2\delta_0=0$. So, with $U^c_k$ formed from $\Delta\Phi$,
$0=I^c_k=a_k\sum_{j<n_\delta}\ip{u_c}{\mathbb T_k^ju_c}$ for every $c$,
and each term is nonnegative because $\mathbb T_k\ge0$; the term $j=0$ is
$a_k\,\omega_k(|U^c_k|^2)$. With $c=\delta_{\mathbf x}$ this gives
$\Delta\mathring\varphi_k(0,\mathbf x)=0$ almost surely, and time
translation invariance gives every time.

(iii) $K^V_k(\mathbf z)$ is a finite sum of the correlations
\[
a_k^2\,\omega_k\bigl(\mathring\varphi_k(n,0)\,\mathring\varphi_k(n',
\mathbf z)\bigr),\qquad0\le n,n'<n_\delta ,
\]
and by translation invariance each of them equals
\[
a_k^2\,\omega_k\bigl(\mathring\varphi_k(0,0)\,\mathring\varphi_k(j,
\pm\mathbf z)\bigr),\qquad j=|n'-n| .
\]
Under the hypothesis $K^V_k(\mathbf z)\to0$, and since it
is constant by (i), $K^V_k=0$, so $\varpi^V_k=0$ and $\varpi_k=0$. As in
(ii), now for $\Phi$, $\omega_k(|\mathring\varphi_k(0,\mathbf x)|^2)=0$ for
every $\mathbf x$, hence $\mathring\varphi_k(x)=0$ almost surely for every
$x\in\Z^4$. Every $A_k$ is then $0$ almost surely, and $C_k(0)=
\omega_k(\vartheta(\bar A_k)A_k)=0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:srladder}
Suppose that $D(n_k,\alpha)$ holds at $\beta_k$, as in
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:transfer}}, and that $C_k(0)>0$ for the packet
$f_0$ of $\Phi$. Then at every spacing $a_k$, \textup{(SR$_0$)} fails for
the slice field of $\Phi$ or for that of $\Delta\Phi$. So along such a
sequence no class of admissible fields closed under $\Phi\mapsto\Delta\Phi$
satisfies \textup{(SR$_0$)}, and Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:spatialrp}}
applies at the spacing $a_k$ to at most one of $\Phi$ and $\Delta\Phi$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:onescale}, part (v) of Proposition~\ref{prop:state}
holds at $\beta_k$: covariances of gauge-invariant local functions, one of
them Lipschitz in each link, decay exponentially in the distance. A
continuous gauge-invariant function of finitely many links is a uniform
limit of Lipschitz ones, which may be averaged over the gauge
transformations at the sites they involve, since these act by isometries.
By Proposition~\ref{prop:spatialflow}(ii), $\varphi_k(x)$ is a uniform limit
of continuous gauge-invariant local functions at the time of $x$, and
$|\Cov(F,F')-\Cov(G,G')|\le2\|F-G\|_\infty\|F'\|_\infty+2\|F'-G'\|_\infty
\|G\|_\infty$. So $\omega_k(\mathring\varphi_k(0,0)\mathring\varphi_k(j,
\mathbf z))\to0$ as $|\mathbf z|\to\infty$. If (SR$_0$) held for both
fields, Theorem~\ref{thm:srladder}(iii) would give $C_k(0)=0$.
\end{proof}

The linear heat flow, the model of the smoothing that the flow is meant to
provide, removes (SR$_0$) at every positive flow time. For $s>0$ let
$p_s(\mathbf z)$ be the probability that the continuous-time random walk on
$\Z^3$ with generator $\Delta$ is at $\mathbf z$ at time $s$, starting from
$0$, so that $\sum_{\mathbf z}p_s(\mathbf z)=1$ and $\sum_{\mathbf z}p_s(\mathbf z)e^{ip\cdot\mathbf z}=
e^{-s\varkappa(p)}$, and let $e^{s\Delta}$ act on the slices of a field on
$\Z^4$ by $(e^{s\Delta}\psi)(t,\mathbf x)=\sum_{\mathbf y}p_s(\mathbf x-
\mathbf y)\psi(t,\mathbf y)$, a series that converges in $L^2$ for fields
with bounded second moments.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:heatrp}
\textup{(i)} Let $\varpi$ be a finite positive measure on $\mathbf T^3$, $s>0$
and $i\in\{1,2,3\}$. If $\varpi$ and $e^{-2s\varkappa}\varpi$ are both of
Poisson type in direction $i$, then $\varpi(\{p:p_i\ne0\})=0$.

\textup{(ii)} Let $\psi_k$ be the field of
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a)} and $s>0$. Then
\textup{(SR$_0$)} holds for $\psi_k$ and fails for $e^{s\Delta}\psi_k$, at
every $k$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Let $g\ge0$ be continuous on $\mathbf T^2$ and $g'=ge^{-2s\varkappa_
\perp}$. Since $e^{-2s\varkappa}=e^{-4s(1-\cos p_i)}e^{-2s\varkappa_\perp}$,
the measure $\nu=\varpi^i_{g'}$ and the image $e^{-4s(1-\cos t)}\nu$ of
$g\,e^{-2s\varkappa}\varpi$ are of Poisson type, with measures $\mu$ and
$\mu^s$. Write $\nu$ as in~\eqref{eq:poissonform} and put
\[
F(c)=\int_{[0,1)}\frac{1-\lambda^2}{1+\lambda^2-2\lambda c}\,d\mu(\lambda)
\]
for $c$ in $\Omega=\C\setminus[1,\infty)$, and $F^s$ likewise with $\mu^s$,
so that $f(t)=F(\cos t)$ for $t\ne0$. The denominator vanishes only at
$c=(1+\lambda^2)/(2\lambda)\ge1$. On a compact $K\subset\Omega$ with
$|c|\le R$ and distance $d>0$ to $[1,\infty)$, it is at least $\frac12$ for
$\lambda\le1/(4R)$ and at least $2\lambda d\ge d/(2R)$ for larger
$\lambda$, so the integrand is bounded on $K$ uniformly in $\lambda$, and
$F$ is analytic on $\Omega$. For $\operatorname{Im}c>0$,
\[
\operatorname{Im}F(c)=\int_{[0,1)}\frac{2\lambda(1-\lambda^2)
\operatorname{Im}c}{|1+\lambda^2-2\lambda c|^2}\,d\mu(\lambda)\ge0 ,
\]
and the same holds for $F^s$. The absolutely continuous parts of
$e^{-4s(1-\cos t)}\nu$ in its two representations agree, so
$F^s(\cos t)=e^{-4s(1-\cos t)}F(\cos t)$ for almost every $t$, hence for
all $c\in(-1,1)$ by continuity, and on $\Omega$, which is connected, by
analyticity: $F^s(c)=e^{-4s(1-c)}F(c)$.

If $\mu((0,1))>0$, then $\operatorname{Im}F>0$ on the upper half plane,
and $\theta(y)=\arg F(iy)\in(0,\pi)$ is continuous in $y>0$. Since
$e^{-4s(1-iy)}=e^{-4s}e^{4isy}$,
\[
\operatorname{Im}F^s(iy)=e^{-4s}|F(iy)|\sin\bigl(4sy+\theta(y)\bigr)\ge0
\]
and $F^s(iy)\ne0$, so the continuous function
$4sy+\theta(y)$ takes its values in the union of the disjoint closed
intervals $[2\pi j,2\pi j+\pi]$, $j\in\Z$; on the connected set $(0,\infty)$
it stays in one of them, which is impossible since it exceeds $4sy$. So
$\mu((0,1))=0$ and $F$ is the constant $B=\mu(\{0\})\ge0$. Then
$\operatorname{Im}F^s(iy)=Be^{-4s}\sin(4sy)$, which is $-Be^{-4s}$ at
$y=3\pi/(8s)$, so $B=0$. Hence $\nu=\mu(\{1\})\delta_0$. For $g=1$ this
says $\int_{\{p_i\ne0\}}e^{-2s\varkappa_\perp}d\varpi=0$, and
$e^{-2s\varkappa_\perp}>0$.

(ii) The covariance of $\psi_k$ is $q_k^{|x_0-y_0|}\prod_{i=1}^3q_k^{|x_i-
y_i|}$, so its measure $\varpi^V_k$ is $T_k\prod_iP_{q_k}(p_i)\frac{dp}
{(2\pi)^3}$ with $T_k=a_k^2\sum_{n,n'<n_\delta}q_k^{|n-n'|}>0$, and that
of $e^{s\Delta}\psi_k$, whose spatial covariance kernel has Fourier
transform $e^{-2s\varkappa}\prod_iP_{q_k}(p_i)$, is $e^{-2s\varkappa}
\varpi^V_k$. Both covariances are bounded and translation invariant. The
law of $\psi_k$ is reflection positive for the spatial reflections through
sites and bisecting links (Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a)); for
$c$ supported in $\{x_i\ge0\}$ the sum of (SR$_0$) is
$\E[\overline{\psi_k(\iota c)}\,\psi_k(c)]\ge0$ with $\psi_k(c)=\sum c_x
\psi_k(x)$ and $\psi_k(\iota c)=\sum c_x\psi_k(\iota x)$, which is (SR$_0$).
If (SR$_0$) held for $e^{s\Delta}\psi_k$, Lemma~\ref{lem:poissontype}(ii)
for both fields and (i) would give $\varpi^V_k(\{p_i\ne0\})=0$, but
$\varpi^V_k$ has a positive density.
\end{proof}

The continuum analogue is the spatially flowed free field of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(c). It is reflection positive in
time, and the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:fourflow}(i), with $x_1$ in
the role of time, shows that it is not reflection positive for the
reflection of $x_1$: it uses only $H_0(0)>0$ and $H_0''(0)<0$, and here
$H_0''(0)=-(2\pi)^{-1}\int p_1^2e^{-2t_fp_1^2}(p_1^2+\mu^2)^{-1}dp_1$. For
one field, (SR$_0$) forces spectral weight at the lattice scale: by part
(i) below with $n=L/a_k$, $\varpi_k(\{|p|_\infty\ge2\})$ is at least
$a_k/(12\delta L)$ times $K^V_k(0)-K^V_k(ne_i)$, the decorrelation of the
time-averaged field over the physical distance $L$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:srrough}
Assume \textup{(SR$_0$)} at the spacing $a_k$ for the slice field of
$\Phi$, and let $i\in\{1,2,3\}$ and $n\ge1$.

\textup{(i)}
\[
\begin{split}
K^V_k(0)-K^V_k(ne_i)&\le n\bigl(K^V_k(0)-K^V_k(e_i)\bigr)\\
&\le\frac{2\pi n}{\pi-2}\,\varpi^V_k\bigl(\{|p_i|\ge2\}\bigr)\\
&\le12\,\delta\,n\,\varpi_k\bigl(\{|p|_\infty\ge2\}\bigr) .
\end{split}
\]

\textup{(ii)} For $h'$ supported in a cube of side $L'$ with $|h'|\le1$,
$a_k\le1$ and $h'_n(\mathbf y)=h'(\mathbf y-na_ke_i)$,
\[
I^{h'-h'_n}_k(\delta)\le\frac4\delta(L'+1)^6\bigl(K^V_k(0)-K^V_k(ne_i)
\bigr) .
\]

\textup{(iii)} If in addition \textup{(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$)} holds for the
slice field of $\Delta^m\Phi$, $m\ge1$, and the spatial factor $h$, with
the constant $C$ for a cube of side $\ell$, then for all large $k$
\[
K^V_k(0)-K^V_k(ne_i)\le1.7\cdot10^9\,\frac{C\Theta_m^2\delta^2\|\Phi\|_
\infty^2}{\ell^3}\,n\,a_k^{4m-3} .
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) By Lemma~\ref{lem:poissontype}(ii) with $g=1$, $\nu=\varpi^{V,i}_{k,1}$
is of Poisson type, and $\hat\nu(n)=K^V_k(ne_i)$. Lemma~\ref{lem:poissontype}
(iii) gives the first two inequalities. The third follows from~\eqref{eq:varpicompare}, which uses only
Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure}(i), and from $4\pi/(\pi-2)<12$, since
$\pi-2>1.14$ and $4\pi<12.57<13.68$.

(ii) With $c_{\mathbf x}=a_k^3h'(a_k\mathbf x)$ the coefficients of
$h'-h'_n$ are $c-c(\cdot-ne_i)$, with Fourier transform $\hat c\,(1-
e^{inp_i})$. So, by~\eqref{eq:varpicompare} and
$|1-e^{inp_i}|^2=2(1-\cos np_i)$,
\[
\begin{split}
I^{h'-h'_n}_k(\delta)&\le\|\hat c\|_\infty^2\,\frac2\delta\int2(1-\cos
np_i)\,d\varpi^V_k\\
&=\frac4\delta\|\hat c\|_\infty^2\bigl(K^V_k(0)-K^V_k(ne_i)\bigr),
\end{split}
\]
because $K^V_k$ is real and even. Finally $\|\hat c\|_\infty\le\sum_{\mathbf
x}a_k^3|h'(a_k\mathbf x)|\le a_k^3(L'/a_k+1)^3\le(L'+1)^3$.

(iii) Proposition~\ref{prop:laplaceladder}(ii) bounds
$\varpi_k(\{|p|_\infty\ge2\})$, and $12\cdot1.4\cdot10^8\le1.7\cdot10^9$.
\end{proof}

So (SR$_0$) for $\Phi$ together with (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) for
$\Delta^2\Phi$ gives, by (ii) and (iii) with $n=L/a_k$, $I^{h'-h'_n}_k(
\delta)\le C'La_k^4$ for spatial translates by a physical distance $L$.
Hence $I^{h'-h'_n}_k(\delta)/I_k(\delta)\to0$ for every bounded $L$
unless $\liminf_ka_k^{-4}I_k(\delta)<\infty$: the time-averaged slice field
becomes invariant, relative to $I_k(\delta)$, under spatial translations by
bounded physical distances. No lower bound on $I_k(\delta)$ along a tuning
is proved here.

\begin{remark}\label{rem:spatialstatus}
For the flowed fields of the Wilson measure along a tuning, the two
statements stand as follows.

(M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) is equivalent to the box bound of
Proposition~\ref{prop:boxform}(i) and, for $\int h\ne0$, follows from the
comparison of Proposition~\ref{prop:boxform}(iii). Exact spatial reflection
positivity (SR$_0$) gives that comparison
(Theorem~\ref{thm:spatialrp}). The Wilson action is reflection positive in
every coordinate hyperplane~\cite{OSei78}, so for states whose restrictions
to gauge-invariant functions are reflection positive for the spatial
reflections, a field $\Phi_x$ without flow, depending on the links within
distance $r$ of $x$, satisfies (SR$_r$); the flowed field
$\Phi_x\circ\Phi_{s_k}$ depends on all links of its slice, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:spatialflow}(i) bounds the influence of links at
distance $R$ only for $R$ beyond a multiple of $s_k=\tau/a_k^2$, so no
(SR$_r$) is known for it. Positivity beyond a fixed distance does not
suffice (Proposition~\ref{prop:rpexamples}(a)). Nor can (SR$_0$) hold for a
field and its Laplacian together: in a state that clusters in space, in
particular under the hypothesis of Theorem~\ref{thm:transfer}, it forces
$C_k(0)=0$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:srladder}, Corollary~\ref{cor:srladder}),
and the linear heat flow destroys it at every positive flow time
(Proposition~\ref{prop:heatrp}). So Theorem~\ref{thm:spatialrp} cannot
give (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) for a class of fields closed under $\Phi\mapsto
\Delta\Phi$, and for one field (SR$_0$) forces spectral weight at the
lattice scale (Proposition~\ref{prop:srrough}). Compactness of $G$ gives
$\bar\varpi_k\le\delta\|\Phi\|_\infty^2$, an upper bound of order one, and
no lower bound on $I_k(\delta)$; by
Proposition~\ref{prop:laplaceladder} a proof valid for the admissible
fields $\Delta^m\Phi$ must suppress the lattice-scale spectral weight of
$\varphi_k$ faster than every power of $a_k$. For $G=\SU(2)$ the estimates of
\cite[Sec.~XII\,E]{BBLR26} give each plaquette a conditional probability
at least $v_*e^{-3\beta_W}$ of being bad \cite[Proposition~386]{BBLR26},
a power of $a_k$ along couplings of order $\log(1/a_k)$, and the
single-link defects of Proposition~\ref{prop:centralsecond} are fixed by the
flow at every flow time, with nonnegative second variation for $N\ge4$ and a
direction of negative second variation for $N=2,3$. Whether the flow over
the time $\tau/a_k^2$ moves the configurations near such defects to
configurations with lattice-scale weight below every power of $a_k$ is not
known; this is the missing estimate.

(Z$_{\mathrm{sl}}$) is equivalent to (M$_Z$) and implies $(\mathrm N_u)$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:mzslice}); for $G=\SU(2)$ it fails along every
sequence with $\beta_{W,k}\le0.348$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:msplit}(v)) and
along the constant tunings (Proposition~\ref{prop:cubecompatible}(ii)). Compactness bounds $D_k(0)$ above and gives no
lower bound on $D_k(\delta)$, which is what (Z$_{\mathrm{sl}}$) requires;
exact spatial reflection positivity together with (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) does
not give it (Proposition~\ref{prop:rpexamples}(b)), and there the term
that breaks it is constant in space, so that no spatial smoothing removes
it. The
missing estimate is that of Remark~\ref{rem:opensplit}: a bound on the
high-energy spectral weight of the slice field by its weight at bounded
physical energy.

At a fixed $k$ both inequalities hold with a constant depending on $k$
whenever $C_k(0)>0$, since then $D_k(\delta)>0$ and $I_k(\delta)>0$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:sliceratio}(i); their content is the uniformity in
$k$, and neither is proved along any sequence of couplings with
$a_k\to0$.
\end{remark}

\emph{Status of (M), (W) and (U).} None of the three statements is proved.
Proposition~\ref{prop:msplit}(i)--(iii) uses translation and
$\vartheta$-invariance and reflection positivity of the states and the
reality of $\Phi$, part (iv) also the transfer matrix, and part (v)
Theorem~\ref{thm:strongratio}; none of them uses the hypothesis of
Theorem~\ref{thm:transfer} or $(\mathrm N_u)$. Proposition
\ref{prop:stationary} is deterministic except for the positivity of the
conditional densities in part (iii). Proposition~\ref{prop:wardform} uses
(M) and the invariance of the states under translations and $\Gamma$,
through Theorem~\ref{thm:osaxioms}(i), (ii), and part (iv) the tree bounds.
Proposition~\ref{prop:fourflow} concerns the free field and limits of
families. Proposition~\ref{prop:tuned}(i) assumes (RC), whose existence
along a tuning is not proved and which contains $(\mathrm N_0)$, and the
hypothesis of Theorem~\ref{thm:transfer} for the bound on $m_*$; parts
(ii) and (iii) assume (M). Lemma~\ref{lem:timekernel} and
Propositions~\ref{prop:mzslice} and~\ref{prop:mlocslice}(i)--(iii) use
translation and $\vartheta$-invariance, reflection positivity and the
positivity of the transfer matrix, and no hypothesis on the tuning; so do
Lemma~\ref{lem:spatialmeasure} and Propositions~\ref{prop:boxform}
and~\ref{prop:laplaceladder}, while Theorem~\ref{thm:spatialrp} assumes
the exact spatial reflection positivity (SR$_0$) of the slice field, which
is not known for the flowed fields and, under the hypothesis of
Theorem~\ref{thm:transfer}, fails for $\Phi$ or for $\Delta\Phi$
(Corollary~\ref{cor:srladder}). Proposition~\ref{prop:centralsecond} is
deterministic. What
remains open is (M$_{\mathrm{loc}}$), whose time direction for $n=2$ is
Proposition~\ref{prop:mlocslice}(i) and whose remainder is the spatial
comparison (M$_{\mathrm{sp}}$) and the bounds of
Proposition~\ref{prop:mlocslice}(iii) for even $n\ge4$; (M$_Z$), which for
product packets is the short-time bound (Z$_{\mathrm{sl}}$) on the slice
field and is strictly stronger than $(\mathrm N_0)$; the Ward identity
$\Delta^X_k\to0$ for one $X\in\mathfrak{so}(3)_{\mathrm{sp}}$; and, given
(RC), (U1) and (U2) (Remark~\ref{rem:opensplit}).


\subsection{Estimates uniform in the coupling}\label{ssec:uniform}

Some local quantities of the Wilson measure can be controlled uniformly in
$\beta_W$, the volume and the boundary condition, by integrating by parts in a
single link. Such estimates hold along every tuning, but they concern local
fields in lattice units, and the three results below show how far they are
from the open inputs: the force has bounded moments but its centred form is a
null vector of the reflected form, the plaquette has fluctuations of order
$\beta_W^{-2}$ but this is a single-site variance, and bare links over a fixed
physical distance become independent Haar variables.

Let $G=\SU(N)$, write $b=\beta_W$, so that the Wilson weight is $e^{bS}$ with
$S=\sum_pN^{-1}\ReTr Q_p$, and give $\g$ the metric $\ip TR=-\Tr(TR)$. For a link
$e$ let $S_e$ be the sum of the six plaquette terms containing $e$, $X_T$ the
derivative along $U_e\mapsto e^{uT}U_e$ at $u=0$, $\nabla_e$ and $\Delta_e$ the
gradient and Laplacian in $U_e$, $q=N^2-1$, $c_N=(N^2-1)/N$, and
\[
E_e=b|\nabla_eS_e|^2,\qquad W_e=E_e-c_NS_e=b|\nabla_eS_e|^2+\Delta_eS_e .
\]
Below, $\omega$ is a Wilson Gibbs state at $b>0$: a finite-volume measure in
which $e$ is not fixed by the boundary condition, or an infinite-volume state
whose conditional law of $U_e$ given the other links is proportional to
$e^{bS_e}dU_e$ with $dU_e$ the Haar measure.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:force}
\textup{(i)} For every unit $T\in\g$, $t\in\R$ and $0\le\eta<N/12$,
\[
\begin{split}
\E_\omega\bigl[e^{t\sqrt b\,X_TS_e}\bigm|U_{e^c}\bigr]&\le e^{3t^2/N},\\
\E_\omega\bigl[e^{\eta E_e}\bigm|U_{e^c}\bigr]&\le(1-12\eta/N)^{-q/2},
\end{split}
\]
so $\E_\omega E_e^m\le m!\,(24/N)^m2^{q/2}$ for $m\ge1$, uniformly in $b$,
the volume and the boundary condition. Moreover $\E_\omega E_e=c_N\E_\omega
S_e$.

\textup{(ii)} For a bounded function $F$ of finitely many links,
continuously differentiable in $U_e$,
$\E_\omega[W_eF]=-\E_\omega\ip{\nabla_eS_e}{\nabla_eF}$. In particular
$\E_\omega[W_eF]=0$ if $F$ does not depend on $U_e$, and
$\E_\omega[W_eW_f]=0$ if no plaquette contains both $e$ and $f$.

\textup{(iii)} Let $\omega$ be reflection positive as in
Appendix~\textup{\ref{app:transfer}}, and let $A=\sum_eh_eW_e$ be a finite
sum over links whose endpoints have times at least $2$. Then
$\omega(\vartheta(\bar A)A)=0$, so $\Psi_A=0$: in the reconstructed Hilbert
space the packets $\sum_eh_eE_e$ and $c_N\sum_eh_eS_e$ define the same vector.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Along $u\mapsto e^{uT}U_e$ a plaquette term containing $e$ is
$N^{-1}\ReTr(e^{uT}V)$ or $N^{-1}\ReTr(Ve^{-uT})$ with $V$ unitary, whose
second derivative is $N^{-1}\ReTr(T^2e^{\pm uT}V)$. Since $-T^2\ge0$ and
$\Tr(-T^2)=1$, $|\Tr(T^2M)|\le1$ for unitary $M$, so the second derivative of
$S_e$ along the curve is at most $6/N$ in absolute value. Taylor's formula with
$u=t/\sqrt b$ gives
$b\bigl(S_e(e^{tT/\sqrt b}U_e)-S_e(U_e)\bigr)\ge t\sqrt bX_TS_e(U_e)-3t^2/N$.
The conditional density is $e^{bS_e}/Z$ with respect to Haar measure, and the
left invariance of Haar measure gives
$\E_\omega[e^{b(S_e(e^{tT/\sqrt b}U_e)-S_e(U_e))}\mid U_{e^c}]=1$, which with
the Taylor bound proves the first inequality. For the second let $G_0$ be a
standard Gaussian vector in $\g$, independent of $\omega$; then
$e^{\eta|Y|^2}=\E e^{\sqrt{2\eta}\ip{G_0}Y}$ for $Y=\sqrt b\nabla_eS_e$, and the
first inequality in the direction of $G_0$ with $t=\sqrt{2\eta}|G_0|$ gives
the bound $\E e^{6\eta|G_0|^2/N}=(1-12\eta/N)^{-q/2}$. With $\eta=N/24$ and
$x^m\le m!\,\eta^{-m}e^{\eta x}$ the moment bound follows. For the mean,
$\sum_AT_A^2=-c_NI$ in an orthonormal basis gives $\Delta_eS_e=-c_NS_e$, and the
integral of the divergence of $e^{bS_e}\nabla_eS_e$ over $G$ vanishes.

(ii) The integral over $U_e$ of the divergence of $e^{bS_e}F\nabla_eS_e$
vanishes, and that divergence is $e^{bS_e}(W_eF+\ip{\nabla_eS_e}{\nabla_eF})$;
integrate the conditional identity. The function $W_f$ depends only on the
links of the plaquettes containing $f$.

(iii) Each $W_e$ in the sum depends on links with times at least $1$, so
$\vartheta(\bar A)$ depends only on links with times at most $0$, none of which
is a link $e$ of the sum. By (ii), $\omega(\vartheta(\bar A)W_e)=0$ for each $e$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:plaqvar}
Let $G=\SU(2)$, $b\ge0$, $\omega$ a Wilson Gibbs state at $b$ and $p$ a
plaquette with a link that is not fixed by the boundary condition. Then
\[
\Var_\omega\bigl(\tfrac12\Tr Q_p\bigr)\ge\frac1{2^{19}(1+6b)^2},\quad
\E_\omega\bigl[1-\tfrac12\Tr Q_p\bigr]\ge\frac1{4b+1}.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $e$ be the link and identify $\SU(2)$ with the unit quaternions. Given the
other links, $U_e=x$ has density proportional to $e^{rx\cdot u}$ with $|u|=1$
and $0\le r\le6b$, since the six staples have norm one, and $\frac12\Tr Q_p=x\cdot
v$ for a unit vector $v$ fixed by the conditioning. Let $X=x\cdot u$ and
$T=1-X$; the law of $T$ has density proportional to $e^{-rt}\sqrt{t(2-t)}$ on
$[0,2]$. Put $n=1+r$. Since $e^{-rt}\le e^2e^{-nt}$, the normalisation is at
most $e^2\int_0^\infty\sqrt{2t}e^{-nt}dt=e^2\sqrt{\pi/2}\,n^{-3/2}<14n^{-3/2}$.
On $I=[\frac1{4n},\frac1{2n}]$ the density is at least
$\frac12n^{-1/2}e^{-1/2}$ and on $J=[\frac1n,\frac3{2n}]$ at least
$\frac12n^{-1/2}e^{-3/2}$, so $P(T\in I)\ge\frac1{224}$ and
$P(T\in J)\ge\frac1{336}$. With an independent copy $T'$,
$\Var T=\frac12\E(T-T')^2\ge P(T\in I)P(T\in J)(2n)^{-2}>2^{-19}n^{-2}$. By
symmetry the conditional covariance of $x$ is diagonal in $u$ and
$u^\perp$, with eigenvalues $\Var X$ and $\E(1-X^2)/3$; on $I$,
$1-X^2=T(2-T)\ge\frac3{8n}$, so $\E(1-X^2)/3\ge\frac1{1792n}\ge2^{-19}n^{-2}$.
Hence the conditional variance of $x\cdot v$ is at least $2^{-19}(1+6b)^{-2}$,
and so is the variance, by the law of total variance. For the mean, integrating
$\frac d{dX}\bigl(e^{rX}(1-X^2)^{3/2}\bigr)$ over $[-1,1]$ gives
$3\E X=r\E(1-X^2)\le r(1-(\E X)^2)$, whence
$\E X\le(\sqrt{9+4r^2}-3)/(2r)\le2r/(2r+3)$ for $r>0$, and
$\E[1-x\cdot v]\ge1-\E X\ge3/(2r+3)\ge1/(4b+1)$.
\end{proof}

For the blocking statement let $G=\SU(2)$, $B\ge4$ an integer and, for a
coarse edge $(x,\mu)$, $V_{(x,\mu)}=\prod_{r=0}^{B-1}U_{(Bx+r\hat\mu,\mu)}$ the
ordered product of the fine links along it.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:blocking}
For every Wilson Gibbs state $\omega$ of $\SU(2)$ at $b>0$ and every set $\mathcal
E$ of $K$ coarse edges,
\[
\begin{split}
\bigl\|\mathrm{Law}_\omega\bigl((V_e)_{e\in\mathcal E}\bigr)-m^{\otimes K}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
&\le\frac K2e^{3b}\Bigl(\frac{4b}{4b+1}\Bigr)^{B-3}\\
&\le\frac K2\exp\Bigl(3b-\frac{B-3}{4b+1}\Bigr),
\end{split}
\]
where $m$ is the Haar measure. If $F$ is a function of the $V_e$ of a set of
coarse edges at positive times, $|F|\le1$, and $\epsilon$ is this bound for
those edges and their reflections, then
$0\le\omega(\vartheta(\bar F^c)F^c)\le6\epsilon$ with $F^c=F-\omega(F)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Select on each coarse edge the fine links with $1\le r\le B-2$. No plaquette
contains two selected links: collinear links do not share a plaquette,
parallel coarse edges are $B$ apart, and a plaquette containing a selected link
of $(x,\mu)$ has its links in direction $\nu\ne\mu$ at $\mu$-offsets $r,r+1\in
[1,B-1]$ from $Bx$, while coarse edges in direction $\nu$ lie at offsets in
$B\Z$. Given the other links, the selected ones are therefore independent, each
with density $e^{r_i\,x\cdot u_i}/Z(r_i)$, $0\le r_i\le6b$. Convolution with
such a density acts on the representation of spin $l\ge1$ by $D^{(l)}(u_i)$
times $I_{l+1}(r_i)/I_1(r_i)$, whose absolute value is at most
$I_2(r_i)/I_1(r_i)$ because $I_\nu(r)$ decreases in $\nu\ge0$, and
$I_2(r_i)/I_1(r_i)=\E X\le4b/(4b+1)$ by the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:plaqvar}, so it contracts the mean-zero part of $L^2$ by
that factor. The density is at most $e^{6b}$ because $Z\ge1$ by Jensen's
inequality, so its distance to $1$ in $L^2$ is at most $e^{3b}$. The
conditional law of $V_e$ is that of a product of $B-2$ such independent
variables between two fixed ones, so its total variation distance to $m$ is at
most $\frac12e^{3b}(4b/(4b+1))^{B-3}$, and the coarse edges are conditionally
independent. Total variation distance from a product is at most the sum over
the factors, and averaging over the conditioning does not increase it.
For the last statement, under $m^{\otimes K}$ the covariance vanishes because
$F$ and its reflection depend on disjoint edges; replacing $\omega$ by
$m^{\otimes K}$ changes $\omega(\vartheta(\bar F)F)$ by at most $2\epsilon$ and
$|\omega(F)|^2$ by at most $4\epsilon$.
