$g_\delta$ be continuous with values in $[0,1]$, equal to $1$ on
$[\frac12+\delta,\frac12+2\delta]\times[\frac18,\frac78]^3$ and supported in
$[\frac12+\frac\delta2,1]\times[0,1]^3$. The supports are at distance at
least $\delta$. For $x$ in the first box and $y$ in the second with
$|y_i-x_i|\le\delta$ for $i=1,2,3$, $2\delta\le|x-y|\le\sqrt{19}\,\delta<
5\delta\le r_0$, so $K(x-y)\ge c(5\delta)^{-8}(\log\frac1{2\delta})^{-p}$;
these pairs have measure $\frac\delta8\cdot8\delta^4=\delta^5$. Hence
$S_2(f_\delta,g_\delta)\ge c\,5^{-8}\delta^{-3}(\log\frac1{2\delta})^{-p}$,
which is unbounded as $\delta\to0$, while $\|f_\delta\|_\infty\|g_\delta\|
_\infty=1$.
\end{proof}

The law of the proposition, with $p=2$, is the one that asymptotic freedom
predicts for the action density. It places no restriction on
(M$_{\mathrm s}$) when $K$ is bounded on $\{|z|_\infty\ge\delta\}$ for every
$\delta>0$.

\emph{The two-point functions.} For $n=2$ the separated moments reduce to
one quantity of the lattice theory. For $\varepsilon>0$ let $B_\varepsilon=
[\varepsilon,\varepsilon+1+a_0]\times[0,1+a_0]^3$ and, for $(r+1)a_k\le
\varepsilon$,
\[
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)=\sup\bigl\{\omega_k\bigl(\vartheta(\overline{
A_k(h)})A_k(h)\bigr):\operatorname{supp}h\subset B_\varepsilon,\ \|h\|_\infty
\le1\bigr\},
\]
which is finite because $|A_k(h)|$ is at most $2\|\Phi\|_\infty
a_k^4\|h\|_\infty$ times the number of sites $x$ with $a_kx\in
B_\varepsilon$. Let $\gamma_k\le e^{-m_*a_k}$ be the norm of
$\mathbb T_k$ on $\Omega_k^\perp$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:limitEbox}
Let $(r+1)a_k\le\varepsilon$ and let $g\in C_c(\R^4)$ be supported in a cube
of side $L$ and in $\{x_0\ge\varepsilon\}$. Then
\[
\omega_k\bigl(\vartheta(\overline{A_k(g)})A_k(g)\bigr)\le\mathsf K_L^2\,
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\,\|g\|_\infty^2 .
\]
The same holds with $\vartheta_\mu$ for $g$ supported in
$\{x_\mu\ge\varepsilon\}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write $g=\sum_mh_m$ with $h_m=\chi^1_mg$, $\chi^1_m$ as in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(vi); $\chi^1_m$ is supported in the closed cube
of side $1$ centred at $m/2$, and $\chi^1_mg\ne0$ requires
$m_\nu\in(2q_\nu-1,2q_\nu+2L+1)$ for the cube $\prod_\nu[q_\nu,q_\nu+L]$,
so at most $\mathsf K_L$ pieces are nonzero, each with $\|h_m\|_\infty\le\|g
\|_\infty$. Fix one, supported in $[s,s+1]\times\prod_i[u_i,u_i+1]$ and in
$\{x_0\ge s'\}$, $s'=\max(s,\varepsilon)$. Let $n_0=\lfloor(s'-\varepsilon)
/a_k\rfloor\ge0$, $v_i=\lfloor u_i/a_k\rfloor$, $v=(n_0,v_1,v_2,v_3)$ and
$h'=h_m(\cdot+a_kv)$. Then $h'$ is supported in $[s'-n_0a_k,s+1-n_0a_k]\times
\prod_i[u_i-v_ia_k,u_i-v_ia_k+1]\subset B_\varepsilon$, since $s'-n_0a_k\in
[\varepsilon,\varepsilon+a_k)$ and $u_i-v_ia_k\in[0,a_k)$. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i), $A_k(h_m)=\tau_{n_0e_0}\tau_{v_\perp}
A_k(h')$ with $v_\perp=(0,v_1,v_2,v_3)$, and by
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(ii),(iii) and its proof,
$\|\Psi_{A_k(h_m)}\|=\|\mathbb T_k^{n_0}\Psi_{\tau_{v_\perp}A_k(h')}\|\le
\|\Psi_{A_k(h')}\|\le\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{1/2}\|g\|_\infty$. The
triangle inequality for the linear map $F\mapsto\Psi_F$ gives
$\|\Psi_{A_k(g)}\|\le\mathsf K_L\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{1/2}\|g\|_\infty$.
For $\vartheta_\mu$ apply this to $g\circ\pi_\mu$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:limitEtwo}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}}, let
$\varepsilon>0$, suppose $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)>0$ for large $k$, and put
$Z_k=\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$.

\textup{(i)} Let $c\in\R$, $d\ge0$, $f$ supported in a cube of side $L$ in
$\{x_\mu\le c-2\varepsilon\}$ and $g$ in a cube of side $L'$ in
$\{x_\mu\ge c+2\varepsilon+d\}$. For $a_k\le\varepsilon$ and $(r+1)a_k\le
\varepsilon$,
\[
Z_k^2\bigl|\omega_k\bigl(A_k(f)A_k(g)\bigr)\bigr|\le\mathsf K_L\mathsf
K_{L'}\,\gamma_k^{\lfloor d/a_k\rfloor}\,\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty .
\]

\textup{(ii)} For $(f,g)\in\mathcal C_2(Q,\delta)$ with $Q$ of side $L$ and
$\delta\ge8\varepsilon$, and $k$ as in \textup{(i)},
\[
Z_k^2\bigl|\omega_k\bigl(A_k(f)A_k(g)\bigr)\bigr|\le\mathsf K_{4L/\delta}^2
\mathsf K_{\delta/4}^2\,\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty .
\]

\textup{(iii)} Every sequence of $k$ has a subsequence along which
$S_2(f,g)=\lim_kZ_k^2\omega_k(A_k(f)A_k(g))$ exists for all $f,g$ with
$\dist_\infty(\operatorname{supp}f,\operatorname{supp}g)>8\varepsilon$. It
is symmetric, bilinear, bounded as in \textup{(ii)}, invariant under the
translations and $B_4$, $S_2(\bar f,\bar g)=\overline{S_2(f,g)}$, and
$\sum_{i,j}\bar c_ic_jS_2(\bar g_i\circ\theta_\mu,g_j)\ge0$ for all $c_i\in
\C$ and $g_i$ supported in $\{x_\mu>4\varepsilon\}$.

\textup{(iv)} For $f,g$ supported in a cube of side $L$, $y\ne0$ and
$\lambda|y|_\infty>L+8\varepsilon$,
\[
\bigl|S_2(f,g(\cdot-\lambda y))\bigr|\le\mathsf K_L^2\|f\|_\infty
\|g\|_\infty\,e^{-m_*(\lambda|y|_\infty-L-4\varepsilon)} .
\]

\textup{(v)} If $\gamma_k\le e^{-m_{\mathrm{lat}}}$ for a fixed $m_{\mathrm{lat}}>0$ and all $k$, every
$S_2$ of \textup{(iii)} vanishes.

Parts \textup{(i)--(iii)} use only the invariance of $\omega_k$ under
translations and $B_4$ and its reflection positivity.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) By the invariance of $\omega_k$ under $\pi_\mu$ and
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i) we may take $\mu=0$. Let $b_k\in a_k\Z$
with $c\le b_k<c+a_k$; by the invariance under the translation by
$-b_ke_0/a_k$ we may take $b_k=0$, so that $f$ is supported in $\{x_0\le
-2\varepsilon\}$ and $g$ in $\{x_0\ge2\varepsilon+d-a_k\}$. Let $h=\bar f
\circ\theta_{0,k}$, supported in $\{x_0\ge2\varepsilon-a_k\}\subset\{x_0\ge
\varepsilon\}$; then $A_k(f)=\vartheta(\overline{A_k(h)})$. Let $n=\lfloor
d/a_k\rfloor$ and $g'=g(\cdot+na_ke_0)$, supported in $\{x_0\ge2\varepsilon
-a_k\}$ because $na_k\le d$. Then $A_k(g)=\tau_nA_k(g')$, and
\[
\omega_k\bigl(A_k(f)A_k(g)\bigr)=\ip{\Psi_{A_k(h)}}{\mathbb T_k^n
\Psi_{A_k(g')}} .
\]
Both vectors are orthogonal to $\Omega_k$, the packets being centred, so the
modulus is at most $\gamma_k^n\|\Psi_{A_k(h)}\|\|\Psi_{A_k(g')}\|$, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox} bounds the norms by $\mathsf K_L\mathcal N_k(
\varepsilon)^{1/2}\|f\|_\infty$ and $\mathsf K_{L'}\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)
^{1/2}\|g\|_\infty$.

(ii) Let $s=\delta/4$ and write $f=\sum_m\chi^s_mf$, $g=\sum_{m'}\chi^s_{m'}
g$; at most $\mathsf K_{L/s}=\mathsf K_{4L/\delta}$ terms of each sum are
nonzero. For a pair of nonzero pieces pick $p,q$ in their supports, and
$\mu$ with $|p_\mu-q_\mu|\ge\delta$. The projections of the two cubes of
side $s$ on the $\mu$ axis contain $p_\mu$ and $q_\mu$, so they are at
distance at least $\delta-2s=\delta/2\ge4\varepsilon$, and (i) applies to
the pair, possibly with $f$ and $g$ exchanged, with $L=L'=\delta/4$ and
$\gamma_k^{\lfloor d/a_k\rfloor}\le1$. Summing over the pairs gives the bound.

(iii) The diagonal argument of the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(i), with (ii) in place of
(M$_{\mathrm s}$) and approximants within $\eta<(\delta-8\varepsilon)/4$,
gives the limit for pairs at distance $\delta>8\varepsilon$. Invariance and
the conjugation identity follow as in Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(ii). For
$g=\sum_ic_ig_i$ supported in $\{x_\mu\ge4\varepsilon+\eta'\}$, $\eta'>0$,
$Z_k^2\omega_k(\vartheta_\mu(\overline{A_k(g)})A_k(g))\ge0$ by
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}, and it equals $Z_k^2\omega_k(A_k(\bar g\circ
\theta_{\mu,k})A_k(g))$; the pair $(\bar g\circ\theta_\mu,g)$ is at distance
at least $8\varepsilon+2\eta'$, and (ii) carries the approximation of
$\theta_{\mu,k}$ by $\theta_\mu$ as in Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(iii).

(iv) By $B_4$-invariance we may take $y_\mu=|y|_\infty=\eta>0$, and by
translation invariance the cube to be $\prod_\nu[q_\nu,q_\nu+L]$. Then $f$
is supported in $\{x_\mu\le c-2\varepsilon\}$ with $c=q_\mu+L+2\varepsilon$,
and $g(\cdot-\lambda y)$ in $\{x_\mu\ge c+2\varepsilon+d\}$ with $d=\lambda
\eta-L-4\varepsilon>0$. By (i) and $\gamma_k^{\lfloor d/a_k\rfloor}\le
e^{-m_*(d-a_k)}$ the lattice quantities obey the bound with
$e^{-m_*(d-a_k)}$, and the limit gives the claim.

(v) In the proof of (ii), now with $\delta>8\varepsilon$, each pair of
pieces satisfies (i) with $d=\delta/2-4\varepsilon>0$, so the lattice
quantity is at most $\mathsf K_{4L/\delta}^2\mathsf K_{\delta/4}^2\|f\|
_\infty\|g\|_\infty e^{-m_{\mathrm{lat}}(d/a_k-1)}$, which tends to $0$.
\end{proof}

Two statements about the lattice two-point function control the small
separations:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(SD)},leftmargin=*]
\item $\limsup_k\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')/\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)<\infty$
for every $\varepsilon'\in(0,\varepsilon)$;
\end{enumerate}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(NE)},leftmargin=*]
\item there is $g_0\in C_c(\R^4)$ supported in $\{x_0>0\}$ with
\[
\liminf_k\frac{\omega_k\bigl(\vartheta(\overline{A_k(g_0)})A_k(g_0)\bigr)}
{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)}>0 .
\]
\end{enumerate}
(SD) bounds the growth of the largest reflected norm of a packet of unit
size as it approaches the reflection plane from the distance $\varepsilon$
to any smaller distance, uniformly in the spacing; (NE) says that a fixed
packet carries a fixed fraction of that largest norm.

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:limitEtwo}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEtwo}}.

\textup{(a)} \textup{(SD)} holds if and only if \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)}
holds for $n=2$.

\textup{(b)} Under \textup{(SD)}, every sequence of $k$ has a subsequence
along which $S_2(f,g)$ exists for every separated pair. It is given by a
distribution on $\mathfrak D_2$, invariant under the translations and $B_4$,
satisfies $S_2(\bar f,\bar g)=\overline{S_2(f,g)}$ and
$S_2(\bar g\circ\theta_\mu,g)\ge0$ for $g$ supported in $\{x_\mu>0\}$, and
for $f,g$ supported in a cube of side $L$, $y\ne0$, $\varepsilon'\in(0,
\varepsilon]$ and $\lambda|y|_\infty>L+4\varepsilon'$,
\[
\bigl|S_2(f,g(\cdot-\lambda y))\bigr|\le C_{\varepsilon'}\mathsf K_L^2
\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty\,e^{-m_*(\lambda|y|_\infty-L-4\varepsilon')}
\]
with $C_{\varepsilon'}=\limsup_k\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')/\mathcal N_k(
\varepsilon)$. The time translations define on the completion
$\mathcal H^{(1)}$ of the functions supported in $\{x_0>0\}$ for the form
$S_2(\bar g\circ\theta_0,g')$ a semigroup $e^{-tH^{(1)}}$ with
$\sigma(H^{(1)})\subset[m_*,\infty)$. Some limit $S_2$ is not identically
zero if and only if $\limsup_k\omega_k(\vartheta(\overline{A_k(g)})A_k(g))
/\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)>0$ for some $g$ supported in $\{x_0>0\}$; under
\textup{(NE)} every limit is not identically zero, and then
$\sigma(H^{(1)})$ is a nonempty subset of $[m_*,\infty)$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
(a) If (M$_{\mathrm s}$) holds for $n=2$, let $h$ be supported in
$B_{\varepsilon'}$ with $\|h\|_\infty\le1$. For $a_k\le\varepsilon'$ the pair
$(\bar h\circ\theta_{0,k},h)$ lies in $\mathcal C_2(Q,\varepsilon')$ for a
cube $Q$ independent of $k$ and $h$, and $\omega_k(\vartheta(\overline{
A_k(h)})A_k(h))=\omega_k(A_k(\bar h\circ\theta_{0,k})A_k(h))$, so
$\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\le M_2(Q,\varepsilon')\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$
for large $k$. Conversely, let $(f,g)\in\mathcal C_2(Q,\delta)$ and
$\varepsilon'=\min(\varepsilon,\delta/8)$. The proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtwo}(i), (ii) with $\varepsilon'$ in place of
$\varepsilon$ gives $|\omega_k(A_k(f)A_k(g))|\le\mathsf K_{4L/\delta}^2
\mathsf K_{\delta/4}^2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty$
for large $k$, and dividing by $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$ and using (SD)
gives (M$_{\mathrm s}$) for $n=2$.

(b) By (a), the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(i) for $n=2$ gives the
limits and the distribution, and that of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(ii),
(iii) the invariance, the conjugation identity and the positivity. The
decay follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtwo}(i) with $\varepsilon'$ in
place of $\varepsilon$, multiplied by $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')/\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)$, as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtwo}(iv).
The semigroup comes from the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(iv)
restricted to single functions. Since $\ip{\Omega_k}{\Psi_{A_k(g)}}=0$,
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(iii) gives $\omega_k(\vartheta(\overline{
A_k(g)})\tau_{t/a_k}A_k(g))\le e^{-m_*t}\omega_k(\vartheta(\overline{
A_k(g)})A_k(g))$, hence $\ip w{e^{-tH^{(1)}}w}\le e^{-m_*t}\|w\|^2$ on a dense
set of $w$, and $\|e^{-tH^{(1)}}\|\le e^{-m_*t}$. If the $\limsup$ is
positive for $g$, a subsequence along which the ratio converges to it has a
further subsequence with $S_2(\bar g\circ\theta_0,g)$ equal to that limit,
by the approximation of $\theta_{0,k}$ by $\theta_0$. Conversely, if
$S_2(\bar g\circ\theta_\mu,g)=0$ for all $\mu$ and all $g$ supported in
$\{x_\mu>0\}$, the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality for these positive forms and
translation invariance give $S_2(f,g)=0$ whenever $f$ and $g$ lie on
opposite sides of a coordinate hyperplane, and the decomposition in the
proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtwo}(ii) splits every separated pair into
such pairs; by $B_4$-invariance $S_2(\bar g\circ\theta_\mu,g)=S_2(\overline{
g\circ\pi_\mu}\circ\theta_0,g\circ\pi_\mu)$, so a nonzero $S_2$ has
$S_2(\bar g\circ\theta_0,g)>0$ for some $g$, and the ratio for $g$ converges
to it along the subsequence. Under (NE), $S_2(\bar g_0\circ\theta_0,g_0)$ is
at least the $\liminf$ in (NE), so $\mathcal H^{(1)}\ne0$.
\end{proof}

\emph{The short-distance comparison.} The comparison (SD) is a statement
about the spectrum of the transfer matrices. For $\Lambda>0$ let
$\mathbb E_k(\Lambda)$ be the spectral projection of $\mathbb T_k$ on
$[e^{-\Lambda a_k},1]$, the states of energy at most $\Lambda$ in physical
units, put $\mathsf K=\mathsf K_{1+a_0}=(5+2a_0)^4$ and, for $(r+1)a_k\le
\varepsilon$,
\[
\begin{split}
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon;\Lambda)=\sup\bigl\{&\ip{\Psi_{A_k(h)}}{\mathbb
E_k(\Lambda)\Psi_{A_k(h)}}:\\
&\operatorname{supp}h\subset B_\varepsilon,\ \|h\|_\infty\le1\bigr\}.
\end{split}
\]
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(UV)},leftmargin=*]
\item For every $\varepsilon'\in(0,\varepsilon)$ there are $\Lambda<\infty$
and $c>0$ with $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon';\Lambda)\ge c\,\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon')$ for all large $k$.
\end{enumerate}
(UV) says that the largest reflected norm of a unit packet near the
reflection plane is carried, up to a fixed fraction, by energies bounded in
physical units.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEsd}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEtwo}}.

\textup{(i)} For $(r+1)a_k\le\varepsilon'<\varepsilon$,
\[
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\le\mathsf K^2\gamma_k^{2\lfloor(\varepsilon-
\varepsilon')/a_k\rfloor}\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\le\mathsf K^2
e^{-2m_*(\varepsilon-\varepsilon'-a_k)}\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon') .
\]

\textup{(ii)} \textup{(SD)} holds if and only if \textup{(UV)} holds.

\textup{(iii)} If \textup{(SD)} holds, then for every $\varepsilon'\in(0,
\varepsilon)$
\[
\limsup_k\frac1{a_k}\log\frac1{\gamma_k}\le\frac{\log(\mathsf K^2
C_{\varepsilon'})}{2(\varepsilon-\varepsilon')},\qquad C_{\varepsilon'}=
\limsup_k\frac{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')}{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)} .
\]
In particular, if $\gamma_k\le e^{-m_{\mathrm{lat}}}$ for a fixed
$m_{\mathrm{lat}}>0$ and all $k$, then $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')/\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)\to\infty$ for every $\varepsilon'\in(0,\varepsilon)$ and
\textup{(SD)} fails; this is the case under the hypotheses of
Corollary~\textup{\ref{cor:limitEstrong}} whenever $\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)>0$ for large $k$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Let $h$ be supported in $B_\varepsilon$ with $\|h\|_\infty\le1$, put
$n=\lfloor(\varepsilon-\varepsilon')/a_k\rfloor$ and $h'=h(\cdot+na_ke_0)$,
supported in a cube of side $1+a_0$ in $\{x_0\ge\varepsilon'\}$. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i) and the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(iii), $\Psi_{A_k(h)}=\mathbb T_k^n
\Psi_{A_k(h')}$, and $\Psi_{A_k(h')}\perp\Omega_k$ because the packet is
centred. So $\|\Psi_{A_k(h)}\|\le\gamma_k^n\|\Psi_{A_k(h')}\|\le\gamma_k^n
\mathsf K\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')^{1/2}$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox}, and
$\gamma_k^n\le e^{-m_*a_kn}\le e^{-m_*(\varepsilon-\varepsilon'-a_k)}$.

(ii) First a version of Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox} for $\mathbb E=\mathbb
E_k(\Lambda)$. For a function $F$ of links in $\{x_0\ge1\}$ and $v\in\Z^4$
with $v_0=0$, the spectral measures of $\mathbb T_k$ at $\Psi_F$ and at
$\Psi_{\tau_vF}$ are measures on $[0,1]$ with the same moments
$\omega_k(\vartheta(\bar F)\tau_jF)=\omega_k(\tau_v(\vartheta(\bar F)
\tau_jF))$, $j\ge0$, so they coincide and $\|\mathbb E\Psi_{\tau_vF}\|=
\|\mathbb E\Psi_F\|$. If $g$ is supported in a cube of side $L$ in
$\{x_0\ge\varepsilon\}$, the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox} writes
$\Psi_{A_k(g)}$ as a sum of at most $\mathsf K_L$ vectors $\mathbb
T_k^{n_m}\Psi_{\tau_{v_m}A_k(h'_m)}$ with $(v_m)_0=0$, $h'_m$ supported in
$B_\varepsilon$ and $\|h'_m\|_\infty\le\|g\|_\infty$; since $\mathbb E$
commutes with $\mathbb T_k$ and $\|\mathbb T_k\|\le1$,
\[
\|\mathbb E\Psi_{A_k(g)}\|\le\mathsf K_L\,\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon;
\Lambda)^{1/2}\|g\|_\infty .
\]

Assume (UV) and let $\varepsilon'\in(0,\varepsilon)$, with $\Lambda$, $c$ as
in (UV). Take $h$ supported in $B_{\varepsilon'}$ with $\|h\|_\infty\le1$
and $\ip{\Psi}{\mathbb E\Psi}\ge\frac c2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')$,
$\Psi=\Psi_{A_k(h)}$, let $n=\lceil(\varepsilon-\varepsilon')/a_k\rceil$ and
$h_n=h(\cdot-na_ke_0)$, supported in a cube of side $1+a_0$ in
$\{x_0\ge\varepsilon\}$. With $\nu$ the spectral measure of $\mathbb T_k$ at
$\Psi$,
\[
\begin{split}
\|\Psi_{A_k(h_n)}\|^2&=\int\lambda^{2n}d\nu(\lambda)\ge e^{-2\Lambda a_kn}
\nu\bigl([e^{-\Lambda a_k},1]\bigr)\\
&\ge e^{-2\Lambda(\varepsilon-\varepsilon'+a_k)}\tfrac c2\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon'),
\end{split}
\]
and Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox} bounds the left side by $\mathsf K^2\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)$. So $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')/\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)
\le2c^{-1}\mathsf K^2e^{2\Lambda(\varepsilon-\varepsilon'+a_k)}$, which is
(SD).

Conversely, let (SD) hold and $\varepsilon'\in(0,\varepsilon)$, and fix a
number $C$ greater than $\limsup_k\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')/\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)$, and put $\Lambda=\log(4C\mathsf K^2)/(\varepsilon-
\varepsilon')$. For $h$, $n$ and $h'$ as in the proof of (i), $\|\mathbb T_k(1-\mathbb E)\|\le
e^{-\Lambda a_k}$, so
\[
\|\Psi_{A_k(h)}\|=\|\mathbb T_k^n\Psi_{A_k(h')}\|\le\|\mathbb E
\Psi_{A_k(h')}\|+e^{-\Lambda a_kn}\|\Psi_{A_k(h')}\|,
\]
and the two forms of Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox} give
\[
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\le2\mathsf K^2\bigl(\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon';
\Lambda)+e^{-2\Lambda a_kn}\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\bigr).
\]
For large $k$, $a_k\le
\frac12(\varepsilon-\varepsilon')$, so $a_kn\ge\frac12(\varepsilon-
\varepsilon')$ and $2C\mathsf K^2e^{-2\Lambda a_kn}\le\frac12$; with
$\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\le C\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$ this gives
$\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\le4C\mathsf K^2\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon';\Lambda)$, which is (UV) at $\varepsilon'$.

(iii) By (i), $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')/\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\ge
\mathsf K^{-2}\gamma_k^{-2n}$ with $n\ge(\varepsilon-\varepsilon')/a_k-1$;
in particular $\mathsf K^2C_{\varepsilon'}\ge1$. For $C>C_{\varepsilon'}$
and large $k$ this gives $2n\log(1/\gamma_k)\le\log(\mathsf K^2C)$, that is
$a_k^{-1}\log(1/\gamma_k)\le\log(\mathsf K^2C)/(2(\varepsilon-\varepsilon'
-a_k))$, and $C\downarrow C_{\varepsilon'}$ gives the bound. Under a lattice
gap the same inequality gives $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')/\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)\ge\mathsf K^{-2}e^{2m_{\mathrm{lat}}((\varepsilon-
\varepsilon')/a_k-1)}\to\infty$. Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEstrong} provides
the lattice gap $m_{\mathrm{lat}}=3.28/(625e^2)$.
\end{proof}

So (SD) bounds the physical gap from above, and it fails in the
strong-coupling range of Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEstrong}. It does not
follow from invariance, reflection positivity and a gap bounded above and
below in physical units: a heavy species with a growing weight removes it.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEheavy}
Let $m>0$, $\varrho\in(0,1)$, $q_k=e^{-ma_k}$, let $\phi^{(1)}_k$ and
$\phi^{(2)}_k$ be independent centred Gaussian fields on $\Z^4$ with
covariances $\prod_\mu q_k^{|x_\mu-y_\mu|}$ and $\prod_\mu
\varrho^{|x_\mu-y_\mu|}$, let $\lambda_k>0$, $\phi_k=\phi^{(1)}_k+\lambda_k
\phi^{(2)}_k$, $A_k(f)=\sum_xa_k^4f(a_kx)\phi_k(x)$, and form $\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)$ with these packets and $r=0$.

\textup{(a)} The law of $\phi_k$ is invariant under translations and $B_4$
and reflection positive for the reflections in coordinate hyperplanes
through sites and bisecting links. For $q_k\ge \varrho$ the spectrum of its
transfer matrix in each coordinate direction on $\Omega_k^\perp$ lies in
$[0,q_k]$ and contains $q_k$: the gap is $m$ in physical units.

\textup{(b)} Given $\varepsilon>0$, there are $\lambda_k$ with $\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon')/\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\to\infty$ for every
$\varepsilon'\in(0,\varepsilon)$, so that \textup{(SD)} fails.

\textup{(c)} For these $\lambda_k$ and every $Z_k$ for which
\textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)} holds at $n=2$, $Z_k^2\omega_k(A_k(f)A_k(g))\to0$
for every separated pair: every limit of the two-point functions vanishes.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a) Each $\phi^{(i)}_k$ is the field of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a), with $q_k$ replaced by $\varrho$
for $i=2$, which has the invariance and the
reflection positivity, with the transfer operators $P_1$, $P_2$ of spectra
$\{q_k^N\}$ and $\{\varrho^N\}$, $1$ simple, given in its proof. The pair
$(\phi^{(1)}_k,\phi^{(2)}_k)$ is then a reversible Markov chain in each
coordinate direction with transfer operator $P_1\otimes P_2$, of spectrum
$\{q_k^N\varrho^{N'}\}$ with $1$ simple, and the argument of that proof gives
$\E[\vartheta(\bar F)F']=\ip{g_F}{g_{F'}}$ for functions $F,F'$ of the pair
at positive times. The functions of $\phi_k$ at positive times are functions
of the pair there, so $\phi_k$ is reflection positive, its transfer matrix
is the restriction of $P_1\otimes P_2$ to an invariant subspace, and its
spectrum on $\Omega_k^\perp$ lies in $[0,\max(q_k,\varrho)]=[0,q_k]$. For a site
$x$ at positive time, $g_{\phi_k(x)}$ is a sum of two orthogonal
eigenvectors, of eigenvalues $q_k$ and $\varrho$, the first one nonzero, so $q_k$
is in the spectrum.

(b) Let $\vartheta$ fix the plane $\{x_0=c\}$, $|c|\le\frac12$, so that
$\vartheta x=(2c-x_0,\mathbf x)$. By independence and centring,
$\omega_k(\vartheta(\overline{A_k(h)})A_k(h))=\mathcal N^{(1)}(h)+
\lambda_k^2\mathcal N^{(2)}(h)$, with $\mathcal N^{(i)}(h)\ge0$ formed with
$\phi^{(i)}_k$. For $h$ supported in $\{x_0\ge a_k\}$,
\[
\begin{split}
\mathcal N^{(2)}(h)&=a_k^8\sum_{\mathbf x,\mathbf y}\overline{H(\mathbf x)}
H(\mathbf y)\prod_{i=1}^3\varrho^{|x_i-y_i|},\\
H(\mathbf x)&=\sum_{x_0}h(a_kx)\varrho^{x_0-c} .
\end{split}
\]
If $h$ is supported in $B_\varepsilon$ with $\|h\|_\infty\le1$ and
$a_k\le1$, then $|H|\le \varrho^{\varepsilon/a_k-1/2}/(1-\varrho)$, at most
$((2+a_0)/a_k)^3$ points $\mathbf x$ have $H(\mathbf x)\ne0$, and
$\sum_{\mathbf y}\prod_i\varrho^{|x_i-y_i|}=((1+\varrho)/(1-\varrho))^3$, so
\[
\mathcal N^{(2)}(h)\le U_k(\varepsilon)=c_\varrho a_k^5\varrho^{2\varepsilon/a_k},\qquad
c_\varrho=\frac{(2+a_0)^3(1+\varrho)^3}{\varrho(1-\varrho)^5} .
\]
For $\varepsilon'>0$ and $\tau\in(0,\frac12)$ let $h_*$ be continuous with
values in $[0,1]$, supported in $B_{\varepsilon'}$ and equal to $1$ on
$D=[\varepsilon'+\tau,\varepsilon'+1]\times[\tau,1]^3$. All terms of
$\mathcal N^{(2)}(h_*)$ are nonnegative; keeping $x=y$ with $x_0=\lceil
(\varepsilon'+\tau)/a_k\rceil$ and $a_k\mathbf x\in[\tau,1]^3$, for
$a_k\le\frac12(1-\tau)$,
\[
\mathcal N^{(2)}(h_*)\ge L_k(\varepsilon')=\Bigl(\frac{1-\tau}2\Bigr)^3
a_k^5\varrho^{2(\varepsilon'+\tau)/a_k+3} .
\]
Let $\mathcal N^{(1)}_k(\varepsilon)$ be the supremum of $\mathcal
N^{(1)}(h)$ over $h$ supported in $B_\varepsilon$ with $\|h\|_\infty\le1$.
For $a_kx,a_ky\in D_\varepsilon=[\varepsilon+\frac14,\varepsilon+1]\times
[\frac14,1]^3$ the covariance of $\phi^{(1)}_k(\vartheta x)$ and
$\phi^{(1)}_k(y)$ is at least $e^{-m(2\varepsilon+6)}$; with the function
$h_*$ for $\varepsilon$ and $\tau=\frac14$ this gives
$\mathcal N^{(1)}_k(\varepsilon)\ge\ell_0=(\frac38)^8e^{-m(2\varepsilon+6)}
>0$ for $a_k\le\frac38$, and it is finite. Put $\lambda_k^2=\mathcal
N^{(1)}_k(\varepsilon)/U_k(\varepsilon)$. Then $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\le
\mathcal N^{(1)}_k(\varepsilon)+\lambda_k^2U_k(\varepsilon)=2\mathcal
N^{(1)}_k(\varepsilon)$ and $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\ge\lambda_k^2
L_k(\varepsilon')$, so for $\tau<\varepsilon-\varepsilon'$
\[
\begin{split}
\frac{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')}{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)}&\ge
\frac{L_k(\varepsilon')}{2U_k(\varepsilon)}\\
&=\Bigl(\frac{1-\tau}2\Bigr)^3\frac{\varrho^4(1-\varrho)^5\,
\varrho^{-2(\varepsilon-\varepsilon'-\tau)/a_k}}{2(2+a_0)^3(1+\varrho)^3}
\to\infty .
\end{split}
\]

(c) Let $(f,g)\in\mathcal C_2(Q_0,\delta)$, $Q_0$ of side $L_0$, and fix
$\varepsilon',\tau>0$ with $\varepsilon'+\tau<\min(\varepsilon,\delta/2)$
and $\tau<\frac12$. For $a_k\le\varepsilon'$ the pair $(\bar h_*\circ
\theta_{0,k},h_*)$ lies in $\mathcal C_2(Q',\varepsilon')$ for a cube $Q'$
independent of $k$, so (M$_{\mathrm s}$) gives $Z_k^2\lambda_k^2
L_k(\varepsilon')\le Z_k^2\omega_k(\vartheta(A_k(h_*))A_k(h_*))\le
M=M_2(Q',\varepsilon')$, that is $Z_k^2\mathcal N^{(1)}_k(\varepsilon)\le MU_k(
\varepsilon)/L_k(\varepsilon')$. The covariance of $\phi^{(1)}_k$ is at
most $1$ and at most $(L_0/a_k+1)^4$ sites carry $f$ or $g$, so
$|\E[A^{(1)}_k(f)A^{(1)}_k(g)]|\le(L_0+1)^8\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty$ for
$a_k\le1$, and
\[
Z_k^2\bigl|\E[A^{(1)}_k(f)A^{(1)}_k(g)]\bigr|\le\frac{M(L_0+1)^8}{\ell_0}
\,\frac{U_k(\varepsilon)}{L_k(\varepsilon')}\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty\to0 .
\]
For the second field the sites carrying $f$ and $g$ are at
$\ell^\infty$ distance at least $\delta/a_k$, and $\sum_{|z|_\infty\ge d}
\prod_\mu \varrho^{|z_\mu|}\le8\varrho^d(1+\varrho)^3/(1-\varrho)^4$, so
$\lambda_k^2|\E[A^{(2)}_k(f)A^{(2)}_k(g)]|\le\lambda_k^2C_1a_k^4
\varrho^{\delta/a_k}\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty$ with $C_1=8(L_0+1)^4(1+\varrho)^3/
(1-\varrho)^4$. Since $Z_k^2\lambda_k^2\le M/L_k(\varepsilon')$,
\[
\begin{split}
&Z_k^2\lambda_k^2\bigl|\E[A^{(2)}_k(f)A^{(2)}_k(g)]\bigr|\\
&\quad\le MC_1\Bigl(\frac2{1-\tau}\Bigr)^3\frac{\varrho^{(\delta-2
\varepsilon'-2\tau)/a_k-3}}{a_k}\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty\to0 ,
\end{split}
\]
because $\delta>2(\varepsilon'+\tau)$. The cross terms vanish.
\end{proof}

In the example the part $\lambda_k^2\mathcal N^{(2)}$ of the reflected norm,
which produces the growth, is carried by energies at least
$a_k^{-1}\log(1/\varrho)\to\infty$, and (UV) fails, by the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(ii), which uses only the invariance,
reflection positivity and the gap. For $\lambda_k=0$ it is the field of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a), for which (SD) and
(M$_{\mathrm s}$) hold. Like that field, it is not a lattice gauge theory; it shows that
(SD), equivalently (UV), is an input on the ultraviolet behaviour of the
Wilson measure that reflection positivity and the gap do not supply.

\emph{The nonvanishing condition.} Under (SD) the condition (NE) is
exactly the nontriviality of the limits. Write $R_k(g)=\omega_k(\vartheta(\overline{
A_k(g)})A_k(g))/\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$ for $g$ supported in
$\{x_0\ge(r+1)a_k\}$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEne}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEtwo}} and
\textup{(SD)}.

\textup{(i)} The following are equivalent: \textup{(NE)}; every limit $S_2$
of Corollary~\textup{\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b)} is not identically zero; every
such limit has $\mathcal H^{(1)}\ne0$.

\textup{(ii)} If $\omega_k(\Phi_0\Phi_z)\ge c_k^2$ for all large $k$ and all
$z\in\Z^4$ with $|z|_\infty\ge2r+1$, then $R_k(g_0)\ge1$ for large $k$ and
every continuous $g_0$ with values in $[0,1]$, equal to $1$ on
$B_\varepsilon$ and supported in $\{x_0\ge\varepsilon/2\}$; in particular
\textup{(NE)} holds.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) The first statement implies the second by
Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b). The second and the third are equivalent:
$\mathcal H^{(1)}\ne0$ means $S_2(\bar g\circ\theta_0,g)>0$ for some $g$
supported in $\{x_0>0\}$, and the proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b)
shows that a limit with this property for no $g$ vanishes identically.

Suppose (NE) fails, so that $\liminf_kR_k(g)=0$ for every $g\in C_c(\R^4)$
supported in $\{x_0>0\}$. Let $g_1,\dots,g_J$ be supported in a cube of side
$L$ and in $\{x_0\ge\eta\}$, $\eta>0$, let $D>0$ be dyadic and
$G=\sum_jg_j(\cdot-jDe_1)$. For large $k$, $jD/a_k\in\Z$ and
$R_k(g_j(\cdot-jDe_1))=R_k(g_j)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i),(iii).
For $i\ne j$ the cross term $\ip{\Psi_{A_k(g_i(\cdot-iDe_1))}}
{\Psi_{A_k(g_j(\cdot-jDe_1))}}$ equals $\omega_k(A_k(\bar g_i(\cdot-iDe_1)
\circ\theta_{0,k})A_k(g_j(\cdot-jDe_1)))$, two packets in cubes of side $L$
whose projections on the $e_1$ axis are at distance at least $|i-j|D-L$; by
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtwo}(i) in the direction $e_1$ with $d=D-L-4
\varepsilon$, its modulus is at most $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\mathsf
K_L^2e^{-m_*(D-L-4\varepsilon-1)}\max_j\|g_j\|_\infty^2$. Hence
$\sum_jR_k(g_j)\le R_k(G)+J^2\mathsf K_L^2e^{-m_*(D-L-4\varepsilon-1)}
\max_j\|g_j\|_\infty^2$ for large $k$, and since $\liminf_kR_k(G)=0$ and $D$
is arbitrary, $\liminf_k\sum_jR_k(g_j)=0$.

Enumerate as $g_1,g_2,\dots$ the elements of the countable set $\mathcal G$
of the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(i) that are supported in
$\{x_0>0\}$, and choose $k_1<k_2<\cdots$ with $\sum_{j\le J}R_{k_J}(g_j)<
1/J$. Then $R_{k_J}(g_j)\to0$ for every $j$. Let $g$ be supported in a cube
of side $L$ and in $\{x_0\ge\eta\}$, and let $\tau\in(0,\min(\eta,1)/2]$. That proof
gives $g_j\in\mathcal G$ with $\|g-g_j\|_\infty<\tau$ and support within
$\tau$ of $\operatorname{supp}g$, so $g-g_j$ is supported in a cube of side
$L+1$ in $\{x_0\ge\eta/2\}$, and by Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox}
\[
R_k(g)^{1/2}\le R_k(g_j)^{1/2}+\mathsf K_{L+1}\Bigl(\frac{\mathcal N_k(
\eta/2)}{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)}\Bigr)^{1/2}\tau .
\]
The ratio has finite $\limsup$, by (SD) if $\eta/2<\varepsilon$ and by
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(i) otherwise. As $\tau$ is arbitrary,
$R_{k_J}(g)\to0$. By Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b) a subsequence of
$(k_J)$ has a limit $S_2$, and by the approximation of $\theta_{0,k}$ by
$\theta_0$ in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(iii), $S_2(\bar g
\circ\theta_0,g)=\lim_JR_{k_J}(g)=0$ for every $g$ supported in
$\{x_0>0\}$. So this limit vanishes identically, and the second statement
fails.

(ii) Let $h$ be supported in $B_\varepsilon$ with $\|h\|_\infty\le1$, and
$(r+1)a_k\le\varepsilon/2$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i),
$\omega_k(\vartheta(\overline{A_k(h)})A_k(h))=\sum_{x,y}a_k^8\overline{h(a_k
x)}h(a_ky)K(x,y)$ with $K(x,y)=\omega_k(\Phi_{\vartheta x}\Phi_y)-c_k^2=
\omega_k(\Phi_0\Phi_{y-\vartheta x})-c_k^2$. For $a_kx_0,a_ky_0\ge
\varepsilon/2$, $x_0,y_0\ge r+1$ and the time component of $y-\vartheta x$
is $x_0+y_0-2c\ge2r+1$, so $K(x,y)\ge0$ and the sum is at most
$\sum_{x,y}a_k^8g_0(a_kx)g_0(a_ky)K(x,y)=\omega_k(\vartheta(A_k(g_0))
A_k(g_0))$, because $|h|\le g_0$. Taking the supremum over $h$ gives
$\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\le\omega_k(\vartheta(A_k(g_0))A_k(g_0))$.
\end{proof}

The condition of (ii) is a correlation inequality of Griffiths type for the
connected two-point function of $\Phi$; it is not proved here for the
Wilson measure.

\emph{Products of packets.} For $n\ge3$ the moment bound reduces to
reflected norms of products. Consider
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(RN)},leftmargin=*]
\item For every $m\ge1$, every cube $Q$ and every $\delta>0$ there are
$R_m(Q,\delta)<\infty$ and $k_0$ such that
\[
Z_k^{2m}\omega_k\bigl(\vartheta(\bar P_k)P_k\bigr)\le R_m(Q,\delta)
\prod_{j=1}^m\|f_j\|_\infty^2,\qquad P_k=\prod_{j=1}^mA_k(f_j),
\]
for all $k\ge k_0$ and $(f_1,\dots,f_m)\in\mathcal C_m(Q,\delta)$ with
supports in $\{x_0\ge\delta\}$.
\end{enumerate}
We write (RN$_m$) for the statement at a fixed $m$, and (M$_{\mathrm s,n}$)
for (M$_{\mathrm s}$) at a fixed $n$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEreflect}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}}.

\textup{(i)} \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s,2m}$)} implies \textup{(RN$_m$)}.

\textup{(ii)} Let $n\ge2$. If \textup{(RN$_i$)} holds for $1\le i\le n-1$, then
\textup{(M$_{\mathrm s,n}$)} holds, with
\[
M_n(Q,\delta)=\mathsf K_{2nL/\delta}^{\,n}\max_{1\le i\le n-1}\bigl(R_i(Q^*,
\delta_n)R_{n-i}(Q^*,\delta_n)\bigr)^{1/2}
\]
for $Q$ of side $L$, $Q^*=[-L-2,L+2]^4$ and $\delta_n=\delta/(16(n+1))$.

\textup{(iii)} \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)} holds if and only if
\textup{(RN)} holds, and \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s,2}$)} if and only if
\textup{(RN$_1$)}. With $Z_k=\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$ as in
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEtwo}}, \textup{(RN$_1$)} is equivalent to
\textup{(SD)}, and \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s,3}$)} follows from \textup{(SD)}
and \textup{(RN$_2$)}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Let $(f_j)\in\mathcal C_m(Q,\delta)$ be supported in $\{x_0\ge\delta\}$
and $a_k\le\delta$. The functions $\bar f_j\circ\theta_{0,k}$ are supported in
$\{x_0\le a_k-\delta\}$, so the $2m$-tuple of these and the $f_j$ lies in
$\mathcal C_{2m}(Q',\delta)$ for a cube $Q'$ containing $Q$ and all
$\theta_{0,k}(Q)$, and $\omega_k(\vartheta(\bar P_k)P_k)=\omega_k(\prod_j
A_k(\bar f_j\circ\theta_{0,k})\prod_jA_k(f_j))$ by
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i). (M$_{\mathrm s,2m}$) gives (RN$_m$) with
$R_m(Q,\delta)=M_{2m}(Q',\delta)$.

(ii) Let $(f_j)\in\mathcal C_n(Q,\delta)$, $s=\delta/(2n)$, and decompose
every $f_j$ with the functions $\chi^s_\kappa$ of the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(vi); at most $\mathsf K_{L/s}=\mathsf
K_{2nL/\delta}$ pieces of each are nonzero, each with norm at most
$\|f_j\|_\infty$. Fix a nonzero term, with pieces in closed cubes
$C_1,\dots,C_n$ of side $s$, points $p\in C_1$ and $q\in C_2$ in the supports
and $\mu$ with $q_\mu-p_\mu\ge\delta$ (after exchanging $f_1$ and $f_2$).
The projections of the $C_j$ on the $\mu$ axis have total length $\delta/2$,
so their complement in $[p_\mu,q_\mu]$ contains an interval of length at
least $\delta/(2(n+1))$; let $c$ be its midpoint. Every projection is at
distance at least $\delta/(4(n+1))=4\delta_n$ from $c$; let $I$ and $J$ be
the indices of the cubes below and above $c$, nonempty since $1\in I$ and
$2\in J$. Translate all pieces by one vector of $a_k\Z^4$ so that $c$ moves
within $a_k/2$ of the plane $\{x_\mu=a_kc_\mu\}$ fixed by $\theta_{\mu,k}$,
$|c_\mu|\le\frac12$, and so that the pieces lie in $Q^*$; the moment does not
change. For $a_k\le2\delta_n$ the pieces of $I$ lie in $\{x_\mu\le a_kc_\mu
-3\delta_n\}$ and those of $J$ in $\{x_\mu\ge a_kc_\mu+3\delta_n\}$. With
$F=\prod_{j\in I}A_k(\bar f'_j\circ\theta_{\mu,k})$ and $G=\prod_{j\in J}
A_k(f'_j)$, $f'_j$ the translated pieces, Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i)
gives $\prod_{j\in I}A_k(f'_j)=\vartheta_\mu(\bar F)$, and by the invariance
under $\pi_\mu$ and the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality for the positive form of
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(iii),
\[
\Bigl|\omega_k\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^nA_k(f'_j)\Bigr)\Bigr|=|\omega_k(
\vartheta(\overline{\pi_\mu F})\pi_\mu G)|\le N_0(\pi_\mu F)N_0(\pi_\mu G).
\]
The factors of $\pi_\mu F$ and $\pi_\mu G$ are supported in $Q^*$ and in
$\{x_0\ge3\delta_n-a_k\}\subset\{x_0\ge\delta_n\}$, pairwise at distance at
least $\delta$, so (RN$_{|I|}$) and (RN$_{|J|}$) bound $Z_k^nN_0(\pi_\mu F)
N_0(\pi_\mu G)$ by $(R_{|I|}R_{|J|})^{1/2}\prod_j\|f_j\|_\infty$ for large $k$.
Summing over the at most $\mathsf K_{2nL/\delta}^n$ terms gives the claim.

(iii) The first two statements follow from (i) and (ii). For $Z_k=\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$, (RN$_1$) at $Q\supset B_{\varepsilon'}$ and
$\delta=\varepsilon'$ gives $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\le R_1\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)$, which is (SD); conversely Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox}
bounds the left side of (RN$_1$) by $\mathsf K_L^2\|f\|_\infty^2$ times
$\mathcal N_k(\delta)/\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$, which is bounded by (SD)
for $\delta<\varepsilon$ and by Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(i) for
$\delta\ge\varepsilon$. The last claim is (ii) for $n=3$.
\end{proof}

The Cauchy--Schwarz inequality of reflection positivity bounds a moment by
the reflected norms of the products of the two groups of packets on the two
sides of a plane, and for $n\ge3$ one group contains at least two packets;
the reduction therefore stops at (RN$_m$) with $m\ge2$, the reflected norms
of products of separated packets. If the packets are jointly Gaussian,
Wick's formula writes $\omega_k(\vartheta(\bar P_k)P_k)$ as a sum of
$(2m-1)!!$ products of covariances of separated pairs, and (RN) follows from
(RN$_1$) by (ii) with $n=2$; for the Wilson measure (RN$_m$) with $m\ge2$ is
not proved.

\emph{Rotations at separated points.} The hypercubic group fixes the
lowest anisotropy that a separated two-point function can carry. Let
$\mathcal P_l$ be the space of homogeneous polynomials of degree $l$ on
$\R^4$, $\mathcal Y_l\subset\mathcal P_l$ the harmonic ones, and
$\mathcal Y_l^{B_4}$ the elements of $\mathcal Y_l$ invariant under $B_4$.
Put
\[
\begin{split}
Y_4(z)&=\sum_\mu z_\mu^4-\tfrac12|z|^4,\\
Y_6(z)&=\sum_\mu z_\mu^6-\tfrac54|z|^2\sum_\mu z_\mu^4+\tfrac5{16}|z|^6 .
\end{split}
\]

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:limitEharm}
$\mathcal Y_l^{B_4}=0$ for odd $l$, and $\dim\mathcal Y_{2j}^{B_4}=
p(j)-p(j-1)$ for $j\ge1$, where $p(j)$ is the number of partitions of $j$
into at most four parts. In particular $\dim\mathcal Y_l^{B_4}$ is $0$, $1$,
$1$, $2$ for $l=2,4,6,8$, and $\mathcal Y_4^{B_4}=\R Y_4$,
$\mathcal Y_6^{B_4}=\R Y_6$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
A sign change $z_\mu\mapsto-z_\mu$ multiplies a monomial by $-1$ to the
power of its degree in $z_\mu$, so a polynomial invariant under the sign
changes contains only monomials of even degree in every variable; it is a
polynomial in $w_\mu=z_\mu^2$, and invariance under the permutations makes
it symmetric in $w_0,\dots,w_3$. The monomial symmetric polynomials
$m_\kappa(w)$, $\kappa$ a partition of $j$ into at most four parts, form a
basis of the symmetric polynomials of degree $j$ in four variables, so
$\dim\mathcal P_{2j}^{B_4}=p(j)$ and $\mathcal P_l^{B_4}=0$ for odd $l$.
By~\cite[Ch.~IV]{SW71}, $\mathcal P_l=\mathcal Y_l\oplus|z|^2\mathcal
P_{l-2}$, and both summands are invariant under $\mathrm O(4)$, so the
projections on them commute with $B_4$ and $\mathcal P_l^{B_4}=\mathcal
Y_l^{B_4}\oplus|z|^2\mathcal P_{l-2}^{B_4}$; multiplication by $|z|^2$ is
injective. This gives the dimension, and $p(0),\dots,p(4)$ are $1,1,2,3,5$.
In $\R^4$, $\Delta|z|^{2i}=2i(2i+2)|z|^{2i-2}$, $\Delta\sum_\mu z_\mu^{2i}=
2i(2i-1)\sum_\mu z_\mu^{2i-2}$ and $\Delta(|z|^2\sum_\mu z_\mu^4)=
24\sum_\mu z_\mu^4+12|z|^4$, so $\Delta Y_4=12|z|^2-\frac12\cdot24|z|^2=0$
and $\Delta Y_6=30\sum_\mu z_\mu^4-\frac54(24\sum_\mu z_\mu^4+12|z|^4)+
\frac5{16}\cdot48|z|^4=0$. Both are invariant and nonzero, since
$Y_4(e_0)=\frac12$ and $Y_6(e_0)=\frac1{16}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEaniso}
Let $S_2$ be a bilinear form on the separated pairs, invariant under the
translations and $B_4$, with $S_2(f,g)=\iint f(x)g(y)K(x-y)\,dx\,dy$ for
separated pairs, where $K$ is square integrable on every compact subset of
$\R^4\setminus\{0\}$.

\textup{(i)} For $\varphi\in C_c(\R^4\setminus\{0\})$ and $g_\varepsilon=
\varepsilon^{-4}\chi(\cdot/\varepsilon)$, $\chi\ge0$ continuous with
$\int\chi=1$ and support in $[-1,1]^4$,
\[
\int K\varphi\,dz=\lim_{\varepsilon\to0}S_2(\varphi,g_\varepsilon),
\]
so $K$ is determined almost everywhere by $S_2$. For $R\in\mathrm O(4)$,
$S_2(f\circ R^{-1},g\circ R^{-1})=S_2(f,g)$ for all separated pairs if and
only if $K\circ R=K$ almost everywhere; in particular $K\circ g=K$ for
$g\in B_4$.

\textup{(ii)} For almost every $\rho>0$, $K(\rho\,\cdot)\in L^2(S^3)$ and
$K(\rho\omega)=\sum_{j\ge0}K_{2j}(\rho,\omega)$ in $L^2(S^3)$, with
$K_{2j}(\rho,\cdot)$ the restriction of an element of
$\mathcal Y_{2j}^{B_4}$. Hence $K_2=0$, $K_0(\rho)$ is the mean of $K$ on
the sphere of radius $\rho$, and there are measurable $k_4,k_6$ with
\[
K_4(\rho,\omega)=k_4(\rho)Y_4(\omega),\qquad K_6(\rho,\omega)=
k_6(\rho)Y_6(\omega).
\]

\textup{(iii)} The following are equivalent: \textup{(a)} $S_2$ is
invariant under $\mathrm O(4)$ on the separated pairs; \textup{(b)} it is
invariant under one $R\in\mathrm O(4)$ of infinite order; \textup{(c)}
$K_{2j}(\rho,\cdot)=0$ for every $j\ge2$ and almost every $\rho$;
\textup{(d)} $K(z)=K_0(|z|)$ for almost every $z$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Let $\varphi$ be supported in $\{|z|_\infty\ge3\eta\}$ and
$\varepsilon\le\eta$; the pair $(\varphi,g_\varepsilon)$ is separated by
at least $2\eta$, and
\[
S_2(\varphi,g_\varepsilon)=\int K(z)\varphi_\varepsilon(z)\,dz,\qquad
\varphi_\varepsilon(z)=\int\varphi(z+y)g_\varepsilon(y)\,dy .
\]
The functions $\varphi_\varepsilon$ are supported in one compact subset of
$\R^4\setminus\{0\}$ and converge uniformly to $\varphi$, and $K$ is
integrable there. For $R\in\mathrm O(4)$ the substitution $x=Ru$, $y=Rv$
gives $S_2(f\circ R^{-1},g\circ R^{-1})=\iint f(u)g(v)K(R(u-v))\,du\,dv$, so
invariance holds if and only if the form with kernel $K\circ R-K$ vanishes
on the separated pairs, and by the formula just proved this happens if and
only if $K\circ R=K$ almost everywhere.

(ii) By Fubini's theorem in polar coordinates, $K(\rho\,\cdot)\in L^2(S^3)$
for almost every $\rho$, and for each of the finitely many $g\in B_4$,
$K(\rho g\,\cdot)=K(\rho\,\cdot)$ for almost every $\rho$. The restrictions
of the spaces $\mathcal Y_l$ to $S^3$ are orthogonal in $L^2(S^3)$ and span
it~\cite[Ch.~IV]{SW71}; each is invariant under $\mathrm O(4)$, so the
orthogonal projections on them commute with composition by elements of
$\mathrm O(4)$. The component of degree $l$ of $K(\rho\,\cdot)$ is
therefore invariant under $B_4$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEharm} gives the
claims.

(iii) Let $G_K$ be the set of $R\in\mathrm O(4)$ with $K\circ R=K$ almost
everywhere. On every closed annulus $A=\{r\le|z|\le r'\}$, $0<r<r'$, the
map $R\mapsto K\circ R$ into $L^2(A)$ is continuous, as one sees by
approximating $K$ in $L^2(A)$ by continuous functions; so $G_K$ is a closed
subgroup of $\mathrm O(4)$, and it contains $B_4$ by (i). By the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:rotations}(iv), which uses only these two properties,
$G_K$ is finite or equal to $\mathrm O(4)$, so (a) and (b) are equivalent
by (i). If (a) holds, let $R_1,R_2,\dots$ be dense in $\mathrm O(4)$; for
almost every $\rho$, $K(\rho R_i\,\cdot)=K(\rho\,\cdot)$ in $L^2(S^3)$ for
every $i$, hence, by continuity of $R\mapsto u\circ R$ in $L^2(S^3)$, for
every $R$, and each component $K_l(\rho,\cdot)$ is the restriction of an
$\mathrm O(4)$-invariant element of $\mathcal Y_l$. Such an element is
constant on spheres, hence equal to $c|z|^l$, and
$\Delta|z|^l=l(l+2)|z|^{l-2}$ vanishes only for $l=0$; so (d) holds. (d)
implies (c), (c) implies (d) because $K_2=0$, and (d) implies (a) by (i).
\end{proof}

So the part of a separated two-point kernel that is invariant under $B_4$
and not under $\mathrm O(4)$ has no components of degree below four, and
its component of degree four has the fixed angular profile $Y_4$, with one
radial coefficient $k_4$; by (i), $\int K(z)Y_4(z)\psi(|z|)\,dz=\lim_{\varepsilon\to0}
S_2(Y_4\psi(|\cdot|),g_\varepsilon)$ for $\psi\in C_c((0,\infty))$, a
separated two-point function of packets. By (iii), (W$_{\mathrm s}$) for
$n=2$ is the vanishing of all $K_{2j}$ with $j\ge2$. Reflection positivity
and clustering do not remove the component of degree four, by
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEproduct}(iv) below.

Two statements about the lattice moments
\[
M_k(x)=\omega_k\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^n(\Phi_{x_j}-c_k)\Bigr),\qquad
x=(x_1,\dots,x_n)\in(\Z^4)^n,
\]
give (W$_{\mathrm s}$). For $0<\delta<\Lambda$ let $\mathcal W_k(\delta,
\Lambda)$ be the set of $x\in(\Z^4)^n$ with $\delta/a_k\le|x_i-x_j|\le
\Lambda/a_k$ for $i\ne j$, $|\cdot|$ being the Euclidean norm. For $n\ge2$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(SB$_n$)},leftmargin=*]
\item for $0<\delta<\Lambda$,
\[
b_n(\delta,\Lambda)=\limsup_k\sup_{x\in\mathcal W_k(\delta,\Lambda)}
Z_k^n|M_k(x)|<\infty ;
\]
\end{enumerate}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(RI$_n$)},leftmargin=*]
\item for $0<\delta<\Lambda$, $\lim_k\sup Z_k^n|M_k(x)-M_k(x')|=0$, the
supremum over the pairs $x,x'\in\mathcal W_k(\delta,\Lambda)$ with
$\bigl||x_i-x_j|-|x'_i-x'_j|\bigr|\le4$ for all $i<j$.
\end{enumerate}
(SB$_n$) is a pointwise bound of Symanzik type on the renormalised lattice
moments at separated points. (RI$_n$) says that at separations of physical
size, that is of order $1/a_k$ lattice units, the renormalised moments
depend on the configuration only through its mutual Euclidean distances,
up to an error that tends to zero, when these distances are changed by at
most four lattice units. This is rotation invariance at large lattice
distance, the form in which the restoration of rotation invariance
predicted by Symanzik's improvement programme enters; neither statement is
proved here for the Wilson measure.

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:limitErot}
Let the states $\omega_k$ be invariant under the translations of $\Z^4$,
let $\Phi$ be as above and $Z_k>0$, and let $n\ge2$.

\textup{(i)} \textup{(SB$_n$)} implies \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)} for this
$n$.

\textup{(ii)} \textup{(SB$_n$)} and \textup{(RI$_n$)} imply that for every
$R\in\mathrm O(4)$ and every separated $n$-tuple
\[
\lim_k\Bigl[\omega_k\Bigl(\prod_jZ_kA_k(f_j\circ R^{-1})\Bigr)-\omega_k
\Bigl(\prod_jZ_kA_k(f_j)\Bigr)\Bigr]=0 .
\]

\textup{(iii)} If \textup{(SB$_n$)} and \textup{(RI$_n$)} hold for every
$n\ge2$, then \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)} holds and \textup{(W$_{\mathrm
s}$)} holds for every $R$. Under the hypothesis of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}} every limit of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}} then has $G_S=\mathrm O(4)$, and with
\textup{(U$_{\mathrm s}$)} the conclusions of
Corollary~\textup{\ref{cor:limitEexact}} hold.

\textup{(iv)} Under \textup{(SB$_2$)} and \textup{(RI$_2$)}, every
subsequence has a further subsequence along which
$\lim_kZ_k^2\omega_k(A_k(f)A_k(g))=\iint f(x)g(y)K(x-y)\,dx\,dy$ for every
separated pair, with $K(z)=K_0(|z|)$ and $K_0$ bounded on every compact
subset of $(0,\infty)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For $u\in\R^4$ let $\varrho_k(u)$ be the point of $\Z^4$ nearest to
$u/a_k$, ties broken by a fixed rule. Then $|u/a_k-\varrho_k(u)|\le1$, the
half diagonal of the unit cube of $\Z^4$, and each set
$\varrho_k^{-1}(x)$ is, up to a null set, the cube of side $a_k$ centred at
$a_kx$. Let $(f_j)\in\mathcal C_n(Q,\delta)$, $Q$ of side $L$, so that all
supports lie in a ball $B$ of radius $L$, points of different supports are
at Euclidean distance between $\delta$ and $2L$, and the same holds for the
rotated functions $f_j\circ R^{-1}$ with the ball $RB$ about the rotated
centre. Let $a_k\le\min(\delta/4,L/2)$ and put $F(u)=\prod_jf_j(u_j)$,
$\mathcal B_k(F)=\omega_k(\prod_jZ_kA_k(f_j))$ and
\[
I_k(F)=\int F(u)\,Z_k^nM_k\bigl(\varrho_k(u_1),\dots,\varrho_k(u_n)\bigr)
\,du .
\]

(i) $\mathcal B_k(F)=Z_k^na_k^{4n}\sum_x\prod_jf_j(a_kx_j)M_k(x)$, the nonzero terms
have $x\in\mathcal W_k(\delta,2L)$, and at most $(3L/a_k)^4$ sites $x_j$
have $a_kx_j\in B$, because the cubes of side $a_k$ about them are disjoint
and lie in a ball of radius $L+a_k\le\frac32L$ and volume at most
$\frac{\pi^2}2(\frac32L)^4<(3L)^4$. So $|\mathcal B_k(F)|\le(b_n(\delta,2L)+1)(3L)^{4n}
\prod_j\|f_j\|_\infty$ for large $k$.

(ii) Since $\varrho_k$ is constant on the cubes,
\[
I_k(F)-\mathcal B_k(F)=\sum_xZ_k^nM_k(x)\int_{\prod_j\varrho_k^{-1}(x_j)}
\bigl(F(u)-F(a_kx)\bigr)du .
\]
A nonzero term has $|u_j-a_kx_j|\le a_k$ for some $u_j$ in the support of
$f_j$, hence $x\in\mathcal W_k(\delta/2,3L)$, and the number of such $x$
is at most $(3L/a_k)^{4n}$, the cubes about the $x_j$ lying in a ball of
radius $L+2a_k\le2L$ and volume $8\pi^2L^4<(3L)^4$. So $|I_k(F)-\mathcal B_k(F)|\le(b_n(\delta/2,3L)+1)
(3L)^{4n}\varpi_F(a_k)$ for large $k$, with $\varpi_F(s)$ the largest
change of $F$ when every $u_j$ moves by at most $s$; it tends to $0$, and
the same holds for $F_R(u)=\prod_jf_j(R^{-1}u_j)$. By the substitution
$u=Rv$,
\[
I_k(F_R)-I_k(F)=\int F(v)Z_k^n\bigl[M_k(x'(v))-M_k(x(v))\bigr]dv,
\]
with $x_j(v)=\varrho_k(v_j)$ and $x'_j(v)=\varrho_k(Rv_j)$. For $v$ in the
support of $F$, $|x_i-x_j|$ and $|x'_i-x'_j|$ differ from $|v_i-v_j|/a_k=
|Rv_i-Rv_j|/a_k$ by at most $2$, hence from each other by at most $4$, and
both configurations lie in $\mathcal W_k(\delta/2,3L)$. By
(RI$_n$) the bracket is at most $\epsilon_k\to0$, and $|I_k(F_R)-I_k(F)|\le
\epsilon_k\prod_j\|f_j\|_{L^1}$.

(iii) The first claims are (i) and (ii) for every $n$, and the rest is
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(vii) and Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEexact}.

(iv) By translation invariance $M_k(x_1,x_2)=G_k(x_2-x_1)$ with
$G_k(y)=M_k(0,y)$; put $\kappa_k(z)=Z_k^2G_k(\varrho_k(z))$. For separated
$f,g$ as above, $y=\varrho_k(v)-\varrho_k(u)$ and $y'=\varrho_k(v-u)$ have
$|y|$ and $|y'|$ within $2$ and $1$ of $|v-u|/a_k$, so (RI$_2$) and the
proof of (ii) give $Z_k^2\omega_k(A_k(f)A_k(g))-\iint f(u)g(v)\kappa_k(v-u)
\,du\,dv\to0$. For $r\le|z|\le r'$ and large $k$, $|\varrho_k(z)|\in[r/(2a_k),
2r'/a_k]$, so $|\kappa_k|\le b_2(r/2,2r')+1$ there, and $|\kappa_k(Rz)-
\kappa_k(z)|\to0$ uniformly there for every $R\in\mathrm O(4)$ by (RI$_2$),
since $|\varrho_k(Rz)|$ and $|\varrho_k(z)|$ differ by at most $2$. A
diagonal subsequence converges weakly-$*$ in $L^\infty$ of every annulus
to some $K$, and then $K\circ R=K$ almost everywhere for every $R$. For
separated $f,g$, $\iint f(u)g(v)\kappa_k(v-u)\,du\,dv=\int\kappa_k(z)
\Theta(z)\,dz$ with $\Theta(z)=\int f(u)g(u+z)\,du$ integrable and
supported in an annulus, so the limit is $\iint f(u)g(v)K(v-u)\,du\,dv$, and
$K(-z)=K(z)$. Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEaniso}(iii) gives $K(z)=K_0(|z|)$
almost everywhere.
\end{proof}

Part (i) does not use the averaged condition, and parts (i), (ii), (iv) use
of the states only their translation invariance. (SB$_n$) alone does not
give (W$_{\mathrm s}$), and neither do reflection positivity, the gap in
every coordinate direction and non-Gaussian moments, by the following
family; it is not a lattice gauge theory, and, as in
Remark~\ref{rem:limitEscope}, the proofs of this subsection use the states
only through the properties in (i) below.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEproduct}
Let $m>0$, $q_k=e^{-ma_k}$, $p\ge1$, let $\phi^{(1)},\dots,\phi^{(p)}$ be
independent copies of the Gaussian field of
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a)}, $\Phi_x=\prod_i
\phi^{(i)}_x$, $A_k(f)=\sum_xa_k^4f(a_kx)\Phi_x$ and $Z_k=1$.

\textup{(i)} The law of $(\phi^{(1)},\dots,\phi^{(p)})$ is invariant under
translations and $B_4$, reflection positive for the reflections in
coordinate hyperplanes through sites and bisecting links, and its transfer
matrix in each coordinate direction has spectrum $\{q_k^N:N\ge0\}$ with $1$
a simple eigenvalue.

\textup{(ii)} For all $n$ and $f_j\in C_c(\R^4)$ the moments converge,
\[
\begin{split}
&\lim_k\omega_k\Bigl(\prod_jA_k(f_j)\Bigr)\\
&\quad=\int\prod_jf_j(u_j)\Bigl(\sum_\pi\prod_{\{a,b\}\in\pi}
e^{-m\|u_a-u_b\|_1}\Bigr)^pdu,
\end{split}
\]
the sum over the perfect matchings $\pi$ of $\{1,\dots,n\}$; for $f_j$
supported in a cube of side $L\ge a_k$ they are at most
$(n!)^p(2L)^{4n}\prod_j\|f_j\|_\infty$. So \textup{(M)},
\textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)}, \textup{(SB$_n$)} and \textup{(U$_{\mathrm
s}$)} hold.

\textup{(iii)} For $p\ge2$ the limit is not Gaussian.

\textup{(iv)} $G_S=B_4$, \textup{(RI$_2$)} fails, and the kernel
$K(z)=e^{-pm\|z\|_1}$ of $S_2$ has $k_4\ne0$ on a set of positive measure.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) In each coordinate direction the field $(\phi^{(1)},\dots,\phi^{(p)})$
is the stationary reversible Markov chain with transition operator
$P^{\otimes p}$, $P$ that of the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a); the spectrum of $P^{\otimes p}$
consists of the products $q^{N_1+\dots+N_p}$, and $1$ is simple. That proof
uses only these properties.

(ii) By independence and Wick's formula, $\omega_k(\prod_j\Phi_{x_j})=
\bigl(\sum_\pi\prod_{\{a,b\}\in\pi}w_k(x_a-x_b)\bigr)^p$ with $w_k(z)=
\prod_\mu q_k^{|z_\mu|}=e^{-ma_k\|z\|_1}$, so $c_k=0$; this is at most the
number of matchings to the power $p$ when $n$ is even and $0$ otherwise,
and the moments are Riemann sums of a bounded continuous function. At most
$(L/a_k+1)^4\le(2L/a_k)^4$ sites $x$ have $a_kx$ in a cube of side $L\ge
a_k$, which gives the bound, hence (M), (M$_{\mathrm s}$) and (SB$_n$), and
the convergence of the whole sequence gives (U$_{\mathrm s}$).

(iii) For $n=4$ write $a=w_{12}w_{34}$, $b=w_{13}w_{24}$, $c=w_{14}w_{23}$
with $w_{ij}=e^{-m\|u_i-u_j\|_1}$. The Gaussian family with the same $S_2$
has the integrand $a^p+b^p+c^p$, and $(a+b+c)^p>a^p+b^p+c^p$ for $p\ge2$ and
$a,b,c>0$; so $S_4$ differs from it for nonnegative nonzero $f_j$.

(iv) $B_4\subset G_S\subset G_{S_2}$, and $G_{S_2}=B_4$ by the argument of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a) with $pm$ in place of $m$. For
$y=(2N,0,0,0)$ and $y'=(N,N,N,N)$, $|y|=|y'|=2N$ and $M_k(0,y)-M_k(0,y')=
e^{-2pma_kN}-e^{-4pma_kN}$; with $N=\lceil\delta/a_k\rceil$ both lie in
$\mathcal W_k(\delta,3\delta)$ for large $k$, and the difference tends to
$e^{-2pm\delta}-e^{-4pm\delta}>0$, so (RI$_2$) fails. Finally
$Y_4(z)=\frac12\sum_\mu z_\mu^4-\sum_{\mu<\nu}z_\mu^2z_\nu^2$, and with
$I_i=\int_\R|t|^ie^{-M|t|}dt=2\,i!/M^{i+1}$, $M=pm$,
\[
\int_{\R^4}K(z)Y_4(z)\,dz=2I_4I_0^3-6I_2^2I_0^2=\frac{768-384}{M^8}>0 .
\]
In polar coordinates the left side is $\|Y_4\|^2_{L^2(S^3)}\int_0^\infty
\rho^7k_4(\rho)\,d\rho$, by the orthogonality of the components in
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEaniso}(ii), so $k_4$ is not almost everywhere
zero.
\end{proof}

For a product kernel $K(z)=\prod_\mu k(z_\mu)$ with $k\ge0$ and moments
$I_i=\int|t|^ik(t)\,dt$ the same computation gives $\int KY_4\,dz=2I_0^2
I_2^2(I_4I_0/I_2^2-3)$: the weighted coefficient $\int\rho^7k_4\,d\rho$
has the sign of the excess kurtosis of $k$, which is $3$ for $e^{-M|t|}$
and $0$ for a Gaussian $k$. The family satisfies every hypothesis of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitErot} except (RI$_2$), so among the lattice
statements used here it is (RI$_n$) that excludes it.

\emph{The scale.} For a family $S=(S_n)$ on the separated tuples and
$\lambda,c>0$ put
\[
(D_{\lambda,c}S)_n(f_1,\dots,f_n)=c^nS_n\bigl(f_1(\lambda\,\cdot),\dots,
f_n(\lambda\,\cdot)\bigr) ;
\]
this is a family on the separated tuples, because the map taking $f$ to
$f(\lambda\,\cdot)$ takes $\mathcal C_n(Q,\delta)$ onto $\mathcal
C_n(\lambda^{-1}Q,\delta/\lambda)$. Moreover
$D_{\lambda,c}D_{\lambda',c'}=D_{\lambda\lambda',cc'}$. For a nontrivial limit $S$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep} let $m(S)=\min(\sigma(H_0)\setminus\{0\})$,
which exists and is at least $m_*$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(iv),
(vi).

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:limitEdil}
Let $S$ be a limit of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}} and
$\lambda,c>0$. For $P$ at positive $0$-times let $P^{\lambda,c}$ be
obtained by replacing every $f_{\beta,j}$ by $f_{\beta,j}(\lambda\,\cdot)$
and every coefficient $c_\beta$ by $c^{n_\beta}c_\beta$. Then
$D_{\lambda,c}S(\theta_0\bar P\cdot P')=S(\theta_0\overline{P^{\lambda,c}}
\cdot P'^{\lambda,c})$, and $\Psi'(P)\mapsto\Psi_0(P^{\lambda,c})$ extends to
a unitary map $V$ of the Hilbert space $\mathcal H'$ of $D_{\lambda,c}S$
onto $\mathcal H_0$ with $V\Omega'=\Omega_0$. With
$H'=\lambda^{-1}V^{-1}H_0V$, $\Psi'(\tau_{te_0}P)=e^{-tH'}\Psi'(P)$ for
$t\ge0$, and $\sigma(H')=\lambda^{-1}\sigma(H_0)$; when $D_{\lambda,c}S$ is
itself a limit of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}}, its Hamiltonian in
the time direction is $H'$, and for nontrivial $S$, $m(D_{\lambda,c}S)=
m(S)/\lambda$. If $S$ is nontrivial and
$D_{\lambda,c}S=S$, then $\lambda=c=1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Since $\theta_0$ is linear, $(f\circ\theta_0)(\lambda\,\cdot)=f(\lambda\,
\cdot)\circ\theta_0$, which gives the identity; it shows that the form of
$D_{\lambda,c}S$ is positive and that $V$ is isometric, and $V$ has dense
range because $P=(P^{1/\lambda,1/c})^{\lambda,c}$. Further
$(f(\cdot-te_0))(\lambda\,\cdot)=(f(\lambda\,\cdot))(\cdot-\frac t\lambda
e_0)$, so $V\Psi'(\tau_{te_0}P)=\Psi_0(\tau_{(t/\lambda)e_0}P^{\lambda,c})=
e^{-(t/\lambda)H_0}V\Psi'(P)$. A semigroup with this property is
determined by its values on the dense set of the $\Psi'(P)$, which gives
the claim on the Hamiltonian of a limit. If $D_{\lambda,c}S=S$, the two
constructions coincide, $\sigma(H_0)=\lambda^{-1}\sigma(H_0)$, and the
least point $m(S)$ of $\sigma(H_0)\setminus\{0\}$ satisfies $m(S)=m(S)/
\lambda$; so $\lambda=1$, and $c^nS_n=S_n$ with $S_n\ne0$ for some $n\ge2$
by Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(vi) gives $c=1$.
\end{proof}

The normalisation of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(ii) fixes the size
of the field, not the scale: assigning the states to the spacings with a
shift produces a dilated limit with the same normalisation.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEscale}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}}, and
suppose that the moments $\omega_k(\prod_jZ_kA_k(f_j))$ converge for every
separated tuple to a nontrivial family $S$. Let $j\ge1$, and let
$\omega''_k=\omega_k$ for even $k$ and $\omega''_k=\omega_{k+j}$ for odd
$k$, with the packets $A''_k$ formed from $\omega''_k$ at the spacing $a_k$.

\textup{(i)} $(\omega''_k)$ satisfies the hypothesis of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}} with $2^j\ell$ in place of $\ell$.

\textup{(ii)} For every choice of $Z''_k>0$ for which \textup{(M$_{\mathrm
s}$)} holds along $(\omega''_k)$, the moments $\omega''_k(\prod_jZ''_k
A''_k(f_j))$ either fail to converge for some separated tuple or converge
to the trivial family.

\textup{(iii)} If $C''_k(0)>0$ for large $k$ and \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)}
holds along $(\omega''_k)$ for $Z''_k=C''_k(\sigma_0)^{-1/2}$, then
\textup{(U$_{\mathrm s}$)} fails for it.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) For odd $k$, $\omega_{k+j}$ satisfies $\mathrm{BP}(n_{k+j},\varpi)$ and
$n_{k+j}a_k=2^jn_{k+j}a_{k+j}\le2^j\ell$.

(ii) For odd $k$, $a_k=2^ja_{k+j}$ gives $A''_k(f)=2^{4j}A_{k+j}(f(2^j\,
\cdot))$. Write $Z''_k=\zeta_kZ_k$ for even $k$ and $Z''_k=\zeta_k2^{-4j}
Z_{k+j}$ for odd $k$. The even moments are $\zeta_k^n$ times moments that
converge to $S_n(f_1,\dots,f_n)$, and the odd ones $\zeta_k^n$ times
moments that converge to $(D_{2^j,1}S)_n(f_1,\dots,f_n)$. Both families are
nontrivial, so (M$_{\mathrm s}$) bounds $\zeta_k$. Along a subsequence
$\zeta_k\to\zeta_e$ on the even and $\zeta_k\to\zeta_o$ on the odd $k$, and
the two limits are $D_{1,\zeta_e}S$ and $D_{2^j,\zeta_o}S$. If they
coincide and $\zeta_e\zeta_o\ne0$, then $D_{2^j,\zeta_o/\zeta_e}S=S$,
contrary to Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEdil}; if exactly one of $\zeta_e,\zeta_o$
vanishes, one limit is trivial and the other not. So a common limit has
$\zeta_e=\zeta_o=0$ and is trivial.

(iii) By Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(ii) every limit is nontrivial,
so by (ii) some separated moment does not converge, and by
Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEexact} (U$_{\mathrm s}$) fails.
\end{proof}

The scale is fixed by a mass. With $f_0$, $\sigma_0$, $C_k$ as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm} and $\gamma_k$ the norm of $\mathbb T_k$
on $\Omega_k^\perp$, consider for $m_0>0$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(MC$_{m_0}$)},leftmargin=*]
\item $\liminf_k a_k^{-1}\log(1/\gamma_k)\ge m_0$, and there are $c_0>0$
and $k_1$ with $C_k(t)\ge c_0e^{-m_0t}C_k(\sigma_0)$ for $k\ge k_1$ and
$t\in a_k\Z$, $t\ge\sigma_0$;
\end{enumerate}
and the statement that the limits differ at most by the scale and the size
of the field:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(O$_{\mathrm s}$)},leftmargin=*]
\item for any two limits $S,S'$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep} along
subsequences there are $\lambda,c>0$ with $S'=D_{\lambda,c}S$.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:limitEunique}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}},
$C_k(0)>0$ for large $k$, $Z_k=C_k(\sigma_0)^{-1/2}$ and
\textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)}.

\textup{(i)} Every limit $S$ of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}}
satisfies
\[
\ip{\Psi_0(f_0)}{e^{-\sigma_0H_0}\Psi_0(f_0)}=1,
\]
and under \textup{(MC$_{m_0}$)} it has $m(S)=m_0$.

\textup{(ii)} Under \textup{(MC$_{m_0}$)} and \textup{(O$_{\mathrm s}$)},
\textup{(U$_{\mathrm s}$)} holds and the moments converge for every
separated tuple.

\textup{(iii)} For the Gaussian field of
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a)} at mass $m$,
\textup{(MC$_m$)} holds; for the same field at two masses $m\ne m'$,
interleaved, \textup{(O$_{\mathrm s}$)} holds and \textup{(MC$_{m_0}$)}
fails for every $m_0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) The first claim and nontriviality are
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(ii). The proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(iv) with $\gamma_k^{t/a_k}$ in place of
$e^{-m_*t}$ gives $\ip v{e^{-tH_0}v}\le\limsup_k\gamma_k^{t/a_k}\|v\|^2\le
e^{-m_0t}\|v\|^2$ on a dense set of $v\in\Omega_0^\perp$, so $m(S)\ge m_0$.
By the approximation of $\theta_{0,k}$ by $\theta_0$, as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(ii), $\ip{\Psi_0(f_0)}{e^{-tH_0}
\Psi_0(f_0)}=\lim_kZ_k^2C_k(t)\ge c_0e^{-m_0t}$ for dyadic $t\ge\sigma_0$.
Since $\Psi_0(f_0)\perp\Omega_0$, its spectral measure for $H_0$ is carried
by $[m(S),\infty)$, and the left side is at most $e^{-m(S)t}\|\Psi_0(f_0)
\|^2$; letting $t\to\infty$ gives $m(S)\le m_0$.

(ii) Let $S,S'$ be two limits and $S'=D_{\lambda,c}S$. By (i) and
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEdil}, $m_0=m(S')=m(S)/\lambda=m_0/\lambda$, so
$\lambda=1$; then $S'_2=c^2S_2$, and the normalisation in (i) gives $c=1$.
Convergence follows as in the proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEexact}.

(iii) The transfer matrix has $\gamma_k=q_k=e^{-ma_k}$. The packet
$\vartheta(\overline{A_k(f_0)})$ involves sites at negative times and
$\tau_{t/a_k}A_k(f_0)$ sites at positive times, so the time factor of the
covariance is $q_k^{y_0-x_0}$ and $C_k(t)=e^{-mt}C_k(0)$; this is
(MC$_m$). At two masses the limits are the two Gaussian families with
kernels $\kappa e^{-m\|z\|_1}$ and $\kappa'e^{-m'\|z\|_1}$, and
$D_{\lambda,c}$ with $\lambda=m/m'$ and $c^2=\kappa'\lambda^8/\kappa$ maps
the first to the second, Gaussian families being determined by $S_2$.
The first part of (MC$_{m_0}$) needs $m_0\le\min(m,m')$, and the second,
since $C_k(t)/C_k(\sigma_0)=e^{-m_k(t-\sigma_0)}$ with $m_k\in\{m,m'\}$,
needs $m_0\ge\max(m,m')$.
\end{proof}

(O$_{\mathrm s}$) is the form, for the separated moments, of the statement
that the continuum theory has no free dimensionless parameter; it is not
proved here, nor is (MC$_{m_0}$) for the Wilson measure along any tuning.
The second part of (MC$_{m_0}$) asks that the reflected correlation of
$f_0$ decay no faster than at the rate $m_0$, which holds when $f_0$
couples to the lowest state; together with the first part it fixes the
mass of every limit. A condition fixing the ratio $C_k(\sigma_0)/C_k(0)$,
the analogue of (RC), fixes the scale within a set of limits related by
dilations only where the corresponding ratio of the limits is injective
along that set, which is not proved.

\emph{The pointwise bound on an axis.} Reflection positivity in the
coordinate directions bounds the separated two-point moments at every point
by their values on a coordinate axis, where they form a completely monotone
function of the distance. For $n=2$ write $g_k(s)=M_k(0,se_0)$ for
$s\in\Z$, and $\lfloor t\rceil=\lfloor t+\frac12\rfloor$. Let $h\in\{1,-1\}$
be such that $\vartheta x=(h-x_0,\mathbf x)$ for $x=(x_0,\mathbf x)\in\Z^4$;
the plane fixed by $\vartheta$ is $\{x_0=h/2\}$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEaxial}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}}, and let
$\gamma_k$ be the norm of $\mathbb T_k$ on $\Omega_k^\perp$.

\textup{(i)} For integers $s\ge2r+3$ and $j\ge0$, $0\le g_k(s+j)\le
\gamma_k^jg_k(s)$, and for $s\ge4r+6$
\[
0\le g_k(s)-g_k(s+j)\le\frac{4j}s\,g_k\bigl(\lceil s/2\rceil\bigr) .
\]

\textup{(ii)} For $y\in\Z^4$ with $|y|_\infty\ge2r+4$, $|M_k(0,y)|\le
g_k(|y|_\infty-1)$.

\textup{(iii)} \textup{(SB$_2$)} holds if and only if $\limsup_kZ_k^2
g_k(\lceil\delta/a_k\rceil)<\infty$ for every $\delta>0$. In that case, for
every $\delta>0$ and with $b(\delta)=\limsup_kZ_k^2g_k(\lceil\delta/(4a_k)
\rceil)$,
\[
Z_k^2|M_k(0,y)|\le\bigl(b(\delta)+1\bigr)e^{-m_*(a_k|y|_\infty-\delta/2)}
\]
for all large $k$ and all $y\in\Z^4$ with $|y|\ge\delta/a_k$; in particular
$b_2(\delta,\Lambda)\le b(\delta)+1$ for every $\Lambda>\delta$.

\textup{(iv)} Under \textup{(SB$_2$)}, \textup{(RI$_2$)} holds if and only
if for $0<\delta<\Lambda$
\[
\lim_k\sup_yZ_k^2\bigl|M_k(0,y)-g_k(\lfloor|y|\rceil)\bigr|=0,
\]
the supremum over the $y\in\Z^4$ with $\delta/a_k\le|y|\le\Lambda/a_k$.

\textup{(v)} Under \textup{(SB$_2$)}, every limit of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}} has $S_2(f,g)=\iint f(x)g(y)K(x-y)\,dx
\,dy$ for the separated pairs, with $K$ measurable on $\R^4\setminus\{0\}$,
$K(-z)=K(z)$, $K\circ g=K$ almost everywhere for $g\in B_4$, and
$|K(z)|\le(b(\delta)+1)e^{-m_*(|z|_\infty-\delta/2)}$ for almost every $z$
with $|z|\ge2\delta$, for every $\delta>0$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For $j\ge r+1$ let $F_j=\Phi_{je_0}-c_k$, a real function of the links with
both endpoints in $\{x_0\ge1\}$. Since $\vartheta\Phi_x=\Phi_{\vartheta x}$,
$\vartheta F_j=\Phi_{(h-j)e_0}-c_k$, and by translation invariance
\[
\omega_k\bigl(\vartheta(F_j)\tau_tF_{j'}\bigr)=M_k\bigl((h-j)e_0,(j'+t)e_0
\bigr)=g_k(j+j'+t-h)
\]
for integers $t\ge0$. In particular $N_0(F_j)^2=g_k(2j-h)$.

(i) By the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(iii), with $s_0=2r+2-h$
and $\Psi=\Psi_{F_{r+1}}$, $g_k(s_0+t)=\ip\Psi{\mathbb T_k^t\Psi}$ for
integers $t\ge0$, and $\ip{\Omega_k}\Psi=\omega_k(F_{r+1})=0$. Since
$\mathbb T_k$ is self-adjoint, fixes $\Omega_k$ and has spectrum on
$\Omega_k^\perp$ in $[0,\gamma_k]$, the spectral measure $\nu$ of
$\mathbb T_k$ at $\Psi$ is a positive measure on $[0,\gamma_k]$, and
$g_k(s_0+t)=\int\lambda^t\,d\nu(\lambda)$, $0^0=1$. As $s_0\le2r+3$, this
gives $g_k(s)\ge0$ and $g_k(s+j)\le\gamma_k^jg_k(s)$ for $s\ge2r+3$. Let
$s\ge4r+6$ and $u=s-s_0$. From $1-\lambda^j\le j(1-\lambda)$ and $\max_{0\le
x\le1}x^p(1-x)=p^p(p+1)^{-p-1}\le(p+1)^{-1}$, applied with $p=\lfloor u/2
\rfloor$ to $\lambda^u(1-\lambda)=\lambda^{\lceil u/2\rceil}\lambda^p(1-
\lambda)$,
\[
\begin{split}
g_k(s)-g_k(s+j)&=\int\lambda^u(1-\lambda^j)\,d\nu\le\frac j{p+1}
\int\lambda^{\lceil u/2\rceil}d\nu\\
&=\frac{j\,g_k(s_0+\lceil u/2\rceil)}{\lfloor u/2\rfloor+1} .
\end{split}
\]
Here $s_0+\lceil u/2\rceil=\lceil(s+s_0)/2\rceil\ge\lceil s/2\rceil\ge2r+3$,
so the numerator is at most $j\,g_k(\lceil s/2\rceil)$, and $\lfloor u/2
\rfloor+1\ge(s-s_0+1)/2\ge(s-2r-2)/2\ge s/4$.

(ii) Let $s=|y|_\infty$. Choose $g\in B_4$, a permutation of the axes
followed by a sign change, with $(gy)_0=s$; then $M_k(0,y)=M_k(0,gy)$, since
$\omega_k$ is invariant under $B_4$ and $g\Phi_x=\Phi_{gx}$. Write
$gy=se_0+v$ with $v_0=0$, and let $j=\lfloor(s+h)/2\rfloor$ and $j'=s+h-j$,
both at least $\lfloor(s-1)/2\rfloor\ge r+1$. By translation invariance
\[
M_k(0,gy)=M_k\bigl((h-j)e_0,j'e_0+v\bigr)=\omega_k\bigl(\vartheta(F_j)
\tau_vF_{j'}\bigr),
\]
and $\tau_vF_{j'}$ is again a function of the links in $\{x_0\ge1\}$. The
Cauchy--Schwarz inequality for the positive form of
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(iii) and $N_0(\tau_vF_{j'})=N_0(F_{j'})$ give
$|M_k(0,y)|\le(g_k(2j-h)g_k(2j'-h))^{1/2}$. If $s+h$ is even, $2j-h=2j'-h=
s$; otherwise the two arguments are $s-1$ and $s+1$. By (i) both products
are at most $g_k(s-1)^2$.

(iii) Let $a_k\le\delta$. The pair $(0,\lceil\delta/a_k\rceil e_0)$ lies in
$\mathcal W_k(\delta,2\delta)$, and (SB$_2$) gives
\[
\limsup_kZ_k^2g_k(\lceil\delta/a_k\rceil)\le b_2(\delta,2\delta).
\] Conversely let $\delta>0$,
$s_1=\lceil\delta/(4a_k)\rceil$ and $|y|\ge\delta/a_k$. Then $|y|_\infty\ge
|y|/2\ge\delta/(2a_k)$, which for large $k$ is at least $2r+4$ and at least
$s_1+1$. By (ii) and (i), and $\gamma_k\le e^{-m_*a_k}$,
\[
|M_k(0,y)|\le g_k(|y|_\infty-1)\le e^{-m_*a_k(|y|_\infty-1-s_1)}g_k(s_1),
\]
and $a_k(1+s_1)\le2a_k+\delta/4\le\delta/2$ for $a_k\le\delta/8$. Since
$Z_k^2g_k(s_1)\le b(\delta)+1$ for large $k$, this is the bound, and
$a_k|y|_\infty\ge\delta/2$ gives the last claim.

(iv) Suppose (RI$_2$), and let $\delta/a_k\le|y|\le\Lambda/a_k$. The point
$y'=\lfloor|y|\rceil e_0$ has $||y|-|y'||\le\frac12$, both pairs $(0,y)$,
$(0,y')$ lie in $\mathcal W_k(\delta/2,2\Lambda)$ for large $k$, and
$M_k(0,y')=g_k(\lfloor|y|\rceil)$; so (RI$_2$) with $(\delta/2,2\Lambda)$
gives the condition. Conversely, let $x,x'\in\mathcal W_k(\delta,\Lambda)$
with $||x_1-x_2|-|x'_1-x'_2||\le4$, $y=x_2-x_1$, $y'=x'_2-x'_1$,
$s=\lfloor|y|\rceil$ and $s'=\lfloor|y'|\rceil$. By translation invariance
$M_k(x)=M_k(0,y)$ and $M_k(x')=M_k(0,y')$, and
\[
\begin{split}
Z_k^2|M_k(x)-M_k(x')|&\le Z_k^2|M_k(0,y)-g_k(s)|\\
&\quad+Z_k^2|g_k(s)-g_k(s')|\\
&\quad+Z_k^2|M_k(0,y')-g_k(s')| .
\end{split}
\]
The first and last terms tend to $0$ uniformly by the condition. Since
$|s-s'|\le5$ and $\min(s,s')\ge\delta/(2a_k)$ for large $k$, (i) bounds the
middle term by $40a_k\delta^{-1}Z_k^2g_k(\lceil\delta/(4a_k)\rceil)$, which
tends to $0$ by (iii).

(v) Let $S$ be a limit along a subsequence, $f,g$ separated, supported in a
cube $Q$ of side $L$ at $\ell^\infty$ distance $\eta>0$, and $\Theta(z)=\int
f(u)g(u+z)\,du$, continuous and supported in $\{|z|_\infty\ge\eta\}\cap
[-L,L]^4$. With $\varrho_k$ as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitErot}
put $K_k(z)=Z_k^2M_k(0,\varrho_k(z))$. By translation invariance,
\[
\begin{split}
&Z_k^2\omega_k\bigl(A_k(f)A_k(g)\bigr)-\int K_k\Theta\,dz=\sum_wZ_k^2
M_k(0,w)\\
&\quad\times\Bigl(a_k^8\sum_xf(a_kx)g(a_kx+a_kw)-\int_{\varrho_k^{-1}(w)}
\Theta\,dz\Bigr) .
\end{split}
\]
The bracket vanishes unless $|w|_\infty\ge\eta/a_k-2$ and $|w|_\infty\le
L/a_k+2$, and it is at most $a_k^4\epsilon_k$, where $\epsilon_k\to0$ by
the uniform continuity of $f$, $g$ and $\Theta$, the inner sum being a
Riemann sum for $\Theta(a_kw)$ with error uniform in $w$. At most
$(2L/a_k+5)^4$ vectors $w$ contribute, and by (iii) with $\delta=\eta/2$
each has $Z_k^2|M_k(0,w)|\le b(\eta/2)+1$ for large $k$. So the difference
tends to $0$. By (iii), $K_k$ is bounded on every set $\{|z|\ge\delta\}$,
$\delta>0$, for large $k$, and a further subsequence converges weakly-$*$
in $L^\infty(\{|z|\ge\delta\})$ for every $\delta=1/i$ to some $K$. Since
$\Theta$ is integrable and supported in $\{|z|\ge\eta\}$, $\int K_k\Theta\,dz
\to\int K\Theta\,dz$, which is the representation. For $|z|\ge2\delta$ and
large $k$, $|\varrho_k(z)|\ge|z|/a_k-1\ge\delta/a_k$ and $a_k|\varrho_k(z)|
_\infty\ge|z|_\infty-a_k$, so (iii) gives $|K_k(z)|\le(b(\delta)+1)
e^{-m_*(|z|_\infty-a_k-\delta/2)}$, a bound that passes to the weak-$*$ limit.
Finally $M_k(0,-w)=M_k(0,w)$ and $M_k(0,gw)=M_k(0,w)$ for $g\in B_4$, while
$\varrho_k(-z)=-\varrho_k(z)$ and $\varrho_k(gz)=g\varrho_k(z)$ off a null
set; so $K_k$ and $K$ are even and $B_4$-invariant almost everywhere.
\end{proof}

So (SB$_2$) is a bound on one function of one variable, the renormalised
two-point moment on a coordinate axis, and (RI$_2$) asks that the
renormalised moment at a point be close to its value on the axis at the
same Euclidean length. Under (SB$_2$) the kernel of (v) is square
integrable on every compact subset of $\R^4\setminus\{0\}$, so
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEaniso} applies to it, and (W$_{\mathrm s}$) for
$n=2$ is the radial symmetry of $K$. Neither part follows from the other
properties. For (RI$_2$) this is Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEproduct}, whose
fields satisfy (SB$_n$) for every $n$. For (SB$_2$) it is part (b) of the
following proposition, whose fields satisfy the separated moment bound with
constants independent of the separation, the mass condition and
(U$_{\mathrm s}$), and concentrate the two-point function on the coordinate
axes, where the bound of (M$_{\mathrm s}$) on averages over regions of
physical size does not control the pointwise values.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEgauss}
Let $m>0$ and $q_k=e^{-ma_k}$.

\textup{(a)} Let $J$ be a finite set, $q_{j,\mu}\in[0,1)$ for $j\in J$ and
$\mu=0,\dots,3$, and let $\psi=(\psi_j(x))_{j\in J,x\in\Z^4}$ be the centred
Gaussian field with $\E\psi_j(x)\psi_{j'}(y)=\delta_{jj'}\prod_\mu
q_{j,\mu}^{|x_\mu-y_\mu|}$, $0^0=1$. Its law is invariant under translations
and reflection positive for the reflections in coordinate hyperplanes
through sites and bisecting links, and in the direction $\mu$ its transfer
matrix $\mathbb T$ has $0\le\mathbb T\le1$, $\mathbb T\Omega=\Omega$ and norm
at most $\max_jq_{j,\mu}$ on $\Omega^\perp$.

\textup{(b)} Let $J=\{0,1,2,3\}$, $q_{j,\mu}=q_k$ for $j=\mu$ and $0$
otherwise, $\Phi_x=\sum_j\psi_j(x)$, $A_k(f)=\sum_xa_k^4f(a_kx)\Phi_x$,
$f_0\ge0$ not zero, $C_k$ formed from $f_0$, and $Z_k=C_k(\sigma_0)^{-1/2}$.
The law is invariant under $B_4$, acting by $\psi_j(x)\mapsto\psi_{\bar gj}
(gx)$ with $\bar g$ the permutation of the axes underlying $g$, so that
$g\Phi_x=\Phi_{gx}$. Moreover $C_k(t)=e^{-mt}C_k(0)$ for $t\in a_k\Z_{\ge0}$
and $a_k^{-3}C_k(0)\to\mathcal J=\int_{\R^3}(\int_\R f_0(s,\mathbf x)
e^{-ms}ds)^2d\mathbf x>0$. There is $B<\infty$ such that for large $k$,
every $n$ and all $f_j\in C_c(\R^4)$ supported in a cube of side $L\ge a_k$,
\[
\Bigl|\omega_k\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^nZ_kA_k(f_j)\Bigr)\Bigr|\le(n-1)!!\,
\bigl(B(2L)^4\bigr)^{n/2}\prod_{j=1}^n\|f_j\|_\infty
\]
for even $n$, the moments vanishing for odd $n$; so \textup{(M$_{\mathrm
s}$)} holds with constants independent of $\delta$, and the growth
condition \textup{(G$_{\mathrm s}$)} posed below holds with $p=0$. The
moments converge along the whole sequence to the Gaussian family with
\[
S_2(f,g)=\frac{e^{m\sigma_0}}{\mathcal J}\sum_{\mu=0}^3\int_{\R^4}\int_\R
f(x)g(x+ue_\mu)e^{-m|u|}\,du\,dx ,
\]
so \textup{(U$_{\mathrm s}$)} holds, and \textup{(MC$_m$)} holds. Further
\textup{(SB$_2$)} and \textup{(RI$_2$)} fail, $G_S=B_4$, and there is no
locally integrable $K$ on $\R^4\setminus\{0\}$ with $S_2(f,g)=\iint f(x)g(y)
K(x-y)\,dx\,dy$ for all separated pairs.

\textup{(c)} Let $m'>m$, $q'_k=e^{-m'a_k}$, and let $\omega_k$ be the law of
the field $\Phi=\psi_1$ of \textup{(a)} with $J=\{1\}$, $q_{1,\mu}=q_k$, for
even $k$, and of $\Phi=\psi_1+\psi_2$ with $J=\{1,2\}$, $q_{1,\mu}=q_k$,
$q_{2,\mu}=q'_k$, for odd $k$; let $f_0\ge0$ be not zero and $Z_k=C_k(
\sigma_0)^{-1/2}$. The laws are invariant under translations and $B_4$,
reflection positive as in \textup{(a)}, with transfer matrices of norm at
most $q_k$ on $\Omega_k^\perp$. \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)} and
\textup{(MC$_m$)} hold along the whole sequence. The moments converge along
the even and along the odd $k$ to Gaussian families $S$ and $S'$ with
continuous kernels $\kappa e^{-m\|z\|_1}$ and $\kappa'(e^{-m\|z\|_1}+
e^{-m'\|z\|_1})$, $\kappa,\kappa'>0$, and $S'\ne D_{\lambda,c}S$ for all
$\lambda,c>0$. So \textup{(O$_{\mathrm s}$)} and \textup{(U$_{\mathrm s}$)}
fail.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a) Fix $\mu=0$ as time; the other directions are the same. The field at
time $0$ has a Gaussian law $\gamma$ with covariance $C=\delta_{jj'}
\prod_{i\ge1}q_{j,i}^{|x_i-y_i|}$, positive definite as a product of the
one-dimensional covariances $q^{|n|}$, $0\le q<1$, whose Fourier transforms
$(1-q^2)/(1-2q\cos p+q^2)$ are positive. Let $(Qh)_j(\mathbf x)=q_{j,0}
h_j(\mathbf x)$ on the finitely supported $h$; $Q$ commutes with $C$, so the
stationary chain $\psi_{t+1}=Q\psi_t+(1-Q^2)^{1/2}\xi_{t+1}$, with $\xi_t$
independent of law $\gamma$, has covariance $Q^{|t-s|}C$, which is that of
the field. As in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a), its
transition operator $P$ satisfies $P\mathcal E_h=\mathcal E_{Qh}$, so on the
$N$-th Wiener chaos, the closure of the parts of the $\mathcal E_h$
homogeneous of degree $N$ in $h$, it acts as the $N$-th tensor power of $Q$
on the symmetric tensors over the space of the $h$ with the inner product
$\ip h{Ch'}$. $Q$ is self-adjoint there with $0\le Q\le q_{\max}=\max_j
q_{j,0}$, so $0\le P\le1$, $P$ fixes the constants and has norm at most
$q_{\max}^N\le q_{\max}$ on the chaos of order $N\ge1$. The rest of that
proof gives reflection positivity for both kinds of planes and identifies
the transfer matrix with $P$.

(b) Here $\E\Phi_x\Phi_y=w_k(y-x)$ with $w_k(z)=q_k^{|z_\mu|}$ if $z\ne0$
is a multiple of $e_\mu$, $w_k(0)=4$, and $w_k(z)=0$ otherwise. Since $(gz)_{\bar gj}=\pm z_j$, the covariance of the
transformed field is that of $\psi$, which gives the $B_4$ invariance;
$\omega_k(\Phi_0)=0$, so $c_k=0$. For $f_0(a_kx)f_0(a_ky)\ne0$ the time
coordinate of $y+(t/a_k)e_0-\vartheta x$ is $x_0+y_0-h+t/a_k\ge1$, so only
the term $\mu=0$ contributes, with $\mathbf x=\mathbf y$, and
\[
C_k(t)=e^{-mt}e^{mha_k}a_k^3\cdot a_k^3\sum_{\mathbf x}\Bigl(a_k
\sum_{x_0}f_0(a_kx)e^{-ma_kx_0}\Bigr)^2 .
\]
The last factor is a Riemann sum converging to $\mathcal J$, which is
positive because $f_0\ge0$ is not zero. For the pairs,
\[
\begin{split}
&\omega_k\bigl(A_k(f)A_k(g)\bigr)\\
&\quad=a_k^3\sum_\mu a_k^5\sum_{x\in\Z^4,\,s\in\Z}f(a_kx)g(a_kx+a_kse_\mu)
e^{-ma_k|s|} .
\end{split}
\]
The inner sums are Riemann sums converging to $\int\!\int f(x)g(x+ue_\mu)
e^{-m|u|}du\,dx$, and since at most $(L+a_k)^4/a_k^4\le(2L)^4/a_k^4$ sites
have $a_kx$ in the cube and $a_k\sum_se^{-ma_k|s|}=a_k(1+q_k)/(1-q_k)\le4/m$
for $ma_k\le1$, they are at most $(4/m)(2L)^4\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty$.
With $Z_k^2a_k^3=e^{m\sigma_0}a_k^3/C_k(0)\to e^{m\sigma_0}/\mathcal J$ this
gives the bound for $n=2$ with $B=32e^{m\sigma_0}/(m\mathcal J)$ and the
limit of $S_2$, and Wick's formula, a sum over the $(n-1)!!$ pairings,
gives the bound for all $n$ and the convergence of all moments. Since
$(n-1)!!\le(n!)^{1/2}$ and $(B(2L)^4)^{n/2}\le(4B^{1/2})^n(1+L)^{2n}$, the
bound has the form of (G$_{\mathrm s}$) with $\beta=\frac12$, $q=2$ and
$p=0$. By (a) the transfer matrix has norm at most $q_k$ on $\Omega_k^\perp$,
so $\liminf_ka_k^{-1}\log(1/\gamma_k)\ge m$, and $C_k(t)=e^{-m(t-\sigma_0)}
C_k(\sigma_0)$; this is (MC$_m$) with $c_0=e^{m\sigma_0}$.

For $s\ge1$, $g_k(s)=w_k(se_0)=q_k^s$, so $Z_k^2g_k(\lceil\delta/a_k\rceil)
