\ge(e^{m\sigma_0}/(2\mathcal J))a_k^{-3}e^{-m(\delta+a_k)}\to\infty$; as
the pair $(0,\lceil\delta/a_k\rceil e_0)$ lies in $\mathcal W_k(\delta,
2\delta)$ for large $k$, (SB$_2$) fails. With $N=\lceil
\delta/a_k\rceil+1$, the points $y=Ne_0$ and $y'=(N-1)e_0+e_1$ have
$N-1\le|y'|\le|y|=N$, both pairs $(0,y)$, $(0,y')$ lie in $\mathcal W_k(
\delta,2\delta)$ for large $k$, $M_k(0,y')=w_k(y')=0$ and $Z_k^2M_k(0,y)=
Z_k^2q_k^N\to\infty$; so (RI$_2$) fails.

Let $T(\varphi)=\frac{e^{m\sigma_0}}{\mathcal J}\sum_\mu\int\varphi(ue_\mu)
e^{-m|u|}du$ for $\varphi\in C_c(\R^4\setminus\{0\})$; then $S_2(f,g)=
T(\Theta)$ with $\Theta(z)=\int f(u)g(u+z)\,du$. If $S_2$ had a locally
integrable kernel $K$, the argument of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEaniso}(i)
would give $T(\varphi)=\int K\varphi\,dz$ for all $\varphi$, $T$ being
even. Take
$\varphi_\varepsilon$ with values in $[0,1]$, equal to $1$ on the segment
$\{ue_0:1\le u\le2\}$ and supported in its $\varepsilon$-neighbourhood
$N_\varepsilon$; then $T(\varphi_\varepsilon)\ge\frac{e^{m\sigma_0}}
{\mathcal J}\int_1^2e^{-mu}du>0$, while $\int|K|\varphi_\varepsilon\le
\int_{N_\varepsilon}|K|\to0$ as $\varepsilon\to0$, since $N_\varepsilon$
decreases to a null set. Let $R\in G_S$ and suppose $R^{-1}e_0$ is not one
of the $\pm e_\nu$; then it has positive distance $3\varepsilon$ from the
union $A$ of the coordinate axes. Take $f,g\ge0$ supported in the balls of
radius $\varepsilon$ about $0$ and $R^{-1}e_0$, with $f(0)>0$ and
$g(R^{-1}e_0)>0$. Then $\Theta$ is supported in the ball of radius
$2\varepsilon$ about $R^{-1}e_0$, which misses $A$, so $S_2(f,g)=0$, while
$S_2(f\circ R^{-1},g\circ R^{-1})=T(\Theta\circ R^{-1})\ge\frac{e^{m
\sigma_0}}{\mathcal J}\int\Theta(R^{-1}ue_0)e^{-m|u|}du>0$, because
$\Theta(R^{-1}e_0)>0$. So $R^{-1}e_0\in\{\pm e_\nu\}$, and applying this to
$\pi_\mu R\in G_S$ gives $R^{-1}e_\mu\in\{\pm e_\nu\}$ for every $\mu$, that
is, $R\in B_4$.

(c) The symmetries, positivity and the norm of the transfer matrices follow
from (a), the covariances $\prod_\mu q^{|z_\mu|}$ being invariant under
$B_4$. Let $C^{(i)}_k$ be formed with the covariance $\prod_\mu
q_i^{|z_\mu|}$, $q_1=q_k$, $q_2=q'_k$. As in (b), the time factor of the
covariance between the two packets of $C^{(i)}_k(t)$ is $q_i^{t/a_k}$ times
that at $t=0$, so $C^{(1)}_k(t)=e^{-mt}C^{(1)}_k(0)$, $C^{(2)}_k(t)=
e^{-m't}C^{(2)}_k(0)$, and $C^{(i)}_k(0)$ converges to
$\mathcal J_1=\iint f_0(x)f_0(y)e^{-m\|\theta_0x-y\|_1}dx\,dy>0$ and
$\mathcal J_2$, defined with $m'$ in place of $m$, by Riemann sums. For even $k$, $C_k=C^{(1)}_k$; for odd $k$,
$C_k=C^{(1)}_k+C^{(2)}_k$, and for $t\ge\sigma_0$
\[
\begin{split}
\frac{C_k(t)}{C_k(\sigma_0)}&\ge\frac{e^{-mt}C^{(1)}_k(0)}{e^{-m\sigma_0}
(C^{(1)}_k(0)+C^{(2)}_k(0))}\\
&\ge\frac{\mathcal J_1}{2(\mathcal J_1+\mathcal J_2)}e^{-m(t-\sigma_0)}
\end{split}
\]
for large $k$. With the norm bound $q_k$ this is (MC$_m$) with $c_0=
e^{m\sigma_0}\mathcal J_1/(2(\mathcal J_1+\mathcal J_2))$. As in the proof
of Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a), the moments converge along the
even and along the odd $k$ to the Gaussian families with the stated
kernels, $\kappa=e^{m\sigma_0}/\mathcal J_1$, $\kappa'=(e^{-m\sigma_0}
\mathcal J_1+e^{-m'\sigma_0}\mathcal J_2)^{-1}$, and the moments of
packets supported in a cube of side $L$ are bounded by $(n-1)!!$ times
$((2L)^4\sup_kZ_k^2a_k^4\sum_z(q_k^{|z|_1}+q_k'^{|z|_1}))^{n/2}\prod_j\|f_j
\|_\infty$, where $a_k^4\sum_zq_k^{|z|_1}=(a_k(1+q_k)/(1-q_k))^4$ is bounded;
this gives (M$_{\mathrm s}$). The kernel of $D_{\lambda,c}S$ is $c^2
\lambda^{-8}\kappa e^{-(m/\lambda)\|z\|_1}$, by the substitution $u=\lambda
x$, $v=\lambda y$. If $S'=D_{\lambda,c}S$, then the two continuous kernels
coincide, since by Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEaniso}(i) a continuous kernel
is determined by $S_2$; at $z=te_0$, after multiplication by $e^{mt}$,
\[
\kappa'\bigl(1+e^{-(m'-m)t}\bigr)=c^2\lambda^{-8}\kappa\,e^{(m-m/\lambda)t}
\]
for all $t>0$. The left side tends to $\kappa'>0$ as $t\to\infty$, which
forces $\lambda=1$, and then the right side is constant while the left side
is strictly decreasing.
\end{proof}

\emph{The mass.} The gap of Theorem~\ref{thm:avgtransfer} gives the first
half of (MC$_{m_0}$) with $m_0=m_*$, and reflection positivity with
(M$_{\mathrm s}$) gives the second half, but with a rate that is in general
larger.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEbracket}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}},
$C_k(0)>0$ for large $k$, $Z_k=C_k(\sigma_0)^{-1/2}$ and
\textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)}. Let $Q$ be a cube that contains
$\operatorname{supp}f_0$ and $\theta_{0,k}(\operatorname{supp}f_0)$ for all
$k$, and $m_1=\sigma_0^{-1}\log(M_2(Q,t_0)\|f_0\|_\infty^2)$.

\textup{(i)} $m_1\ge m_*$; $\liminf_ka_k^{-1}\log(1/\gamma_k)\ge m_*$; and
for large $k$ and $t\in a_k\Z$ with $t\ge\sigma_0$,
\[
C_k(t)\ge e^{-m_1(t-\sigma_0)}C_k(\sigma_0) .
\]

\textup{(ii)} Every limit $S$ of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}} has
$m_*\le m(S)\le m_1$. If \textup{(MC$_{m_0}$)} holds, then $m_*\le m_0\le
m_1$.

\textup{(iii)} If $S$ and $S'$ are limits of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}} with $S'=D_{\lambda,c}S$, then
$\lambda=m(S)/m(S')$ lies in $[m_*/m_1,m_1/m_*]$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) By Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(i),(ii), $(M_2(Q,t_0)\|f_0\|_\infty^2
)^{-1}\le C_k(\sigma_0)/C_k(0)\le e^{-m_*\sigma_0}$ for large $k$, which gives
$m_1\ge m_*$ and $C_k(\sigma_0)/C_k(0)\ge e^{-m_1\sigma_0}$. The second claim
is $\gamma_k\le e^{-m_*a_k}$. By the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(i), $C_k(t)=\int\lambda^{t/a_k}d\nu(
\lambda)$ with a positive measure $\nu$, so by H\"older's inequality, with
$i=t/a_k$ and $p=\sigma_0/a_k$, $C_k(\sigma_0)^i\le C_k(0)^{i-p}C_k(t)^p$.
Hence
\[
C_k(t)\ge C_k(\sigma_0)\Bigl(\frac{C_k(\sigma_0)}{C_k(0)}\Bigr)^{(t-\sigma_0)/
\sigma_0}\ge e^{-m_1(t-\sigma_0)}C_k(\sigma_0) .
\]

(ii) $m(S)\ge m_*$ is Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(iv). As in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEunique}(i), (i) gives $\ip{\Psi_0(f_0)}{e^{-tH_0}
\Psi_0(f_0)}=\lim_kZ_k^2C_k(t)\ge e^{-m_1(t-\sigma_0)}$ for dyadic
$t\ge\sigma_0$, while the left side is at most $e^{-m(S)t}\|\Psi_0(f_0)
\|^2$; so $m(S)\le m_1$. Under (MC$_{m_0}$), $m(S)=m_0$ by
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEunique}(i).

(iii) By Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEdil}, $m(S')=m(S)/\lambda$, and (ii)
applies to both.
\end{proof}

So the two halves of (MC$_{m_0}$) hold separately, with the rates $m_*$
and $m_1$, and (MC$_{m_0}$) asks that they coincide. Since $C_k(t)\le
\gamma_k^{(t-\sigma_0)/a_k}C_k(\sigma_0)$ for $t\ge\sigma_0$, by the spectral
representation, its second half gives $a_k^{-1}\log(1/\gamma_k)\le m_0$ for
$k\ge k_1$, so (MC$_{m_0}$) says that the lattice gaps in physical units
converge to $m_0$ and that the reflected correlation of $f_0$ decays at
this rate uniformly in $k$. This is not implied by the other
properties: the interleaved two-mass field of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEunique}(iii) has them and violates (MC$_{m_0}$) for
every $m_0$. Nor is it necessary for (U$_{\mathrm s}$): for the products of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEproduct} with $p\ge2$, the transfer matrix has
norm $q_k$ on $\Omega_k^\perp$, attained on the first chaos of one factor,
while the covariance of $\Phi$ is $e^{-pma_k\|z\|_1}$ and $C_k(t)=e^{-pmt}
C_k(0)$ for $f_0\ge0$; so (MC$_{m_0}$) would need $m_0\le m$ and $m_0\ge
pm$, while (U$_{\mathrm s}$) holds, also with $Z_k=C_k(\sigma_0)^{-1/2}$,
which converges to a positive limit.

\emph{The dilations.} (O$_{\mathrm s}$) is a statement about the set of
limits. It is equivalent to a statement about the lattice moments alone:
convergence after a change of the spacing by a bounded factor. Write
$B_k(f_1,\dots,f_n)=\omega_k(\prod_jZ_kA_k(f_j))$ and $(D_{\lambda,c}
B_k)(f_1,\dots,f_n)=c^nB_k(f_1(\lambda\,\cdot),\dots,f_n(\lambda\,\cdot))$.
Since $A_k(f(\lambda\,\cdot))=\lambda^{-4}\sum_x(\lambda a_k)^4f(\lambda
a_kx)(\Phi_x-c_k)$, $D_{\lambda,c}B_k$ is the moment family of the packets of
the same states formed at the spacing $\lambda a_k$, with the constants
$c\lambda^{-4}Z_k$. Consider
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(DS)},leftmargin=*]
\item there are $\Lambda\ge1$, $\lambda_k\in[\Lambda^{-1},\Lambda]$,
$c_k>0$ and a nontrivial family $T$ with $D_{\lambda_k,c_k}B_k\to T$ on
every separated tuple.
\end{enumerate}
A family is trivial if it vanishes on all separated tuples of length
$n\ge1$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEdilate}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}},
$C_k(0)>0$ for large $k$, $Z_k=C_k(\sigma_0)^{-1/2}$ and \textup{(M$_{\mathrm
s}$)}, and let $m_1$ be as in Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:limitEbracket}}.

\textup{(i)} \textup{(DS)} implies \textup{(O$_{\mathrm s}$)}: every limit
$S$ of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}} has $T=D_{\lambda,c}S$ for some
$\lambda,c>0$.

\textup{(ii)} \textup{(O$_{\mathrm s}$)} implies \textup{(DS)} with
$\Lambda=m_1/m_*$, and $T$ can be taken to be any limit of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}}.

\textup{(iii)} Under \textup{(DS)}, \textup{(U$_{\mathrm s}$)} holds if and
only if $(\lambda_k,c_k)$ converges in $(0,\infty)^2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For $\lambda>0$ the map $f\mapsto f(\lambda\,\cdot)$ is a bijection of the
separated tuples. Let $(f_j)\in\mathcal C_n(Q,\delta)$, $\lambda_k\to
\lambda\in[\Lambda^{-1},\Lambda]$. For large $k$ the tuples $(f_j(\lambda_k
\,\cdot))$, $(f_j(\lambda\,\cdot))$ and the telescoping tuples with one
factor $f_i(\lambda_k\,\cdot)-f_i(\lambda\,\cdot)$ lie in one $\mathcal C_n(
Q',\delta/(2\Lambda))$, and $f_i(\lambda_k\,\cdot)\to f_i(\lambda\,\cdot)$
uniformly; so (M$_{\mathrm s}$) and multilinearity give $B_k(f_1(\lambda_k
\,\cdot),\dots)-B_k(f_1(\lambda\,\cdot),\dots)\to0$. Hence, along a
subsequence with $B_k\to S$ on every separated tuple,
\[
B_k\bigl(f_1(\lambda_k\,\cdot),\dots,f_n(\lambda_k\,\cdot)\bigr)\to S_n\bigl(
f_1(\lambda\,\cdot),\dots,f_n(\lambda\,\cdot)\bigr) .\tag{$*$}
\]
By Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(ii) every limit is nontrivial.

(i) Let $S$ be the limit along a subsequence, and pass to a further one
with $\lambda_k\to\lambda$ and $c_k\to c\in[0,\infty]$. By ($*$), $T_n(f)=
\lim_kc_k^nB_k(f(\lambda_k\,\cdot))$ with $B_k(f(\lambda_k\,\cdot))\to
S_n(f(\lambda\,\cdot))$. If $c=\infty$, then $S_n(f(\lambda\,\cdot))=0$ for
every separated tuple of length $n\ge1$, and $S$ is trivial; if $c=0$, $T$
is trivial. So $0<c<\infty$ and $T=D_{\lambda,c}S$. For two limits,
$D_{\lambda,c}S=D_{\lambda',c'}S'$ gives $S'=D_{\lambda/\lambda',c/c'}S$.

(ii) Let $\mathcal G$ be the countable set of the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(i), $F_1,F_2,\dots$ the separated tuples of its
elements, and $d(B,B')=\sum_i2^{-i}\min(1,|B(F_i)-B'(F_i)|)$ for families
$B,B'$. By the approximation in that proof, a family bounded on every
$\mathcal C_n(Q,\delta)$ by a multiple of $\prod_j\|f_j\|_\infty$ that
vanishes at every $F_i$ vanishes on all separated tuples; such bounds hold
for every $D_{\lambda,c}S$ with $S$ a limit. Fix a limit $S$, let $I=[m_*/
m_1,m_1/m_*]$, $e_k(\lambda,c)=d(D_{\lambda,c}B_k,S)$, and choose
$(\lambda_k,c_k)\in I\times(0,\infty)$ with $e_k(\lambda_k,c_k)\le
\inf_{I\times(0,\infty)}e_k+1/k$. Suppose $e_k(\lambda_k,c_k)\ge\varepsilon>0$
along a subsequence. A further subsequence has $B_k\to S'$ on every
separated tuple, and (O$_{\mathrm s}$) gives $S=D_{\lambda,c}S'$ with
$\lambda\in I$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEbracket}(iii). Then
$D_{\lambda,c}B_k\to S$ on every separated tuple by ($*$) with constant
$\lambda_k=\lambda$, so $e_k(\lambda,c)\to0$ and $e_k(\lambda_k,c_k)\to0$, a
contradiction. Hence $e_k(\lambda_k,c_k)\to0$. Now take any subsequence and
a further one with $\lambda_k\to\lambda\in I$ and $B_k\to S'$. Some $F_i$,
of length $n_i\ge1$, has $S(F_i)\ne0$, and $c_k^{n_i}B_k(F_i(\lambda_k\,
\cdot))\to S(F_i)$. If $c_k\to\infty$ along a further subsequence, then
$B_k(F_l(\lambda_k\,\cdot))\to0$ for every $l$ with $n_l\ge1$, so by ($*$)
$D_{\lambda,1}S'$ vanishes at every $F_l$, and $S'$ is trivial; if $c_k\to0$,
then $S(F_i)=0$. So a further subsequence has $c_k\to c\in(0,\infty)$, and
$D_{\lambda_k,c_k}B_k\to D_{\lambda,c}S'$ on every separated tuple by ($*$).
This family agrees with $S$ at every $F_l$, hence everywhere. Every
subsequence thus has a further subsequence along which $D_{\lambda_k,c_k}
B_k\to S$, which is (DS) with $T=S$.

(iii) If $(\lambda_k,c_k)\to(\lambda,c)$, the proof of (i) gives $S=
D_{1/\lambda,1/c}T$ for every limit $S$, which is (U$_{\mathrm s}$).
Conversely, under (U$_{\mathrm s}$) let $S$ be the limit. By the proof of
(i), $(c_k)$ has no subsequence tending to $0$ or $\infty$, so
$(\lambda_k,c_k)$ lies in a compact subset of $(0,\infty)^2$, and every
limit point $(\lambda,c)$ has $D_{\lambda,c}S=T$. Two limit points
$(\lambda,c)$, $(\lambda',c')$ give $D_{\lambda/\lambda',c/c'}S=S$, so
$\lambda=\lambda'$ and $c=c'$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEdil}.
\end{proof}

So (O$_{\mathrm s}$) says that the spacings $a_k$ can be corrected by
bounded factors $\lambda_k$ so that the moments of the same states converge,
and under it (U$_{\mathrm s}$) is the convergence of the corrections and of
the constants, which (MC$_{m_0}$) gives by Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEunique}(ii).
Neither
(O$_{\mathrm s}$) nor (U$_{\mathrm s}$) follows from the properties proved
here together with (MC$_{m_0}$): the fields of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEgauss}(c) are reflection positive, invariant
under translations and $B_4$, have the gap $ma_k$ in every coordinate
direction and satisfy (M$_{\mathrm s}$) and (MC$_m$), and their two limits
are not related by any $D_{\lambda,c}$. Conversely (O$_{\mathrm s}$) does
not give (MC$_{m_0}$) (Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEunique}(iii)). The proofs of
Propositions~\ref{prop:limitEaxial}, \ref{prop:limitEbracket}
and~\ref{prop:limitEdilate} use the averaged condition only through
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(iii) and the bound $\gamma_k\le e^{-m_*a_k}$,
which the fields of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEgauss} have with $m$ in
place of $m_*$.

\emph{Temperedness.} The Schwinger functions of~\cite{OS73} are linear
functionals on the space $\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$ of the
$F\in\mathcal S(\R^{4n})$ that vanish, with all their derivatives, at every
$x=(x_1,\dots,x_n)$ with $x_i=x_j$ for some $i\ne j$, and~\cite{OS75} adds the
linear growth condition: $|S_n(F)|\le\sigma_n|F|_{sn}$ on
$\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$ for a fixed integer $s$ and $\sigma_n\le\alpha
(n!)^{\beta'}$, with the norms $|F|_w=\sup_{x,\,|\gamma|\le w}(1+|x|^2)^{w/2}
|\partial^\gamma F(x)|$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:hermite}. Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}
gives the $S_n$ only on $C^\infty_c(\mathfrak D_n)$. The test functions of
$\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$ approach the coinciding points, where (M$_{\mathrm
s}$) gives no control, since its constants $M_n(Q,\delta)$ may grow arbitrarily
as $\delta\to0$. The following rate closes this gap.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(G$_{\mathrm s}$)},leftmargin=*]
\item \emph{Growth at coinciding points.} There are $K,\beta,p,q\ge0$ such
that for every $n\ge1$, every cube $Q$ of side $L$ and every $\delta\in(0,1]$
there is $k_0$ with
\[
\Bigl|\omega_k\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^nZ_kA_k(f_j)\Bigr)\Bigr|\le
K^n(n!)^\beta(1+L)^{qn}\delta^{-pn}\prod_{j=1}^n\|f_j\|_\infty
\]
for all $k\ge k_0$ and $(f_1,\dots,f_n)\in\mathcal C_n(Q,\delta)$.
\end{enumerate}
Since $\mathcal C_n(Q,\delta)\subset\mathcal C_n(Q,1)$ for $\delta\ge1$,
(G$_{\mathrm s}$) implies (M$_{\mathrm s}$), and the limits of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(i) obey the same bound.

For $x\in\R^{4n}$ write $x_{j\nu}$ for the coordinates of $x_j$, $|x|$ for the
Euclidean norm, $|x|_\infty=\max_{j,\nu}|x_{j\nu}|$, and
$d(x)=\min_{i\ne j}|x_i-x_j|_\infty$. For a coordinate direction $\mu$ let
$\mathcal S^{\mu+}_0(\R^{4n})$ be the set of $F\in\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$ with
$F(x)=0$ whenever $x_{j\mu}\le0$ for some $j$, and
$V_\mu=\{x\in\mathfrak D_n:x_{j\mu}>0\text{ for all }j\}$. Such an $F$
vanishes with all its derivatives on $\{x_{j\mu}\le0\}$, by continuity from the
interior of this set. Fix $\eta_0\in C^\infty_c((-\frac12,\frac12))$ with
$\eta_0\ge0$ and $\int\eta_0=1$, let $\psi=\mathbf 1_{[-1/2,1/2)}*\eta_0$, so
that $0\le\psi\le1$, $\operatorname{supp}\psi\subset(-1,1)$ and
$\sum_{m\in\Z}\psi(t-m)=1$, and put $C_\psi=\max(1,\|\psi'\|_\infty,\|\psi''\|
_\infty)$. For $h>0$ and $m=(m_1,\dots,m_n)\in(\Z^4)^n$ let
$\psi^h_m(x)=\prod_{j,\nu}\psi(x_{j\nu}/h-m_{j\nu})$; call $m$
\emph{separated} if $|m_i-m_j|_\infty\ge4$ for $i\ne j$, and let $E_h$ be the
sum of $\psi^h_m$ over the separated $m$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:limitEcut}
\textup{(i)} $E_h$ is smooth with values in $[0,1]$, $E_h=0$ on $\{d<2h\}$ and
$E_h=1$ on $\{d\ge6h\}$. For every multi-index $\gamma\ne0$,
$|\partial^\gamma E_h|\le2^{4n}h^{-|\gamma|}\prod_{j,\nu}\|\psi^{(\gamma_{j\nu})}
\|_\infty$; in particular $|\partial^\gamma(1-E_h)|\le(2C_\psi)^{4n}
h^{-|\gamma|}$ for all $\gamma\in\{0,1,2\}^{4n}$.

\textup{(ii)} Let $F\in\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$, $\alpha$ a multi-index and
$w,N,w'$ integers with $w'\ge\max(w,|\alpha|+N)$. If
$|x_i-x_j|_\infty=t\le1$ for some $i\ne j$, then
\[
(1+|x|^2)^{w/2}|\partial^\alpha F(x)|\le3^w\frac{(4t)^N}{N!}|F|_{w'} .
\]
If $F\in\mathcal S^{\mu+}_0(\R^{4n})$ and $0<x_{j\mu}=t\le1$ for some $j$, the
same holds with $2^wt^N/N!$ in place of $3^w(4t)^N/N!$.

\textup{(iii)} $C^\infty_c(\mathfrak D_n)$ is dense in $\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$
for every norm $|\cdot|_w$. Every $F\in\mathcal S^{\mu+}_0(\R^{4n})$ is, for
every $w$, a $|\cdot|_w$-limit of finite sums of products
$f_1\otimes\cdots\otimes f_n$ with $f_j\in C^\infty_c(\R^4)$ supported in
$\{x_\mu>0\}$ and with pairwise disjoint supports.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i) The sum of all $\psi^h_m$ is $1$, and at each $x$ at most two integers
$m_{j\nu}$ have $\psi(x_{j\nu}/h-m_{j\nu})\ne0$, so at most $2^{4n}$ terms are
nonzero near $x$; this gives smoothness and the derivative bounds, the same
ones holding for $1-E_h$, the sum over the other $m$. If $\psi^h_m(x)\ne0$,
then $|x_j-hm_j|_\infty<h$ for all $j$. For separated $m$ this gives
$|x_i-x_j|_\infty>h|m_i-m_j|_\infty-2h\ge2h$, so $E_h=0$ where $d<2h$. If
$d(x)\ge6h$, it gives $h|m_i-m_j|_\infty>4h$ for every $m$ with
$\psi^h_m(x)\ne0$, so all these $m$ are separated and $E_h(x)=1$.

(ii) Let $u=x_i-x_j$, let $x(\tau)$ be $x$ with $x_i$ replaced by $x_j+\tau
u$, and $g(\tau)=\partial^\alpha F(x(\tau))$. All derivatives of $g$ vanish
at $\tau=0$, where two arguments coincide, so Taylor's formula gives
$|g(1)|\le\sup_\tau|g^{(N)}(\tau)|/N!$, and $g^{(N)}=(u\cdot\nabla_{x_i})^N
\partial^\alpha F$ is at most $|u|_1^N\le(4t)^N$ times $\max_{|\gamma|=N}
|\partial^{\gamma+\alpha}F(x(\tau))|\le|F|_{w'}(1+|x(\tau)|^2)^{-w/2}$. Since
$|x-x(\tau)|\le|u|\le2t\le2$, $(1+|x|^2)^{1/2}\le3(1+|x(\tau)|^2)^{1/2}$. In the
second case move $x_{j\mu}$ from $0$ to $t$ instead; the path has length
$t\le1$, which gives the factor $2^w$.

(iii) Let $F\in\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$, fix $w$, let $\chi\in C^\infty_c(
\R^{4n})$ with values in $[0,1]$ equal to $1$ on $\{|x|\le1\}$, $\chi_R=\chi(
\cdot/R)$ with $R\ge1$, and $0<h\le\frac16$. Then $\chi_RE_hF\in C^\infty_c(
\mathfrak D_n)$ by (i), and $F-\chi_RE_hF=(1-E_h)F+(1-\chi_R)E_hF$. By the
Leibniz rule and (i), $(1+|x|^2)^{w/2}|\partial^\gamma((1-E_h)F)(x)|$ for
$|\gamma|\le w$ is at most $c_wh^{-w}\max_{\alpha\le\gamma}(1+|x|^2)^{w/2}
|\partial^\alpha F(x)|$ with $c_w$ depending only on $w$, $n$ and $\psi$, and
it vanishes unless $d(x)<6h$; there (ii) with $N=w+1$ bounds it by
$c_w3^w24^{w+1}h|F|_{2w+1}$. The second term is supported in $\{|x|\ge R\}$
and its $|\cdot|_w$ norm is at most $c_{w,h}(1+R^2)^{-1/2}|F|_{w+1}$. Taking
$h$ small and then $R$ large proves the first claim. For the second, let
$\eta\in C^\infty(\R)$ with values in $[0,1]$, $\eta=0$ on $(-\infty,1]$ and
$\eta=1$ on $[2,\infty)$, and $G=\chi_RE_h\prod_j\eta(x_{j\mu}/h)F$, which is
smooth with compact support in $V_\mu$. The additional term
$E_h(1-\prod_j\eta(x_{j\mu}/h))F$ vanishes unless $0<x_{j\mu}<2h$ for some
$j$, and the second case of (ii) bounds it in the same way. Finally, every
point of $V_\mu$ has a neighbourhood $U_1\times\cdots\times U_n$ of open
cubes in $\{x_\mu>0\}$ with pairwise disjoint closures; finitely many of them
cover $\operatorname{supp}G$, a partition of unity
subordinate to them~\cite[Sect.~1.4]{Hor03} writes $G$ as a finite sum of
smooth functions supported in such products, and for each the partial sums
of the Fourier series in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(i) are
finite sums of products of the required form converging in
$C^\infty$ with supports in a fixed compact set, hence in every $|\cdot|_w$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:limitEextend}
Let $n\ge2$, $C,p',q'\ge0$, and let $S$ be a multilinear form on the separated
$n$-tuples with
\[
|S(f_1,\dots,f_n)|\le C(1+L)^{q'}\delta^{-p'}\prod_{j=1}^n\|f_j\|_\infty
\]
for every cube $Q$ of side $L$, every $\delta\in(0,1]$ and $(f_1,\dots,f_n)
\in\mathcal C_n(Q,\delta)$. Let $S$ also denote the distribution on
$\mathfrak D_n$ with $S(f_1\otimes\cdots\otimes f_n)=S(f_1,\dots,f_n)$ for
the separated tuples of smooth functions, which exists and is unique by the
proof of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}(i)}. There is a number $c_\star$,
independent of $n$, $C$, $p'$, $q'$ and $S$, such that, with
$w=\lceil q'\rceil+\lceil p'\rceil+12n+1$,
\[
|S(F)|\le c_\star^n\,C\,6^{q'}16^{p'}\,|F|_w\qquad\text{for }F\in
C^\infty_c(\mathfrak D_n).
\]
Hence $S$ extends uniquely to a linear functional on $\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$
that is continuous for $|\cdot|_w$, with the same bound.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\emph{One piece.} Let $h\in(0,\frac1{16}]$, $m$ separated, $z_j=hm_j$, and
$G\in C^\infty(\R^{4n})$ supported in $\prod_j(z_j+(-h,h)^4)$. The open cubes
$U_j=z_j+(-\frac32h,\frac32h)^4$ are at mutual $\ell^\infty$ distance at least
$h$, and lie in a cube $Q$ of side $L\le2\max_j|z_j|_\infty+3h$. Let
$B=\prod_j(z_j+[-2h,2h]^4)$, $e_\kappa(x)=\prod_j\exp(i\pi\kappa_j\cdot(x_j-
z_j)/(2h))$ for $\kappa\in(\Z^4)^n$, $c_\kappa=(4h)^{-4n}\int_BG\,\bar e_\kappa$,
and $\varphi_j\in C^\infty_c(U_j)$ with values in $[0,1]$, equal to $1$ on
$z_j+[-h,h]^4$. With $\mathsf D=\prod_{i=1}^{4n}(1-(2h/\pi)^2\partial_i^2)$
over the $4n$ coordinates, $\mathsf D\bar e_\kappa=\prod_i(1+\kappa_i^2)\bar
e_\kappa$, so integration by parts gives $|c_\kappa|\le\prod_i(1+\kappa_i^2)^{-1}
\|\mathsf DG\|_\infty$, and the same with powers of $\mathsf D$ shows that the
partial sums of $\sum_\kappa c_\kappa\prod_j\varphi_j(x_j)e_{\kappa_j}(x_j)$
converge to $G$ in $C^\infty$ with supports in $\prod_j\operatorname{supp}
\varphi_j$. Hence $S(G)$ is the sum of the values of $S$ on the terms. Each
term is $c_\kappa$ times a product in $\mathcal C_n(Q,h)$ with factors of norm
at most $1$, and $\sum_{k\in\Z}(1+k^2)^{-1}\le1+2\int_0^\infty\frac{dt}{1+t^2}
<5$, so
\[
|S(G)|\le C(1+L)^{q'}h^{-p'}\,5^{4n}\,\|\mathsf DG\|_\infty .
\]

\emph{Decomposition.} Let $F\in C^\infty_c(\mathfrak D_n)$, $h_l=2^{-l-4}$
and $E_l=E_{h_l}$. Since $d$ is bounded below on $\operatorname{supp}F$,
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEcut}(i) gives $E_lF=F$ for large $l$, and with
$E_{l+1}-E_l=E_{l+1}(1-E_l)-E_l(1-E_{l+1})$,
\[
F=E_0F+\sum_{l\ge0}\bigl(E_{l+1}(1-E_l)F-E_l(1-E_{l+1})F\bigr),
\]
a finite sum. Each term has the form $\sum_m\psi^h_mH$ over separated $m$,
with $(h,H)$ equal to $(h_0,F)$, $(h_{l+1},(1-E_l)F)$ or $(h_l,(1-E_{l+1})F)$;
in the last two cases $H=(1-E_{h'})F$ with $h/h'\in\{\frac12,2\}$, and $H$
vanishes where $d\ge12h$.

\emph{Derivatives.} Write $\mathsf D=\prod_i\mathsf D_i$ and $\psi^h_m=\prod_i
\psi_i$. Since $\mathsf D_i(\psi_iG)=\psi_iG-(2h/\pi)^2(\psi_i''G+2\psi_i'
\partial_iG+\psi_i\partial_i^2G)$ and $|\psi_i^{(k)}|\le C_\psi h^{-k}$,
induction over the coordinates gives
\[
|\mathsf D(\psi^h_mH)|\le b_\psi^{4n}\max_{\gamma\in\{0,1,2\}^{4n}}h^{|\gamma|}
|\partial^\gamma H|,\qquad b_\psi=1+2C_\psi,
\]
on the box $X_m=\prod_j(hm_j+[-h,h]^4)$, outside which $\psi^h_m=0$, since
$1+16C_\psi/\pi^2\le b_\psi$. For $H=(1-E_{h'})F$, the Leibniz rule,
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEcut}(i), $h/h'\le2$ and $h\le1$ give $h^{|\gamma|}
|\partial^\gamma H|\le3^{|\gamma|}(2C_\psi)^{4n}\max_{\alpha\le\gamma}
|\partial^\alpha F|$. Put $w_0=\lceil q'\rceil+8n$ and $N=\lceil p'\rceil+4n+1$,
so that $w\ge\max(w_0,8n+N)$. At a point of the support of $H$, $d\le12h\le
\frac34$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEcut}(ii) gives $(1+|x|^2)^{w_0/2}
|\partial^\alpha F(x)|\le3^{w_0}(48h)^N|F|_w/N!$ for $|\alpha|\le8n$; for $H=F$
the same quantity is at most $|F|_w$.

\emph{Summation.} For $x\in X_m$, $|z_j|_\infty\le|x|_\infty+h$, so the cube
of the first step has $1+L\le2(1+|x|_\infty)\le2(1+|x|^2)^{1/2}$. Evaluating at
the point of the compact box $X_m$ where $(1+|x|^2)^{-w_0/2}$ is largest, and
using $q'-w_0\le-8n$ and $(1+|x|^2)^{4n}\ge\prod_{j,\nu}(1+x_{j\nu}^2)$, the
piece $\psi^h_mH$ of a term of the second or third kind contributes at most
\[
C2^{q'}h^{-p'}\bigl(5b_\psi\cdot2C_\psi\cdot9\bigr)^{4n}3^{w_0}\frac{(48h)^N}
{N!}|F|_w\sup_{x\in X_m}\prod_{j,\nu}(1+x_{j\nu}^2)^{-1} .
\]
The sum over $m\in(\Z^4)^n$ of the supremum is the $4n$-th power of
$\sum_{k\in\Z}\sup_{|t-hk|\le h}(1+t^2)^{-1}\le3+2\int_0^\infty\frac{du}{1+h^2u^2}
<\frac7h$. With $48^N/N!\le e^{48}$, $3^{w_0}\le3^{q'+1}3^{8n}$ and
$h^{N-p'-4n}\le h$, the terms of the second and third kinds at the scale $h$
contribute at most $C6^{q'}c_1^nh|F|_w$ with $c_1=3^{10}e^{48}(5\cdot7\cdot9
\cdot2C_\psi b_\psi)^4$, and each $h_l$ occurs in at most two of them, with
$\sum_l2h_l=\frac14$. The term $E_0F$ contributes at most
$C2^{q'}16^{p'}(5\cdot7\cdot16\,b_\psi)^{4n}|F|_w$ by the same count without
the Taylor factor. This gives the bound with $c_\star=c_1+(5\cdot7\cdot16\,
b_\psi)^4$.

The extension follows from the density of $C^\infty_c(\mathfrak D_n)$ in
$\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$ for $|\cdot|_w$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEcut}(iii)), and
it is unique by the same density.
\end{proof}

For a function $F$ of $n$ points put
\begin{gather*}
(\Theta_\mu F)(x)=F(\theta_\mu x_1,\dots,\theta_\mu x_n),\\
(\tau_vF)(x)=F(x_1-v,\dots,x_n-v),\\
(F\otimes F')(x,x')=F(x)F'(x').
\end{gather*}
For $F_n\in\mathcal S^{\mu+}_0(\R^{4n})$
and $F'_{n'}\in\mathcal S^{\mu+}_0(\R^{4n'})$, $\Theta_\mu\bar F_n\otimes
F'_{n'}\in\mathcal S_0(\R^{4(n+n')})$, because at a coincidence of a
reflected point with an unreflected one some argument of $F_n$ or of
$F'_{n'}$ has $\mu$-coordinate at most $0$.

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:limitEtempered}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}} and
\textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)}, and let $S_n$ be limits as in
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}}.

\textup{(i)} Let $n\ge2$, and suppose that there are $C,p',q'\ge0$ such that
\[
|S_n(f_1,\dots,f_n)|\le C(1+L)^{q'}\delta^{-p'}\prod_j\|f_j\|_\infty
\]
on $\mathcal C_n(Q,\delta)$ for every cube $Q$ of side $L$ and
$\delta\in(0,1]$. Then $S_n$
extends uniquely to a linear functional on $\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$ continuous
for the topology of $\mathcal S(\R^{4n})$, and $|S_n(F)|\le c_\star^nC6^{q'}
16^{p'}|F|_w$ with $w$ and $c_\star$ as in
Lemma~\textup{\ref{lem:limitEextend}}.

\textup{(ii)} Under \textup{(G$_{\mathrm s}$)}, for every $n\ge1$ and
$F\in\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$,
\[
|S_n(F)|\le\sigma_n|F|_{sn},\qquad\sigma_n=(6^q16^pc_\star K)^n(n!)^\beta,
\]
with $s=\lceil p\rceil+\lceil q\rceil+13$, and $\sigma_n\le e^{6^q16^pc_\star K}(n!)^{\beta+1}$, since $y^n\le e^yn!$ for
$y\ge0$; this is the linear growth condition of~\cite{OS75}.

\textup{(iii)} Suppose that the hypothesis of \textup{(i)} holds for every
$n\ge2$, with constants depending on $n$, and put $S_0=1$. Then the extended
$S_n$ are symmetric, invariant under the translations, $B_4$ and $G_S$, and
satisfy $S_n(\bar F)=\overline{S_n(F)}$. For every $\mu$ and every finite
family $F_n\in\mathcal S^{\mu+}_0(\R^{4n})$, $0\le n\le n_0$ \textup{(}$F_0\in
\C$\textup{)},
\[
\sum_{n,n'=0}^{n_0}S_{n+n'}\bigl(\Theta_\mu\bar F_n\otimes F_{n'}\bigr)\ge0 ,
\]
and there are vectors $\Psi_\mu(F)$ of the space $\mathcal H_\mu$ of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}(iii)} whose inner products are these
sums, which agree with $\Psi_\mu(P)$ for separated polynomials $P$ of smooth
functions, are dense in $\mathcal H_\mu$, and satisfy
$e^{-tH_\mu}\Psi_\mu(F)=\Psi_\mu(\tau_{te_\mu}F)$ for $t\ge0$. For
$y\perp e_\mu$, $y\ne0$, and $F_n\in\mathcal S^{\mu+}_0(\R^{4n})$,
$F'_{n'}\in\mathcal S^{\mu+}_0(\R^{4n'})$,
\[
\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}S_{n+n'}\bigl(\Theta_\mu\bar F_n\otimes\tau_{\lambda y}
F'_{n'}\bigr)=S_n(\Theta_\mu\bar F_n)\,S_{n'}(F'_{n'}) .
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) This is Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEextend}; continuity for $|\cdot|_w$ is
continuity in $\mathcal S(\R^{4n})$.

(ii) For $n=1$, $S_1=0$. For $n\ge2$ the limits satisfy the hypothesis of (i)
with $C=K^n(n!)^\beta$, $q'=qn$ and $p'=pn$, and $\lceil qn\rceil+\lceil pn
\rceil+12n+1\le sn$, while $|F|_w\le|F|_{sn}$ for $w\le sn$.

(iii) Let $g$ be a permutation of the arguments, or the diagonal action of a
translation, of an element of $B_4$ or of an element of $G_S$. Then $F\mapsto
F\circ g^{-1}$ maps $C^\infty_c(\mathfrak D_n)$ onto itself and is continuous
for the topology of $\mathcal S(\R^{4n})$. The distributions
$F\mapsto S_n(F\circ g^{-1})$ and $S_n$ agree on the products of separated
smooth functions by the symmetry of $S_n$, by
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(ii) and by the definition of $G_S$, hence on
$C^\infty_c(\mathfrak D_n)$ by the uniqueness in
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(i), hence on $\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$ by density;
the conjugation identity follows in the same way. Choose an integer $s'$ such
that the bounds of (i) for $n\le2n_0$ hold with $|\cdot|_{s'n}$, and by
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEcut}(iii) finite sums $P^{(i)}_n$ of products of smooth
functions with disjoint supports in $\{x_\mu>0\}$ with $|P^{(i)}_n-F_n|_{2s'n_0}
\to0$, and $P^{(i)}_0=F_0$. Then $P^{(i)}=\sum_nP^{(i)}_n$ is a separated
polynomial at positive $\mu$-times, and $\sum_{n,n'}S_{n+n'}(\Theta_\mu\bar
P^{(i)}_n\otimes P^{(i)}_{n'})=S(\theta_\mu\bar P^{(i)}\cdot P^{(i)})\ge0$ by
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(iii), each $\Theta_\mu\bar P^{(i)}_n\otimes
P^{(i)}_{n'}$ being a finite sum of products of separated smooth functions.
Since $|G\otimes G'|_w\le|G|_w|G'|_w$ and $|\Theta_\mu G|_w=|G|_w$,
$\Theta_\mu\bar P^{(i)}_n\otimes P^{(i)}_{n'}\to\Theta_\mu\bar F_n\otimes
F_{n'}$ in $|\cdot|_{s'(n+n')}$, and the positivity passes to the limit. The
same convergence, applied to $P^{(i)}-P^{(i')}$, shows that
$\Psi_\mu(P^{(i)})$ is a Cauchy sequence in $\mathcal H_\mu$; its limit
$\Psi_\mu(F)$ does not depend on the approximants and has the stated inner
products. Separated polynomials of smooth functions are among the families
$F$, and their vectors are dense in $\mathcal H_\mu$: those of separated
polynomials of continuous functions are dense by construction, and each of
them is a limit of vectors of smooth ones, by uniform approximation with
supports in slightly larger sets and the bound of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(i). The relation with $e^{-tH_\mu}$ holds for the
$P^{(i)}$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(iv) and passes to the limit because
$\tau_{te_\mu}$ preserves $\mathcal S^{\mu+}_0$ and is continuous. For the
last claim, by Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(v) applied to every pair of
monomials, $\ip{\Psi_\mu(P)}{U_\mu(\lambda y)\Psi_\mu(P')}=S(\theta_\mu\bar
P\cdot\tau_{\lambda y}P')$ tends to $S(\theta_\mu\bar P)S(P')=\ip{\Psi_\mu(P)}
{\Omega_\mu}\ip{\Omega_\mu}{\Psi_\mu(P')}$ for separated polynomials $P,P'$ at
positive $\mu$-times; the $U_\mu(\lambda y)$ are unitary and these vectors
are dense, so $U_\mu(\lambda y)$ converges weakly to the projection on
$\C\Omega_\mu$. With $U_\mu(\lambda y)\Psi_\mu(F')=\Psi_\mu(\tau_{\lambda
y}F')$, obtained as for $e^{-tH_\mu}$, and $\ip{\Psi_\mu(F_n)}{\Omega_\mu}=
S_n(\Theta_\mu\bar F_n)$, $\ip{\Omega_\mu}{\Psi_\mu(F'_{n'})}=S_{n'}(F'_{n'})$,
this is the claim.
\end{proof}

With (W$_{\mathrm s}$) and (U$_{\mathrm s}$),
Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEexact} and Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtempered} give the
axioms of~\cite{OS73,OS75}. The positivity of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtempered}(iii) contains that of~\cite{OS73}, whose test
functions are supported where $0<x_{10}<\dots<x_{n0}$, and the symmetry of the
$S_n$ makes the order of the arguments irrelevant.

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:limitEOS}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}},
\textup{(G$_{\mathrm s}$)}, \textup{(W$_{\mathrm s}$)} and
\textup{(U$_{\mathrm s}$)}. Then the moments converge for every separated
tuple, and their limits, extended to $\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$ by
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEtempered}} and with $S_0=1$, satisfy the linear
growth condition of~\cite{OS75} and the axioms of~\cite{OS73} of Euclidean
invariance, reflection positivity, symmetry and clustering. The Hilbert space
and the Hamiltonian of their reconstruction are $\mathcal H_0$ and $H_0$, with
$\sigma(H_0)\subset\{0\}\cup[m_*,\infty)$ and $\ker H_0=\C\Omega_0$. If
moreover $C_k(0)>0$ for large $k$ and $Z_k=C_k(\sigma_0)^{-1/2}$, they are not
the Schwinger functions of the vacuum alone.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
(G$_{\mathrm s}$) gives (M$_{\mathrm s}$), and Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEexact}
gives convergence, invariance under the translations and $\mathrm O(4)$, so
$G_S=\mathrm O(4)$, and properties (i)--(vi) of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}. Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtempered}(ii) gives the
growth condition, and part (iii) for $\mu=0$ gives the invariance on
$\mathcal S_0$, positivity and clustering, and identifies the Hilbert space
built from $\mathcal S^{0+}_0$ with $\mathcal H_0$ and its time translations
with $e^{-tH_0}$; Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(iv) gives the spectrum.
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(ii) gives $\mathcal H_0\ne\C\Omega_0$, so
some $S_n$ with $n\ge2$ is not zero, by Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(vi).
\end{proof}

By the reconstruction theorem of~\cite{OS75}, Schwinger functions with these
properties are those of a Wightman theory on $\mathcal H_0$ with Hamiltonian
$H_0$. So the averaged condition along the tuning, (G$_{\mathrm s}$) with the
constants of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(ii), (W$_{\mathrm s}$) and
(U$_{\mathrm s}$) together give (E) for the field $\Phi$ at separated points,
with mass gap at least $m_*$. None of these four statements is proved. For
$n=2$ the rate in (G$_{\mathrm s}$) is a rate in (SD):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(SD$_{\mathrm p}$)},leftmargin=*]
\item there are $C_0,p_0\ge0$ such that for every $\varepsilon'\in(0,
\varepsilon]$ there is $k_0$ with $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\le C_0(
\varepsilon/\varepsilon')^{p_0}\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$ for $k\ge k_0$.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEtwotempered}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEtwo}}.

\textup{(a)} \textup{(SD$_{\mathrm p}$)} holds if and only if there are
$C,p',q'\ge0$ such that for every cube $Q$ of side $L$ and $\delta\in(0,1]$
there is $k_0$ with
\[
Z_k^2\bigl|\omega_k\bigl(A_k(f)A_k(g)\bigr)\bigr|\le C(1+L)^{q'}\delta^{-p'}
\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty
\]
for $k\ge k_0$ and $(f,g)\in\mathcal C_2(Q,\delta)$. One can take $q'=8$ and
$p'=8+p_0$ in one direction and $p_0=p'$ in the other.

\textup{(b)} Under \textup{(SD$_{\mathrm p}$)} every limit $S_2$ of
Corollary~\textup{\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b)} extends uniquely to a linear
functional on $\mathcal S_0(\R^8)$ with $|S_2(F)|\le C'|F|_{\lceil p_0\rceil
+41}$, invariant under the translations and $B_4$, with
$S_2(\Theta_\mu\bar f\otimes f)\ge0$ for $f\in\mathcal S^{\mu+}_0(\R^4)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a) Let (SD$_{\mathrm p}$) hold, $(f,g)\in\mathcal C_2(Q,\delta)$ with
$\delta\le1$, and $\varepsilon'=\min(\varepsilon,\delta/8)$. The proof of
Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(a) gives, for large $k$ depending only on
$\delta$, $Z_k^2|\omega_k(A_k(f)A_k(g))|\le\mathsf K_{4L/\delta}^2\mathsf
K_{\delta/4}^2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1}\|f\|
_\infty\|g\|_\infty$, and $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')/\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)
\le\max(1,C_0)\max(1,8\varepsilon)^{p_0}\delta^{-p_0}$. Since $\mathsf
K_{4L/\delta}^2=(8L/\delta+3)^8\le11^8(1+L)^8\delta^{-8}$ and $\mathsf
K_{\delta/4}^2\le(\frac72)^8$, the bound holds with $q'=8$, $p'=8+p_0$.
Conversely, the proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(a) gives $\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon')\le M\,\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$ for large $k$, where $M$
is the constant of the bound for a cube $Q_0$ of side $L_0=2(\varepsilon+2+
2a_0)$, which contains $B_{\varepsilon'}$ and $\theta_{0,k}(B_{\varepsilon'})$
for all $k$ and $\varepsilon'\le\varepsilon$, and for the separation
$\min(\varepsilon',1)$. So $M\le C(1+L_0)^{q'}\max(1,\varepsilon^{-p'})
(\varepsilon/\varepsilon')^{p'}$, which is (SD$_{\mathrm p}$) with $p_0=p'$.

(b) By (a) the limits satisfy the hypothesis of
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEextend} with $n=2$, $q'=8$, $p'=8+p_0$, and $\lceil q'
\rceil+\lceil p'\rceil+25=\lceil p_0\rceil+41$. Invariance and positivity
follow as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtempered}(iii), from those of
Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b) and Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEcut}(iii) with
$n=1$.
\end{proof}

The rate cannot be dropped: the structure that Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}
and Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b) give at separated points does not imply
temperedness.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEnotempered}
\textup{(i)} Let $K\ge0$ be integrable on the compact subsets of
$\R^4\setminus\{0\}$ and $S_2(F)=\iint F(x,y)K(x-y)\,dx\,dy$ for $F\in
C^\infty_c(\mathfrak D_2)$. If $|S_2(F)|\le C|F|_w$ on $C^\infty_c(\mathfrak
D_2)$, then $\int_{\delta\le|z|_\infty\le1}K\le c_wC\delta^{-w}$ for
$\delta\in(0,1]$, with $c_w$ depending only on $w$. Conversely, if
$\int_{\delta\le|z|_\infty\le\rho}K\le C(1+\rho)^{q'}\delta^{-p'}$ for
$0<\delta\le1\le\rho$, then $S_2$ extends to $\mathcal S_0(\R^8)$ as in
Lemma~\textup{\ref{lem:limitEextend}}.

\textup{(ii)} Let $K(z)=\int_1^\infty e^{\sqrt\lambda-\lambda\|z\|_1}d\lambda$
for $z\ne0$, $\|z\|_1=\sum_\nu|z_\nu|$, and $S_2(f,g)=\iint f(x)g(y)K(x-y)\,dx
\,dy$ for separated pairs. Then $S_2$ is given by a distribution on
$\mathfrak D_2$, is symmetric, invariant under the translations and $B_4$,
real on real functions, bounded on $\mathcal C_2(Q,\delta)$ by $L^8\mathsf
k(\delta)\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty$ with $\mathsf k(\delta)=\int_1^\infty
e^{\sqrt\lambda-\lambda\delta}d\lambda$, and satisfies $|S_2(f,g(\cdot-\lambda
y))|\le e\mathsf k(1)L^8\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty e^{-(\lambda|y|_\infty-L)}$ for
$f,g$ supported in a cube of side $L$ and $\lambda|y|_\infty\ge L+1$. For $g$
supported in $\{x_\mu>0\}$, $S_2(\bar g\circ\theta_\mu,g)\ge0$; the time
translations define on the completion $\mathcal H^{(1)}\ne0$ of the functions
supported in $\{x_0>0\}$ for the form $S_2(\bar g\circ\theta_0,g')$ a semigroup
$e^{-tH^{(1)}}$ with $\sigma(H^{(1)})\subset[1,\infty)$. These are the
properties of Corollary~\textup{\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b)}, with $m_*=1$. But
$\int_{\delta\le|z|_\infty\le2\delta}K\ge240\,\delta^4e^{1/(64\delta)}$ for
$\delta\le\frac1{32}$, so $S_2$ is bounded by no norm $|\cdot|_w$ on
$C^\infty_c(\mathfrak D_2)$ and does not extend to a continuous functional on
$\mathcal S_0(\R^8)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Let $\chi_0\in C^\infty(\R)$ be even, nonincreasing on $[0,\infty)$, equal
to $1$ on $[0,1]$ and to $0$ on $[2,\infty)$, $\chi_1(z)=\prod_\nu\chi_0(z_\nu)$,
and $\psi_\delta(z)=\chi_1(z)-\chi_1(2z/\delta)$. Then $0\le\psi_\delta\le1$,
$\psi_\delta=1$ on $\{\delta\le|z|_\infty\le1\}$, $\psi_\delta=0$ on
$\{|z|_\infty\le\delta/2\}\cup\{|z|_\infty\ge2\}$, and $|\partial^\gamma
\psi_\delta|\le c_\gamma\delta^{-|\gamma|}$. With $\phi\in C^\infty_c((-1,
1)^4)$, $\phi\ge0$, $\int\phi=1$, the function $F_\delta(x,y)=\phi(x)
\psi_\delta(x-y)$ lies in $C^\infty_c(\mathfrak D_2)$, has $|F_\delta|_w\le c_w
\delta^{-w}$, and $S_2(F_\delta)=\int\psi_\delta K\ge\int_{\delta\le|z|_\infty
\le1}K$. For the converse, $|S_2(f,g)|\le\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty\int_Qdx\int_{
\delta\le|z|_\infty\le\max(L,1)}K\le2^{q'}C(1+L)^{q'+4}\delta^{-p'}\|f\|_\infty
\|g\|_\infty$ on $\mathcal C_2(Q,\delta)$, and $S_2$ is the distribution of
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEextend} by uniqueness.

(ii) For $|z|_\infty\ge\delta$, $\|z\|_1\ge\delta$ and $K(z)\le\mathsf
k(\delta)$; for $\|z\|_1=u\ge1$ and $\lambda\ge1$, $\lambda u\ge\lambda+u-1$, so
$K(z)\le e\mathsf k(1)e^{-u}$. Hence $K$ is continuous on $\R^4\setminus\{0\}$,
which gives the distribution, and the two bounds follow, since $\|x-y\|_1\ge
|x-y|_\infty$. Symmetry, reality and invariance hold because $K$ is real, even
and a function of $\|z\|_1$, which $B_4$ preserves. Let $g,g'$ be supported
in $\{x_0\ge t_0\}$, $t_0>0$. Substituting $x\mapsto\theta_0x$ and $y\mapsto
y+te_0$,
\[
S_2\bigl(\bar g\circ\theta_0,g'(\cdot-te_0)\bigr)=\int_1^\infty e^{\sqrt\lambda
-\lambda t}\,\mathsf B_\lambda(G_\lambda,G'_\lambda)\,d\lambda ,
\]
where $G'_\lambda$ is formed from $g'$ as $G_\lambda$ from $g$, with
\begin{gather*}
G_\lambda(\mathbf x)=\int g(x_0,\mathbf x)e^{-\lambda x_0}dx_0,\\
\mathsf B_\lambda(u,v)=\iint\bar u(\mathbf x)v(\mathbf y)e^{-\lambda\|\mathbf
x-\mathbf y\|_1}d\mathbf x\,d\mathbf y\qquad(u,v\colon\R^3\to\C) .
\end{gather*}
The integrand is bounded by $e^{\sqrt\lambda-2\lambda t_0}\|g\|_1
\|g'\|_1$. From $e^{-\lambda|\varsigma|}=\frac1\pi\int_\R\frac{\lambda}
{\lambda^2+k^2}e^{ik\varsigma}dk$ one obtains
\[
\mathsf B_\lambda(u,u)=\pi^{-3}\int|\hat u(k)|^2\prod_{i=1}^3\frac\lambda
{\lambda^2+k_i^2}\,dk\ge0 .
\]
So the form is positive, and
\[
\|g(\cdot-te_0)\|^2=\int_1^\infty e^{\sqrt\lambda-2\lambda t}\mathsf
B_\lambda(G_\lambda,G_\lambda)\,d\lambda\le e^{-2t}\|g\|^2 .
\]
The time
translations therefore induce symmetric contractions $\mathsf T(t)$ of
$\mathcal H^{(1)}$ with $\mathsf T(s)\mathsf T(t)=\mathsf T(s+t)$ and $\|\mathsf
T(t)\|\le e^{-t}$, strongly continuous by dominated convergence, so $\mathsf
T(t)=e^{-tH^{(1)}}$ with $H^{(1)}\ge1$, as in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:osaxioms}(iv). For $g\ge0$ not zero, $G_\lambda\ge0$ is not
zero and $\mathsf B_\lambda(G_\lambda,G_\lambda)>0$, so $\mathcal H^{(1)}\ne0$.
Positivity for $\mu\ne0$ follows from $\theta_\mu\pi_\mu=\pi_\mu\theta_0$ and
the invariance under $\pi_\mu$. Finally, let $0<|z|_\infty\le2\delta\le\frac1
{16}$, so that $u=\|z\|_1\le8\delta\le\frac14$, and $\lambda_0=(4u)^{-2}\ge1$.
For $\lambda\in[\lambda_0,2\lambda_0]$, $\sqrt\lambda-\lambda u\ge\frac1{4u}-
\frac1{8u}=\frac1{8u}$, so $K(z)\ge\lambda_0e^{1/(8u)}\ge e^{1/(64\delta)}$, and
$\{\delta\le|z|_\infty\le2\delta\}$ has volume $240\delta^4$. By (i), a bound
$|S_2(F)|\le C|F|_w$ would give $240\delta^4e^{1/(64\delta)}\le c_wC\delta^{-w}$
for all small $\delta$, which is false; and a continuous functional on
$\mathcal S_0(\R^8)$ is bounded by some $C|\cdot|_w$.
\end{proof}

The kernel of (ii) is the superposition, with weight $e^{\sqrt\lambda}d
\lambda$, of the kernels $e^{-\lambda\|z\|_1}$ of the limits of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a). By (i), for a two-point function
with a nonnegative kernel, a power rate as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEextend} is also necessary for temperedness.

\begin{remark}\label{rem:limitEcontact}
\emph{Coinciding points.} The axioms of~\cite{OS73,OS75} involve only
$\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$, and positivity involves only test functions whose
reflected and unreflected arguments are separated. Under (G$_{\mathrm s}$)
the bound of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtempered}(ii) extends by the Hahn--Banach
theorem~\cite{RS80} to a functional on all of $\mathcal S(\R^{4n})$ with the
same bound; averaging over the finite group generated by the permutations of
the arguments and the diagonal action of $B_4$, which preserve every
$|\cdot|_w$, makes it symmetric and $B_4$-invariant. Every such extension
agrees with $S_n$ on $\mathcal S_0(\R^{4n})$ and so satisfies the positivity
of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtempered}(iii); two of them differ by a distribution
supported on the coinciding points. Translation invariance of the extension
is not claimed here. For the action density the extension cannot be an
integral: under the hypothesis of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsing}, the
functions $F_\delta$ of the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnotempered}(i) are supported in one compact set
and bounded by $\|\phi\|_\infty$, while, since $|z|\le2|z|_\infty$,
$S_2(F_\delta)\ge\int_{\delta\le|z|_\infty\le2\delta}K\ge240\cdot4^{-8}c\,
\delta^{-4}(\log\frac1\delta)^{-p}$ for $2\delta\le\min(1,r_0/2)$; so no
extension of $S_2$ to $\R^8$ is a distribution of order zero, and its values
at coinciding points are fixed only by a renormalisation, which the axioms do
not see. The same law satisfies the hypothesis of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtempered}(i) for $n=2$: if in addition $K(z)\le
C|z|^{-8}$ for $z\ne0$, then $|S_2(f,g)|\le\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty L^4C\int_{
|z|\ge\delta}|z|^{-8}dz=\frac{\pi^2}2CL^4\delta^{-4}\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty$ on
$\mathcal C_2(Q,\delta)$, which is that hypothesis with $q'=p'=4$, while (M)
fails by Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsing}. At strong coupling
(Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEstrong}) every limit vanishes at separated points,
and its extension is $0$.
\end{remark}

\emph{Asymptotic freedom at short distance.} Renormalised perturbation
theory predicts how the two-point function of $\Phi$ behaves at short
distance, and this behaviour can be posed as a condition on the lattice
states. Let $G_k(z)=\omega_k(\Phi_0\Phi_z)-
c_k^2$ for $z\in\Z^4$, as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitErot}(iv),
so that $\omega_k((\Phi_x-c_k)(\Phi_y-c_k))=G_k(y-x)$; let
$\log^+t=\max(\log t,0)$ and, for $\gamma\ge0$ and $\rho>0$,
\[
\ell(\rho)=1+\log^+\frac1\rho,\qquad w_\gamma(\rho)=\rho^{-8}\ell(\rho)^
{-\gamma} .
\]
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(AF)},leftmargin=*]
\item There are $\gamma\ge0$, $0<c_1\le C_1$, $r_0\in(0,1]$ and $k_0$ such
that for all $k\ge k_0$ and $z\in\Z^4$ with $|z|_\infty\ge2r+1$
\[
Z_k^2|G_k(z)|\le C_1w_\gamma(a_k|z|),
\]
and $Z_k^2G_k(z)\ge c_1w_\gamma(a_k|z|)$ if moreover $a_k|z|\le r_0$.
\end{enumerate}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(AF$_\infty$)},leftmargin=*]
\item There are $\gamma\ge0$ and $C_{\mathrm{AF}}>0$ such that every limit
$S_2$ of $Z_k^2\omega_k(A_k(f)A_k(g))$ on the separated pairs along a
subsequence is $S_2(f,g)=\iint f(x)g(y)K(x-y)\,dx\,dy$ with $K$ locally
integrable on $\R^4\setminus\{0\}$ and
\[
\lim_{\rho\to0}\ \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{0<|z|\le\rho}\Bigl||z|^8\Bigl(
\log\frac1{|z|}\Bigr)^\gamma K(z)-C_{\mathrm{AF}}\Bigr|=0 .
\]
\end{enumerate}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(AF$_\beta$)},leftmargin=*]
\item For some $\Lambda>0$, with $L_k=\log(1/(a_k\Lambda))$, the couplings
are $\beta_{W,k}=2N/g_k^2$ with $g_k^{-2}=2b_0L_k+(b_1/b_0)\log L_k+O(1)$.
\end{enumerate}
(AF) is the two-sided form, at every lattice distance from $2r+1$ to
$r_0/a_k$, of the law $|x-y|^{-8}(\log\frac1{|x-y|})^{-\gamma}$, with only
a power bound at physical distances; (AF$_\infty$) is the exact law of the
limit at short distance; (AF$_\beta$) is the two-loop tuning of
\cite[Remark~247]{BBLR26}. None of the three is proved here for the Wilson
measure.

\begin{remark}\label{rem:limitEafpert}
\emph{The perturbative values.} Renormalised perturbation theory gives the
following values; they are predictions and no proof below uses them. Let
$F^a_{\mu\nu}$ be the field strength normalised by the action $\frac14\int
F^a_{\mu\nu}F^a_{\mu\nu}$, with generators $\Tr(T^aT^b)=\frac12\delta^{ab}$,
and let $\beta(g)=-b_0g^3+O(g^5)$, $b_0=11N/(48\pi^2)$~\cite{GW73,Pol73}.
The trace of the energy--momentum tensor, $\Theta=(\beta(g)/2g)F^a_{\mu\nu}
F^a_{\mu\nu}$, is finite and invariant under the renormalisation
group~\cite{CDJ77}, so $\ip{\Theta(x)}{\Theta(0)}$, the connected two-point
function, is $|x|^{-8}F(\bar g(|x|))$ with $\bar g(|x|)$ the running coupling
at the scale $1/|x|$, and $F(g)=\frac14b_0^2g^4\,T_0(1+O(g^2))$, where
$T_0|x|^{-8}$ is the connected two-point function of $F^a_{\mu\nu}
F^a_{\mu\nu}$ in the free theory. There, in the Feynman gauge, the
propagator of the gauge field is $\delta^{ab}\delta_{\mu\nu}D(x)$ with
$D(x)=1/(4\pi^2|x|^2)$, and that of the field strength, which is gauge
independent, is $\ip{F^a_{\mu\nu}(x)}{F^b_{\rho\sigma}(0)}=-\delta^{ab}
P_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}$ with
\[
P_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}=\delta_{\nu\sigma}D_{\mu\rho}-\delta_{\nu\rho}
D_{\mu\sigma}-\delta_{\mu\sigma}D_{\nu\rho}+\delta_{\mu\rho}D_{\nu\sigma},
\]
$D_{\mu\rho}=\partial_\mu\partial_\rho D=M_{\mu\rho}/(2\pi^2|x|^4)$, where $M=4uu^{\mathsf T}-1$,
$u=x/|x|$. Since $\operatorname{tr}M=0$ and $\operatorname{tr}M^2=12$, each
of the four squared terms of the bracket, summed over $\mu,\nu,\rho,\sigma$,
gives $4\cdot12$, the two cross terms with a trace vanish and the other four
give $-12$ each, so the sum of the squares is $(192-96)(2\pi^2|x|^4)^{-2}=
24\pi^{-4}|x|^{-8}$, and Wick's theorem gives $T_0=2(N^2-1)\cdot24\pi^{-4}=
48(N^2-1)/\pi^4$. With $\bar g(|x|)^{-2}=2b_0\log\frac1{|x|\Lambda}(1+o(1))$
this gives, as $|x|\to0$,
\[
\ip{\Theta(x)}{\Theta(0)}=\frac{3(N^2-1)}{\pi^4}\,|x|^{-8}\Bigl(\log\frac1
{|x|}\Bigr)^{-2}(1+o(1)),
\]
which is (AF$_\infty$) with $\gamma=2$ for every $N$ and with
$C_{\mathrm{AF}}=3(N^2-1)/\pi^4$, a constant free of $b_0$ and $\Lambda$.\footnote{For $\SU(2)$, $C_{\mathrm{AF}}=9/\pi^4\approx0.092$; for
$\SU(3)$, $C_{\mathrm{AF}}=24/\pi^4\approx0.246$.}
For the sum $\Phi$ of $N-\ReTr U_p$ over the $24$ plaquettes with a corner
at $0$, $U_p=\exp(ia_k^2g_kF^a_{\mu\nu}T^a+\cdots)$ gives $N-\ReTr U_p=
\frac14a_k^4g_k^2F^a_{\mu\nu}F^a_{\mu\nu}+\cdots$ (no sum) for $p$ in the
plane $\{\mu,\nu\}$, and each plane carries four of the $24$ plaquettes, so
$\Phi_x=\frac12a_k^4g_k^2F^a_{\mu\nu}F^a_{\mu\nu}+\cdots=-(a_k^4/b_0)
\Theta(1+O(g_k^2))$, up to a constant and fields of higher dimension. Thus
$Z_ka_k^4\to b_0$ makes $Z_kA_k(f)$ tend to $-\Theta(f)$; then (AF) holds
with $\gamma=2$ and $Z_k$ of order $a_k^{-4}$, and $G_k(z)$ at a fixed
lattice distance is of order $g_k^4$, that is of order $\beta_{W,k}^{-2}$
under (AF$_\beta$).
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:limitEaf}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}} and
\textup{(AF)} for the constants $Z_k$.

\textup{(i)} Let $Q$ be a cube of side $L$ and $\delta\in(0,1]$. For
$k\ge k_0$ with $a_k\le\min(1,\delta/(2r+1))$ and for $(f,g)\in\mathcal
C_2(Q,\delta)$,
\[
Z_k^2\bigl|\omega_k\bigl(A_k(f)A_k(g)\bigr)\bigr|\le100\,C_1(1+L)^4
\delta^{-4}\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty .
\]
So \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s,2}$)} holds; every limit $S_2$ along a
subsequence satisfies
\[
|S_2(f,g)|\le C_1\iint|f(x)||g(y)|w_\gamma(|x-y|)\,dx\,dy
\]
on the separated pairs, and extends uniquely to a continuous linear
functional on $\mathcal S_0(\R^8)$ with $|S_2(F)|\le C|F|_{33}$.

\textup{(ii)} If $f,g\ge0$ form a separated pair and $|x-y|\le r_0$ for
$x\in\operatorname{supp}f$, $y\in\operatorname{supp}g$, then $S_2(f,g)\ge
c_1\iint f(x)g(y)w_\gamma(|x-y|)\,dx\,dy$. In particular no limit $S_2$
vanishes identically, and the moment bound \textup{(M)} of
Sec.~\textup{\ref{ssec:osaxioms}}, posed for the packets $Z_kA_k(f)$, fails
for $n=2$ and $Q=[0,1]^4$.

\textup{(iii)} For every $\varepsilon>0$, $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)>0$ for
large $k$ and
\[
0<\liminf_kZ_k^2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\le\limsup_kZ_k^2\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)<\infty .
\]

\textup{(iv)} \textup{(SD$_{\mathrm p}$)} holds with $p_0=4$, hence
\textup{(SD)} and \textup{(UV)}, and \textup{(NE)} holds. Every limit
$S_2$ of Corollary~\textup{\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b)} is not identically zero,
extends to $\mathcal S_0(\R^8)$ as in
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:limitEtwotempered}(b)}, and its Hamiltonian
$H^{(1)}$ has a nonempty spectrum in $[m_*,\infty)$.

\textup{(v)} If the transfer matrices $\mathbb T_k$ have spectrum on
$\Omega_k^\perp$ in $[0,e^{-m_{\mathrm{lat}}}]$ for a fixed $m_{\mathrm{lat}}
>0$ and all $k$, then \textup{(AF)} fails for every choice of $Z_k$; this
is the case under the hypotheses of
Corollary~\textup{\ref{cor:limitEstrong}}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) By Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i) and translation invariance,
\[
\omega_k(A_k(f)A_k(g))=\sum_{x,y}a_k^8f(a_kx)g(a_ky)G_k(y-x).
\]
A nonzero
term has $|a_kx-a_ky|_\infty\ge\delta$, so $|y-x|_\infty\ge\delta/a_k\ge
2r+1$, and (AF) with $\ell\ge1$ bounds $Z_k^2|G_k(y-x)|$ by $C_1(a_k|y-x|)
^{-8}\le C_1(a_k|y-x|_\infty)^{-8}$. There are $(2j+1)^4-(2j-1)^4=64j^3+16j
\le80j^3$ points $v\in\Z^4$ with $|v|_\infty=j\ge1$, so with $J=\lceil
\delta/a_k\rceil$
\[
\begin{aligned}
\sum_{|v|_\infty\ge J}a_k^4(a_k|v|_\infty)^{-8}&\le80a_k^{-4}\sum_{j\ge J}
j^{-5}\\
&\le80a_k^{-4}\Bigl(J^{-5}+\frac{J^{-4}}4\Bigr)\le100\,\delta^{-4},
\end{aligned}
\]
while at most $(L/a_k+1)^4$ sites $x$ have $a_kx\in Q$, and $a_k^4(L/a_k+1)
^4\le(1+L)^4$. This gives the bound, and (M$_{\mathrm s,2}$) follows since
$\mathcal C_2(Q,\delta)\subset\mathcal C_2(Q,1)$ for $\delta\ge1$. In the
same sum $Z_k^2|G_k(y-x)|\le C_1w_\gamma(a_k|y-x|)$, and $(x,y)\mapsto|f(x)|
|g(y)|w_\gamma(|x-y|)$ is continuous with compact support in $\R^8$, since
$w_\gamma$ is continuous on $(0,\infty)$; its Riemann sums converge to its
integral, which gives the bound on $S_2$. The extension is
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEextend} with $n=2$, $C=100C_1$, $q'=p'=4$ and
$w=4+4+24+1=33$.

(ii) For large $k$ every nonzero term of the sum has $|y-x|_\infty\ge2r+1$
and $a_k|y-x|\le r_0$, so the lower bound of (AF) gives $Z_k^2\omega_k(A_k(
f)A_k(g))\ge c_1\sum_{x,y}a_k^8f(a_kx)g(a_ky)w_\gamma(a_k|y-x|)$, and the
Riemann sums converge as in (i). The integral is positive when $f$ and $g$
are not zero. For (M), let $s_0=r_0/2$ and $0<\delta\le r_0/16$; let $f$ be
continuous with values in $[0,1]$, equal to $1$ on $[\frac12-2\delta,
\frac12-\delta]\times[\frac{s_0}4,\frac{3s_0}4]^3$ and supported in
$[\frac12-3\delta,\frac12-\frac\delta2]\times[0,s_0]^3$, and $g$ continuous
with values in $[0,1]$, equal to $1$ on $[\frac12+\delta,\frac12+2\delta]
\times[\frac{s_0}8,\frac{7s_0}8]^3$ and supported in $[\frac12+\frac\delta2,
\frac12+3\delta]\times[0,s_0]^3$. The supports lie in $[0,1]^4$, at distance
$\delta$, and all their points are within $(36\delta^2+3s_0^2)^{1/2}\le r_0$
of each other. The pairs with $x$ in the first box, $x_0+\delta\le y_0\le
x_0+4\delta$ with $y_0\in[\frac12+\delta,\frac12+2\delta]$, and $|y_i-x_i|
\le\delta$ for $i=1,2,3$ have measure $\frac{\delta s_0^3}8\cdot\delta\cdot
8\delta^3=s_0^3\delta^5$, $f(x)g(y)=1$ and $2\delta\le|x-y|\le\sqrt{19}\,
\delta<5\delta$, where $w_\gamma\ge(5\delta)^{-8}\ell(2\delta)^{-\gamma}$.
Hence $S_2(f,g)\ge c_15^{-8}s_0^3\delta^{-3}(1+\log\frac1{2\delta})^{-\gamma}$,
which is unbounded as $\delta\to0$, while $\|f\|_\infty\|g\|_\infty=1$; under
(M) the limit would be bounded by $M_2(Q)$, as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsing}.

(iii) Let $Q_0$ be the cube of side $L_0=2(\varepsilon+2+2a_0)$ of the proof
of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEtwotempered}(a), which contains
$B_{\varepsilon'}$ and $\theta_{0,k}(B_{\varepsilon'})$ for all $k$ and
$\varepsilon'\le\varepsilon$. For $h$ supported in $B_{\varepsilon'}$ with
$\|h\|_\infty\le1$ and $a_k\le\varepsilon'$, $\omega_k(\vartheta(\overline{
A_k(h)})A_k(h))=\omega_k(A_k(\bar h\circ\theta_{0,k})A_k(h))$, and the pair
lies in $\mathcal C_2(Q_0,\varepsilon')$; so by (i)
\[
Z_k^2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\le100\,C_1(1+L_0)^4\max(1,\varepsilon'^{-4})
\]
for large $k$, which with $\varepsilon'=\varepsilon$ is the upper bound.
Suppose that $Z_k^2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\to0$ along a subsequence. By
(M$_{\mathrm s,2}$) and the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(i) a further
subsequence has a limit $S_2$ on all separated pairs. Let $g$ be supported in
a cube of side $L$ in $\{x_0\ge\eta\}$, $\eta>0$, and $t\ge4\varepsilon+1$
dyadic. For large $k$, $\bar g\circ\theta_{0,k}$ is supported in $\{x_0\le
-\eta+a_k\}$ and $g(\cdot-te_0)$ in $\{x_0\ge\eta+t\}$, so with $c=2
\varepsilon-\eta+a_k$ and $d=0$ the inequality of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtwo}(i), multiplied by $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$,
whose proof does not use $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)>0$, gives
\[
Z_k^2\bigl|\omega_k\bigl(A_k(\bar g\circ\theta_{0,k})A_k(g(\cdot-te_0))
\bigr)\bigr|\le\mathsf K_L^2Z_k^2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\|g\|_\infty^2\to0 .
\]
By the approximation of $\theta_{0,k}$ by $\theta_0$ in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(iii), $S_2(\bar g\circ\theta_0,g(\cdot-te_0))=0$.
The construction in the proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b), which
uses only (M$_{\mathrm s,2}$), gives a Hilbert space $\mathcal H^{(1)}$ and
a self-adjoint $H^{(1)}\ge0$ with $S_2(\bar g\circ\theta_0,g(\cdot-te_0))=
\|e^{-tH^{(1)}/2}w\|^2$, $w$ the vector of $g$. Since $e^{-tH^{(1)}/2}$ is
injective, $w=0$, that is $S_2(\bar g\circ\theta_0,g)=0$, for every $g$
supported in $\{x_0>0\}$, and by the proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b)
$S_2$ vanishes identically. This contradicts (ii). So $\liminf_kZ_k^2
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)>0$, and in particular $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)
>0$ for large $k$.

(iv) Let $c_\varepsilon=\frac12\liminf_kZ_k^2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$
and $C_2=100\,C_1(1+L_0)^4$. By (iii), for $\varepsilon'\in(0,\varepsilon]$
and large $k$,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')&\le\frac{C_2\max(1,\varepsilon'^{-4})}
{c_\varepsilon}\,\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\\
&\le C_0\Bigl(\frac\varepsilon{\varepsilon'}\Bigr)^4\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon),
\end{aligned}
\]
where $C_0=C_2\max(1,\varepsilon^{-4})/c_\varepsilon$; the second
inequality holds because $\max(1,\varepsilon'^{-4})$ is at most
$\max(1,\varepsilon^{-4})(\varepsilon/\varepsilon')^4$. This is (SD$_{\mathrm p}$) with $p_0=4$; it gives (SD),
and (UV) by Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(ii). Let $S^\sharp_2$ be a limit
of Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b), formed with $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)
^{-1}$ in place of $Z_k^2$. By (i) and (iii) a further subsequence has
$Z_k^2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\to\lambda\in(0,\infty)$ and a limit $S_2$
with the constants $Z_k$, and then $S^\sharp_2=S_2/\lambda$, which is not
identically zero by (ii). By Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEne}(i) this is
(NE), and Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b) and
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEtwotempered}(b) give the rest.

(v) Under (AF), (iii) and (iv) give $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)>0$ for large
$k$ and (SD), which Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(iii) excludes under a
lattice gap; Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEstrong} provides the gap.
\end{proof}

So the short-distance law, in the two-sided form (AF), gives the two inputs
(SD) and (NE) of Sec.~\ref{ssec:limitE} for $n=2$, with the power rate
(SD$_{\mathrm p}$) that temperedness needs, and it excludes the trivial
limits of the strong-coupling range. It says nothing about $n\ge3$: the
bounds (RN$_m$) with $m\ge2$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEreflect} are
not consequences of it. It also fixes the constants $Z_k$ up to bounded
factors.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEafscale}
Assume \textup{(AF)} for the constants $Z_k$, and let $z_0\in\Z^4$ with
$|z_0|_\infty\ge2r+1$.

\textup{(i)} For large $k$, $G_k(z_0)>0$ and
\[
c_1|z_0|^{-8}\le Z_k^2a_k^8\,\ell(a_k|z_0|)^\gamma\,G_k(z_0)\le C_1|z_0|^{-8} .
\]

\textup{(ii)} $Z_k\ge c_1^{1/2}|z_0|^{-4}\|\Phi\|_\infty^{-1}a_k^{-4}\ell(a_k
|z_0|)^{-\gamma/2}$ for large $k$; in particular $Z_k\to\infty$.

\textup{(iii)} For large $k$ and every $z\in\Z^4$ with $|z|_\infty\ge2r+1$ and
$a_k|z|\le r_0$,
\[
\frac{c_1}{C_1}\,\frac{w_\gamma(a_k|z|)}{w_\gamma(a_k|z_0|)}\le\frac{G_k(z)}
{G_k(z_0)}\le\frac{C_1}{c_1}\,\frac{w_\gamma(a_k|z|)}{w_\gamma(a_k|z_0|)} .
\]

\textup{(iv)} Under the hypothesis of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}}, if \textup{(AF)} also holds for
constants $Z'_k$ with an exponent $\gamma'$, then $\gamma'=\gamma$ and
$Z'_k/Z_k$ is bounded above and below by positive constants; both are
comparable to $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$ for every $\varepsilon>0$.

\textup{(v)} Under \textup{(AF$_\beta$)}, $Z_ka_k^4$ is bounded above and
below by positive constants if and only if $\beta_{W,k}^\gamma G_k(z_0)$ is.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) is (AF) at $z=z_0$, valid for large $k$ because $a_k|z_0|\to0$. (ii)
follows from (i), since $|G_k(z_0)|=|\Cov(\Phi_0,\Phi_{z_0})|\le\Var(\Phi_0)
\le\|\Phi\|_\infty^2$ by the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality and translation
invariance, and $\ell(a_k|z_0|)^\gamma a_k^8\to0$. (iii) is the quotient
of the two bounds of (AF) at $z$ and $z_0$. For (iv), Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEaf}(iii)
applied to $Z_k$ and to $Z'_k$ makes $Z_k^2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$ and
${Z'_k}^2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$ bounded above and below, hence
$Z'_k/Z_k$; and (i) for both gives $\ell(a_k|z_0|)^{\gamma-\gamma'}$ bounded
above and below, which forces $\gamma'=\gamma$ since $\ell(a_k|z_0|)\to
\infty$. For (v), (AF$_\beta$) gives $\beta_{W,k}/\log(1/a_k)\to4Nb_0>0$,
and $\ell(a_k|z_0|)/\log(1/a_k)\to1$, so by (i) $Z_k^2a_k^8$ is bounded
above and below exactly when $\beta_{W,k}^\gamma G_k(z_0)$ is.
\end{proof}

By Remark~\ref{rem:limitEafpert}, perturbation theory predicts $\gamma=2$ and
$G_k(z_0)$ of order $\beta_{W,k}^{-2}$, hence $Z_k$ of order $a_k^{-4}$, and
for the plaquette sum $Z_ka_k^4\to b_0$ in the normalisation of $\Theta$.
Part (iii) is the content of (AF) without reference to $Z_k$: the connected
correlation of $\Phi$ decays from the lattice distance $|z_0|$ to every
distance up to $r_0/a_k$ like the eighth power of the distance, with the
logarithmic correction, within fixed factors. Its lower half is a lower
bound on correlations at physical separations, the kind of input that upper
bounds do not supply (compare Proposition~\ref{prop:upperbounds}).

The two-sided law is consistent with the other properties of
Sec.~\ref{ssec:limitE} and implies neither (W$_{\mathrm s}$) nor the exact
law (AF$_\infty$).

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEafexample}
Let $\gamma\ge0$, $\mu_j=2^{4(j-1)}(1+j)^{-\gamma/2}$ for $j\ge0$, let
$a_k\le1$ and $J_k=\lfloor\log_2(2/a_k)\rfloor$, let $\phi^{(j)}_k$,
$0\le j\le J_k$, be independent copies of the field of
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a)} with $q_k$ replaced by
$q_{k,j}=e^{-2^{j-1}a_k}$, and put $\phi_k=\sum_{j\le J_k}\mu_j\phi^{(j)}_k$,
$A_k(f)=\sum_xa_k^4f(a_kx)\phi_k(x)$, $r=0$ and $Z_k=1$.

\textup{(a)} The law of $\phi_k$ is invariant under translations and $B_4$
and reflection positive for the reflections in coordinate hyperplanes
through sites and bisecting links, and the spectrum of its transfer matrix
in each coordinate direction on $\Omega_k^\perp$ lies in $[0,e^{-a_k/2}]$
and contains $e^{-a_k/2}$: the gap is $\frac12$ in physical units.

\textup{(b)} \textup{(AF)} holds with this $\gamma$, $r_0=\frac12$,
$c_1=e^{-1}2^{-16-\gamma}$ and $C_1=\max(2^\gamma(A_\gamma+B),B')$, where
$A_\gamma=\sum_{i\ge0}2^{-8i}(1+i)^\gamma$, $B=\sum_{i\ge1}2^{8i}e^{-2^{i-1}}$
and $B'=\sum_{j\ge0}\sup_{t\ge2^j}t^8e^{-t}$.

\textup{(c)} The moments converge along the whole sequence to the Gaussian
family with $S_2(f,g)=\iint f(x)g(y)K(x-y)\,dx\,dy$, $K(v)=h(\|v\|_1)$,
$h(u)=\sum_{j\ge0}\mu_j^2e^{-2^{j-1}u}$. \textup{(G$_{\mathrm s}$)} holds with
$\beta=\frac12$ and $p=q=2$, and \textup{(U$_{\mathrm s}$)} holds; the
conclusions of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEaf}}(i)--(iv) hold with
$m_*=\frac12$. The group $G_S$ is $B_4$, so \textup{(W$_{\mathrm s}$)}
fails, and \textup{(AF$_\infty$)} fails.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a) As in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEheavy}(a), with $J_k+1$
independent fields in place of two: the tuple is a reversible Markov chain
in each coordinate direction with transfer operator $\bigotimes_jP_j$, of
spectrum $\{\prod_jq_{k,j}^{N_j}\}$ with $1$ simple, the functions of
$\phi_k$ at positive times are functions of the tuple there, and the vector
of $\phi_k(x)$ at positive time has a nonzero component of eigenvalue
$q_{k,0}=e^{-a_k/2}$, the largest $q_{k,j}$.

(b) The covariance is $G_k(z)=\sum_{j\le J_k}\mu_j^2e^{-2^{j-1}u}$ with
$u=a_k\|z\|_1$; for $z\ne0$ let $\rho=a_k|z|$, so that $\rho\le u\le2\rho$
and $u\ge a_k$. If $u\le2$, let $j_*=\lfloor\log_2(2/u)\rfloor\ge0$, so that
$\frac12<2^{j_*-1}u\le1$. The terms with $j=j_*-i$, $i\ge0$, are at most
$\mu_{j_*}^22^{-8i}(1+i)^\gamma$, because $(1+j_*)/(1+j)\le1+i$; those with
$j=j_*+i$, $i\ge1$, at most $\mu_{j_*}^22^{8i}e^{-2^{i-1}}$, because
$2^{j-1}u>2^{i-1}$. So $G_k(z)\le(A_\gamma+B)(2^{j_*-1})^8(1+j_*)^{-\gamma}
\le(A_\gamma+B)\rho^{-8}(1+j_*)^{-\gamma}$. Here $1+j_*\ge\max(1,\log_2(2/u))
\ge\max(1,\log_2(1/\rho))$, which is at least $\frac12\ell(\rho)$, so
$G_k(z)\le2^\gamma(A_\gamma+B)w_\gamma(\rho)$. If $u>2$, then $\rho>1$,
$2^{j-1}u\ge2^j$ and $\mu_j^2\le2^{8(j-1)}$, so $G_k(z)\le u^{-8}\sum_j
(2^{j-1}u)^8e^{-2^{j-1}u}\le B'u^{-8}\le B'w_\gamma(\rho)$. For the lower
bound let $\rho\le\frac12$, so $u\le1$; then $j_*\le J_k$ because $u\ge a_k$,
and the term $j_*$ alone is at least $(2u)^{-8}(1+j_*)^{-\gamma}e^{-1}$.
Since $(2u)^{-8}\ge(4\rho)^{-8}$ and $1+j_*\le1+\log_2(2/\rho)=2+\log(1/\rho)/
\log2\le2\ell(\rho)$, because $1/\log2<1.5$, this is at least $e^{-1}
2^{-16-\gamma}w_\gamma(\rho)$. The finiteness of $A_\gamma$, $B$, $B'$
follows from the ratio test, since $t^8e^{-t}$ decreases for $t\ge8$.

(c) $\omega_k(A_k(f)A_k(g))=\sum_{x,y}a_k^8f(a_kx)g(a_ky)h_k(a_k\|y-x\|_1)$
with $h_k$ the partial sum of $h$ up to $J_k$. On a separated pair at
distance $\delta$ only $u\ge\delta$ occurs, where $0\le h-h_k\le\sum_{j>J_k}
\mu_j^2e^{-2^{j-1}\delta}\to0$, and $h$ is continuous on $(0,\infty)$; so
the Riemann sums converge to $\iint f(x)g(y)K(x-y)\,dx\,dy$. The fields are
centred Gaussian, so the moments of separated tuples are sums over the
$(n-1)!!$ pairings of products of these two-point functions, and they
converge as well; this gives (U$_{\mathrm s}$). Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEaf}(i)
and $(n-1)!!\le(n!)^{1/2}$ bound the moments of tuples in $\mathcal
C_n(Q,\delta)$, $\delta\le1$, by $(100C_1)^{n/2}(n!)^{1/2}(1+L)^{2n}
\delta^{-2n}\prod_j\|f_j\|_\infty$ for large $k$, which is
(G$_{\mathrm s}$). The proofs of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEaf}(i)--(iv) use
the states only through their invariance, reflection positivity and gap, as
in Remark~\ref{rem:limitEscope}. $K$ is continuous on $\R^4\setminus\{0\}$ and
$h$ is strictly decreasing, so by Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEaniso}(i) an
$R\in\mathrm O(4)$ lies in $G_S$ only if $\|Rv\|_1=\|v\|_1$ for all $v$; then
$R$ permutes the vertices $\pm e_\mu$ of the unit ball of $\|\cdot\|_1$, and
$R\in B_4$. Finally, choose $i_1$ with $((1+i)/(2+i))^\gamma\ge\frac12$ for
$i\ge i_1$. Since $\mu_{i+1}^2=2^8((1+i)/(2+i))^\gamma\mu_i^2$,
\[
h(\rho)\ge\sum_{i\ge i_1}\mu_{i+1}^2e^{-2^i\rho}\ge2^7\Bigl(h(2\rho)-
\sum_{i<i_1}\mu_i^2\Bigr),
\]
and $h(2\rho)\to\infty$ as $\rho\to0$, so $\liminf_{\rho\to0}h(\rho)/
h(2\rho)\ge2^7$. With $v=\rho e_0$ and $v'=\frac\rho2(1,1,1,1)$, $|v|=|v'|=
\rho$, $\|v\|_1=\rho$ and $\|v'\|_1=2\rho$, while (AF$_\infty$), $K$ being
continuous, would give $K(v)/K(v')\to1$.
\end{proof}

The example is not a lattice gauge theory. With (AF$_\infty$) in place of
(AF), the leading term at short distance is rotation invariant: if $K$ is
as in (AF$_\infty$) and square integrable on compact subsets of $\R^4
\setminus\{0\}$, the components $K_{2j}(\rho,\cdot)$, $j\ge1$, of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEaniso}(ii) satisfy $\rho^8(\log\frac1\rho)
^\gamma\|K_{2j}(\rho,\cdot)\|_{L^2(S^3)}\to0$ as $\rho\to0$ along almost every
$\rho$, because the projection onto $\mathcal Y_{2j}$, $j\ge1$, has norm one
and annihilates the constant $C_{\mathrm{AF}}$. This is not
(W$_{\mathrm s}$), which asks that these components vanish.

\begin{remark}\label{rem:limitEafstatus}
\emph{Status of (AF).} No part of (AF), (AF$_\infty$) or (AF$_\beta$) is
proved for the Wilson measure, in any regime of couplings that tends to the
continuum, and (AF) fails in the strong-coupling range
(Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEaf}(v)). Ba{\l}aban's renormalisation
group~\cite{Bal84a,Bal85a,Bal87,Bal88,Bal89b} constructs effective actions in
a small-field approximation with a coupling constant renormalisation by
recursive renormalisation-group equations~\cite{Bal87}, and proves the
ultraviolet stability of four-dimensional pure lattice gauge theories, that
is, bounds on the partition function in a box of fixed physical size uniform
in the spacing~\cite{Bal89b}; these bounds concern the partition function,
not the two-point function of a local field, and the box is fixed.
Magnen, Rivasseau and S\'en\'eor~\cite{MRS93} give the basis for a
construction of the Schwinger functions of the $\SU(2)$ gauge field with a
fixed infrared cut-off and no ultraviolet cut-off, in a regularised axial
gauge; these are correlations of the gauge field in a gauge, with an
infrared cut-off, not of a gauge-invariant composite field such as $\Phi$,
and they are not states of the Wilson measure along a lattice tuning. An
asymptotically free ultraviolet limit has been constructed by convergent
expansions in the running coupling for the two-dimensional Gross--Neveu
model~\cite{GK85}, not for a gauge theory in four dimensions. What is
missing is exactly the following inequality, for $\Phi$ the plaquette sum,
the torus limits $\omega_k$ along the tuning (AF$_\beta$), $\gamma=2$ and
some $Z_k>0$, $0<c_1\le C_1$, $r_0>0$: for large $k$ and all $z\in\Z^4$
with $|z|_\infty\ge2r+1$, where $G_k(z)=\omega_k(\Phi_0\Phi_z)-\omega_k(
\Phi_0)^2$,
\[
Z_k^2|G_k(z)|\le C_1w_2(a_k|z|),
\]
and $Z_k^2G_k(z)\ge c_1w_2(a_k|z|)$ if moreover $a_k|z|\le r_0$;
equivalently, by Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEafscale}(iii), the
$Z_k$-free two-sided comparison of $G_k(z)$ with $G_k(z_0)\,w_2(a_k|z|)/
w_2(a_k|z_0|)$ for $2r+1\le|z|_\infty$, $a_k|z|\le r_0$, together with the
upper bound for $a_k|z|>r_0$ with $Z_k^2=w_2(a_k|z_0|)/G_k(z_0)$. It is open, both halves, already in a box of
fixed physical size. Given it, Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEaf} supplies (SD$_{\mathrm
p}$) and (NE) for $n=2$; (RN$_m$) for $m\ge2$, (W$_{\mathrm s}$),
(U$_{\mathrm s}$) and the averaged condition along the tuning stay open.
\end{remark}

\emph{Products and the two-point properties.} After
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEreflect} the separated moments of order
$n\ge3$ rest on (RN$_m$), $m\ge2$, and the rate (G$_{\mathrm s}$) on a rate
in these bounds. Functions of two independent copies of the field of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a) show what the two-point properties do
not give. Write $\gamma_2$ for the standard Gaussian measure on $\R^2$,
$\|\cdot\|_p$ for the norm of $L^p(\gamma_2)$, and $T_M(u)=\max(-M,\min(M,u))$
for $M>0$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:limitEtrunc}
Let $m>0$, $q_k=e^{-ma_k}$, let $\phi^{(1)}_k,\phi^{(2)}_k$ be independent
copies of the field of Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a)},
$\xi_x=(\phi^{(1)}_k(x),\phi^{(2)}_k(x))$, let $U\colon\R^2\to\R$ be
continuous, in $L^p(\gamma_2)$ for every $p<\infty$, with $\int
U\,d\gamma_2=0$, and put $\Phi_x=U(\xi_x)$ and $A_k(f)=\sum_xa_k^4f(a_kx)
\Phi_x$, with $r=0$.

\textup{(i)} The law of $\Phi$ is invariant under translations and $B_4$ and
reflection positive for the reflections in coordinate hyperplanes through
sites and bisecting links, and in each coordinate direction the spectrum of
its transfer matrix on $\Omega_k^\perp$ lies in $[0,q_k]$. It contains $q_k$
if $\int U(u)u_i\,d\gamma_2(u)\ne0$ for $i=1$ or $i=2$.

\textup{(ii)} Let $U_M=T_M\circ U-\int T_M\circ U\,d\gamma_2$, so that
$|U_M|\le2M$. For $n\ge1$ and random vectors $\zeta_1,\dots,\zeta_n$ in
$\R^2$, each of law $\gamma_2$,
\begin{gather*}
\Bigl|\E\prod_iU(\zeta_i)\Bigr|\le\|U\|_n^n,\\
\Bigl|\E\prod_iU_M(\zeta_i)-\E\prod_iU(\zeta_i)\Bigr|\le e_n(M),
\end{gather*}
with $e_n(M)=2n(2\|U\|_n)^{n-1}\|U\mathbf 1_{\{|U|>M\}}\|_n\to0$ as
$M\to\infty$. Hence, if $f_j$ is supported in a cube of side $L_j$, the
moments $\omega_k(\prod_jA_k(f_j))$ formed with $U_M$ and with $U$ differ by
at most $e_n(M)\prod_j(L_j+a_k)^4\|f_j\|_\infty$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i) As in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEheavy}(a), the pair
$(\phi^{(1)}_k,\phi^{(2)}_k)$ is invariant, reflection positive, and in
each coordinate direction a reversible Markov chain with transfer operator
$P\otimes P$, of spectrum $\{q_k^{N+N'}\}$ with $1$ simple; the functions of
$\Phi$ at positive times are functions of the pair there, so $\Phi$ has the
stated invariance and positivity, and its transfer matrix is the restriction
of $P\otimes P$ to an invariant subspace. For a site $x$ at time $s\ge1$,
$g_{\Phi_x}$ is $(P\otimes P)^s$ applied to $U(\xi_x)$, viewed as a function
of the field at one time, and $P\otimes P$ acts as $q_k$ on the linear
functions of that field. By Gaussian integration by parts the orthogonal
projection of $U(\xi_x)$ on these linear functions has inner product
$\int U(u)u_i\,d\gamma_2(u)$ with $\phi^{(i)}_k(x)$, so it is not zero, and
$g_{\Phi_x}$ has a nonzero component of eigenvalue $q_k$.

(ii) The first bound is H\"older's inequality with $n$ exponents $n$. Since
$|T_Mu|\le|u|$, $|T_Mu-u|\le|u|\mathbf 1_{\{|u|>M\}}$ and $\int
U\,d\gamma_2=0$,
\[
\|U_M\|_n\le2\|U\|_n,\qquad\|U_M-U\|_n\le2\|U\mathbf 1_{\{|U|>M\}}\|_n .
\]
The difference of the two products is the sum over $j$ of the products
$\prod_{i<j}U_M(\zeta_i)\,(U_M-U)(\zeta_j)\prod_{i>j}U(\zeta_i)$, and
H\"older's inequality for each of them gives the second bound. By dominated
convergence $e_n(M)\to0$, as $|U|^n$ is integrable. The moments
are $\sum_{x_1,\dots,x_n}a_k^{4n}\prod_jf_j(a_kx_j)\E\prod_jU(\xi_{x_j})$,
each $\xi_{x_j}$ has law $\gamma_2$, and $\sum_xa_k^4|f_j(a_kx)|\le(L_j+
a_k)^4\|f_j\|_\infty$.
\end{proof}

The moments of exponentials are explicit. For a centred Gaussian vector
$(X_1,\dots,X_n)$ with unit variances and covariances $\rho_{ii'}$, $t>0$, and
$u_{ii'}=e^{t^2\rho_{ii'}}-1$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:limitEcover}
\E\prod_{i=1}^n\bigl(e^{tX_i-t^2/2}-1\bigr)=\sum_G\prod_{\{i,i'\}\in G}
u_{ii'} ,
\end{equation}
the sum over the sets $G$ of pairs whose union is $\{1,\dots,n\}$. Indeed
$\E\prod_{i\in S}e^{tX_i-t^2/2}=\prod_{\{i,i'\}\subset S}(1+u_{ii'})$ for
$S\subset\{1,\dots,n\}$; expanding the product of the factors
$e^{tX_i-t^2/2}-1$ and these products, the coefficient of
$\prod_{\{i,i'\}\in G}u_{ii'}$ is the sum of $(-1)^{n-|S|}$ over the $S$
that contain every pair of $G$, which is $1$ if $G$ covers $\{1,\dots,n\}$
and $0$ otherwise. When all $\rho_{ii'}\ge0$, every term is nonnegative and
nondecreasing in each $\rho_{ii'}$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEthree}
In the setting of Lemma~\textup{\ref{lem:limitEtrunc}} let $t_k>0$ with
$t_k\to\infty$, $\lambda_k=e^{-t_k^2/2}$ and
\[
U_k(u)=u_1+\lambda_k\bigl(e^{t_ku_2-t_k^2/2}-1\bigr),
\]
let $\varepsilon>0$, form $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$ with $\Phi_x=U_k(\xi_x)$,
and put $Z_k=\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$ and $\ell_0=(\frac38)^8
e^{-m(2\varepsilon+6)}$.

\textup{(a)} The spectrum of the transfer matrix on $\Omega_k^\perp$ lies in
$[0,q_k]$ and contains $q_k$: the gap is $m$ in physical units. For large
$k$, $\ell_0\le\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\le2(1+2a_0)^8$. \textup{(SD$_{\mathrm
p}$)} holds with $p_0=0$; hence \textup{(SD)}, \textup{(UV)},
\textup{(RN$_1$)} and \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s,2}$)} hold. \textup{(NE)} holds,
and every limit $S_2$ of Corollary~\textup{\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b)} is
$S_2(f,g)=z\iint f(x)g(y)e^{-m\|x-y\|_1}dx\,dy$ with $z\in[\frac12(1+2a_0)^{-8},
\ell_0^{-1}]$.

\textup{(b)} Let $n\ge3$, $\eta=1/(4m(3n+5))$, $c_j=3j\eta e_1$, and let
$f_1,\dots,f_n$ be continuous with values in $[0,1]$, $f_j$ equal to $1$ on
$c_j+[-\frac\eta2,\frac\eta2]^4$ and supported in $c_j+[-\eta,\eta]^4$.
Then $(f_j)\in\mathcal C_n(Q,\eta)$ for a cube $Q$ of side $3n\eta$, and for
large $k$
\[
Z_k^n\,\omega_k\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^nA_k(f_j)\Bigr)\ge\frac{(\eta/2)^{4n}
e^{nt_k^2/4}}{2^{n(n-1)/2}(2(1+2a_0)^8)^{n/2}}\to\infty .
\]
So \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s,n}$)} fails for every $n\ge3$.

\textup{(c)} Let $j\ge2$, $\eta'=1/(4m(3j+10))$, and let $f_1,\dots,f_j$ be
as in \textup{(b)}, with $\eta'$ in place of $\eta$ and centres
$2\eta'e_0+3i\eta'e_1$, $i=1,\dots,j$. They are supported in
$\{x_0\ge\eta'\}$, $(f_i)\in\mathcal C_j(Q',\eta')$ for a cube $Q'$ of side
$3j\eta'$, and for large $k$, with $P_k=\prod_iA_k(f_i)$,
\[
Z_k^{2j}\,\omega_k\bigl(\vartheta(\bar P_k)P_k\bigr)\ge\frac{(\eta'/2)^{8j}
e^{jt_k^2/2}}{2^{j(2j-1)}(2(1+2a_0)^8)^j}\to\infty .
\]
So \textup{(RN$_j$)} fails for every $j\ge2$.

\textup{(d)} There are $M_k\to\infty$ such that \textup{(a)--(c)} hold for
the fields $\Phi_x=U_{k,M_k}(\xi_x)/(2M_k)$, with $U_{k,M}$ formed from $U_k$
as in Lemma~\textup{\ref{lem:limitEtrunc}(ii)}, which take values in
$[-1,1]$, with $\frac12\ell_0$ and $4(1+2a_0)^8$ in place of $\ell_0$ and
$2(1+2a_0)^8$ and the right sides of \textup{(b)}, \textup{(c)} halved. For
these fields $Z_k\to\infty$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a) The spectrum is given by Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEtrunc}(i), since
$\int U_ku_1\,d\gamma_2=1$. Let $\rho(z)=\prod_\mu q_k^{|z_\mu|}=e^{-ma_k\|z\|_1}$ be the
covariance of $\phi^{(i)}_k$ and $E_x=e^{t_k\phi^{(2)}_k(x)-t_k^2/2}$. By
independence and centring, for $h$ supported in $B_{\varepsilon'}$ with
$\|h\|_\infty\le1$ and $(r+1)a_k\le\varepsilon'$,
\[
\omega_k\bigl(\vartheta(\overline{A_k(h)})A_k(h)\bigr)=\sum_{x,y}a_k^8
\overline{h(a_kx)}h(a_ky)\bigl(\rho+\lambda_k^2(e^{t_k^2\rho}-1)\bigr),
\]
with $\rho=\rho(y-\vartheta x)$, since $\E[(E_x-1)(E_y-1)]=e^{t_k^2\rho(x-y)}-1$
by \eqref{eq:limitEcover}. For $a_kx_0,a_ky_0\ge\varepsilon'$ the time
component of $y-\vartheta x$ is at least $2\varepsilon'/a_k-1$, so
$\rho\le e^{-m(2\varepsilon'-a_k)}$, and $\lambda_k^2e^{t_k^2\rho}\le
e^{-t_k^2(1-e^{-m(2\varepsilon'-a_k)})}\to0$. As $\sum_xa_k^4|h(a_kx)|\le
(1+2a_0)^4$ and $\rho\le1$, $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\le2(1+2a_0)^8$ for
large $k$. For the function $h_*\ge0$ of the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEheavy}(b), formed for $\varepsilon$ with
$\tau=\frac14$, both parts are nonnegative, and that proof gives
$\sum_{x,y}a_k^8h_*(a_kx)h_*(a_ky)\rho(y-\vartheta x)\ge\ell_0$; so
$\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\ge\ell_0$, and the ratio in (NE) with $g_0=h_*$
is at least $\ell_0/(2(1+2a_0)^8)$. Hence $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\le C_0\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)$ with $C_0=2(1+2a_0)^8/\ell_0$ for every $\varepsilon'\in
(0,\varepsilon]$ and large $k$, which is (SD$_{\mathrm p}$) with $p_0=0$.
(UV) follows by Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(ii), (RN$_1$) by
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEreflect}(iii) and (M$_{\mathrm s,2}$) by
Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(a), whose proofs use the state only through
the properties of Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEtrunc}(i). For $(f,g)\in\mathcal
