C_2(Q,\delta)$, $Q$ of side $L$, the sites carrying $f$ and $g$ have
$\|a_k(x-y)\|_1\ge\delta$, so the part of $Z_k^2\omega_k(A_k(f)A_k(g))$ with
$\lambda_k^2$ is at most $Z_k^2(L+1)^8e^{-t_k^2(1-e^{-m\delta})}\|f\|_\infty
\|g\|_\infty\to0$, and the other part is $Z_k^2$ times a Riemann sum of the
continuous function $f(x)g(y)e^{-m\|x-y\|_1}$; along a subsequence with
$Z_k^2\to z$ the limit is the stated one.

(b) Distinct $c_j$ differ by $3\eta$ along $e_1$, so the supports are at
$\ell^\infty$ distance at least $\eta$; they lie in
$[2\eta,(3n+1)\eta]\times[-\eta,\eta]^3$, of $\ell^1$ diameter at most
$(3n-1)\eta+6\eta=1/(4m)$. Expanding $\prod_jU_k(\xi_{x_j})$ over the sets
$I$ of indices that take the first summand, independence gives
\[
\E\prod_jU_k(\xi_{x_j})=\sum_I\E\Bigl[\prod_{j\in I}\phi^{(1)}_k(x_j)\Bigr]
\lambda_k^{n-|I|}\E\Bigl[\prod_{j\notin I}(E_{x_j}-1)\Bigr] .
\]
The first factor is a sum of products of the covariances $\rho>0$ by
Wick's formula, and the second is nonnegative by \eqref{eq:limitEcover};
the term $I=\emptyset$ and the term $G$ of all pairs in
\eqref{eq:limitEcover} give
\[
\E\prod_jU_k(\xi_{x_j})\ge\lambda_k^n\prod_{i<i'}\bigl(e^{t_k^2
\rho(x_i-x_{i'})}-1\bigr).
\]
For sites in the supports $\rho\ge e^{-1/4}\ge\frac34$; for
$\frac34t_k^2\ge\log2$, each factor is at least $\frac12e^{t_k^2\rho}$, and
the right side is at least $2^{-n(n-1)/2}\exp(t_k^2n(\frac38(n-1)-\frac12))
\ge2^{-n(n-1)/2}e^{nt_k^2/4}$, since $n\ge3$. All terms of the moment are
nonnegative; keeping the sites with $a_kx_j\in c_j+[-\frac\eta2,\frac\eta2]^4$,
at least $(\eta/(2a_k))^4$ for each $j$ when $a_k\le\eta/2$, and using
$Z_k^2\ge\frac12(1+2a_0)^{-8}$ from (a) gives the bound.

(c) The supports lie in $[\eta',3\eta']\times[2\eta',(3j+1)\eta']\times
[-\eta',\eta']^2$, at $\ell^\infty$ distance at least $\eta'$ from each other.
By Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i), $\vartheta(\bar P_k)=\prod_iA_k(f_i\circ
\theta_{0,k})$, and the $2j$ functions $f_i\circ\theta_{0,k}$, $f_i$ are
nonnegative with supports in $\{x_0\le-\eta'+a_k\}$ and $\{x_0\ge\eta'\}$,
whose union has $\ell^1$ diameter at most $(6\eta'+a_k)+(3j-1)\eta'+4\eta'
\le(3j+10)\eta'=1/(4m)$ for $a_k\le\eta'$. The proof of (b) with $n=2j$
applies, the map $x\mapsto\vartheta x$ being a bijection of $\Z^4$.

(d) Choose $M_k\ge k$ with $e_n(M_k)\le1/k$ for $n\le k$, $e_n$ formed with
$U_k$, and $\|U_{k,M_k}-U_k\|_2\le\frac12$. Then $\int U_{k,M_k}(u)u_1\,
d\gamma_2\ge\frac12$, and Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEtrunc} gives the spectrum and
shows that, for each fixed $n$ and fixed supports, the moments and the
reflected norms formed with $U_{k,M_k}$ differ from those of $U_k$ by
$O(1/k)$, uniformly in the functions of norm at most $1$ with supports in
the given cubes; in particular $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')$ changes by at
most $(1+2a_0)^8/k$. The field $U_{k,M_k}(\xi_x)/(2M_k)$ has packets
$A_k/(2M_k)$, reflected norms $\mathcal N_k/(4M_k^2)$ and $Z_k$ multiplied
by $2M_k$, so the moments of $Z_kA_k(f_j)$ are those formed with
$U_{k,M_k}$ and its own $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$, and the claims follow
from (a)--(c).
\end{proof}

The fields of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEthree} have every property that
this subsection uses at separated points, the gap included, and (SD),
(SD$_{\mathrm p}$), (UV), (NE) and nontrivial two-point limits. The
exponential part has variance $\lambda_k^2(e^{t_k^2}-1)=1-e^{-t_k^2}$ at
each site, correlations that vanish at every fixed distance, and moments of
every order $n\ge3$ that grow like $e^{nt_k^2/4}$ at points closer than
$1/(4m)$; it does not enter the two-point functions. So (RN$_m$), $m\ge2$,
does not follow from the invariance, the reflection positivity in the
coordinate hyperplanes, the transfer-matrix gap and the two-point
properties; any argument that uses the state only through these, such as a
chessboard or multiple-reflection estimate or a bound by powers of the
transfer matrix, gives neither (RN$_2$) nor (M$_{\mathrm s,3}$). The
choice of $Z_k$ does not matter: the unnormalised separated two-point moments
are bounded in $k$, so every normalisation for which some separated
two-point limit is nonzero has $\liminf_kZ_k>0$, and for it the moments in
(b) and (c) still tend to infinity; this applies to $Z_k=C_k(\sigma_0)^{-1/2}$
of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(ii) for $f_0\ge0$, $f_0\ne0$. Part (d)
takes into account that $\Phi$ is bounded for the Wilson measure: for
bounded fields (M$_{\mathrm s}$) is automatic when $Z_k$ is bounded, and in
(d) $Z_k\to\infty$.

The rate (G$_{\mathrm s}$) is a rate in (RN). Consider
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(RN$_{\mathrm p}$)},leftmargin=*]
\item there are $K_1,\beta_1,p_1,q_1\ge0$ such that for every $m\ge1$, every
cube $Q$ of side $L$ and every $\delta\in(0,1]$ there is $k_0$ with
\[
Z_k^{2m}\omega_k\bigl(\vartheta(\bar P_k)P_k\bigr)\le K_1^m(m!)^{\beta_1}
(1+L)^{q_1m}\delta^{-p_1m}\prod_{j=1}^m\|f_j\|_\infty^2
\]
for $P_k=\prod_{j=1}^mA_k(f_j)$, all $k\ge k_0$ and all $(f_1,\dots,f_m)\in
\mathcal C_m(Q,\delta)$ with supports in $\{x_0\ge\delta\}$.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitErate}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}}.

\textup{(i)} \textup{(G$_{\mathrm s}$)} implies \textup{(RN$_{\mathrm p}$)}
with $K_1=4^\beta7^{2q}K^2$, $\beta_1=2\beta$, $q_1=2q$, $p_1=2p$.

\textup{(ii)} \textup{(RN$_{\mathrm p}$)} implies \textup{(G$_{\mathrm s}$)}
with $K=2^8e^4\,5^{q_1/2}(16e^2)^{p_1/2}K_1^{1/2}$,
$\beta=4+\frac12(\beta_1+p_1)$, $q=4+\frac12q_1$, $p=4+\frac12p_1$.

\textup{(iii)} With $Z_k=\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$ as in
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEtwo}}, the case $m=1$ of the bound in
\textup{(RN$_{\mathrm p}$)}, with its own constants, holds if and only if
\textup{(SD$_{\mathrm p}$)} holds. So under \textup{(SD$_{\mathrm p}$)},
\textup{(G$_{\mathrm s}$)} holds if and only if the cases $m\ge2$ hold with
constants of the form in \textup{(RN$_{\mathrm p}$)}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Let $(f_j)\in\mathcal C_m(Q,\delta)$ have supports in $\{x_0\ge\delta\}$,
$Q=\prod_\nu[q_\nu,q_\nu+L]$, let $s\ge\delta$ be the least time in the
supports, $n_0=\lfloor(s-\delta)/a_k\rfloor$, $v_\nu=\lfloor q_\nu/a_k\rfloor$
for $\nu\ne0$, $v_\perp=(0,v_1,v_2,v_3)$, and $f'_j=f_j(\cdot+a_k(n_0e_0+
v_\perp))$, supported in $[\delta,L+2]\times[0,L+1]^3$ for $a_k\le1$. With
$P'_k=\prod_jA_k(f'_j)$, $P_k=\tau_{n_0e_0}\tau_{v_\perp}P'_k$ by
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i), and, as in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox}, $\omega_k(\vartheta(\bar P_k)P_k)=\|\mathbb
T_k^{n_0}\Psi_{\tau_{v_\perp}P'_k}\|^2\le\omega_k(\vartheta(\bar P'_k)
P'_k)$. The last is the moment of the $2m$ functions $\bar f'_j\circ
\theta_{0,k}$, $f'_j$, which lie in $Q''=[-L-3,L+3]^4$ and are pairwise at
$\ell^\infty$ distance at least $\delta$ for $a_k\le\delta$, the reflected
ones being supported in $\{x_0\le-\delta+a_k\}$. (G$_{\mathrm s}$) with
$n=2m$ and $Q''$, of side $2L+6$, gives the bound with $((2m)!)^\beta\le
4^{\beta m}(m!)^{2\beta}$ and $7+2L\le7(1+L)$.

(ii) The moments of order one vanish. For $n\ge2$, part (ii) of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEreflect} gives (M$_{\mathrm s,n}$) with
$M_n(Q,\delta)=\mathsf K_{2nL/\delta}^n\max_i(R_iR_{n-i})^{1/2}$, $R_i$ taken
at $Q^*$, of side $2L+4$, and $\delta_n=\delta/(16(n+1))\le1$. By
(RN$_{\mathrm p}$) and $i!(n-i)!\le n!$,
\begin{multline*}
(R_iR_{n-i})^{1/2}\le K_1^{n/2}(n!)^{\beta_1/2}\bigl(5(1+L)\bigr)^{q_1n/2}\\
\times\bigl(16(n+1)\bigr)^{p_1n/2}\delta^{-p_1n/2} .
\end{multline*}
From $e^n\ge n^n/n!$, $(n+1)^n\le e\,n^n\le e^{n+1}n!\le e^{2n}n!$. For
$\delta\le1$, $4nL/\delta+3\le4n(1+L)/\delta$, so $\mathsf K_{2nL/\delta}^n
\le4^{4n}n^{4n}(1+L)^{4n}\delta^{-4n}\le4^{4n}e^{4n}(n!)^4(1+L)^{4n}
\delta^{-4n}$. Multiplying gives the constants stated.

(iii) If (SD$_{\mathrm p}$) holds, Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox} gives, for $f$
supported in a cube of side $L$ in $\{x_0\ge\delta\}$ and large $k$,
\[
Z_k^2\omega_k\bigl(\vartheta(\overline{A_k(f)})A_k(f)\bigr)\le\mathsf K_L^2
\|f\|_\infty^2\frac{\mathcal N_k(\delta)}{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)} ,
\]
with $\mathsf K_L^2\le3^8(1+L)^8$. The ratio is at most $C_0\max(1,\varepsilon)^{p_0}
\delta^{-p_0}$ for $\delta\le\varepsilon$ and at most $\mathsf K^2$ for
$\delta>\varepsilon$, by Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(i). Conversely, if
the case $m=1$ holds, let $\varepsilon'\in(0,\varepsilon]$ and $h$ be
supported in $B_{\varepsilon'}\subset[0,L_0]^4$, $L_0=\varepsilon+1+a_0$;
the bound with $\delta=\min(\varepsilon',1)$ gives $\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon')\le C_0(\varepsilon/\varepsilon')^{p_1}\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)$ with $C_0=K_1(1+L_0)^{q_1}\max(1,\varepsilon^{-p_1})$.
The last claim follows from (i), (ii).
\end{proof}

Bounds on each moment do not give the factor $K^n(n!)^\beta$: in the next
fields every moment is bounded uniformly in the separation, and
(G$_{\mathrm s}$) fails.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEfactorial}
In the setting of Lemma~\textup{\ref{lem:limitEtrunc}} let $t>0$,
$U(u)=e^{tu_2-t^2/2}-1$, $\varepsilon>0$, form $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$
with $\Phi_x=U(\xi_x)$, and put $Z_k=\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$, with
$\ell_0$ as in Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:limitEthree}}.

\textup{(a)} The gap is $m$ in physical units. For large $k$, $t^2\ell_0\le
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\le(1+2a_0)^8(e^{t^2}-1)$, \textup{(SD$_{\mathrm
p}$)} holds with $p_0=0$, \textup{(NE)} holds, and for every $n$ and all
$f_j$ supported in a cube of side $L$, separated or not,
\[
\Bigl|\omega_k\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^nZ_kA_k(f_j)\Bigr)\Bigr|\le
\frac{2^ne^{t^2n(n-1)/2}(L+1)^{4n}}{(t^2\ell_0)^{n/2}}\prod_{j=1}^n\|f_j\|
_\infty ;
\]
in particular \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)} and \textup{(RN)} hold.

\textup{(b)} Let $n$ be even with $n\ge e\log2/t^2+\frac12$, $s=\lceil
n^{1/4}\rceil$, $\eta=1/(12(1+m)s)$, let $v_1,\dots,v_n$ be distinct points of
$\{0,\dots,s-1\}^4$, and let $f_j$ be as in
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:limitEthree}(b)} with centres $c_j=3\eta v_j$.
Then $(f_j)\in\mathcal C_n(Q,\eta)$ for a cube $Q$ of side at most $1$, and
for large $k$
\[
Z_k^n\,\omega_k\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^nA_k(f_j)\Bigr)\ge\frac{(\eta/2)^{4n}
e^{t^2n(n-1)/(2e)}}{2^{n+1}\bigl((1+2a_0)^8(e^{t^2}-1)\bigr)^{n/2}} .
\]
\textup{(G$_{\mathrm s}$)} fails for all $K,\beta,p,q$, and no limit of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}} satisfies the bound of
\textup{(G$_{\mathrm s}$)}.

\textup{(c)} For every sequence $M_k\to\infty$, \textup{(a)} and \textup{(b)}
hold for the fields $U_{M_k}(\xi_x)/(2M_k)$, with values in $[-1,1]$, with
$4^n$ in place of $2^n$, $\frac12t^2\ell_0$ in place of $t^2\ell_0$, and the
right side of \textup{(b)} halved, for $k$ large depending on $n$; for these
fields $Z_k\to\infty$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a) The gap is Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEtrunc}(i), with $\int U(u)u_2\,d\gamma_2
=t$. As in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEthree}(a), the
reflected norm of $A_k(h)$ is $\sum_{x,y}a_k^8\overline{h(a_kx)}h(a_ky)
(e^{t^2\rho}-1)$, with $\rho=\rho(y-\vartheta x)\in(0,1]$; the upper bound
follows, and the lower one from $e^{t^2\rho}-1\ge t^2\rho$ and the function
$h_*$, which also bounds the ratio in (NE) with $g_0=h_*$ below by
$t^2\ell_0/((1+2a_0)^8(e^{t^2}-1))$, and (SD$_{\mathrm p}$) with $p_0=0$. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEtrunc}(ii) the moments are at most $\|U\|_n^n\prod_j
(L+1)^4\|f_j\|_\infty$, and $\|U\|_n\le\|e^{tu_2-t^2/2}\|_n+1=e^{t^2(n-1)/2}
+1\le2e^{t^2(n-1)/2}$. This gives (M$_{\mathrm s}$), and (RN) by
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEreflect}(i).

(b) Distinct $v_j$ differ by at least $1$ in some coordinate, so the supports
are at $\ell^\infty$ distance at least $\eta$; they lie in a cube of side
$3\eta s\le1$, of $\ell^1$ diameter at most $12\eta s\le1/m$, so
$\rho\ge e^{-1}$ for all sites in them. By \eqref{eq:limitEcover} and its
monotonicity, $\E\prod_jU(\xi_{x_j})$ is at least its value for the
Gaussian vector $X_i=e^{-1/2}Y+(1-e^{-1})^{1/2}W_i$ with $Y,W_1,\dots,W_n$
independent standard normal, whose covariances are all $e^{-1}$.
Conditionally on $Y$ the factors are independent, and with
$\sigma=te^{-1/2}$ this value is $\E[(e^{\sigma Y-\sigma^2/2}-1)^n]$. For
$n$ even the integrand is nonnegative, and it is at least
$2^{-n}e^{n\sigma Y-n\sigma^2/2}$ where $y_0=(\log2+\sigma^2/2)/\sigma\le
Y$. Since $\E[e^{n\sigma Y}\mathbf 1_{\{Y\ge y_0\}}]=e^{n^2\sigma^2/2}
\mathbb P(Y\ge y_0-n\sigma)\ge\frac12e^{n^2\sigma^2/2}$ for $n\sigma\ge
y_0$, that is $n\ge\log2/\sigma^2+\frac12$, the value is at least
$2^{-n-1}e^{\sigma^2n(n-1)/2}$. Keeping the sites with $a_kx_j\in
c_j+[-\frac\eta2,\frac\eta2]^4$, as in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEthree}(b), gives the bound. If
(G$_{\mathrm s}$) held, these moments would be at most $K^n(n!)^\beta
2^{qn}\eta^{-pn}$, and $\eta^{-1}\le24(1+m)n^{1/4}$; the logarithm of this
is $O(n\log n)$, while the logarithm of the lower bound is $t^2n(n-1)/(2e)-
O(n\log n)$, a contradiction for large even $n$. The limits of
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep}(i) satisfy the same lower bound.

(c) By Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEtrunc}(ii), $|\E\prod_jU_{M_k}(\zeta_j)|\le
(2\|U\|_n)^n$, the moments and reflected norms change by at most
$e_n(M_k)\prod_j(L_j+1)^4\|f_j\|_\infty\to0$ for each fixed $n$, and
$\int U_{M_k}(u)u_2\,d\gamma_2\to t$; the rescaling by $2M_k$ is that of the
proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEthree}(d).
\end{proof}

One inequality between the moments and the separated two-point functions
gives (M$_{\mathrm s}$) and (G$_{\mathrm s}$) from (SD) and
(SD$_{\mathrm p}$). For a cube $Q$ and $\delta>0$ put
\[
\mu_k(Q,\delta)=\sup Z_k^2\bigl|\omega_k\bigl(A_k(h)A_k(h')\bigr)\bigr| ,
\]
the supremum over the $(h,h')\in\mathcal C_2(Q,\delta)$ with
$\|h\|_\infty,\|h'\|_\infty\le1$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(GD)},leftmargin=*]
\item There are $K_0,\beta_0\ge0$ such that for every $n\ge3$, every cube $Q$
and every $\delta>0$ there is $k_0$ with
\[
\Bigl|\omega_k\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^nZ_kA_k(f_j)\Bigr)\Bigr|\le K_0^n(n!)^{
\beta_0}\mu_k(Q,\delta)^{n/2}\prod_{j=1}^n\|f_j\|_\infty
\]
for all $k\ge k_0$ and $(f_1,\dots,f_n)\in\mathcal C_n(Q,\delta)$.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEdomination}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}}.

\textup{(i)} Under \textup{(GD)}, \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s,2}$)} implies
\textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)}. With $Z_k=\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$ as in
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEtwo}}, \textup{(GD)} and \textup{(SD)} imply
\textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)}, and \textup{(GD)} and \textup{(SD$_{\mathrm p}$)}
imply \textup{(G$_{\mathrm s}$)} with $K=\max(1,K_0)C_2^{1/2}$, $\beta=\beta_0$,
$q=4$, $p=4+\frac12p_0$, where $C_2=(\frac{77}2)^8\max(1,C_0)\max(1,
8\varepsilon)^{p_0}$.

\textup{(ii)} If for every $k$ the packets $A_k(f)$ form a centred Gaussian
family, \textup{(GD)} holds with $K_0=1$, $\beta_0=\frac12$.

\textup{(iii)} \textup{(GD)} fails for $n=3$ in the fields of
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:limitEthree}}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) (M$_{\mathrm s,2}$) gives $\mu_k(Q,\delta)\le M_2(Q,\delta)$ for large
$k$, and Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(a) gives (M$_{\mathrm s,2}$) from
(SD). Under (SD$_{\mathrm p}$) the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEtwotempered}(a) gives $\mu_k(Q,\delta)\le
C_2(1+L)^8\delta^{-8-p_0}$ for $Q$ of side $L$, $\delta\le1$ and large $k$,
which is the case $n=2$; the moments of order one vanish.

(ii) By Wick's formula the moment is $0$ for odd $n$ and, for even $n$, the
sum over the $(n-1)!!$ perfect matchings of the products of the
$\omega_k(A_k(f_a)A_k(f_b))$; each pair lies in $\mathcal C_2(Q,\delta)$, and
$((n-1)!!)^2\le(n-1)!!\,n!!=n!$.

(iii) There (M$_{\mathrm s,2}$) holds, so $\mu_k(Q,\eta)$ is bounded, while
the left side of (GD) for the functions of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEthree}(b) with $n=3$ tends to infinity.
\end{proof}

So the case $m=1$ of (RN) is (SD), and its rate is (SD$_{\mathrm p}$)
(Propositions~\ref{prop:limitEreflect}(iii) and~\ref{prop:limitErate}(iii));
the cases $m\ge2$ are a further input on the Wilson measure, which the
two-point properties, reflection positivity and the gap do not supply
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEthree}); (G$_{\mathrm s}$) is (RN) with
constants $K_1^m(m!)^{\beta_1}(1+L)^{q_1m}\delta^{-p_1m}$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitErate}), and bounds on each moment do not give
the dependence on $n$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEfactorial}). The single
inequality (GD), of Gaussian domination type, gives (M$_{\mathrm s}$) from
(SD) and (G$_{\mathrm s}$) from (SD$_{\mathrm p}$); it holds for Gaussian
packets, fails in the fields of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEthree}, and is not
proved here for the Wilson measure.

\emph{The effective energy.} Along each lattice of distances from the
reflection plane the largest reflected norm is logarithmically convex, and
(SD) becomes a bound, in physical units, on its rate of decay. For
$(r+2)a_k\le s$ with $\mathcal N_k(s)>0$ put
\[
\mathfrak E_k(s)=\frac1{2a_k}\log\frac{\mathcal N_k(s)}{\mathcal N_k(s+a_k)} .
\]\footnote{For one correlator this is the effective mass of lattice
spectroscopy; here it is taken of the largest reflected norm, a supremum over
packets.}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:limitElogconvex}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:avgtransfer}}, let
$(r+2)a_k\le s$ and $\mathcal N(n)=\mathcal N_k(s+na_k)$ for integers $n\ge0$.

\textup{(i)} $\mathcal N(n)$ is the supremum of $\|\mathbb T_k^n
\Psi_{A_k(h)}\|^2$ over $h$ supported in $B_s$ with $\|h\|_\infty\le1$;
$\mathcal N(n+1)\le\gamma_k^2\mathcal N(n)$, and $\mathcal N(n)^2\le
\mathcal N(n-1)\mathcal N(n+1)$ for $n\ge1$.

\textup{(ii)} If $\mathcal N(0)>0$, then $\mathcal N(n)>0$ for every $n$, and
$n\mapsto\mathfrak E_k(s+na_k)$ is nonincreasing, with values in
$[a_k^{-1}\log(1/\gamma_k),\infty)\subset[m_*,\infty)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(i) Since $B_{s+na_k}=B_s+na_ke_0$, $h$ is supported in $B_{s+na_k}$ if and
only if $h'=h(\cdot+na_ke_0)$ is supported in $B_s$, and then $A_k(h)=
\tau_nA_k(h')$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i), so $\Psi_{A_k(h)}=
\mathbb T_k^n\Psi_{A_k(h')}$ as in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(iii). The packets are centred, so
$\Psi_{A_k(h')}\perp\Omega_k$ and $\|\mathbb T_k^{n+1}\Psi_{A_k(h')}\|\le
\gamma_k\|\mathbb T_k^n\Psi_{A_k(h')}\|$. For a vector $\Psi$ and
$f(n)=\|\mathbb T_k^n\Psi\|^2=\ip{\mathbb T_k^{n-1}\Psi}{\mathbb
T_k^{n+1}\Psi}$, the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality gives $f(n)^2\le f(n-1)
f(n+1)\le\mathcal N(n-1)\mathcal N(n+1)$ when $\Psi=\Psi_{A_k(h')}$;
the supremum over $h'$ gives the last claim.

(ii) For $h'$ supported in $B_s$, $h''=h'(\cdot+a_ke_0)$ is supported in
$\{x_0\ge s-a_k\}$ and $(r+1)a_k\le s-a_k$, so $\Psi_{A_k(h')}=\mathbb T_k
\Psi_{A_k(h'')}$ lies in the range of $\mathbb T_k$, which is orthogonal to
$\ker\mathbb T_k=\ker\mathbb T_k^n$. So $\mathbb T_k^n\Psi_{A_k(h')}\ne0$
when $\Psi_{A_k(h')}\ne0$, and $\mathcal N(n)>0$ by (i). The convexity in
(i) says that $\log\mathcal N(n-1)-\log\mathcal N(n)\ge\log\mathcal N(n)-
\log\mathcal N(n+1)$, and the bound $\mathcal N(n+1)\le\gamma_k^2
\mathcal N(n)$ gives the lower bound, with $\gamma_k\le e^{-m_*a_k}$.
\end{proof}

For a single packet, $(2a_k)^{-1}\log(\|\mathbb T_k^n\Psi\|^2/
\|\mathbb T_k^{n+1}\Psi\|^2)$ decreases, as $n\to\infty$, to the least point
of the support of its spectral measure, written in physical energy; so
$\mathfrak E_k(s)$ is the energy, in physical units, at which the largest
reflected norm decays at the distance $s$ from the plane.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEenergy}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEtwo}}, and let
$C_{\varepsilon'}$ be as in Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:limitEsd}(iii)}.

\textup{(i)} \textup{(SD)} holds if and only if $\limsup_k\mathfrak
E_k(s)<\infty$ for every $s\in(0,\varepsilon)$. Under \textup{(SD)},
$\limsup_k\mathfrak E_k(s)\le\log(\mathsf K^4C_{s/2})/s$; conversely, for
$\varepsilon'\in(0,\varepsilon)$,
\[
\limsup_k\frac{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')}{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)}\le
\mathsf K^2\exp\Bigl(2(\varepsilon-\varepsilon')\limsup_k\mathfrak
E_k(\varepsilon')\Bigr).
\]

\textup{(ii)} Under \textup{(SD$_{\mathrm p}$)}, for every $s\in(0,
\varepsilon]$ and all large $k$,
\[
\mathfrak E_k(s)\le\frac{\log(\mathsf K^4C_0)+p_0\log(2\varepsilon/s)}
{s-2a_k} .
\]
Conversely, if there are $p,E_0\ge0$ and, for every $\varepsilon'\in(0,
\varepsilon]$, a $k_0$ with $\mathfrak E_k(s)\le p/(2s)+E_0$ for all
$s\in[\varepsilon',\varepsilon]$ and $k\ge k_0$, then
\textup{(SD$_{\mathrm p}$)} holds with $p_0=p$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For $s\in(0,\varepsilon]$ and large $k$, $\mathcal N_k(s)\ge\mathsf K^{-2}
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)>0$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(i), so
$\mathfrak E_k(s)$ is defined. Let $n=\lfloor s/(2a_k)\rfloor$. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElogconvex}(ii), applied at $s-na_k$,
$\mathfrak E_k(s)\le\mathfrak E_k(s-ia_k)$ for $1\le i\le n$, so
\[
\mathfrak E_k(s)\le\frac1n\sum_{i=1}^n\mathfrak E_k(s-ia_k)=\frac1{2a_kn}
\log\frac{\mathcal N_k(s-na_k)}{\mathcal N_k(s)} .
\]
Since $s-na_k\ge s/2$ and $s\le\varepsilon$,
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(i) gives $\mathcal N_k(s-na_k)\le\mathsf
K^2\mathcal N_k(s/2)$ and $\mathcal N_k(s)\ge\mathsf K^{-2}\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)$, and $2a_kn\ge s-2a_k$. Hence
\[
\mathfrak E_k(s)\le\frac1{s-2a_k}\log\Bigl(\mathsf K^4\frac{\mathcal
N_k(s/2)}{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)}\Bigr),
\]
which gives the bound in (i) under (SD), and the bound in (ii) under
(SD$_{\mathrm p}$) at $\varepsilon'=s/2$.

Conversely, let $\varepsilon'\in(0,\varepsilon)$, $E'>\limsup_k\mathfrak
E_k(\varepsilon')$ and $n=\lceil(\varepsilon-\varepsilon')/a_k\rceil$. For
large $k$, Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElogconvex}(ii) gives
\[
\log\frac{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')}{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon'+na_k)}=
2a_k\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\mathfrak E_k(\varepsilon'+ja_k)\le2a_knE' ,
\]
and $2a_kn\le2(\varepsilon-\varepsilon'+a_k)$. Since $\varepsilon'+na_k\ge
\varepsilon$, Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(i) gives $\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon'+na_k)\le\mathsf K^2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$. Letting
$k\to\infty$ and $E'\downarrow\limsup_k\mathfrak E_k(\varepsilon')$ gives
the inequality of (i), hence (SD). Under the hypothesis of the converse in
(ii) the same sum is at most $\sum_j(pa_k/(\varepsilon'+ja_k)+2a_kE_0)\le
pa_k/\varepsilon'+p\log((\varepsilon+a_k)/\varepsilon')+2(\varepsilon+
a_k)E_0$, so for $a_k\le\min(\varepsilon',\varepsilon,1)$
\[
\frac{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')}{\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)}\le\mathsf
K^2e^{p+2(\varepsilon+1)E_0}2^p\Bigl(\frac\varepsilon{\varepsilon'}
\Bigr)^p ,
\]
which is (SD$_{\mathrm p}$) for $\varepsilon'<\varepsilon$; for
$\varepsilon'=\varepsilon$ it holds with $C_0\ge1$.
\end{proof}

So (SD) says that at every distance from the reflection plane the largest
reflected norm decays at a rate bounded in physical units, and
(SD$_{\mathrm p}$) that this rate grows at most like $p_0/(2s)$ as $s\to0$,
up to the logarithm in (ii). Under a gap $m_{\mathrm{lat}}$ in lattice units
the rate is at least $m_{\mathrm{lat}}/a_k$ at every distance, which is the
failure of (SD) in Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(iii). By
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(ii) the bound on the rate is equivalent to
the spectral condition (UV).

\emph{The abelian theory.} For $G=\U(1)$ the positivity behind
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEne}(ii) is a theorem.

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:limitEabelian}
Let $G=\U(1)$ with the Wilson weight and $\beta_k\ge0$, assume the
hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEtwo}}, and let
$\Phi=b_0-\sum_jb_j\cos\theta_j$ with $b_j\ge0$ for $j\ge1$ and each
$\theta_j$ an integer combination of the angles of the links with both
endpoints in $[-r,r]^4$; the sum of $1-\cos\theta_p$ over the $24$
plaquettes with a corner at $0$ is an example. Then $K_k(z)\ge0$ for all
$z\in\Z^4$. If \textup{(SD)} holds, then \textup{(NE)} holds, every limit
$S_2$ of Corollary~\textup{\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b)} is not identically zero,
and $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\le\omega_k(\vartheta(A_k(g_0))A_k(g_0))$ for
large $k$ and every $g_0$ as in Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:limitEne}(ii)}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Let $\mu_L$ be the Wilson measure on $\Lambda_L$. Its density with respect
to the product of the normalised Lebesgue measures on the link angles is
$Z^{-1}\exp(\beta_k\sum_p\cos\theta_p)$, where each $\theta_p$ is a signed
sum of four link angles. By Ginibre's inequality~\cite{Gin70},
$\int\cos\theta\cos\theta'd\mu_L\ge\int\cos\theta\,d\mu_L\int\cos\theta'
d\mu_L$ for any two integer combinations $\theta,\theta'$ of link angles.
So $\Cov_{\mu_L}(\Phi_0,\Phi_z)=\sum_{j,l\ge1}b_jb_l\Cov_{\mu_L}(\cos
\theta_j,\cos\tau_z\theta_l)\ge0$ once $L$ is large, and the torus limit
preserves this inequality for the local functions $\Phi_0$, $\Phi_z$. This
is the hypothesis of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEne}(ii), whose proof gives
the last claim, and Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEne}(i) gives the others.
\end{proof}

For $\U(1)$ the averaged condition fails in the Coulomb phase of the Villain
theory (Corollary~\ref{cor:avgcoulomb}), and (SD) fails under a gap in
lattice units (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(iii)); whether the
hypotheses of the corollary hold together for $\U(1)$ along any tuning is
not decided here. What the corollary excludes is the mechanism of the
example below, whose two-point function changes sign.

\emph{Spatial momentum.} The spatial translations act on the reconstructed
space. For $v\in\Z^4$ with $v_0=0$, $\omega_k(\vartheta(\tau_v\bar F)
\tau_vF')=\omega_k(\tau_v(\vartheta(\bar F)F'))=\omega_k(\vartheta(\bar F)F')$
for $F,F'$ at positive times, so $\Psi_F\mapsto\Psi_{\tau_vF}$ extends to a
unitary $U_k(v)$; $U_k$ is a representation of $\Z^3$ that commutes with
$\mathbb T_k$, since $\tau_v\tau_1=\tau_1\tau_v$. By the spectral theorem for
the commuting unitaries $U_k(e_i)$ there is a projection-valued measure
$\mathsf P_k$ on $(-\pi,\pi]^3$ with $U_k(v)=\int e^{ip\cdot v}\,d\mathsf
P_k(p)$. For $K>0$ let $\mathbb P_k(K)=\mathsf P_k(\{|p|_\infty\le Ka_k\})$,
the states of spatial momentum at most $K$ in physical units; it commutes
with $\mathbb T_k$ and $\mathbb E_k(\Lambda)$. For $(r+1)a_k\le s$ let
$\mathcal N^{\mathrm{sp}}_k(s;K)$ be the supremum of $\|\mathbb P_k(K)
\Psi_{A_k(h)}\|^2$ over $h$ supported in $B_s$ with $\|h\|_\infty\le1$, and
consider
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(LM)},leftmargin=*]
\item for some $K<\infty$, $c>0$ and $\varepsilon_1\in(0,\varepsilon]$,
$\mathcal N^{\mathrm{sp}}_k(\varepsilon_1;K)\ge c\,\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$
for all large $k$.
\end{enumerate}
It says that, up to a fixed fraction, the largest reflected norm is carried
by spatial momenta bounded in physical units, as (UV) says for the energy.
The proofs below use the Fej\'er weights: for an integer $N\ge1$,
\[
F_N(q)=\sum_{|n|<N}\frac1N\Bigl(1-\frac{|n|}N\Bigr)e^{iqn}=\Bigl(\frac{\sin
(Nq/2)}{N\sin(q/2)}\Bigr)^2
\]
for $0<|q|\le\pi$, $F_N(0)=1$. Then $0\le F_N\le1$, $F_N(q)\ge(2/\pi)^2$ for
$N|q|\le\pi$, because $\sin x\ge2x/\pi$ on $[0,\frac\pi2]$ and $N\sin(|q|/2)
\le N|q|/2$, and $F_N(q)\le\pi^2/(Nq)^2$ for $0<|q|\le\pi$, because
$\sin(|q|/2)\ge|q|/\pi$. For $v\in\Z^4$ with $v_0=0$ put $f^N_v=\prod_{i=1}^3
N^{-1}(1-|v_i|/N)_+$, so that $\sum_vf^N_v=1$ and $\sum_vf^N_ve^{ip\cdot v}=
F_N^{(3)}(p)=\prod_iF_N(p_i)$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitElm}
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEtwo}} and
\textup{(SD)}. Then \textup{(NE)} holds if and only if \textup{(LM)} holds.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Two facts are used. First, for $g$ supported in a cube of side $L$ in
$\{x_0\ge s\}$, $(r+1)a_k\le s$, the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox}
writes $\Psi_{A_k(g)}$ as a sum of at most $\mathsf K_L$ vectors $\mathbb
T_k^nU_k(v)\Psi_{A_k(h')}$ with $h'$ supported in $B_s$ and $\|h'\|_\infty
\le\|g\|_\infty$; since $\mathbb P_k(K)$ commutes with $\mathbb T_k$ and
$U_k$,
\[
\|\mathbb P_k(K)\Psi_{A_k(g)}\|\le\mathsf K_L\,\mathcal N^{\mathrm{sp}}_k
(s;K)^{1/2}\|g\|_\infty .
\]
Second, for $g$ supported in $\{x_0\ge s\}$ and $g^N=\sum_vf^N_vg(\cdot-
a_kv)$, Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i) gives $A_k(g^N)=\sum_vf^N_v\tau_v
A_k(g)$, so $\Psi_{A_k(g^N)}=F^{(3)}_N(\mathsf P_k)\Psi_{A_k(g)}$, where
$F^{(3)}_N(\mathsf P_k)=\int F^{(3)}_N\,d\mathsf P_k$. By (SD) and
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(i), for every $s>0$ there is $C(s)$ with
$\mathcal N_k(s)\le C(s)\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$ for large $k$.

Let (NE) hold with $g_0$ supported in $\{x_0\ge t_0\}$, $t_0>0$, and in a
cube of side $L$, and $c_0>0$ with $4c_0<\liminf_kR_k(g_0)$. Let $\delta\in(0,1]$,
$a_k\le\delta$ and $N=\lfloor\delta/a_k\rfloor\ge\delta/(2a_k)$. The
function $g_0^N-g_0$ is supported in a cube of side $L+2$ in $\{x_0\ge t_0\}$
and bounded by $\varrho(\delta)=\sup\{|g_0(y)-g_0(y')|:|y-y'|_\infty\le
\delta\}$, because $f^N_v\ne0$ only for $|a_kv|_\infty<\delta$. For
$|p|_\infty>Ka_k$ some $|p_i|>Ka_k$, so $F^{(3)}_N(p)\le(\pi/(NKa_k))^2\le
(2\pi/(\delta K))^2$. With Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox},
\[
\begin{split}
&\|(1-\mathbb P_k(K))\Psi_{A_k(g_0)}\|\\
&\quad\le\|\Psi_{A_k(g_0-g_0^N)}\|+
\|(1-\mathbb P_k(K))F^{(3)}_N(\mathsf P_k)\Psi_{A_k(g_0)}\|\\
&\quad\le\Bigl(\mathsf K_{L+2}\varrho(\delta)+\frac{4\pi^2\mathsf K_L
\|g_0\|_\infty}{\delta^2K^2}\Bigr)\mathcal N_k(t_0)^{1/2} .
\end{split}
\]
Fix $\delta$, and then $K$, so that the bracket is at most
$(c_0/C(t_0))^{1/2}$. For large $k$ this gives
\[
\|\mathbb P_k(K)\Psi_{A_k(g_0)}\|\ge(2c_0^{1/2}-c_0^{1/2})\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon)^{1/2},
\]
and the first fact
gives $\mathcal N^{\mathrm{sp}}_k(t_0;K)\ge c_0\mathsf K_L^{-2}\|g_0\|
_\infty^{-2}\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$. If $t_0\le\varepsilon$ this is (LM)
with $\varepsilon_1=t_0$. If $t_0>\varepsilon$, every $h$ supported in
$B_{t_0}$ is $h'(\cdot-na_ke_0)$ with $n\ge0$ and $h'$ supported in a cube of
side $1+a_0$ in $\{x_0\ge\varepsilon\}$, so $\|\mathbb P_k(K)\Psi_{A_k(h)}\|
\le\|\mathbb P_k(K)\Psi_{A_k(h')}\|$ and $\mathcal N^{\mathrm{sp}}_k(t_0;K)
\le\mathsf K^2\mathcal N^{\mathrm{sp}}_k(\varepsilon;K)$, which is (LM) with
$\varepsilon_1=\varepsilon$.

Conversely, let (LM) hold and, for large $k$, $h_k$ be supported in
$B_{\varepsilon_1}$ with $\|h_k\|_\infty\le1$ and $\|\mathbb P_k(K)\Psi_k\|^2
\ge\frac c2\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$, $\Psi_k=\Psi_{A_k(h_k)}$.

\emph{Energy.} Let $n=\lfloor\varepsilon_1/(2a_k)\rfloor$ and $h'_k=h_k(
\cdot+na_ke_0)$, supported in a cube of side $1+a_0$ in $\{x_0\ge
\varepsilon_1/2\}$. Then $\Psi_k=\mathbb T_k^n\Psi_{A_k(h'_k)}$ and
$\|\mathbb T_k^n(1-\mathbb E_k(\Lambda))\|\le e^{-\Lambda a_kn}$, so for
$a_k\le\varepsilon_1/4$, by Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox},
\[
\|(1-\mathbb E_k(\Lambda))\Psi_k\|\le e^{-\Lambda\varepsilon_1/4}\mathsf K
\,C(\tfrac{\varepsilon_1}2)^{1/2}\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{1/2} .
\]
Fix $\Lambda$ with
\[
e^{-\Lambda\varepsilon_1/4}\mathsf KC(\tfrac{\varepsilon_1}2)^{1/2}\le(c/8)^{1/2}.
\]
Since $\mathbb E_k(\Lambda)$ and $\mathbb P_k(K)$
commute, $\|\mathbb E_k(\Lambda)\mathbb P_k(K)\Psi_k\|\ge(c/8)^{1/2}
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{1/2}$.

\emph{Smoothing.} Let $\sigma>0$, $M=\lfloor\sigma/a_k\rfloor$, $N=
\lfloor\pi/(Ka_k)\rfloor$, both at least $1$, $w_n=M^{-1}(1-|n-M|/M)_+$ for
$n\in\Z$, which vanish unless $1\le n\le2M-1$ and add up to $1$, and
\[
g_k=\sum_{n,v}w_nf^N_v\,h_k(\cdot-a_k(ne_0+v)) .
\]
By Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(i), $\Psi_{A_k(g_k)}=W(\mathbb T_k)
F^{(3)}_N(\mathsf P_k)\Psi_k$ with $W(\lambda)=\sum_nw_n\lambda^n\ge
\lambda^{2M}$ on $[0,1]$. On the range of $\mathbb E_k(\Lambda)\mathbb
P_k(K)$ the joint spectrum of $\mathbb T_k$ and $U_k$ lies where
$\lambda\ge e^{-\Lambda a_k}$, so $W\ge e^{-2\Lambda\sigma}$, and
$|p|_\infty\le Ka_k$, so $N|p_i|\le\pi$ and $F^{(3)}_N\ge(2/\pi)^6$. Hence
\[
\omega_k\bigl(\vartheta(\overline{A_k(g_k)})A_k(g_k)\bigr)\ge4c'
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon),
\]
with $4c'=\frac c8e^{-4\Lambda\sigma}(2/\pi)^{12}$.

\emph{Compactness.} $A_k(g)$ depends only on the values of $g$ on $a_k\Z^4$.
Let $u(t)=M^{-1}(1-|t/a_k-M|/M)_+$, $\varsigma(t)=N^{-1}(1-|t/a_k|/N)_+$,
$\kappa(y)=u(y_0)\prod_{i=1}^3\varsigma(y_i)$ and
\[
G_k(y)=\sum_{z\in\Z^4}\kappa(y-a_kz)h_k(a_kz) .
\]
Then $G_k(a_kx)=g_k(a_kx)$, so $A_k(G_k)=A_k(g_k)$. The function $G_k$ is supported in $S=B_{\varepsilon_1}+[0,
2\sigma]\times[-\pi/K,\pi/K]^3$, a compact subset of $\{x_0\ge
\varepsilon_1\}$ independent of $k$. For $t,t'\in\R$, at most $2M$ terms of
$\sum_{n\in\Z}u(t-a_kn)$ are nonzero, so this sum is at most $2$, and
$\sum_n|u(t-a_kn)-u(t'-a_kn)|\le4|t-t'|/(Ma_k)\le8|t-t'|/\sigma$; likewise
$\sum_n\varsigma(t-a_kn)\le2$ and $\sum_n|\varsigma(t-a_kn)-\varsigma(t'-
a_kn)|\le8K|t-t'|/\pi$, for $a_k\le\min(\sigma,\pi/K)$. Writing the
difference of two products of four factors as a telescoping sum, $|G_k|\le
16$ and $G_k$ is Lipschitz with constant $8(8/\sigma+24K/\pi)$ for the
$\ell^\infty$ norm. By the Arzel\`a--Ascoli theorem the $G_k$ lie in a
compact subset of $C(S)$.

\emph{One packet.} Let $L_S$ be the side of a cube containing $S$ and
$\eta>0$ with $\mathsf K_{L_S}C(\varepsilon_1)^{1/2}\eta\le c'^{1/2}$. A
finite set $G^1,\dots,G^J$ of the $G_k$ is an $\eta$-net, so for each large
$k$ there is $j$ with $\|G_k-G^j\|_\infty\le\eta$, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:limitEbox} gives $\|\Psi_{A_k(G_k)}-\Psi_{A_k(G^j)}\|\le
c'^{1/2}\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{1/2}$, hence $R_k(G^j)\ge c'$. Let
$g_0=\sum_jG^j(\cdot-jDe_1)$ with $D$ dyadic. As in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEne}(i), for large $k$
\[
R_k(g_0)\ge\sum_jR_k(G^j)-16^2J^2\mathsf K_{L_S}^2e^{-m_*(D-L_S-4
\varepsilon-1)} ,
\]
and $R_k\ge0$. For $D$ large the right side is at least $c'/2$ for all large
$k$, and $g_0$ is continuous and supported in $\{x_0\ge\varepsilon_1\}$:
this is (NE).
\end{proof}

\emph{An example.} (NE) is not implied by (SD) together with the properties
used so far: in the following Gaussian field the largest reflected norms
are carried by spatial momenta of order $\pi/a_k$ at energies bounded in
physical units, so that (UV) holds and (LM) fails.\footnote{$D_k$ depends on $p$ only through the product of the four
factors $2-2\cos p_\mu$, so the energy of a spatial momentum is small only
where all three spatial factors are large, near the corner $(\pi,\pi,\pi)$
of the Brillouin zone; the packets that carry the largest reflected norm live
there, and smooth test functions do not see them.}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:limitEflat}
Let $m>0$, $A_k=(128(\cosh(ma_k)-1))^{-1}$,
\[
D_k(p)=1+A_k\prod_{\mu=0}^3(2-2\cos p_\mu),\qquad p\in(-\pi,\pi]^4,
\]
let $\phi_k$ be the centred Gaussian field on $\Z^4$ with covariance
$\int e^{ip\cdot(x-y)}D_k(p)^{-1}dp/(2\pi)^4$, $A_k(f)=\sum_xa_k^4f(a_kx)
\phi_k(x)$, and form $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$ and $R_k$ with these
packets and $r=0$.

\textup{(a)} The law of $\phi_k$ is invariant under translations and $B_4$
and reflection positive for the reflections in coordinate hyperplanes through
sites and bisecting links. Its transfer matrix in each coordinate direction
has spectrum on $\Omega_k^\perp$ in $[0,e^{-ma_k}]$ containing
$e^{-ma_k}$: the gap is $m$ in physical units.

\textup{(b)} For every $\varepsilon>0$, $\varepsilon'\in(0,\varepsilon]$ and
all large $k$,
\[
2^{-15}me^{-4m(\varepsilon+2)}a_k^4\le\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon),\qquad
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\le(2+a_0)^4a_k^4 ;
\]
so \textup{(SD$_{\mathrm p}$)} holds with $p_0=0$, and \textup{(UV)}.

\textup{(c)} $R_k(g)\to0$ for every $g\in C_c(\R^4)$ supported in
$\{x_0>0\}$: \textup{(NE)} fails, and every limit $S_2$ of
Corollary~\textup{\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b)} vanishes identically.

\textup{(d)} With $Z_k=\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$,
\textup{(M$_{\mathrm s}$)} holds and every limit of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:limitEsep}} is trivial. With $Z_k=C_k(\sigma_0)
^{-1/2}$, formed from any $f_0$ as before Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:limitEnorm}}
with $C_k(0)>0$, \textup{(M$_{\mathrm s,2}$)} fails.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(a) $D_k$ is positive and invariant under permutations and sign changes of
the coordinates, so the covariance is positive definite, real, and
invariant under translations and $B_4$. Write $u_\mu=2-2\cos p_\mu$, and for
$p\in(-\pi,\pi]^3$ let $b(p)=A_ku_1u_2u_3$, $\lambda(p)\in[0,1)$ the root of
$\lambda+\lambda^{-1}=2+1/b(p)$ ($\lambda=0$ if $b(p)=0$), $E(p)=
-\log\lambda(p)$ and $w(p)=(1-\lambda(p))/(1+\lambda(p))=\tanh(E(p)/2)$. Then
$1+b\,u_0=b\lambda^{-1}(1-\lambda e^{ip_0})(1-\lambda e^{-ip_0})$, and
expanding $(1-\lambda^2)/((1-\lambda e^{ip_0})(1-\lambda e^{-ip_0}))=\sum_t
\lambda^{|t|}e^{-ip_0t}$ and using $b(\lambda^{-1}-\lambda)=1/w$,
\[
\frac1{1+b\,u_0}=w\sum_{t\in\Z}\lambda^{|t|}e^{-ip_0t},
\]
also for $b=0$. So the covariance of $\phi_k(t,\mathbf x)$ and $\phi_k(s,
\mathbf y)$ is $\int e^{ip\cdot(\mathbf x-\mathbf y)}w(p)\lambda(p)^{|t-s|}
dp/(2\pi)^3$. Let $\gamma$ be the centred Gaussian law on $\R^{\Z^3}$ with
covariance the multiplier $w$, $\Lambda$ the convolution with multiplier
$\lambda$, and $\xi_t$ independent of law the centred Gaussian with
multiplier $w(1-\lambda^2)\ge0$. The stationary chain $\phi_{t+1}=\Lambda
\phi_t+\xi_{t+1}$ has covariance with multiplier $w\lambda^{|t-s|}$, so it is
the field; it is reversible because $\Lambda$ and the covariance of
$\gamma$ commute and are symmetric. The proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a) applies with the operator $\Lambda$
in place of $q$: the transition operator satisfies $P\mathcal E_h=
\mathcal E_{\Lambda h}$, so it acts on the $N$-th Wiener chaos as the $N$-th
tensor power of the restriction of $\Lambda$ to the one-particle space, the
completion of the finitely supported $h$ for $\int|\hat h|^2w\,dp/(2\pi)^3$,
on which $\Lambda$ is multiplication by $\lambda$. Since $b\le64A_k$ with
equality only at $p=(\pi,\pi,\pi)$, $\cosh E=1+1/(2b)\ge1+1/(128A_k)=
\cosh(ma_k)$, so $0\le\lambda\le e^{-ma_k}$, with equality at that point.
Hence $0\le P\le e^{-ma_kN}$ on the $N$-th chaos for $N\ge1$, and
$e^{-ma_k}$ lies in the spectrum on the first, $\lambda$ being continuous and
$w$ positive. Reflection positivity and the identification of the transfer
matrix with $P$ follow as there, and $B_4$ gives the other directions.

For $h$ supported in $\{x_0\ge1\}$ and $\vartheta$ fixing $\{x_0=c\}$,
$|c|\le\frac12$, the time component of $y-\vartheta x$ is $x_0+y_0-2c\ge0$,
and
\[
\omega_k\bigl(\vartheta(\overline{A_k(h)})A_k(h)\bigr)=\int w|\hat H|^2
\frac{dp}{(2\pi)^3},
\]
where
\[
\hat H(p)=\sum_{x_0}a_k\lambda^{x_0-c}\hat h_{x_0}(p),\qquad\hat h_{x_0}(p)
=a_k^3\sum_{\mathbf x}h(a_kx)e^{ip\cdot\mathbf x} .
\]
Let $h$ be supported in $\{x_0\ge t\}$ with $t\ge a_k$. Since
$\sum_{x_0\ge t/a_k}\lambda^{x_0-c}\le\lambda^{t/a_k-1/2}/(1-\lambda)$ and
$w/(1-\lambda)\le1$, the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality with the weights
$\lambda^{x_0-c}$ gives
\[
w|\hat H|^2\le a_k^2\lambda^{2t/a_k-1}\sum_{x_0}|\hat h_{x_0}|^2 .\tag{$*$}
\]

(b) For $h$ supported in $B_{\varepsilon'}$ with $\|h\|_\infty\le1$ and
$a_k\le\min(\varepsilon',1)$, ($*$) with $\lambda\le1$ and Parseval's
identity give at most $a_k^8$ times the number of sites $x$ with $a_kx\in
B_{\varepsilon'}$, which is at most $(2+a_0)^4a_k^{-4}$. For the lower bound
let $a_k\le\frac1{20}$, $ma_k\le2$, let $b_0$ be continuous with values in
$[0,1]$, supported in $[\varepsilon,\varepsilon+1]$ and equal to $1$ on
$[\varepsilon+\frac14,\varepsilon+\frac34]$, let $b_1$ be the function equal
to $1$ on $[\frac14,\frac34]$, to $0$ outside $[0,1]$ and linear in between,
and $h_k(y)=b_0(y_0)\prod_{i=1}^3\cos(\pi y_i/a_k)b_1(y_i)$, supported in
$B_\varepsilon$ with $\|h_k\|_\infty\le1$. Since $\cos(\pi x_i)=(-1)^{x_i}$,
$\hat H(p)=T(\lambda)\prod_i\beta(p_i+\pi)$ with $T(\lambda)=\sum_{x_0}a_k
b_0(a_kx_0)\lambda^{x_0-c}$ and $\beta(q)=\sum_na_kb_1(a_kn)e^{iqn}$. On
$Q=\{p:|p_i-\pi|\le\frac\pi3\bmod2\pi\}$, $u_i\ge3$, so $b\ge27A_k$ and
$\cosh E-1\le1/(54A_k)\le4(\cosh(ma_k)-1)\le\cosh(2ma_k)-1$, since $\cosh
2x-1=2(\cosh x-1)(\cosh x+1)$; so $ma_k\le E\le2ma_k$ on $Q$. Then $w\ge
\tanh(ma_k/2)\ge ma_k/4$, and $T(\lambda)\ge e^{-2ma_k((\varepsilon+1)/a_k+
1/2)}\sum_{x_0}a_kb_0(a_kx_0)\ge e^{-2m(\varepsilon+2)}/4$. By Parseval's
identity $\int_{-\pi}^\pi|\beta|^2dq/2\pi=a_k^2\sum_nb_1(a_kn)^2\ge a_k/4$,
and since $|1-e^{iq}|\ge1$ for $|q|\ge\frac\pi3$ and $b_1$ is $4$-Lipschitz,
\[
\int_{\frac\pi3\le|q|\le\pi}|\beta|^2\frac{dq}{2\pi}\le a_k^2\sum_n|b_1(a_kn)
-b_1(a_kn-a_k)|^2\le48a_k^3 ,
\]
so $\int_{|q|\le\pi/3}|\beta|^2dq/2\pi\ge a_k/8$. Restricting the integral
to $Q$ gives
\[
\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)\ge\frac{ma_k}4\cdot\frac{e^{-4m(\varepsilon+2)}}{16}\Bigl(\frac{a_k}8\Bigr)^3 .
\]
(SD$_{\mathrm p}$) follows with $C_0=2^{15}(2+a_0)^4e^{4m(\varepsilon+2)}/m$, and (UV) by Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}(ii),
whose proof uses only the invariance, the reflection positivity and the gap.

(c) Let $g$ be supported in $\{x_0\ge t_0\}$, $t_0>0$, and in a cube of side
$L$, and $\delta\in(0,1]$. Let $V$ be the set of $p$ with $|p_i|<\delta$ for
some $i$. There $u_i<\delta^2$ for that $i$, so $b\le16A_k\delta^2$ and
$\cosh E-1\ge4(\cosh(ma_k)-1)/\delta^2\ge2(ma_k/\delta)^2$; since $\cosh x-1
\le x^2$ for $0\le x\le1$, $E\ge ma_k/\delta$ for $ma_k\le\delta$. By ($*$)
and Parseval's identity the part of the integral over $V$ is at most
$e^{-(m/\delta)(2t_0-a_k)}a_k^8$ times $\sum_x|g(a_kx)|^2\le(L+1)^4a_k^{-4}
\|g\|_\infty^2$. On the complement, $|p_1|\ge\delta$ and $|1-e^{ip_1}|\ge
2\delta/\pi$, and ($*$) with $\lambda\le1$ bounds that part by
\[
\begin{split}
&\Bigl(\frac\pi{2\delta}\Bigr)^2a_k^8\sum_x|g(a_kx)-g(a_kx-a_ke_1)|^2\\
&\quad\le\Bigl(\frac\pi{2\delta}\Bigr)^2(L+2)^4a_k^4\varrho(a_k)^2,
\end{split}
\]
with $\varrho$ the modulus of continuity of $g$ for $|\cdot|_\infty$. With
(b),
\[
\limsup_kR_k(g)\le\frac{2^{15}(L+2)^4\|g\|_\infty^2}{m\,e^{-4m(\varepsilon+
2)}}e^{-2mt_0/\delta},
\]
and $\delta\to0$ gives $R_k(g)\to0$. The last claim is
Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b).

(d) By (b) and Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(a), whose proof uses only the
invariance, the reflection positivity and the gap, (M$_{\mathrm s,2}$) holds
for $Z_k=\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$. The moments of a centred Gaussian
field are sums over pairings of products of covariances, and every pair of
a separated tuple is separated; so (M$_{\mathrm s}$) holds with $M_n\le
(n-1)!!M_2^{n/2}$, and by (c) every limit vanishes for $n\ge1$. For $Z_k=
C_k(\sigma_0)^{-1/2}$, (M$_{\mathrm s,2}$) would give, as in the proof of
Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(a), $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon')\le
M_2(Q,\varepsilon')C_k(\sigma_0)$ for $\varepsilon'<\varepsilon$, while
$C_k(\sigma_0)\le C_k(0)=R_k(f_0)\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(i), $R_k(f_0)\to0$ by (c), and $\mathcal
N_k(\varepsilon')\ge\mathsf K^{-2}\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)$.
\end{proof}

So (NE) is an input of its own: reflection positivity, the invariance, a
gap bounded above and below in physical units and (SD$_{\mathrm p}$) do not
give it, and with the normalisation $Z_k=\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$
the separated moment bound holds for every $n$ while every limit is trivial.
By Proposition~\ref{prop:limitElm}, under (SD) it is exactly the statement
that bounded spatial momenta carry a fixed fraction of the largest reflected
norm, and by Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEabelian} it follows from (SD) for
$\U(1)$. The example is not a lattice gauge theory. For the Wilson measure
along the tuning the regimes are these: under a gap in lattice units, in
particular in the strong-coupling range of Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEstrong},
(SD) fails and the rate $\mathfrak E_k$ diverges like $a_k^{-1}$; under the
short-distance law (AF) of Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEaf}, which asymptotic
freedom predicts, (SD$_{\mathrm p}$) and (NE) hold; which of the two
occurs, if either, is not decided here.

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{figures/fig_limitlogic.png}
\caption{The conditions of Sec.~\ref{ssec:limitE}, given the averaged condition
along the tuning. Bottom: inputs on reflected norms of packets under the
Wilson measure, none of them proved. Middle: lattice bounds; (M$_{\mathrm
s}$) is (RN) (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEreflect}), and (G$_{\mathrm s}$)
is (RN) with factorial constants (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitErate}). Top:
what the bounds give in the limit (Theorems~\ref{thm:limitEsep}
and~\ref{thm:limitEtempered}, Corollaries~\ref{cor:limitEOS}
and~\ref{cor:limitEexact}). From the bottom row: Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtwo}
and Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEtwotempered} for $n=2$,
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEdomination} for every $n$,
Theorems~\ref{thm:limitErot} and~\ref{thm:limitEunique}. Crossed arrows
fail in examples: the two-point properties do not give (RN$_m$), $m\ge2$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEthree}), and the bounds of the middle row do
not give (W$_{\mathrm s}$) (Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a)).}\label{fig:limitlogic}
\end{figure*}

\begin{remark}\label{rem:limitEscope}
\emph{What is proved.} Given the averaged condition along the tuning,
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEsep} turns (M$_{\mathrm s}$) into Schwinger
functions at separated points that are symmetric, invariant under the
translations and $B_4$, reflection positive in every coordinate direction,
with a Hamiltonian of gap $m_*$ in every coordinate direction and
exponential clustering in every direction; with the constants of
Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(ii) they are nontrivial, and with
(W$_{\mathrm s}$) and (U$_{\mathrm s}$) they converge and are
$\mathrm O(4)$-invariant (Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEexact}). The averaged
condition enters only through Lemma~\ref{lem:limitElattice}(iii), that is,
through Corollary~\ref{cor:avggap}. For the two-point functions,
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtwo} and Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo} reduce
(M$_{\mathrm s}$) for $n=2$ and nontriviality to (SD) and (NE), statements about
reflected norms of single packets under the Wilson measure. For the
rotations, Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEaniso} shows that the anisotropy of a
separated two-point kernel has no components of degree below four and the
angular profile $Y_4$ in degree four, and Theorem~\ref{thm:limitErot}
derives (W$_{\mathrm s}$) from (SB$_n$) and (RI$_n$); for the limit,
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEunique} derives (U$_{\mathrm s}$) from
(MC$_{m_0}$) and (O$_{\mathrm s}$). For the test functions of~\cite{OS73},
Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEtempered} extends every $S_n$ whose separated moments
grow at most like a power of the inverse separation, and under
(G$_{\mathrm s}$) gives the linear growth condition of~\cite{OS75},
reflection positivity and clustering on $\mathcal S_0$
(Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEOS}); for $n=2$, (G$_{\mathrm s}$) is the rate
(SD$_{\mathrm p}$) in (SD) (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEtwotempered}). For
every $n$, (G$_{\mathrm s}$) is (RN) with constants
$K_1^m(m!)^{\beta_1}(1+L)^{q_1m}\delta^{-p_1m}$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitErate}), and the inequality (GD) gives
(M$_{\mathrm s}$) from (SD) and (G$_{\mathrm s}$) from (SD$_{\mathrm p}$)
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEdomination}).

\emph{Which inputs are needed.} (M$_{\mathrm s}$) with constants that keep a
reflected correlation fixed is not implied by the averaged condition, by
Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEstrong}. (W$_{\mathrm s}$) is not implied by the
other properties: the Gaussian field of
Proposition~\ref{prop:osobstruction}(a) is invariant under translations and
$B_4$, reflection positive in the coordinate hyperplanes, has gap $ma_k$ in
every coordinate direction, satisfies (M) and hence (M$_{\mathrm s}$) by
Theorem~\ref{thm:ostree}(i), and the proofs above use the averaged condition
only through these properties, with $m$ in place of $m_*$. Its limit has
the continuous kernel $\kappa e^{-m\|x-y\|_1}$, so invariance under $R$ on
the separated pairs forces $\|Rz\|_1=\|z\|_1$ for $z\ne0$, and $G_S=B_4$.
The same example at two interleaved masses violates (U$_{\mathrm s}$), by
the argument of Proposition~\ref{prop:uniquelimit}(ii); it satisfies
(O$_{\mathrm s}$) and violates (MC$_{m_0}$) for every $m_0$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEunique}(iii)). Products of independent copies of
the field are not Gaussian, have the same properties and $G_S=B_4$, and
violate (RI$_2$) (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEproduct}). The normalisation
by $C_k(\sigma_0)$ does not fix the scale (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEscale}).
The bounds (RN$_m$), $m\ge2$, are not implied by the two-point properties:
the fields of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEthree}, bounded ones included, have
the invariance, the reflection positivity, gap $m$, (SD), (SD$_{\mathrm p}$),
(UV), (NE) and nonzero two-point limits, and violate (M$_{\mathrm s,n}$) for
every $n\ge3$ and (RN$_m$) for every $m\ge2$. Bounds at each order do not
give (G$_{\mathrm s}$): the fields of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEfactorial}
satisfy (M$_{\mathrm s}$), (RN) and (SD$_{\mathrm p}$), with moment bounds
uniform in the separation, and their moments grow faster than
$K^n(n!)^\beta$ for all $K,\beta$.

\emph{Short-distance law.} The two-sided condition (AF), the lattice form of
the law $|x-y|^{-8}(\log\frac1{|x-y|})^{-\gamma}$ that asymptotic freedom
predicts with $\gamma=2$ (Remark~\ref{rem:limitEafpert}), gives
(M$_{\mathrm s,2}$), (SD$_{\mathrm p}$) with $p_0=4$, (SD), (UV) and (NE),
excludes trivial limits and fails in the strong-coupling range
(Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEaf}). It fixes $Z_k$ up to bounded factors, with
$Z_k\to\infty$ and $Z_k$ comparable to $\mathcal N_k(\varepsilon)^{-1/2}$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEafscale}), it is consistent with a gap in
physical units and with (U$_{\mathrm s}$), and it implies neither
(W$_{\mathrm s}$) nor the exact law (AF$_\infty$)
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEafexample}). It is not proved for the Wilson
measure; the missing inequality is stated in
Remark~\ref{rem:limitEafstatus}.

\emph{Relation to Theorem~\ref{thm:srladder}.} Under the hypothesis of
Theorem~\ref{thm:transfer}, Corollary~\ref{cor:srladder} shows that, when
$C_k(0)>0$, (SR$_0$) fails at every spacing for the slice field of $\Phi$
or for that of $\Delta\Phi$. Nothing here
uses (SR$_0$): the positivity used is that of the Wilson measure for
products of packets with supports at distance at least $\varepsilon$ from
the reflection plane, that is, for functions of links at lattice distance at
least $\varepsilon/a_k-r$ from it, and $r\ge1$ for every nonconstant
$\Phi$. The nontriviality of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(ii) comes
from (M$_{\mathrm s}$) and the choice of $Z_k$, not from positivity at short
distance.

\emph{What is not proved.} (M$_{\mathrm s}$) for $n\ge3$ with the constants
of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnorm}(ii), equivalently the bounds (RN$_m$),
$m\ge2$, on reflected norms of products of separated packets
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEreflect}), which do not follow from the
two-point properties, reflection positivity and the gap
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEthree}), and the inequality (GD) that gives them
from (SD) (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEdomination}); (SD), equivalently the spectral
condition (UV) (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEsd}), which fails under a gap in
lattice units, is not implied by reflection positivity and a gap bounded
in physical units (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEheavy}), is a bound in
physical units on the rate $\mathfrak E_k$ at which the largest reflected
norm decays (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEenergy}), and follows from the
short-distance law (AF) (Theorem~\ref{thm:limitEaf}); (NE), which under (SD) is the
nontriviality of every limit (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEne}) and the
condition (LM) that spatial momenta bounded in physical units carry a fixed
fraction of the largest reflected norm (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitElm}),
which follows from (SD) for $\U(1)$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEabelian}) and
is not implied by (SD$_{\mathrm p}$), reflection positivity, the invariance
and a gap bounded above and below in physical units
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEflat});
(W$_{\mathrm s}$), and (SB$_n$), (RI$_n$), of which (SB$_2$) is a bound on
the axis and is not implied by the separated moment bounds
(Propositions~\ref{prop:limitEaxial} and~\ref{prop:limitEgauss}(b));
(U$_{\mathrm s}$), and (MC$_{m_0}$), of which the two halves hold separately
with different rates (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEbracket}), and
(O$_{\mathrm s}$), equivalently (DS) (Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEdilate});
whether $\mathrm O(4)$ invariance of $S_2$
together with the other properties gives (W$_{\mathrm s}$); the rate
(G$_{\mathrm s}$) at coinciding points, equivalently (RN$_{\mathrm p}$)
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitErate}), and its form (SD$_{\mathrm p}$) for
$n=2$, which with (M$_{\mathrm s}$) does not give (G$_{\mathrm s}$)
(Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEfactorial}); and the averaged condition along
the tuning. None of these is proved
here, and the mass gap of the continuum theory is not proved. The extension
of the $S_n$ to the test functions of~\cite{OS73} with the linear growth
condition of~\cite{OS75} is therefore proved only under (G$_{\mathrm s}$),
and it does not follow from the properties at separated points: the
two-point function of Proposition~\ref{prop:limitEnotempered}(ii) has those
that Corollary~\ref{cor:limitEtwo}(b) gives and is not tempered.
\end{remark}

\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}

For every compact simple gauge group, four-dimensional lattice Yang--Mills
theory has a unique infinite-volume state, exponential clustering and a
transfer-matrix gap whenever $N\lvert\beta\rvert<\frac2{29}\kappa_G$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:groups}), and plaquette blocks extend the range for
$\SU(2)$ and $\SU(3)$ (Theorems~\ref{thm:blocks} and~\ref{thm:su3blocks}).
One averaged estimate in a cube of fixed physical size, the bound
$\mathrm{BP}(n_k,\varpi)$ with $n_ka_k\le\ell$, places the spectrum of every
continuum limit in $\{0\}\cup[16\varpi/(625e^2\ell),\infty)$, so every
nontrivial limit has a mass gap (Theorem~\ref{thm:avgtransfer}). The
averaged estimate holds on every torus at strong coupling
(Corollary~\ref{cor:bpranges}). The free massive scalar and Proca fields satisfy the
averaged estimate itself on every torus, uniformly in the lattice spacing
(Corollary~\ref{cor:ffuniform}). For the scalar field the best constant is
the response of one cube to a constant shift of its boundary data, and it
converges, as the spacing goes to zero, to $s^2\langle1,(-\Delta+s^2)^{-1}1\rangle$
on the unit cube with $s=m\ell$ (Theorems~\ref{ffs:thm:exact}
and~\ref{ffs:thm:limit}); no constant survives as $m\ell\to0$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:ffupper}), so for these fields the estimate holds
exactly when a mass is present at the scale of the cube. The free photon violates it at the
diffusive rate $\varpi\le8\pi^2(n+1)^2/L^2$ (Theorem~\ref{rD:maxwell}); every
measure satisfying it has local observables of finite susceptibility at the
scale of the cube (Proposition~\ref{rD:suscept}); and for $\SU(2)$ at
weak coupling it holds on plaquette traces and on Wilson loops of fixed
size with a constant independent of $\beta$ (Corollary~\ref{rDtwo:bpplaquette}
and Theorem~\ref{rDtwo:loops}), while on all gauge-invariant functions it
fails at fixed $n$ as $\beta\to\infty$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:bpfixed}). The estimate is not
monotone in the Hessian (Theorem~\ref{thm:rSnonmono}), but it survives
convex perturbations with bounded Hessian, in particular convex plaquette
potentials added to the Proca field (Theorem~\ref{thm:rSwitten} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:rSplaquette}); bounded perturbations can destroy it, as the
Ising model shows (Proposition~\ref{prop:rSising}), and the Wilson action has
flat directions in every gauge, so these arguments do not reach it
(Corollary~\ref{cor:rSnoapply}).

Two inputs remain: the averaged estimate along the tuning, which is the
cube estimate (D) in averaged form, and a nontrivial continuum limit with the
properties of Sec.~\ref{ssec:limitE}, which is (E). Without any of them the torus limits
exist and are reflection positive at every coupling, and their two-point
functions have renormalised continuum limits with a positive Hamiltonian
(Theorem~\ref{rE:two}); the short-distance law (AF) together with the
averaged estimate gives a nontrivial two-point theory with a mass gap, and
every nontrivial limit of these correlations along the whole tuning forces
(SD), (UV), (NE) and a bounded physical gap (Theorem~\ref{rE:suffices} and Proposition~\ref{rE:necessary}).
Along a lattice axis the connected plaquette correlation of every torus is
given by a transfer-matrix spectral formula at every coupling: it is
nonnegative and log-convex, and it is either positive at all separations
or zero at all of them; at strong coupling its leading term is
$108((1+\delta)/2)^{4t+1}\beta^{4t}$ (Theorems~\ref{rEtwo:axial}
and~\ref{rEtwo:taylor}). The lower half of (AF) along an axis is equivalent,
up to logarithms, to a lower bound at the cutoff scale together with a
lower bound on low-energy spectral weight at every intermediate scale
(Corollary~\ref{rEtwo:step}).
Given both inputs, the results above give the continuum theory a mass
gap; without them the mass gap is not proved here.

Lattice gauge fields also define four-dimensional gauge theories with
chiral fermions in every anomaly-free representation, once the lattice
field is interpolated to the continuum and coupled to continuum
fermions~\cite{Wit26}. The mass gap problem concerns the pure gauge
theory, to which every result here refers.

transfer matrix to Osterwalder and Schrader~\cite{OS73,OS75}, and their
lattice form, with the first proof of a gap at strong coupling, to
Osterwalder and Seiler~\cite{OSei78,Sei82}. The uniqueness method is
Dobrushin's~\cite{Dob68,Dob70,DS85}, the curvature method is that of Bakry
and \'Emery~\cite{BE85,BGL14} in the form of Shen, Zhu and Zhu~\cite{SZZ23},
the covariance estimate under Dobrushin's condition is F\"ollmer's~\cite{Fol82},
the chessboard estimate is that of Fr\"ohlich, Israel, Lieb and
Simon~\cite{FILS78} and Fr\"ohlich and Spencer~\cite{FS82}, disagreement
percolation is due to van den Berg and Maes~\cite{vdBM94}, the
Brascamp--Lieb inequality to Brascamp and Lieb~\cite{BL76}, the
correlation representation to Helffer and Sj\"ostrand~\cite{HS94} and
Naddaf and Spencer~\cite{NS97}, the perturbation lemma to Holley and
Stroock~\cite{HS87}, the derivative of a matrix function to Daleckii and
Krein~\cite{DK65}, and the strict positivity of the transfer matrix to
L\"uscher~\cite{Lus77},
the gauge fixing on a forest is Creutz's~\cite{Cre77}, and the curvature of
Lie groups is Milnor's~\cite{Mil76}. The analysis of the single-link law
uses results of Barta~\cite{Bar37}, Biernacki and Krzy\.z~\cite{BK55},

the continuum problem is the one formulated by Jaffe and Witten~\cite{JW06}
and studied by Ba{\l}aban~\cite{Bal84a,Bal85a,Bal89b} through the
renormalisation group, in the account of Dimock~\cite{Dim13}; gauge fields
with cut-offs were constructed by Brydges, Fr\"ohlich and
Seiler~\cite{BFS79} and by Magnen, Rivasseau and S\'en\'eor~\cite{MRS93};
recent probabilistic work on lattice Yang--Mills
theory is due to Chatterjee~\cite{Chat19,Chat19b}, Basu and

\section{The comparison lemma}\label{app:comparison}

Let $(G,\delta)$ be a compact group with a bi-invariant metric of diameter
$D$, $\Omega=G^A$, and $C_{ee'}$ the coefficients of Sec.~\ref{sec:state},
with row and column sums at most $\alpha<1$ and $C_{ee'}=0$ unless $e,e'$ share
a plaquette, so that $|e-e'|\le1$ for the distance of link midpoints. Fix
$\lambda>0$ with $\theta=\alpha e^\lambda<1$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:comparison}
Let $S\subset A$, possibly infinite, and let $\mu_1,\mu_2$ be probability
measures on $\Omega$ invariant under $\gamma^F_e$ for every $e\notin S$. Let
$T\subset A\setminus S$ be finite, $w_b=e^{-\lambda\dist(b,T)}$, and suppose
$\sum_{z\in S}w_z<\infty$. There is a coupling of $\mu_1,\mu_2$ whose link
distances $\varrho(b)=\E\,\delta(\sigma_b,\sigma'_b)$ satisfy
\[
\sum_{b\notin S}w_b\varrho(b)\le\frac{\theta D}{1-\theta}\sum_{z\in S}w_z .
\]
If $S=\emptyset$ then $\mu_1=\mu_2$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The couplings form a weakly compact set and $\Psi(\pi)=\sum_bw_b\E_\pi\delta(
\sigma_b,\sigma'_b)$ is weakly continuous because $\sum_bw_b<\infty$; let $\pi^*$ be
a minimiser. For $e\notin S$ replace the pair $(\sigma_e,\sigma'_e)$ by a sample of
an optimal coupling of $\gamma^F_e(\cdot\mid\sigma)$ and $\gamma^F_e(\cdot\mid
\sigma')$, chosen measurably~\cite[Cor.~5.22]{Vil09}. Both $\mu_i$ are
invariant, so the result is again a coupling, and changing the finitely
many links that enter $\gamma^F_e$ one at a time gives $W_1\le\sum_{e'}C_{ee'}
\delta(\sigma_{e'},\sigma'_{e'})$. Minimality gives $\varrho(e)\le\sum_{e'}C_{ee'}
\varrho(e')$. Multiply by $w_e$ and sum over $e\notin S$; where $C_{ee'}\ne0$,
$w_e\le e^\lambda w_{e'}$, so
\[
\sum_{e\notin S}w_e\varrho(e)\le\theta\Bigl[\sum_{e'\notin S}w_{e'}\varrho(e')
+\sum_{z\in S}w_z\varrho(z)\Bigr],
\]
all sums are finite, and $\varrho\le D$ on $S$. If $S=\emptyset$, $\varrho\equiv0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:decay}
Let $\mu$ be the Gibbs measure of $\gamma^F$, $F$ a local function with
support $S_F$, $L_F$-Lipschitz in each link, and $H$ bounded with finite
support $S_H$ disjoint from $S_F$. Then
\[
|\Cov_\mu(F,H)|\le\|H\|_\infty L_F\frac{\theta D}{1-\theta}|S_H|\,
e^{-\lambda\dist(S_F,S_H)}.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Condition on the links of $S_H$~\cite[Ch.~3]{Kal21}; the conditional
measures $\mu^\sigma=\mu(\cdot\mid\sigma_{S_H})$ are invariant under
$\gamma^F_e$ for $e\notin S_H$. Lemma~\ref{lem:comparison} with $S=S_H$ and
$T=S_F$, for which $w_b=1$ on $S_F$ and $w_z\le e^{-\lambda\dist(S_F,S_H)}$ on
$S_H$, gives
$|\mu^\sigma(F)-\mu^{\sigma'}(F)|\le L_F\sum_{b\in S_F}\varrho(b)\le
L_F\frac{\theta D}{1-\theta}|S_H|e^{-\lambda\dist(S_F,S_H)}$ for any two
boundary values. Since $H$ depends only on $\sigma_{S_H}$,
$\Cov_\mu(F,H)=\iint H(\sigma)\bigl(\mu^\sigma(F)-\mu^{\sigma'}(F)\bigr)\,
d\mu(\sigma)\,d\mu(\sigma')$, and the bound follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:blockcomparison}
Let the blocks partition $A$, let $C_{Bj}$ be the block coefficients of
Sec.~\ref{sec:plaqblocks}, with $\sum_BC_{Bj}\le\alpha<1$ for every $j$ and
$C_{Bj}=0$ unless every link of $B$ lies within distance $2$ of $j$, and fix
$\lambda>0$ with $\theta=\alpha e^{2\lambda}<1$. Let $S\subset A$ be a union
of blocks and let $\mu_1,\mu_2$ be invariant under $\gamma^{\mathcal B}_B$ for
every block $B\not\subset S$. With $T$, $w_b$ as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:comparison} and $\sum_{z\in S}w_z<\infty$, there is a coupling
with $\sum_{b\notin S}w_b\varrho(b)\le\frac{\theta D}{1-\theta}\sum_{z\in S}w_z$,
and $\mu_1=\mu_2$ if $S=\emptyset$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Take a minimiser $\pi^*$ of $\Psi$ as before. For a block $B\not\subset S$
replace $(\sigma_B,\sigma'_B)$ by an optimal coupling, for the metric $d$, of
the two block laws; it is again a coupling, and changing the boundary links
one at a time bounds the expected new $d$-distance by $\sum_jC_{Bj}\varrho(j)$.
Put $\bar w_B=\max_{i\in B}w_i$. Minimality gives
\[
\sum_{i\in B}w_i\varrho(i)\le\bar w_B\sum_jC_{Bj}\varrho(j),
\]
and $\bar w_B\le e^{2\lambda}w_j$ whenever $C_{Bj}\ne0$. Summing over the
blocks outside $S$ and using the column sums,
\[
\sum_{b\notin S}w_b\varrho(b)\le\theta\Bigl[\sum_{j\notin S}w_j\varrho(j)+
\sum_{z\in S}w_z\varrho(z)\Bigr],
\]
which gives the claim as in Lemma~\ref{lem:comparison}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:dscomparison}
Let $n\ge2$ be even, let $V_x$, $x\in2\Z^4$, be the translates of
Sec.~\ref{sec:bypass}, and suppose that $D(n,\alpha)$ holds.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)},leftmargin=*]
\item If a probability measure $\mu$ on $G^A$ is invariant under
$\gamma^F_e$ for every $e$ in a finite set $V$, it is invariant under
$\gamma_V$.
\item Fix $\lambda>0$ with $\theta=\alpha e^{\lambda\rho_n}<1$. Let $S\subset
A$, and let $\mu_1,\mu_2$ be invariant under $\gamma_{V_x}$ for every $x$
with $V_x\cap S=\emptyset$. With $T$, $w_b$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:comparison}
and $\sum_{z\in S}w_z<\infty$, there is a coupling with
\[
\sum_{b\notin S}w_b\varrho(b)\le\frac{D(1+c_ne^{\lambda\rho_n})}{1-\theta}
\sum_{z\in S}w_z ,
\]
and $\mu_1=\mu_2$ if $S=\emptyset$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) Let $\nu_\eta$ be the conditional law of $Q_V$ given the links $\eta$
outside $V$. For bounded $f$ on $G^V$ and bounded $g$ of $\eta$,
$\gamma^F_e(fg)=g\,\gamma^F_ef$ for $e\in V$, so invariance gives $\int
g(\eta)\bigl(\nu_\eta(\gamma^F_ef)-\nu_\eta(f)\bigr)\,d\mu=0$ for all $g$;
taking $f$ in a countable dense set of continuous functions, $\nu_\eta$ is
invariant under every $\gamma^F_e(\cdot\mid\cdot,\eta)$, $e\in V$, for
$\mu$-almost every $\eta$. So is $\gamma_V(\cdot\mid\eta)$, whose
conditional laws of single links are the $\gamma^F_e$. The composition $P_\eta$
of the kernels $\gamma^F_e$, $e\in V$, in a fixed order has a density with
respect to Haar measure on $G^V$ bounded below by a positive constant,
because each single-link density is at least $e^{-12N^2\beta}$ times its
maximum over the group. A Markov kernel with such a lower bound has at most
one invariant probability measure (Doeblin's condition), so
$\nu_\eta=\gamma_V(\cdot\mid\eta)$.

(b) Let $\pi^*$ minimise $\Psi$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:comparison} and call $x$
admissible if $V_x\cap S=\emptyset$. For admissible $x$ replace
$(\sigma_{V_x},\sigma'_{V_x})$ by an optimal coupling, for $d_{V_x}$, of the
two block laws; both $\mu_i$ are invariant, so the result is a coupling, and
changing the boundary links one at a time bounds the new expected
$d_{V_x}$-distance by $\sum_jC_{V_x,j}\varrho(j)$. With $\bar w_x=\max_{i\in
V_x}w_i$, minimality gives
\[
\sum_{i\in V_x}w_i\varrho(i)\le\bar w_x\sum_{j\notin V_x}C_{V_x,j}\varrho(j),
\]
and $\bar w_x\le e^{\lambda\rho_n}w_j$ whenever $C_{V_x,j}\ne0$. Let $S'$ be
the set of links outside $S$ that lie in a translate meeting $S$; each is
within distance $\rho_n$ of a link of $S$, so $\sum_{i\in S'}w_i\le
c_ne^{\lambda\rho_n}\sum_{z\in S}w_z$. A link outside $S\cup S'$ lies in
$N_n$ translates, all admissible. Summing over admissible $x$ and using
$\sum_{x:\,j\notin V_x}C_{V_x,j}=K_{c(j)}\le\alpha N_n$,
\[
N_n\sum_{i\notin S\cup S'}w_i\varrho(i)\le\theta N_n\Bigl[\sum_{i\notin
S\cup S'}w_i\varrho(i)+\sum_{i\in S\cup S'}w_i\varrho(i)\Bigr].
\]
All sums are finite and $\varrho\le D$, so $\sum_{i\notin S\cup S'}w_i
\varrho(i)\le\frac{\theta D}{1-\theta}(1+c_ne^{\lambda\rho_n})\sum_{z\in S}
w_z$; adding $\sum_{i\in S'}w_i\varrho(i)\le Dc_ne^{\lambda\rho_n}\sum_{z\in
S}w_z$ gives the bound. If $S=\emptyset$ then $S'=\emptyset$ and
$\varrho\equiv0$.
\end{proof}

The covariance bound of Corollary~\ref{cor:decay} follows from part (b) in
the same way, with $\theta D/(1-\theta)$ replaced by
$D(1+c_ne^{\lambda\rho_n})/(1-\theta)$, since the conditional measures
$\mu^\sigma$ there are invariant under $\gamma_{V_x}$ for $V_x\cap
S_H=\emptyset$ by part (a).

\section{The plaquette blocks}\label{app:plaqblocks}

Table~\ref{tab:plaqblocks} lists the block plaquettes of
Sec.~\ref{sec:plaqblocks} modulo $2\Z^4$: the plaquette at $x$ in the plane
$\{\mu,\nu\}$ is a block plaquette exactly when $x$ modulo two and
$\{\mu,\nu\}$ appear in the table. Eight lie in the plane $\{0,1\}$ and eight
in $\{2,3\}$; together they contain the $48$ active links of a cell of side
two, each once.

\begin{table}[h]

(digits $x_0x_1x_2x_3$ modulo two) and the plane.}
\label{tab:plaqblocks}
\begin{ruledtabular}
\begin{tabular}{cc|cc}
$x$&plane&$x$&plane\\
\colrule
0000&$\{0,1\}$&0000&$\{2,3\}$\\
0001&$\{0,1\}$&0011&$\{2,3\}$\\
